## I. How a Retired Provision Merchant Felt the Impulse of Spring / 一、一位退休食品杂货商感到了春的冲动

===Original===
Mr. Dickson McCunn completed the polishing of his smooth cheeks with the towel, glanced appreciatively at their reflection in the looking-glass, and then permitted his eyes to stray out of the window. In the little garden lilacs were budding, and there was a gold line of daffodils beside the tiny greenhouse. Beyond the sooty wall a birch flaunted its new tassels, and the jackdaws were circling about the steeple of the Guthrie Memorial Kirk. A blackbird whistled from a thorn-bush, and Mr. McCunn was inspired to follow its example. He began a tolerable version of “Roy’s Wife of Aldivalloch.”

===Chinese===
迪克森·麦坎（Dickson McCunn）先生用毛巾把光洁的双颊最后擦拭停当，满意地朝镜中的面容瞥了一眼，然后任由目光溜出窗外。小小的花园里丁香正在抽芽，小型玻璃温室旁有一线金黄的水仙。煤烟熏黑的围墙外，一株白桦摇曳着新生的柔荑，寒鸦绕着格思里纪念教堂（Guthrie Memorial Kirk）的尖顶盘旋。一只乌鸫在荆棘丛中啭鸣，麦坎先生一时心动，也要效仿一番，便吹起了一支还算像样的《奥尔德维洛克的罗伊之妻》（Roy's Wife of Aldivalloch）。

===Original===
He felt singularly lighthearted, and the immediate cause was his safety razor. A week ago he had bought the thing in a sudden fit of enterprise, and now he shaved in five minutes, where before he had taken twenty, and no longer confronted his fellows, at least one day in three, with a countenance ludicrously mottled by sticking-plaster. Calculation revealed to him the fact that in his fifty-five years, having begun to shave at eighteen, he had wasted three thousand three hundred and seventy hours﻿—or one hundred and forty days﻿—or between four and five months﻿—by his neglect of this admirable invention. Now he felt that he had stolen a march on Time. He had fallen heir, thus late, to a fortune in unpurchasable leisure.

===Chinese===
他觉得心情格外轻快，而直接的原因是他的安全剃刀。一星期前，他一时兴起买下了这玩意儿，如今刮脸只消五分钟，从前却要二十分钟；他也再不会每隔三天便有一天，顶着一张被橡皮膏贴得斑斑驳驳、滑稽可笑的脸去见人了。他细细一算，发现自己五十五年来——他从十八岁起开始刮脸——由于怠慢了这项了不起的发明，总共虚掷了三千三百七十个小时——折合一百四十天——也就是四五个月的时光。此刻他觉得自己抢在了时间前头。虽然迟暮，他到底继承了一笔金钱买不到的闲暇之财。

===Original===
He began to dress himself in the sombre clothes in which he had been accustomed for thirty-five years and more to go down to the shop in Mearns Street. And then a thought came to him which made him discard the grey-striped trousers, sit down on the edge of his bed, and muse.

===Chinese===
他开始穿上那身深色衣裳——三十多年来，他一直都是穿着它下米恩斯街（Mearns Street）的店铺。可就在这时，一个念头浮上心头，使他脱下灰条纹裤子，坐到床沿上沉思起来。

===Original===
Since Saturday the shop was a thing of the past. On Saturday at half-past eleven, to the accompaniment of a glass of dubious sherry, he had completed the arrangements by which the provision shop in Mearns Street, which had borne so long the legend of D. McCunn, together with the branches in Crossmyloof and the Shaws, became the property of a company, yclept the United Supply Stores, Limited. He had received in payment cash, debentures and preference shares, and his lawyers and his own acumen had acclaimed the bargain. But all the weekend he had been a little sad. It was the end of so old a song, and he knew no other tune to sing. He was comfortably off, healthy, free from any particular cares in life, but free too from any particular duties. “Will I be going to turn into a useless old man?” he asked himself.

===Chinese===
自星期六起，那家店铺已成过眼云烟。星期六上午十一点半，就着一杯来路可疑的雪利酒，他办完了全部手续——米恩斯街上那家门楣上长久题着“D. 麦坎”字号的食品杂货铺，连同克罗斯迈洛夫（Crossmyloof）与肖斯（the Shaws）的两家分店，从此归于一家名唤“联合供应商店有限公司”（United Supply Stores, Limited）的企业。他收到的对价是现款、债券和优先股，他的律师们连同他自己的精明，都交口称赞这笔买卖。可是整个周末他都有些郁郁寡欢。一支唱了这么多年的老歌就此终了，而他又不会别的调子。他衣食优裕，身体康健，生活中没有什么特别的烦忧，却也没有什么特别的职守。“俺难道要变成个没用的老头儿了不成？”他问自己。

===Original===
But he had woke up this Monday to the sound of the blackbird, and the world, which had seemed rather empty twelve hours before, was now brisk and alluring. His prowess in quick shaving assured him of his youth. “I’m no’ that dead old,” he observed, as he sat on the edge of the bed, to his reflection in the big looking-glass.

===Chinese===
可是这个星期一，他在乌鸫的啼声中醒来。十二个小时前还显得颇为空虚的世界，如今又变得生机勃勃、处处诱人。飞快刮脸的本事让他确信自己依然年轻。“俺还没老透呢，”他坐在床沿上，对着大镜子里的自己说。

===Original===
It was not an old face. The sandy hair was a little thin on the top and a little grey at the temples, the figure was perhaps a little too full for youthful elegance, and an athlete would have censured the neck as too fleshy for perfect health. But the cheeks were rosy, the skin clear, and the pale eyes singularly childlike. They were a little weak, those eyes, and had some difficulty in looking for long at the same object, so that Mr. McCunn did not stare people in the face, and had, in consequence, at one time in his career acquired a perfectly undeserved reputation for cunning. He shaved clean, and looked uncommonly like a wise, plump schoolboy. As he gazed at his simulacrum he stopped whistling “Roy’s Wife” and let his countenance harden into a noble sternness. Then he laughed, and observed in the language of his youth that “There was life in the auld dowg yet.” In that moment the soul of Mr. McCunn conceived the Great Plan.

===Chinese===
这不是一张苍老的脸。红黄色的头发头顶略稀、鬓角微霜；要论年轻人的挺拔，身形或许稍嫌富态；在运动健将眼里，那脖颈也嫌肉多，算不得十分康健。可是他双颊红润，皮肤清朗，一双浅色的眼睛透着异样的孩子气。那双眼睛有点儿没精神，难以长时间盯住同一件东西，因此麦坎先生从不正视人面，结果早年在营生上还平白落了个狡黠的名声。他脸刮得干干净净，活脱脱像个聪明丰满的学童。他凝视着镜中那个同自己一模一样的人，停住了吹《罗伊之妻》，让面容凝成一种高贵的严峻。随即他笑了，用他年轻时的乡音说道：“这条老狗还挺有活头呢。”就在这一刻，麦坎先生的灵魂里孕育出了那项宏图大计（the Great Plan）。

===Original===
The first sign of it was that he swept all his business garments unceremoniously on to the floor. The next that he rootled at the bottom of a deep drawer and extracted a most disreputable tweed suit. It had once been what I believe is called a Lovat mixture, but was now a nondescript subfusc, with bright patches of colour like moss on whinstone. He regarded it lovingly, for it had been for twenty years his holiday wear, emerging annually for a hallowed month to be stained with salt and bleached with sun. He put it on, and stood shrouded in an odour of camphor. A pair of thick nailed boots and a flannel shirt and collar completed the equipment of the sportsman. He had another long look at himself in the glass, and then descended whistling to breakfast. This time the tune was “Macgregor’s Gathering,” and the sound of it stirred the grimy lips of a man outside who was delivering coals﻿—himself a Macgregor﻿—to follow suit. Mr. McCunn was a very fountain of music that morning.

===Chinese===
宏图的第一步，是他毫不在意地把全部生意行头一扫而光，尽数拂到地板上。第二步，是他往一只深抽屉的底里翻捣半天，掏出一套寒酸透顶的花呢衣裳。它从前想必就是所谓的洛瓦特混色（Lovat mixture），如今已成了一种说不出名堂的暗色，只有几处鲜亮的色斑，像玄武岩上的青苔。他深情地端详着它——二十年来它一直是他的假日行头，每年都要出世一个神圣的月份，被海水渍出盐痕，被日光晒得发白。他把它穿上身，周身罩在一股樟脑气味里。再蹬上一双厚底钉靴，套上法兰绒衬衫和领子，这位运动家的装备便齐整了。他又对着镜子照了半晌，然后吹着口哨下楼吃早饭。这回吹的是《麦格雷戈集结曲》（Macgregor's Gathering），门外一个送煤人——他本人恰好也姓麦格雷戈——闻声也不禁翕动乌黑的嘴唇跟着吹了起来。那天早晨，麦坎先生真是一座音乐的喷泉。

===Original===
Tibby, the aged maid, had his newspaper and letters waiting by his plate, and a dish of ham and eggs frizzling near the fire. He fell to ravenously but still musingly, and he had reached the stage of scones and jam before he glanced at his correspondence. There was a letter from his wife now holidaying at the Neuk Hydropathic. She reported that her health was improving, and that she had met various people who had known somebody who had known somebody else whom she had once known herself. Mr. McCunn read the dutiful pages and smiled. “Mamma’s enjoying herself fine,” he observed to the teapot. He knew that for his wife the earthly paradise was a hydropathic, where she put on her afternoon dress and every jewel she possessed when she rose in the morning, ate large meals of which the novelty atoned for the nastiness, and collected an immense casual acquaintance with whom she discussed ailments, ministers, sudden deaths, and the intricate genealogies of her class. For his part he rancorously hated hydropathics, having once spent a black week under the roof of one in his wife’s company. He detested the food, the Turkish baths (he had a passionate aversion to baring his body before strangers), the inability to find anything to do and the compulsion to endless small talk. A thought flitted over his mind which he was too loyal to formulate. Once he and his wife had had similar likings, but they had taken different roads since their child died. Janet! He saw again﻿—he was never quite free from the sight﻿—the solemn little white-frocked girl who had died long ago in the spring.

===Chinese===
年迈的女仆蒂比（Tibby）已把报纸和信件摆在他的餐盘旁，火边一碟火腿煎蛋正滋滋作响。他狼吞虎咽地吃了起来，心里却仍在沉思，直到进行到烤饼蘸果酱那一程，才瞥了一眼来信。有一封是他妻子的，她此刻正在纽克水疗院（Neuk Hydropathic）休养。信里说她的身体日渐好转，还说她遇见了形形色色的人，这些人认识某人，某人又认识另一位她当年也认识的人。麦坎先生读完这几页恪尽妻职的信笺，微笑起来。“妈妈玩得挺痛快呢，”他对着茶壶说道。他知道，对他妻子来说，人间天堂莫过于一所水疗院：清晨一起床就穿上午后裙服、戴齐她全部的首饰，吃下一顿顿大餐——花样之新足以抵偿滋味之劣——还结识下一大帮泛泛之交，同她们谈论疾病、牧师、暴卒，以及她那个阶层盘根错节的亲缘谱系。至于他自己，他对水疗院恨之入骨，曾陪妻子在其中一家熬过了黑暗的一星期。他厌恶那里的伙食，厌恶土耳其浴（他对在生人面前裸露身体有一种火热的憎恶），厌恶找不到事做，又厌恶没完没了的寒暄客套。一个念头从他心头掠过，但他对妻子忠心，不肯让它成形。想当年，他和妻子原有着相近的喜好，可自打孩子死去，两人便分道而行了。珍妮特（Janet）！他又看见了——这景象他从没能完全摆脱——那个神情肃穆、身穿白裙的小姑娘，多年前在春天里夭折了。

===Original===
It may have been the thought of the Neuk Hydropathic, or more likely the thin clean scent of the daffodils with which Tibby had decked the table, but long ere breakfast was finished the Great Plan had ceased to be an airy vision and become a sober well-masoned structure. Mr. McCunn﻿—I may confess it at the start﻿—was an incurable romantic.

===Chinese===
也许是因为想到了纽克水疗院，更可能是因为蒂比装点餐桌的水仙散发的那股清淡幽香，早饭尚未吃完，宏图大计便已不再是缥缈的空想，而成了一座砌得严严实实的建筑。麦坎先生——我不妨开篇就坦白——是个不可救药的浪漫主义者。

===Original===
He had had a humdrum life since the day when he had first entered his uncle’s shop with the hope of some day succeeding that honest grocer; and his feet had never strayed a yard from his sober rut. But his mind, like the Dying Gladiator’s, had been far away. As a boy he had voyaged among books, and they had given him a world where he could shape his career according to his whimsical fancy. Not that Mr. McCunn was what is known as a great reader. He read slowly and fastidiously, and sought in literature for one thing alone. Sir Walter Scott had been his first guide, but he read the novels not for their insight into human character or for their historical pageantry, but because they gave him material wherewith to construct fantastic journeys. It was the same with Dickens. A lit tavern, a stagecoach, post-horses, the clack of hoofs on a frosty road, went to his head like wine. He was a Jacobite not because he had any views on Divine Right, but because he had always before his eyes a picture of a knot of adventurers in cloaks, new landed from France, among the western heather.

===Chinese===
自从当年他头一回走进舅舅的店铺、巴望有朝一日继承那位诚实杂货商的家业以来，他过的便是一种单调刻板的日子，双脚从未越出那道安分的车辙一码远。可他的心思，却像那尊垂死的角斗士（Dying Gladiator）一般，早已神游万里。少年时代他就在书籍之间远航，书籍给了他一个世界，任他凭异想天开的奇想安排自己的前程。倒不是说麦坎先生是所谓博览群书之人。他读得又慢又挑剔，在文学里只寻求一样东西。沃尔特·司各特爵士（Sir Walter Scott）是他最早的向导，但他读那些小说，不为其中对人性的洞见，也不为历史场面的壮观，只因它们供给他构筑奇幻旅程的材料。对狄更斯（Dickens）也是如此。一盏灯火的酒馆、一辆马车、换乘的驿马、马蹄在霜路上嘚嘚的声响，都像酒一样直冲他的脑门。他是个詹姆斯党人，并非因为他对君权神授有什么主张，而是因为他眼前总悬着一幅图景：一小簇披斗篷的冒险家，刚从法兰西登陆，正置身西部的石楠荒原间。

===Original===
On this select basis he had built up his small library﻿—Defoe, Hakluyt, Hazlitt and the essayists, Boswell, some indifferent romances and a shelf of spirited poetry. His tastes became known, and he acquired a reputation for a scholarly habit. He was president of the Literary Society of the Guthrie Memorial Kirk, and read to its members a variety of papers full of a gusto which rarely became critical. He had been three times chairman at Burns Anniversary dinners, and had delivered orations in eulogy of the national Bard; not because he greatly admired him﻿—he thought him rather vulgar﻿—but because he took Burns as an emblem of the un-Burns-like literature which he loved. Mr. McCunn was no scholar and was sublimely unconscious of background. He grew his flowers in his small garden-plot oblivious of their origin so long as they gave him the colour and scent he sought. Scent, I say, for he appreciated more than the mere picturesque. He had a passion for words and cadences, and would be haunted for weeks by a cunning phrase, savouring it as a connoisseur savours a vintage. Wherefore long ago, when he could ill afford it, he had purchased the Edinburgh *Stevenson*. They were the only large books on his shelves, for he had a liking for small volumes﻿—things he could stuff into his pocket in that sudden journey which he loved to contemplate.

===Chinese===
在这番精挑细选的基础上，他建立起自己小小的藏书——笛福（Defoe）、哈克卢特（Hakluyt）、黑兹利特（Hazlitt）和诸家随笔作家、鲍斯韦尔（Boswell），几部平平的传奇，还有一架子生气勃勃的诗集。他的趣味渐为人知，他也得了个学者气的名声。他是格思里纪念教堂文学会的会长，常向会员宣读五花八门的论文，兴味淋漓，却绝少苛评。他三次出任彭斯（Burns）诞辰纪念晚宴的主席，发表演说颂扬这位民族诗人；倒不是他多么钦佩彭斯——他觉得彭斯相当粗俗——而是因为他把彭斯当作他所钟爱的那种非彭斯式文学的象征。麦坎先生算不上学者，而且浑然不觉学问还讲究背景。他在自己那一小方园圃里种花，只要花开出他寻求的颜色和香气，他便不问花的来历。我说香气，是因为他所欣赏的，远不止于表面的光景如画。他对字词和音调怀着炽热的嗜好，一个精巧的短语会萦绕他心头数星期不去，他像品酒行家品味名酿那样细细品味它。因此之故，多年前在他还狠不下这笔钱的时候，他就购置了爱丁堡版《斯蒂文森》（*Stevenson*）。那是他书架上仅有的大部头，因为他偏爱小开本——偏爱那种能塞进衣袋、以备他爱构想的突然启程之用的书。

===Original===
Only he had never taken it. The shop had tied him up for eleven months in the year, and the twelfth had always found him settled decorously with his wife in some seaside villa. He had not fretted, for he was content with dreams. He was always a little tired, too, when the holidays came, and his wife told him he was growing old. He consoled himself with tags from the more philosophic of his authors, but he scarcely needed consolation. For he had large stores of modest contentment.

===Chinese===
只是那旅程他从未成行。一年里，店铺把他拴住十一个月，到了第十二个月，他总是规规矩矩地陪妻子安顿在某处海滨别墅里。他并不烦恼，因为有梦便已满足。每逢假期来临，他也总有些疲惫，妻子便说他渐渐老了。他引几位作家中较富哲思的隽语自宽，其实他几乎无须宽慰。因为他有的是大宗朴素的知足。

===Original===
But now something had happened. A spring morning and a safety razor had convinced him that he was still young. Since yesterday he was a man of a large leisure. Providence had done for him what he would never have done for himself. The rut in which he had travelled so long had given place to open country. He repeated to himself one of the quotations with which he had been wont to stir the literary young men at the Guthrie Memorial Kirk:

===Chinese===
可是如今，事情起了变化。一个春天的早晨和一把安全剃刀，让他确信自己依然年轻。从昨天起，他成了一大段闲暇的主人。上苍替他做成了他自己永远下不了手的事。他走了这么久的那条车辙，已让位于开阔的原野。他向自己复诵起一句引文——从前他就惯用这类引文去激励格思里纪念教堂里的文学青年：

===Original===
“What’s a man’s age? He must hurry more, that’s all
					

===Chinese===
“人的年岁算得了什么？他只须加紧赶路，如此而已：

===Original===
					Cram in a day, what his youth took a year to hold:
					

===Chinese===
“一天里塞进青年时一年才装得下的东西：

===Original===
					When we mind labour, then only, we’re too old﻿—
					

===Chinese===
“唯有当我们怕起劳苦，那时才真老了——

===Original===
					What age had Methusalem when he begat Saul?”

===Chinese===
“玛土撒拉（Methusalem）生扫罗（Saul）那年，年岁几何？”

===Original===
He would go journeying﻿—who but he?﻿—pleasantly.

===Chinese===
他要去旅行了——除了他还有谁？——一路快快活活地。

===Original===
It sounds a trivial resolve, but it quickened Mr. McCunn to the depths of his being. A holiday, and alone! On foot, of course, for he must travel light. He would buckle on a pack after the approved fashion. He had the very thing in a drawer upstairs, which he had bought some years ago at a sale. That and a waterproof and a stick, and his outfit was complete. A book, too, and, as he lit his first pipe, he considered what it should be. Poetry, clearly, for it was the Spring, and besides poetry could be got in pleasantly small bulk. He stood before his bookshelves trying to select a volume, rejecting one after another as inapposite. Browning﻿—Keats, Shelley﻿—they seemed more suited for the hearth than for the roadside. He did not want anything Scots, for he was of opinion that Spring came more richly in England and that English people had a better notion of it. He was tempted by the Oxford Anthology, but was deterred by its thickness, for he did not possess the thin-paper edition. Finally he selected Izaak Walton. He had never fished in his life, but *The Complete Angler* seemed to fit his mood. It was old and curious and learned and fragrant with the youth of things. He remembered its falling cadences, its country songs and wise meditations. Decidedly it was the right scrip for his pilgrimage.

===Chinese===
这个决心听来琐屑，却让麦坎先生周身深处都为之一振。度个假，而且是独自一人！当然要徒步，因为他必须轻装。他要照着内行认可的样式背上一只背包。楼上抽屉里正有这么一件现成货，是几年前一场拍卖会上买的。有了它，再加一件雨衣、一根手杖，行头便齐备了。还得带一本书，于是他点上头一斗烟，琢磨该带哪一本。显然是诗，因为时当春季，何况诗集还能买到小巧可人的开本。他站在书架前挑选，一本接一本都觉得文不对题。布朗宁（Browning）——济慈（Keats）、雪莱（Shelley）——这些似乎更宜于炉边，而不宜于路旁。他不要苏格兰风味的东西，因为依他看，春意在英格兰来得更浓郁，英格兰人对春也更有会心。《牛津诗选》（Oxford Anthology）让他动过心，可那厚部头又叫他却步，因为他没有薄纸本。最后他选中了伊扎克·沃尔顿（Izaak Walton）。他生平从未钓过鱼，可《垂钓者大全》（*The Complete Angler*）似乎正合他此刻的心境。这本书古老、古雅、渊博，散发着万物青春的芬芳。他记得它那低回渐落的调子，它的田园歌谣和睿智的冥想。毫无疑问，这正是他这趟朝圣该带的行囊。

===Original===
Characteristically he thought last of where he was to go. Every bit of the world beyond his front door had its charms to the seeing eye. There seemed nothing common or unclean that fresh morning. Even a walk among coal-pits had its attractions.﻿ ﻿… But since he had the right to choose, he lingered over it like an epicure. Not the Highlands, for Spring came late among their sour mosses. Some place where there were fields and woods and inns, somewhere, too, within call of the sea. It must not be too remote, for he had no time to waste on train journeys; nor too near, for he wanted a countryside untainted. Presently he thought of Carrick. A good green land, as he remembered it, with purposeful white roads and public-houses sacred to the memory of Burns; near the hills but yet lowland, and with a bright sea chafing on its shores. He decided on Carrick, found a map and planned his journey.

===Chinese===
照他的脾性，去哪儿这件事他想在了最后。在他这双善看的眼睛里，自家门外世界的每一寸都有它的动人之处。在那个清新的早晨，仿佛再没有什么平凡或污秽的东西，连在煤坑之间走一走都有它的吸引处。……不过既然有权挑选，他便像个美食家似的细细咂摸。不去高地，因为春天来到高地那些酸涩的沼泽上总归太迟。要去一个有田野、有树林、有小客店的地方，还得离海不远，一声唤便听得见。不能太远，因为他没有工夫把光阴耗在火车上；也不能太近，因为他要一片未曾玷污的乡野。不一会儿，他想起了卡里克（Carrick）。照他的记忆，那是一片美好的绿色土地，有目标坚定的白色公路，有奉祀彭斯英灵的酒馆；倚着山丘却仍属低地，明亮的海水在岸边不住激荡。他定下了卡里克，找出一张地图，筹划起旅程来。

===Original===
Then he routed out his knapsack, packed it with a modest change of raiment, and sent out Tibby to buy chocolate and tobacco and to cash a cheque at the Strathclyde Bank. Till Tibby returned he occupied himself with delicious dreams.﻿ ﻿… He saw himself daily growing browner and leaner, swinging along broad highways or wandering in bypaths. He pictured his seasons of ease, when he unslung his pack and smoked in some clump of lilacs by a burnside﻿—he remembered a phrase of Stevenson’s somewhat like that. He would meet and talk with all sorts of folk; an exhilarating prospect, for Mr. McCunn loved his kind. There would be the evening hour before he reached his inn, when, pleasantly tired, he would top some ridge and see the welcoming lights of a little town. There would be the lamp-lit after-supper time when he would read and reflect, and the start in the gay morning, when tobacco tastes sweetest and even fifty-five seems young. It would be holiday of the purest, for no business now tugged at his coattails. He was beginning a new life, he told himself, when he could cultivate the seedling interests which had withered beneath the far-reaching shade of the shop. Was ever a man more fortunate or more free?

===Chinese===
然后他翻出背包，装进一身简单的换洗衣裳，又打发蒂比出门去买巧克力和烟草，去斯特拉斯克莱德银行（Strathclyde Bank）兑一张支票。在蒂比回来之前，他沉醉在甜美的遐想里。……他看见自己一天天晒得更黑、变得更瘦，摆开大步走在宽广大路上，或在幽径间徜徉。他想象自己歇肩的时辰：卸下背包，在溪畔一丛丁香旁吸着烟——他记得斯蒂文森（Stevenson）有过一句差不多正是这样的话。他会遇见形形色色的人，同他们攀谈；这前景令人振奋，因为麦坎先生素来爱他的同类。还会有抵达客栈前的黄昏时分，他带着惬意的疲惫翻上某道山梁，望见一座小镇迎人的灯火。还会有晚饭后灯下读书遐想的时光，以及明快清晨的启程——那时烟草最是甘美，连五十五岁也显得年轻。这将是最纯粹的假日，因为如今再没有生意来扯他的衣摆。他告诉自己：一种新生活开始了，在这生活里，他可以培植那些曾在店铺铺天盖地的浓荫下枯萎的、幼苗般的志趣。世间可有比他更幸运、更自由的人么？

===Original===
Tibby was told that he was going off for a week or two. No letters need be forwarded, for he would be constantly moving, but Mrs. McCunn at the Neuk Hydropathic would be kept informed of his whereabouts. Presently he stood on his doorstep, a stocky figure in ancient tweeds, with a bulging pack slung on his arm, and a stout hazel stick in his hand. A passerby would have remarked an elderly shopkeeper bent apparently on a day in the country, a common little man on a prosaic errand. But the passerby would have been wrong, for he could not see into the heart. The plump citizen was the eternal pilgrim; he was Jason, Ulysses, Eric the Red, Albuquerque, Cortez﻿—starting out to discover new worlds.

===Chinese===
他告诉蒂比，自己要出门一两个星期。信件无须转寄，因为他会不断挪动地方；不过纽克水疗院的麦坎太太会随时得知他的行踪。不多时，他已站在自家门阶上——一个身着旧花呢的矮壮身影，臂上挎着塞得鼓胀的背包，手里握一根结实的榛木手杖。过路人看到的，无非是一个上了年纪的店主，看样子是打算到乡间去消磨一日，一个办着平淡差事的寻常小人物。可这位过路人看错了，因为他看不透人心。这位丰满的市民正是那永恒的朝圣者；他是伊阿宋（Jason）、尤利西斯（Ulysses）、红发埃里克（Eric the Red）、阿尔布克尔克（Albuquerque）、科尔特斯（Cortez）——正启程去发现新世界。

===Original===
Before he left Mr. McCunn had given Tibby a letter to post. That morning he had received an epistle from a benevolent acquaintance, one Mackintosh, regarding a group of urchins who called themselves the “Gorbals Die-Hards.” Behind the premises in Mearns Street lay a tract of slums, full of mischievous boys with whom his staff waged truceless war. But lately there had started among them a kind of unauthorised and unofficial Boy Scouts, who, without uniform or badge or any kind of paraphernalia, followed the banner of Sir Robert Baden-Powell and subjected themselves to a rude discipline. They were far too poor to join an orthodox troop, but they faithfully copied what they believed to be the practices of more fortunate boys. Mr. McCunn had witnessed their pathetic parades, and had even passed the time of day with their leader, a red-haired savage called Dougal. The philanthropic Mackintosh had taken an interest in the gang and now desired subscriptions to send them to camp in the country.

===Chinese===
动身之前，麦坎先生交给蒂比一封信去投寄。那天早晨，他收到一位热心肠的熟人麦金托什（Mackintosh）的来信，说的是一伙自称“戈尔巴尔死硬帮（Gorbals Die-Hards）”的顽童。米恩斯街店铺的后身是一片贫民窟，满是捣蛋的男孩，他的店员们同他们打着无休无止的战争。可近来，那帮孩子中间兴起了一种未经核准、不合官规章法的童子军——没有制服，没有徽章，也没有任何行头，却追随着罗伯特·贝登堡爵士（Sir Robert Baden-Powell）的旗帜，甘受一种粗厉的纪律约束。他们穷得根本入不了正规童军团，却忠实地模仿着他们所认为的、比他们幸运的孩子们的做法。麦坎先生见识过他们那可怜兮兮的列队操演，甚至同他们的头领寒暄过几句——那是个红头发的野孩子，名叫杜加尔（Dougal）。那位慈善家麦金托什对这帮孩子上了心，如今想募集捐款，送他们到乡间去露营。

===Original===
Mr. McCunn, in his new exhilaration, felt that he could not deny to others what he proposed for himself. His last act before leaving was to send Mackintosh ten pounds.


===Chinese===
麦坎先生正怀着新的兴奋，觉得自己既打算给自己这份好处，便不能不给旁人也来一份。他动身前的最后一件事，是给麦金托什汇去了十英镑。

## II. Of Mr. John Heritage and the Difference in Points of View / 二、约翰·赫里蒂奇先生与观点的分歧

===Original===
Dickson McCunn was never to forget the first stage in that pilgrimage. A little after midday he descended from a grimy third-class carriage at a little station whose name I have forgotten. In the village nearby he purchased some new-baked buns and ginger biscuits, to which he was partial, and followed by the shouts of urchins, who admired his pack﻿—“Look at the auld man gaun to the schule”﻿—he emerged into open country. The late April noon gleamed like a frosty morning, but the air, though tonic, was kind. The road ran over sweeps of moorland where curlews wailed, and into lowland pastures dotted with very white, very vocal lambs. The young grass had the warm fragrance of new milk. As he went he munched his buns, for he had resolved to have no plethoric midday meal, and presently he found the burnside nook of his fancy, and halted to smoke. On a patch of turf close to a grey stone bridge he had out his Walton and read the chapter on “The Chavender or Chub.” The collocation of words delighted him and inspired him to verse. “Lavender or Lub”﻿—“Pavender or Pub”﻿—“Gravender or Grub”﻿—but the monosyllables proved too vulgar for poetry. Regretfully he desisted.

===Chinese===
迪克森·麦坎永远忘不了那趟朝圣的头一程。刚过晌午，他从一节灰扑扑的三等车厢里下来，走在一座小站的月台上——车站的名字我已经忘了。在附近的村子里，他买了几只刚出炉的小圆面包和姜汁饼干，那是他的偏嗜；一帮顽童艳羡着他的背包，追在他身后起哄——“瞧这老头儿上学去喽”——他就这样走进了开阔的乡野。四月末的正午晴光闪闪，宛如一个霜天的清晨，空气虽然凛冽提神，却也温厚。道路掠过一片片荒原，鹬鸟在原上哀鸣，又驶入低地草场，草场上点缀着白得晃眼、咩咩不休的羊羔。新生的青草带着新牛奶般的暖香。他一路走一路嚼着面包，因为已打定主意中午不吃得过饱；不久，他果然寻到了想象中那处溪畔的角落，停下来吸烟。在一座青石桥旁的一小方草皮上，他掏出他那本沃尔顿，读起了讲“查文德鱼，或称雅罗鱼”（The Chavender or Chub）的那一章。这个词语的搭配叫他心花怒放，诗兴大发。“Lavender 或 Lub”——“Pavender 或 Pub”——“Gravender 或 Grub”——可惜那些单音节词到底太俗，入不得诗。他只得遗憾作罢。

===Original===
The rest of the road was as idyllic as the start. He would tramp steadily for a mile or so and then saunter, leaning over bridges to watch the trout in the pools, admiring from a dry-stone dyke the unsteady gambols of newborn lambs, kicking up dust from strips of moor-burn on the heather. Once by a fir-wood he was privileged to surprise three lunatic hares waltzing. His cheeks glowed with the sun; he moved in an atmosphere of pastoral, serene and contented. When the shadows began to lengthen he arrived at the village of Cloncae, where he proposed to lie. The inn looked dirty, but he found a decent widow, above whose door ran the legend in homemade lettering, “Mrs. brockie tea and Coffee,” and who was willing to give him quarters. There he supped handsomely off ham and eggs, and dipped into a work called *Covenanting Worthies*, which garnished a table decorated with seashells. At half-past nine precisely he retired to bed and unhesitating sleep.

===Chinese===
余下的路程同开头一样田园牧歌。他总是扎实走上约莫一英里，再信步闲逛一阵，伏在桥栏上看水潭里的鳟鱼，倚着干垒石墙观赏新生羊羔蹒跚的欢跳，从石楠荒原上一条条烧荒的地带上踢起尘土。在一片杉树林边，他有幸撞见三只发疯的野兔在跳华尔兹。他的双颊被太阳晒得发烫；他行走在一派牧歌的气氛里，安宁而满足。待到日影渐长，他到了克朗基（Cloncae）村，打算就在此地过夜。村里的客栈看着肮脏，但他寻到一位体面的寡妇，门楣上一行自家手书的字样——“Mrs. brockie tea and Coffee”（布罗基太太，茶与咖啡）——愿意留他住宿。在那里，他美美地吃了一顿火腿煎蛋当晚餐，随手翻了一翻装点着贝壳的桌上一部题为《盟约派豪杰》（*Covenanting Worthies*）的著作。九点半整，他便上床，毫不迟疑地睡去。

===Original===
Next morning he awoke to a changed world. The sky was grey and so low that his outlook was bounded by a cabbage garden, while a surly wind prophesied rain. It was chilly, too, and he had his breakfast beside the kitchen fire. Mrs. Brockie could not spare a capital letter for her surname on the signboard, but she exalted it in her talk. He heard of a multitude of Brockies, ascendant, descendant and collateral, who seemed to be in a fair way to inherit the earth. Dickson listened sympathetically, and lingered by the fire. He felt stiff from yesterday’s exercise, and the edge was off his spirit.

===Chinese===
第二天早晨醒来，世界已换了模样。天色灰暗，低得叫人的眼界不出一座白菜园，阴沉沉的风预示着雨。天气也冷，他就在厨房炉火边用了早饭。布罗基太太在招牌上舍不得给自家的姓一个大写字母，谈起话来却把它抬得极高。他听说了许许多多布罗基家的人，上辈的、下辈的、旁支的，这一大家子看来正稳稳当当地走在承受整个大地的路上。迪克森同情地听着，在炉边流连不去。他感到昨天一番锻炼浑身发僵，心气也不如昨日锋锐。

===Original===
The start was not quite what he had pictured. His pack seemed heavier, his boots tighter, and his pipe drew badly. The first miles were all uphill, with a wind tingling his ears, and no colours in the landscape but brown and grey. Suddenly he awoke to the fact that he was dismal, and thrust the notion behind him. He expanded his chest and drew in long draughts of air. He told himself that this sharp weather was better than sunshine. He remembered that all travellers in romances battled with mist and rain. Presently his body recovered comfort and vigour, and his mind worked itself into cheerfulness.

===Chinese===
启程的滋味同他设想的颇有出入。背包仿佛重了，靴子仿佛紧了，烟斗也抽得不畅。头几英里全是上坡，风刮得耳朵生疼，景色里除了棕褐便是灰暗。他猛然醒悟自己正自郁闷，便一把将这念头抛到身后。他挺起胸膛，深深地吸进一口口长气。他告诫自己，这种凛冽天气胜过晴日。他想起传奇小说里的旅人无不与雾雨搏斗。不多时，他的身体恢复了舒坦和劲头，心境也自己转出了快活。

===Original===
He overtook a party of tramps and fell into talk with them. He had always had a fancy for the class, though he had never known anything nearer it than city beggars. He pictured them as philosophic vagabonds, full of quaint turns of speech, unconscious Borrovians. With these samples his disillusionment was speedy. The party was made up of a ferret-faced man with a red nose, a draggle-tailed woman, and a child in a crazy perambulator. Their conversation was one-sided, for it immediately resolved itself into a whining chronicle of misfortunes and petitions for relief. It cost him half a crown to be rid of them.

===Chinese===
他赶上一伙流浪汉，同他们攀谈起来。他素来对这一阶层抱有遐想，虽然他见识过的，充其量不过是城里的乞丐。他把他们想象成哲人般的游荡者，满口古怪的妙语，是天生的博罗式人物（Borrovians，即乔治·博罗笔下的流浪哲人）。这几个标本叫他迅速幻灭。这一伙人由一个红鼻头的黄鼠狼脸汉子、一个衣衫拖沓的女人和一个歪歪扭扭童车里的孩子组成。谈话是单方面的，因为三句话不到，就化成了一篇哭哭啼啼的倒霉史，外加请求周济。破费两先令六便士，他才把他们打发掉。

===Original===
The road was alive with tramps that day. The next one did the accosting. Hailing Mr. McCunn as “Guv’nor,” he asked to be told the way to Manchester. The objective seemed so enterprising that Dickson was impelled to ask questions, and heard, in what appeared to be in the accents of the Colonies, the tale of a career of unvarying calamity. There was nothing merry or philosophic about this adventurer. Nay, there was something menacing. He eyed his companion’s waterproof covetously, and declared that he had had one like it which had been stolen from him the day before. Had the place been lonely he might have contemplated highway robbery, but they were at the entrance to a village, and the sight of a public-house awoke his thirst. Dickson parted with him at the cost of sixpence for a drink.

===Chinese===
那天一路上到处是流浪汉。下一个索性主动搭腔。他管麦坎先生叫“老板”，问去曼彻斯特（Manchester）的路怎么走。这目标看来如此雄心勃勃，迪克森忍不住盘问起来，结果听那人用一种殖民地口音，讲了一部灾祸连绵不断的生涯史。这位冒险家身上毫无快活或哲理可言。非但如此，还有些咄咄逼人。他觊觎地盯着同伴的雨衣，宣称自己也有一件一模一样的，昨天刚被人偷走。倘若地方偏僻，他说不定会盘算一下拦路抢劫；好在两人已到一座村口，酒馆的招牌唤醒了他的酒瘾。迪克森破费六便士给他买了杯酒，就此分道扬镳。

===Original===
He had no more company that morning except an aged stone-breaker whom he convoyed for half a mile. The stone-breaker also was soured with the world. He walked with a limp, which, he said, was due to an accident years before, when he had been run into by “ane o’ thae damned velocipeeds.” The word revived in Dickson memories of his youth, and he was prepared to be friendly. But the ancient would have none of it. He inquired morosely what he was after, and, on being told, remarked that he might have learned more sense. “It’s a daft-like thing for an auld man like you to be traivellin’ the roads. Ye maun be ill-off for a job.” Questioned as to himself he became, as the newspapers say, “reticent,” and having reached his bing of stones, turned rudely to his duties. “Awa’ hame wi’ ye,” were his parting words. “It’s idle scoondrels like you that maks wark for honest folk like me.”

===Chinese===
那天上午再没有别的同伴，只有一位年迈的碎石工，他陪着走了半英里。碎石工也一肚子对世界的怨气。他走路瘸腿，据他自己说，是多年前一场事故留下的，当时他被“一辆那号该死的老式脚踏车（velocipeed）”撞了。这个词唤醒了迪克森年轻时的记忆，他便存心交个朋友。可这位老人家偏偏不领情。他阴沉沉地打听迪克森是来干什么的，听完之后，说这把年纪本该更明白些事理。“你这岁数的老汉，还满大路瞎逛荡，真是桩疯癫事体。你八成是找不着活儿干了吧。”反过来问到他自己的情形，他就变得——用报纸上的话来说——“讳莫如深”了；走到他那堆碎石跟前，便粗声粗气地转身干起活来。“快滚回家去吧，”这是他的临别赠言，“就是你们这号闲汉二流子，才害得俺们这样的老实人有干不完的活。”

===Original===
The morning was not a success, but the strong air had given Dickson such an appetite that he resolved to break his rule, and, on reaching the little town of Kilchrist, he sought luncheon at the chief hotel. There he found that which revived his spirits. A solitary bagman shared the meal, who revealed the fact that he was in the grocery line. There followed a well-informed and most technical conversation. He was drawn to speak of the United Supply Stores, Limited, of their prospects and of their predecessor, Mr. McCunn, whom he knew well by repute but had never met. “Yon’s the clever one,” he observed. “I’ve always said there’s no longer head in the city of Glasgow than McCunn. An old-fashioned firm, but it has aye managed to keep up with the times. He’s just retired, they tell me, and in my opinion it’s a big loss to the provision trade.﻿ ﻿…” Dickson’s heart glowed within him. Here was Romance; to be praised incognito; to enter a casual inn and find that fame had preceded him. He warmed to the bagman, insisted on giving him a liqueur and a cigar, and finally revealed himself. “I’m Dickson McCunn,” he said, “taking a bit holiday. If there’s anything I can do for you when I get back, just let me know.” With mutual esteem they parted.

===Chinese===
这天上午算不得成功，但强劲的空气给了迪克森一副好胃口，他决计破一回例，到小镇基尔克里斯特（Kilchrist）后，便进了当地头号客栈用午餐。在那里，他遇见了让他精神一振的事。同桌独自用饭的是个行商，言谈间露出自己干的是食品杂货行当。接下去便是一场消息灵通、极尽专门之能事的谈话。那人不由自主地谈起联合供应商店有限公司，谈它的前景，也谈它的前身麦坎先生——此人他久闻大名，却从未谋面。“那位就是个精明人，”他说道，“俺一向讲，整个格拉斯哥（Glasgow）城里，再没有比麦坎更灵光的脑袋了。老派的商号，可人家一直跟得上时势。听说他刚退下来，依俺看，这是食品杂货行当的一大损失。……”迪克森的心头热烘烘地烧了起来。这才是浪漫；被人不着痕迹地称道；随便走进一家客栈，发现名声竟先他而至。他对这位行商顿生好感，执意请他喝了一杯利口酒、抽了一支雪茄，最后亮明了身份。“我就是迪克森·麦坎，”他说，“出来度几天假。回头我要是能帮你什么忙，尽管开口。”两人相互敬重地分手了。

===Original===
He had need of all his good spirits, for he emerged into an unrelenting drizzle. The environs of Kilchrist are at the best unlovely, and in the wet they were as melancholy as a graveyard. But the encounter with the bagman had worked wonders with Dickson, and he strode lustily into the weather, his waterproof collar buttoned round his chin. The road climbed to a bare moor, where lagoons had formed in the ruts, and the mist showed on each side only a yard or two of soaking heather. Soon he was wet; presently every part of him, boots, body and pack, was one vast sponge. The waterproof was not waterproof, and the rain penetrated to his most intimate garments. Little he cared. He felt lighter, younger, than on the idyllic previous day. He enjoyed the buffets of the storm, and one wet mile succeeded another to the accompaniment of Dickson’s shouts and laughter. There was no one abroad that afternoon, so he could talk aloud to himself and repeat his favourite poems. About five in the evening there presented himself at the Black Bull Inn at Kirkmichael a soaked, disreputable, but most cheerful traveller.

===Chinese===
他正用得着浑身的好兴致，因为一出镇子，便走进了绵绵不绝的毛毛雨里。基尔克里斯特的郊区即便在晴日也谈不上悦目，一逢下雨，更萧索得像座坟场。但同那位行商的邂逅在迪克森身上创造了奇迹，他扣紧雨衣领子围住下巴，雄赳赳地迎着天气大步走去。道路爬上一片光秃秃的荒原，车辙里积成了一汪汪潟湖，浓雾使得两侧各只剩得一两码浸透的石楠可辨。不多时他湿了；随即浑身上下，靴子、身体加背包，变成了整整一块大海绵。雨衣并不挡雨，雨水一直渗透到贴身的衣衫。他毫不在意。比起前一天田园牧歌的日子，他觉得自己更轻快、更年轻了。他领受着风暴的拳脚，一英里湿路接着一英里湿路，伴着迪克森的呼喊与大笑。那天下午路上不见人影，他尽可以高声自言自语，背诵心爱的诗篇。傍晚五点来钟，柯克迈克尔（Kirkmichael）的黑牛客栈（Black Bull Inn）里便来了一位浑身湿透、狼狈不堪却兴高采烈的旅人。

===Original===
Now the Black Bull at Kirkmichael is one of the few very good inns left in the world. It is an old place and an hospitable, for it has been for generations a haunt of anglers, who above all other men understand comfort. There are always bright fires there, and hot water, and old soft leather armchairs, and an aroma of good food and good tobacco, and giant trout in glass cases, and pictures of Captain Barclay of Urie walking to London, and Mr. Ramsay of Barnton winning a horse-race, and the three-volume edition of the Waverley Novels with many volumes missing, and indeed all those things which an inn should have. Also there used to be﻿—there may still be﻿—sound vintage claret in the cellars. The Black Bull expects its guests to arrive in every stage of dishevelment, and Dickson was received by a cordial landlord, who offered dry garments as a matter of course. The pack proved to have resisted the elements, and a suit of clothes and slippers were provided by the house. Dickson, after a glass of toddy, wallowed in a hot bath, which washed all the stiffness out of him. He had a fire in his bedroom, beside which he wrote the opening passages of that diary he had vowed to keep, descanting lyrically upon the joys of ill weather. At seven o’clock, warm and satisfied in soul, and with his body clad in raiment several sizes too large for it, he descended to dinner.

===Chinese===
须知柯克迈克尔的黑牛客栈，是世上仅存的几家头等好客栈之一。这是家老店，而且好客，因为世世代代它都是垂钓客的聚脚地，而垂钓客比谁都懂得舒适。那里永远有旺旺的炉火、热水、老旧柔软的皮扶手椅，有好菜好烟的香气，有玻璃柜里的巨鳟，有乌里的巴克利上尉（Captain Barclay of Urie）步行去伦敦的画像，有巴宁顿的拉姆齐先生（Mr. Ramsay of Barnton）赢得赛马的画像，还有缺了许多卷的三卷本《威弗利小说》（Waverley Novels）——总之，一家客栈应有的东西，那里一应俱全。地窖里从前还存着——兴许至今还存着——醇正的陈年红葡萄酒。黑牛客栈的客人历来是以各种湿淋淋的狼狈模样登门的，迪克森便受到店主热诚的接待，干衣裳照例奉上。背包倒是顶住了风雨，客栈又备下一套衣裤和拖鞋。迪克森喝了一杯托迪热酒，便泡进热水澡里舒展翻腾，把一身僵乏都泡化了。他的卧室里有炉火，他坐在炉边，写起他发愿要记的那本日记的开头几则，抒情地大谈坏天气的种种乐处。七点钟，他心灵温暖而满足，身上套着一套大了好几号的衣裳，下楼用晚餐去了。

===Original===
At one end of the long table in the dining-room sat a group of anglers. They looked jovial fellows, and Dickson would fain have joined them; but, having been fishing all day in the Loch o’ the Threshes, they were talking their own talk, and he feared that his admiration for Izaak Walton did not qualify him to butt into the erudite discussions of fishermen. The landlord seemed to think likewise, for he drew back a chair for him at the other end, where sat a young man absorbed in a book. Dickson gave him good evening and got an abstracted reply. The young man supped the Black Bull’s excellent broth with one hand, and with the other turned the pages of his volume. A glance convinced Dickson that the work was French, a literature which did not interest him. He knew little of the tongue and suspected it of impropriety.

===Chinese===
饭厅里那张长桌的一头，坐着一伙垂钓客。看模样都是快活人，迪克森很愿意凑过去；可他们在打谷湖（Loch o’ the Threshes）钓了一整天鱼，谈的是自家的行话，他担心自己崇拜伊扎克·沃尔顿，还够不上贸然插进渔人们掉书袋的议论。店主似乎也这么想，便在长桌另一头替他拉开一把椅子，那里坐着一位埋头看书的年轻人。迪克森向他道了晚上好，得到的回答心不在焉。那年轻人一手喝着黑牛客栈上好的肉汤，一手翻着书页。迪克森一眼断定那是一本法文书，而法国文学他不感兴趣。他对那种语言所知甚少，还疑心它不大正经。

===Original===
Another guest entered and took the chair opposite the bookish young man. He was also young﻿—not more than thirty-three﻿—and to Dickson’s eye, was the kind of person he would have liked to resemble. He was tall and free from any superfluous flesh; his face was lean, fine-drawn and deeply sunburnt so that the hair above showed oddly pale; the hands were brown and beautifully shaped, but the forearm revealed by the loose cuffs of his shirt was as brawny as a blacksmith’s. He had rather pale blue eyes, which seemed to have looked much at the sun, and a small moustache the colour of ripe hay. His voice was low and pleasant, and he pronounced his words precisely, like a foreigner.

===Chinese===
又进来一位客人，在书生气的年轻人对面落了座。此人也很年轻——至多三十三岁——在迪克森眼里，正是他自己愿意长成的那类人。他身材高挑，没有一丝赘肉；脸瘦削清癯，晒得黢黑，反衬得上面的头发出奇地浅淡；一双手棕色，形状秀美，可衬衫松垮的袖口里露出的前臂，却壮健得像铁匠的一般。他有一双淡蓝色的眼睛，像是看惯了太阳的样子，唇上一撇小胡子，颜色如成熟的干草。他嗓音低沉悦耳，吐字精确，像个外国人。

===Original===
He was very ready to talk, but in defiance of Dr. Johnson’s warning, his talk was all questions. He wanted to know everything about the neighbourhood﻿—who lived in what houses, what were the distances between the towns, what harbours would admit what class of vessel. Smiling agreeably, he put Dickson through a catechism to which he knew none of the answers. The landlord was called in, and proved more helpful. But on one matter he was fairly at a loss. The catechist asked about a house called Darkwater, and was met with a shake of the head. “I know no sic-like name in this countryside, sir,” and the catechist looked disappointed.

===Chinese===
他很健谈，只是全然违背约翰逊博士（Dr. Johnson）的告诫，他的谈话全是提问。他想了解这一带的一切——什么人住什么宅子，镇与镇之间距离多少，什么港口进得去什么级别的船只。他笑容可掬地拿迪克森当问答手册来考，迪克森却一问三不知。店主被请了来，果然管用得多。但有一桩事他也全然摸不着头脑。这位考问者打听一座叫达克沃特（Darkwater）的宅子，得到的却是一个摇头的回应。“这一带乡间，俺没听说过这么个名号，先生。”于是考问者面露失望之色。

===Original===
The literary young man said nothing, but ate trout abstractedly, one eye on his book. The fish had been caught by the anglers in the Loch o’ the Threshes, and phrases describing their capture floated from the other end of the table. The young man had a second helping, and then refused the excellent hill mutton that followed, contenting himself with cheese. Not so Dickson and the catechist. They ate everything that was set before them, topping up with a glass of port. Then the latter, who had been talking illuminatingly about Spain, rose, bowed and left the table, leaving Dickson, who liked to linger over his meals, to the society of the ichthyophagous student.

===Chinese===
那位好文学的年轻人一言不发，只管心不在焉地吃着鳟鱼，一只眼睛仍盯在书上。鱼是垂钓客们从打谷湖里钓来的，描述捕鱼经过的语句从长桌那头源源飘来。年轻人添了第二份，随后却谢绝了接着上来的上好山羊肉，只吃了点奶酪了事。迪克森和那位考问者却不然。摆上来的东西他们一扫而光，末了还各干了一杯波特酒。随后，方才一直在妙趣横生地谈论西班牙的那位，起身鞠躬离席而去，把喜欢在饭桌上流连的迪克森，留给了那位食鱼的学人做伴。

===Original===
He nodded towards the book. “Interesting?” he asked.

===Chinese===
他朝那本书点点头。“有意思吗？”他问。

===Original===
The young man shook his head and displayed the name on the cover. “Anatole France. I used to be crazy about him, but now he seems rather a back number.” Then he glanced towards the just-vacated chair. “Australian,” he said.

===Chinese===
年轻人摇摇头，把封面上的名字亮给他看。“阿纳托尔·法朗士（Anatole France）。从前我为他疯魔过一阵，如今看来他有点过时了。”随后他瞥了一眼刚刚空出的那张椅子。“澳大利亚人。”他说。

===Original===
“How d’you know?”

===Chinese===
“你怎么知道的？”

===Original===
“Can’t mistake them. There’s nothing else so lean and fine produced on the globe today. I was next door to them at Pozières and saw them fight. Lord! Such men! Now and then you had a freak, but most looked like Phoebus Apollo.”

===Chinese===
“错不了。当今世上再没有出产得这般精瘦、这般漂亮的人了。在波齐耶尔（Pozières）我跟他们紧挨着作战，亲眼见过他们打。天哪！那样的人！偶尔也出个怪物，可大多数看着都像福玻斯·阿波罗（Phoebus Apollo）。”

===Original===
Dickson gazed with a new respect at his neighbour, for he had not associated him with battlefields. During the war he had been a fervent patriot, but, though he had never heard a shot himself, so many of his friends’ sons and nephews, not to mention cousins of his own, had seen service, that he had come to regard the experience as commonplace. Lions in Africa and bandits in Mexico seemed to him novel and romantic things, but not trenches and airplanes which were the whole world’s property. But he could scarcely fit his neighbour into even his haziest picture of war. The young man was tall and a little round-shouldered; he had shortsighted, rather prominent brown eyes, untidy black hair and dark eyebrows which came near to meeting. He wore a knickerbocker suit of bluish-grey tweed, a pale blue shirt, a pale blue collar and a dark blue tie﻿—a symphony of colour which seemed too elaborately considered to be quite natural. Dickson had set him down as an artist or a newspaper correspondent, objects to him of lively interest. But now the classification must be reconsidered.

===Chinese===
迪克森怀着新的敬意打量邻座，因为此前他从没把此人同战场联系起来。大战期间他曾是个热烈的爱国者，而且，虽然他自己从未听过一声枪响，他朋友的儿子们、侄子们，更不消说他自家的那些表亲，见过阵仗的实在太多，他早已把这份经历看作寻常事。非洲的狮子、墨西哥的强盗，在他看来还算新奇浪漫，战壕和飞机却是全世界共有的东西，谈不上浪漫。可是要把邻座安进他心目中最模糊的战争图景里，也几乎办不到。这年轻人高个子，背有点驼；一双近视的、相当突出的棕色眼睛，一头蓬乱的黑发，两道浓眉几乎连成一线。他穿一套蓝灰色花呢的灯笼裤套装，浅蓝衬衫、浅蓝领子、深蓝领带——一场色彩的交响，考究得未免过了头，显得不大自然。迪克森本已断定他不是艺术家就是报社记者——这两类人都是他极感兴趣的对象。可如今这番归类只好重新考虑了。

===Original===
“So you were in the war,” he said encouragingly.

===Chinese===
“这么说，你上过战场。”他鼓励道。

===Original===
“Four blasted years,” was the savage reply. “And I never want to hear the name of the beastly thing again.”

===Chinese===
“该死的四年，”回答得咬牙切齿，“往后那玩意儿的名字，我一个字也不想再听见。”

===Original===
“You said he was an Australian,” said Dickson, casting back. “But I thought Australians had a queer accent, like the English.”

===Chinese===
“你方才说他是澳大利亚人，”迪克森回想着道，“可我原以为澳大利亚人口音古怪，跟英格兰人似的。”

===Original===
“They’ve all kind of accents, but you can never mistake their voice. It’s got the sun in it. Canadians have got grinding ice in theirs, and Virginians have got butter. So have the Irish. In Britain there are no voices, only speaking tubes. It isn’t safe to judge men by their accent only. You yourself I take to be Scotch, but for all I know you may be a senator from Chicago or a Boer General.”

===Chinese===
“他们什么样的口音都有，可他们的嗓音你绝不会听错。那嗓音里有太阳。加拿大人的嗓音里有磨牙的冰，弗吉尼亚人的嗓音里有黄油。爱尔兰人也有。在不列颠，根本没有嗓音，只有传声筒。光凭口音断人，靠不住。我瞧你是苏格兰人，可说不定你是芝加哥来的参议员，或者一位布尔人的将军哩。”

===Original===
“I’m from Glasgow. My name’s Dickson McCunn.” He had a faint hope that the announcement might affect the other as it had affected the bagman at Kilchrist.

===Chinese===
“俺是格拉斯哥人。俺名叫迪克森·麦坎。”他隐隐指望这句自报家门，也能像在基尔克里斯特打动那位行商一样打动对方。

===Original===
“Golly, what a name!” exclaimed the young man rudely.

===Chinese===
“乖乖，这算个什么名字！”年轻人唐突地叫道。

===Original===
Dickson was nettled. “It’s very old Highland,” he said. “It means the son of a dog.”

===Chinese===
迪克森被刺恼了。“这是极古老的高地姓氏，”他说，“意思是一条狗的儿子。”

===Original===
“Which﻿—Christian name or surname?” Then the young man appeared to think he had gone too far, for he smiled pleasantly. “And a very good name too. Mine is prosaic by comparison. They call me John Heritage.”

===Chinese===
“哪一个——教名还是姓氏？”年轻人随即似乎自觉话说得过了头，便愉快地一笑。“这名字也委实不坏。相比之下，我的就平淡无奇了。人家叫我约翰·赫里蒂奇（John Heritage）。”

===Original===
“That,” said Dickson, mollified, “is like a name out of a book. With that name by rights you should be a poet.”

===Chinese===
“那个名字，”迪克森气平了些，“倒像是从书里走出来的。顶着那样的名字，按理你就该做个诗人。”

===Original===
Gloom settled on the young man’s countenance. “It’s a dashed sight too poetic. It’s like Edwin Arnold and Alfred Austin and Dante Gabriel Rossetti. Great poets have vulgar monosyllables for names, like Keats. The new Shakespeare when he comes along will probably be called Grubb or Jubber, if he isn’t Jones. With a name like yours I might have a chance. *You* should be the poet.”

===Chinese===
阴云罩上了年轻人的脸。“它诗意得也太过煞风景了。活像埃德温·阿诺德（Edwin Arnold）、阿尔弗雷德·奥斯汀（Alfred Austin）和但丁·加布里埃尔·罗塞蒂（Dante Gabriel Rossetti）。大诗人的名字都是俗气的单音节，比如济慈。将来的新莎士比亚，要是不叫琼斯（Jones），多半会叫格拉布（Grubb）或者贾伯（Jubber）。有你自己这么个名字，我兴许还有点指望。该当诗人的是*你*。”

===Original===
“I’m very fond of reading,” said Dickson modestly.

===Chinese===
“俺很爱读书。”迪克森谦虚地说。

===Original===
A slow smile crumpled Mr. Heritage’s face. “There’s a fire in the smoking-room,” he observed as he rose. “We’d better bag the armchairs before these fishing louts take them.” Dickson followed obediently. This was the kind of chance acquaintance for whom he had hoped, and he was prepared to make the most of him.

===Chinese===
一个缓缓的微笑揉皱了赫里蒂奇先生的脸。“吸烟室里有炉火，”他起身说道，“咱们最好赶在那帮打鱼的粗汉之前，把扶手椅抢占下来。”迪克森乖乖地跟了去。这正是他盼望的那种萍水相逢的路伴，他打算好好加以利用。

===Original===
The fire burned bright in the little dusky smoking-room, lighted by one oil-lamp. Mr. Heritage flung himself into a chair, stretched his long legs and lit a pipe.

===Chinese===
小小昏暗的吸烟室里，一盏油灯照明，炉火熊熊。赫里蒂奇先生一屁股陷进椅子，伸开长腿，点上一斗烟。

===Original===
“You like reading?” he asked. “What sort? Any use for poetry?”

===Chinese===
“你喜欢读书？”他问。“读哪一路的？诗还用得上吗？”

===Original===
“Plenty,” said Dickson. “I’ve aye been fond of learning it up and repeating it to myself when I had nothing to do. In church and waiting on trains, like. It used to be Tennyson, but now it’s more Browning. I can say a lot of Browning.”

===Chinese===
“多着呢，”迪克森说，“俺一向喜欢把诗背熟，没事的时候在心里默诵。做礼拜的时候啦，等火车的时候啦。从前背的是丁尼生（Tennyson），如今更多是布朗宁。布朗宁俺能背好一大截。”

===Original===
The other screwed his face into an expression of disgust. “I know the stuff. ‘Damask cheeks and dewy sister eyelids.’ Or else the Ercles vein﻿—‘God’s in His Heaven, all’s right with the world.’ No good, Mr. McCunn. All back numbers. Poetry’s not a thing of pretty round phrases or noisy invocations. It’s life itself, with the tang of the raw world in it﻿—not a sweetmeat for middle-class women in parlours.”

===Chinese===
对方把脸拧出一副厌恶的表情。“那一套我领教过。‘锦缎的双颊，带露的姊妹眼睑。’再不然就是埃克尔斯腔（the Ercles vein）的咆哮——‘上帝在他的天堂里，世间一切都好。’不行啊，麦坎先生。全是过期货。诗不是漂亮的圆滑辞句，不是大声的祈告。诗是生命本身，带着生猛世界的辛烈味道——不是给客厅里中产阶级太太们吃的甜点心。”

===Original===
“Are you a poet, Mr. Heritage?”

===Chinese===
“你是诗人吗，赫里蒂奇先生？”

===Original===
“No, Dogson, I’m a paper-maker.”

===Chinese===
“不是，狗逊（Dogson），我是造纸的。”

===Original===
This was a new view to Mr. McCunn. “I just once knew a paper-maker,” he observed reflectively. “They called him Tosh. He drank a bit.”

===Chinese===
这在麦坎先生是桩新鲜见识。“俺从前倒是认识过一个造纸的，”他若有所思地说，“人家管他叫托什（Tosh）。他好喝两口。”

===Original===
“Well, I don’t drink,” said the other. “I’m a paper-maker, but that’s for my bread and butter. Some day for my own sake I may be a poet.”

===Chinese===
“这个嘛，我可不喝酒，”对方说，“我是造纸的，不过那是为了糊口。有朝一日，为了我自己，我或许能成为诗人。”

===Original===
“Have you published anything?”

===Chinese===
“你出版过什么没有？”

===Original===
The eager admiration in Dickson’s tone gratified Mr. Heritage. He drew from his pocket a slim book. “My firstfruits,” he said, rather shyly.

===Chinese===
迪克森语气里热切的仰慕让赫里蒂奇先生大为受用。他从口袋里掏出一本薄薄的书。“我的初熟果实。”他说，颇有些腼腆。

===Original===
Dickson received it with reverence. It was a small volume in grey paper boards with a white label on the back, and it was lettered: “*Whorls﻿—John Heritage’s Book*.” He turned the pages and read a little. “It’s a nice wee book,” he observed at length.

===Chinese===
迪克森毕恭毕敬地接了过来。那是一小册灰纸面装帧的书，书脊上贴着白标签，题着：《旋涡——约翰·赫里蒂奇之书》（*Whorls—John Heritage’s Book*）。他翻了翻，读了几句。“真是本讨人喜欢的小书。”他终于说道。

===Original===
“Good God, if you call it nice, I must have failed pretty badly,” was the irritated answer.

===Chinese===
“老天爷，你要是管它叫讨人喜欢，那我一定败得相当惨。”回答带着火气。

===Original===
Dickson read more deeply and was puzzled. It seemed worse than the worst of Browning to understand. He found one poem about a garden entitled “Revue.” “Crimson and resonant clangs the dawn,” said the poet. Then he went on to describe noonday:

===Chinese===
迪克森往深里读，读得满腹狐疑。要论难懂，它似乎比布朗宁最艰深之作还要更糟。他找到一首咏花园的诗，题为《巡礼》（“Revue”）。“绯红而铿然，黎明当啷作响。”诗人如此吟道。接着他描写起正午：

===Original===
“Sunflowers, tall Grenadiers, ogle the roses’ short-skirted ballet.
					

===Chinese===
“向日葵，高个儿的掷弹兵，向玫瑰的短裙芭蕾直抛媚眼。

===Original===
					The fumes of dark sweet wine hidden in frail petals
					

===Chinese===
娇弱花瓣里藏着的暗色甜酒气息

===Original===
					Madden the drunkard bees.”

===Chinese===
熏得醉汉般的蜜蜂发了狂。”

===Original===
This seemed to him an odd way to look at things, and he boggled over a phrase about an “epicene lily.” Then came evening: “The painted gauze of the stars flutters in a fold of twilight crape,” sang Mr. Heritage; and again, “The moon’s pale leprosy sloughs the fields.”

===Chinese===
在他看来，这真是看事物的一路怪径，还有一句咏“不雌不雄的百合”（epicene lily）的，更叫他磕磕绊绊。接着写到了黄昏：“星辰的彩绘轻纱，在暮色绉纱的一道褶里颤动，”赫里蒂奇先生这样歌吟；又道：“月亮苍白的麻风，蜕皮似的剥落在田野上。”

===Original===
Dickson turned to other verses which apparently enshrined the writer’s memory of the trenches. They were largely compounded of oaths, and rather horrible, lingering lovingly over sights and smells which everyone is aware of, but most people contrive to forget. He did not like them. Finally he skimmed a poem about a lady who turned into a bird. The evolution was described with intimate anatomical details which scared the honest reader.

===Chinese===
迪克森又翻到另一些诗，显然供奉着作者对战壕的回忆。这些诗大半由咒骂构成，相当骇人，缠绵地流连于那些人人心里有数、多数人却设法忘却的景象与气味。他不喜欢。最后他浏览了一首写一位太太变成鸟儿的诗。那场演变用了详尽入里的解剖学细节来描写，吓了这位老实读者一跳。

===Original===
He kept his eyes on the book for he did not know what to say. The trick seemed to be to describe nature in metaphors mostly drawn from music-halls and haberdashers’ shops, and, when at a loss, to fall to cursing. He thought it frankly very bad, and he laboured to find words which would combine politeness and honesty.

===Chinese===
他的眼睛停留在书上，因为不知说什么好。照他看，这套诀窍就是用大多取自杂耍场和针线铺的比喻来描写自然，一旦词穷，便落到咒骂上去。他坦白地认为这写得很糟，于是搜肠刮肚，想找出既不失礼又诚实的话来。

===Original===
“Well?” said the poet.

===Chinese===
“怎么样？”诗人说。

===Original===
“There’s a lot of fine things here, but﻿—but the lines don’t just seem to scan very well.”

===Chinese===
“这里头有不少好东西，可是——可是这些诗行，念起来总不大合得上格律。”

===Original===
Mr. Heritage laughed. “Now I can place you exactly. You like the meek rhyme and the conventional epithet. Well, I don’t. The world has passed beyond that prettiness. You want the moon described as a Huntress or a gold disc or a flower﻿—I say it’s oftener like a beer barrel or a cheese. You want a wealth of jolly words and real things ruled out as unfit for poetry. I say there’s nothing unfit for poetry. Nothing, Dogson! Poetry’s everywhere, and the real thing is commoner among drabs and pothouses and rubbish heaps than in your Sunday parlours. The poet’s business is to distil it out of rottenness, and show that it is all one spirit, the thing that keeps the stars in their place.﻿ ﻿… I wanted to call my book ‘*Drains*,’ for drains are sheer poetry, carrying off the excess and discards of human life to make the fields green and the corn ripen. But the publishers kicked. So I called it ‘*Whorls*,’ to express my view of the exquisite involution of all things. Poetry is the fourth dimension of the soul.﻿ ﻿… Well, let’s hear about your taste in prose.”

===Chinese===
赫里蒂奇先生大笑。“这下我可以把你定准了。你喜欢的是驯顺的韵脚和陈套的形容语。我可不。世界早已走过了那种漂亮阶段。你们要月亮被写成女猎手、金盘或者花朵——我说它倒更常常像只啤酒桶，或者一块干酪。你们要一大堆欢快的词藻，而把真实的事物当作不宜入诗一概剔除。我说，没有什么东西不宜入诗。没有什么，狗逊！诗无处不在，而在荡妇、下等酒馆和垃圾堆之间，真实之物反倒比你们星期天的客厅里更常见。诗人的营生，就是把它从腐朽里蒸馏出来，证明它本是同一种精神，就是那把星辰安顿在各自位置上的东西。……我本想给我的书取名《阴沟》（*Drains*），因为阴沟纯粹就是诗，它运走人生的过剩与弃物，好教田野青绿、谷粒成熟。可出版商不答应。于是我管它叫《旋涡》（*Whorls*），以表达我对万物精妙盘旋之见的敬意。诗是灵魂的第四维。……好了，谈谈你的散文趣味吧。”

===Original===
Mr. McCunn was much bewildered, and a little inclined to be cross. He disliked being called Dogson, which seemed to him an abuse of his etymological confidences. But his habit of politeness held.

===Chinese===
麦坎先生如坠五里雾中，还有几分要恼。他不喜欢被人唤作狗逊——在他看来，这等于滥用了他透露姓氏语源的信任。但他素有礼貌的习惯，勉强忍住了。

===Original===
He explained rather haltingly his preferences in prose.

===Chinese===
他相当结巴地讲了自己在散文上的偏好。

===Original===
Mr. Heritage listened with wrinkled brows.

===Chinese===
赫里蒂奇先生皱着眉头听。

===Original===
“You’re even deeper in the mud than I thought,” he remarked. “You live in a world of painted laths and shadows. All this passion for the picturesque! Trash, my dear man, like a schoolgirl’s novelette heroes. You make up romances about gipsies and sailors and the blackguards they call pioneers, but you know nothing about them. If you did, you would find they had none of the gilt and gloss you imagine. But the great things they have got in common with all humanity you ignore. It’s like﻿—it’s like sentimentalising about a pancake because it looked like a buttercup, and all the while not knowing that it was good to eat.”

===Chinese===
“你陷得比我想的还深，”他评道，“你活在一个人造板条和影子的世界里。满脑子对如画风光的热衷！垃圾，我亲爱的先生，跟女学生言情读物里的英雄一个样。你拿吉卜赛人、水手，还有他们所谓拓荒者的那些恶棍，编造种种传奇，可你对他们一无所知。倘若真知道，你就会发现他们根本没有你想象的那种金光和光泽。可他们与全人类共有的那些大处，你却视而不见。这就好比——好比对着一张煎饼自作多情，只因为它看上去像朵毛茛花，却始终不知道它拿来吃是好的。”

===Original===
At that moment the Australian entered the room to get a light for his pipe. He wore a motorcyclist’s overalls and appeared to be about to take the road. He bade them good night and it seemed to Dickson that his face, seen in the glow of the fire, was drawn and anxious, unlike that of the agreeable companion at dinner.

===Chinese===
正在这时，那澳大利亚人进屋来借火点烟斗。他穿着摩托车手的连衣裤，看样子正要上路。他向两人道了晚安，而在迪克森眼里，他那张映着炉火的脸紧绷而忧郁，同晚餐时那位和蔼的同伴判若两人。

===Original===
“There,” said Mr. Heritage, nodding after the departing figure. “I dare say you have been telling yourself stories about that chap﻿—life in the bush, stock-riding and the rest of it. But probably he’s a bank-clerk from Melbourne.﻿ ﻿… Your romanticism is one vast self-delusion and it blinds your eye to the real thing. We have got to clear it out and with it all the damnable humbug of the Celt.”

===Chinese===
“瞧，”赫里蒂奇先生冲着那远去的背影点了点头，说道，“我敢说你准在自个儿给那家伙编故事——丛林生活啦，牧场上骑马赶牲口啦，等等。可他多半是墨尔本（Melbourne）来的一个银行职员。……你的浪漫主义是一场彻头彻尾的自欺，它蒙住了你的眼，叫你看不见真实之物。咱们得把它清算出去，连同凯尔特人那套可恶的骗人把戏一道。”

===Original===
Mr. McCunn, who spelt the word with a soft *C*, was puzzled. “I thought a kelt was a kind of a no-weel fish,” he interposed.

===Chinese===
麦坎先生把这个字读作软音的 *C*，闻之茫然，插嘴道：“俺还当 kelt 是一种病歪歪的鱼呢。”

===Original===
But the other, in the flood-tide of his argument, ignored the interruption. “That’s the value of the war,” he went on. “It has burst up all the old conventions, and we’ve got to finish the destruction before we can build. It is the same with literature and religion and society and politics. At them with the axe, say I. I have no use for priests and pedants. I’ve no use for upper classes and middle classes. There’s only one class that matters, the plain man, the workers, who live close to life.”

===Chinese===
但对方正说到论证的洪峰上，全不理会这番打岔。“这正是战争的价值，”他接着说，“它把一切旧惯例都炸开了花，咱们必须先毁个干净，才能动工新建。文学、宗教、社会、政治，莫不如此。依我说，抡起斧子朝它们砍去。神父和学究，我用不着。上等阶级和中产阶级，我也用不着。顶要紧的只有一个阶级，那就是平民，是工人，他们贴着生活住。”

===Original===
“The place for you,” said Dickson dryly, “is in Russia among the Bolsheviks.”

===Chinese===
“适合你的地方，”迪克森干巴巴地说，“是俄国，到布尔什维克（Bolsheviks）中间去。”

===Original===
Mr. Heritage approved. “They are doing a great work in their own fashion. We needn’t imitate all their methods﻿—they’re a trifle crude and have too many Jews among them﻿—but they’ve got hold of the right end of the stick. They seek truth and reality.”

===Chinese===
赫里蒂奇先生深以为然。“他们正在以自己的路数干一桩大事业。他们的种种手段咱们不必照搬——未免粗粝了些，其间犹太人也太多了些——可他们抓住了棍子的正确一端。他们寻求真理与现实。”

===Original===
Mr. McCunn was slowly being roused.

===Chinese===
麦坎先生的火气在慢慢地被激起来。

===Original===
“What brings you wandering hereaways?” he asked.

===Chinese===
“是什么风把你吹到这一带来游荡的？”他问。

===Original===
“Exercise,” was the answer. “I’ve been kept pretty closely tied up all winter. And I want leisure and quiet to think over things.”

===Chinese===
“锻炼身体，”对方答道，“整个冬天我都拴得脱不开身。我还想要点闲暇和清静，好把种种事情想个明白。”

===Original===
“Well, there’s one subject you might turn your attention to. You’ll have been educated like a gentleman?”

===Chinese===
“这个嘛，有一桩题目你倒不妨留意留意。你受的是绅士教育吧？”

===Original===
“Nine wasted years﻿—five at Harrow, four at Cambridge.”

===Chinese===
“白白荒废的九年——五年在哈罗（Harrow），四年在剑桥（Cambridge）。”

===Original===
“See here, then. You’re daft about the working-class and have no use for any other. But what in the name of goodness do you know about workingmen?﻿ ﻿… I come out of them myself, and have lived next door to them all my days. Take them one way and another, they’re a decent sort, good and bad like the rest of us. But there’s a wheen daft folk that would set them up as models﻿—close to truth and reality, says you. It’s sheer ignorance, for you’re about as well acquaint with the workingman as with King Solomon. You say I make up fine stories about tinklers and sailor-men because I know nothing about them. That’s maybe true. But you’re at the same job yourself. You ideelise the workingman, you and your kind, because you’re ignorant. You say that he’s seeking for truth, when he’s only looking for a drink and a rise in wages. You tell me he’s near reality, but I tell you that his notion of reality is often just a short working day and looking on at a footba’-match on Saturday.﻿ ﻿… And when you run down what you call the middle-classes that do three-quarters of the world’s work and keep the machine going and the working man in a job, then I tell you you’re talking havers. Havers!”

===Chinese===
“那你也听俺说。你对工人阶级疯魔得不行，别的阶级一概看不上。可老天爷在上，你到底知道工人些什么呢？……俺就是打他们中间出来的，一辈子都同他们门挨着门住。横看竖看，他们是正经人家，同俺们旁人一样有好有坏。偏有那么一小撮糊涂虫，要把他们供成模范——说什么贴近真理、贴近现实。纯属无知，因为你对工人的熟识，跟对所罗门王（King Solomon）的熟识差不了多少。你说俺编补锅匠和水手的好听故事，是因为俺对他们一无所知。这兴许不假。可你自个儿干的也是这同一行。你把工人理想化了，你和你那一类人，就因为你无知。你说他在寻求真理，其实他不过想寻杯酒喝，盼着工钱涨一涨。你对俺说他贴近现实，俺告诉你，他心里那点现实，往往不过是短一点的工时，外加星期六站着看一场足球。……还有，你贬损的那个你所谓的中产阶级，干着全世界四分之三的活计，维持着机器运转，让工人有工可做——俺告诉你，你这是满嘴胡吣。胡吣！”

===Original===
Mr. McCunn, having delivered his defence of the bourgeoisie, rose abruptly and went to bed. He felt jarred and irritated. His innocent little private domain had been badly trampled by this stray bull of a poet. But as he lay in bed, before blowing out his candle, he had recourse to Walton, and found a passage on which, as on a pillow, he went peacefully to sleep:

===Chinese===
麦坎先生发表完这番对中产阶级的辩护，霍然起身，上床睡了。他只觉心头震恼。他那方清白的小小私域，叫这头诗人的迷途公牛践踏得不轻。可他躺在床上，吹灭蜡烛之前，又求助于沃尔顿，寻到一段文字，便枕着它，如同枕着一只枕头，安然入睡：

===Original===
“As I left this place, and entered into the next field, a second pleasure entertained me; ’twas a handsome milkmaid, that had not yet attained so much age and wisdom as to load her mind with any fears of many things that will never be, as too many men too often do; but she cast away all care, and sang like a nightingale; her voice was good, and the ditty fitted for it; it was the smooth song that was made by *Kit Marlow* now at least fifty years ago. And the milkmaid’s mother sung an answer to it, which was made by *Sir Walter Raleigh* in his younger days. They were old-fashioned poetry, but choicely good; I think much better than the strong lines that are now in fashion in this critical age.”


===Chinese===
“当我离开此地，走进下一片田野时，又一桩乐事款待了我；那是一位俊俏的挤奶姑娘，年纪和见识还未长到那样的地步，以致像太多的人时常做的那样，为许多永不会发生的事装满一脑袋忧惧；她把烦扰尽数抛开，唱得像一只夜莺；她的嗓音美好，曲子也配得上它；那是*基特·马洛*（Kit Marlow）所作的一首婉转的歌，至今至少已有五十年了。挤奶姑娘的母亲又唱了一支答歌，是*沃尔特·雷利爵士*（Sir Walter Raleigh）年轻时所作。它们都是旧派的诗，却是上上之选；我以为，比当今这吹毛求疵的年代里时兴的那些硬朗诗行，要好得多。”

## III. How Childe Roland and Another Came to the Dark Tower / 三、罗兰公子与另一人如何来到黑暗之塔

===Original===
Dickson woke with a vague sense of irritation. As his recollections took form they produced a very unpleasant picture of Mr. John Heritage. The poet had loosened all his placid idols, so that they shook and rattled in the niches where they had been erstwhile so secure. Mr. McCunn had a mind of a singular candour, and was prepared most honestly at all times to revise his views. But by this iconoclast he had been only irritated and in no way convinced. “*Sich* poetry!” he muttered to himself as he shivered in his bath (a daily cold tub instead of his customary hot one on Saturday night being part of the discipline of his holiday). “And yon blethers about the workingman!” he ingeminated as he shaved. He breakfasted alone, having outstripped even the fishermen, and as he ate he arrived at conclusions. He had a great respect for youth, but a line must be drawn somewhere. “The man’s a child,” he decided, “and not like to grow up. The way he’s besotted on everything daft-like, if it’s only *new*. And he’s no rightly young either﻿—speaks like an auld dominie, whiles. And he’s rather impident,” he concluded, with memories of “Dogson.”﻿ ﻿… He was very clear that he never wanted to see him again; that was the reason of his early breakfast. Having clarified his mind by definitions, Dickson felt comforted. He paid his bill, took an affectionate farewell of the landlord, and at 7:30 precisely stepped out into the gleaming morning.

===Chinese===
迪克森醒来，心头浮着一股说不清的恼意。随着回忆渐渐成形，约翰·赫里蒂奇先生在他脑中拼出了一幅极不愉快的画像。那位诗人把他安安稳稳的诸般偶像全摇松了，害得它们在原先安置得妥妥帖帖的壁龛里晃荡作响。麦坎先生心地坦率得出奇，随时都诚心诚意准备修正自己的见解。可对于这个捣毁圣像的人，他只觉得光火，丝毫没有被说服。“这号诗！”他在浴缸里打着哆嗦喃喃自语（每天洗冷水浴，代替他惯常的周六晚热水浴，是他这趟假日修身养性的功课之一）。“还有那些关于工人的胡吣！”他刮脸时又念叨了一遍。他连垂钓客们都起得早，独自用了早饭，边吃边得出了结论。他一向敬重青年，但总得有个界限。“此人是个孩子，”他断定，“而且看不出有长大的指望。他疯魔的那一路，桩桩件件都是糊涂行径，只要够*新*就行。再说他也算不得真年轻——说起话来倒像个老塾师似的，时不时。而且他相当没规矩，”他末了下了断语，脑子里冒出“狗逊”那一声。……他心里明明白白，再也不想见到此人了；所以他才吃得这么早。用几条定义把心思理清之后，迪克森觉得宽慰了。他结了账，同店主亲亲热热道了别，7:30 整，迈步走进了明晃晃的清晨。

===Original===
It was such a day as only a Scots April can show. The cobbled streets of Kirkmichael still shone with the night’s rain, but the storm clouds had fled before a mild south wind, and the whole circumference of the sky was a delicate translucent blue. Homely breakfast smells came from the houses and delighted Mr. McCunn’s nostrils; a squalling child was a pleasant reminder of an awakening world, the urban counterpart to the morning song of birds; even the sanitary cart seemed a picturesque vehicle. He bought his ration of buns and ginger biscuits at a baker’s shop whence various ragamuffin boys were preparing to distribute the householders’ bread, and took his way up the Gallows Hill to the Burgh Muir almost with regret at leaving so pleasant a habitation.

===Chinese===
只有苏格兰的四月才拿得出这样的天气。柯克迈克尔的鹅卵石街道还闪着夜雨的湿光，风暴云却已在和煦的南风前逃散，天穹四际是一片纤巧的半透明蔚蓝。家家户户飘出家常早饭的香气，熏得麦坎先生满心欢喜；一个嚎哭的孩子令人愉快地想起这世界正在醒来，正是鸟儿晨歌的城市对位；连环卫马车瞧着都像一件入画的家什。他在一家面包房买了定量的小圆面包和姜汁饼干——店门口一群衣衫褴褛的顽童正准备去给各家各户送面包——随后便上了绞架山（Gallows Hill），朝伯勒荒原（Burgh Muir）走去，离开这么可心的居所，几乎有些恋恋不舍。

===Original===
A chronicle of ripe vintages must pass lightly over small beer. I will not dwell on his leisurely progress in the bright weather, or on his luncheon in a coppice of young firs, or on his thoughts which had returned to the idyllic. I take up the narrative at about three o’clock in the afternoon, when he is revealed seated on a milestone examining his map. For he had come, all unwitting, to a turning of the ways, and his choice is the cause of this veracious history.

===Chinese===
记载陈年佳酿的编年史，对淡啤酒只该一笔带过。我不会细写他在晴朗天气里从容的行进，不细写他在一片幼杉树丛里用的午餐，也不细写他已经回转田园牧歌的心绪。我要从下午三点钟左右接起叙述，那时的他，正坐在一块里程碑上研究地图。因为他已经全然不觉地走到了一个岔路口，而他的选择，正是这部信史得以成立的缘由。

===Original===
The place was high up on a bare moor, which showed a white lodge among pines, a white cottage in a green nook by a burnside, and no other marks of human dwelling. To his left, which was the east, the heather rose to a low ridge of hill, much scarred with peat-bogs, behind which appeared the blue shoulder of a considerable mountain. Before him the road was lost momentarily in the woods of a shooting-box, but reappeared at a great distance climbing a swell of upland which seemed to be the glacis of a jumble of bold summits. There was a pass there, the map told him, which led into Galloway. It was the road he had meant to follow, but as he sat on the milestone his purpose wavered. For there seemed greater attractions in the country which lay to the westward. Mr. McCunn, be it remembered, was not in search of brown heath and shaggy wood; he wanted greenery and the Spring.

===Chinese===
这地方高踞在一片光秃秃的荒原上，原上只看得见松林中一座白色门房、溪畔绿湾里一座白色村舍，再无别的人居痕迹。他左手边是东面，石楠爬上一道低矮的山脊，脊上满是泥炭沼的疤痕，山脊背后耸出一座大山的蓝色肩头。他面前，道路一时隐没在一座猎场的树林里，随后在极远处重新出现，爬上一道高地漫坡，那坡像是重重险峰堆叠而成的堡垒斜坡。地图告诉他，那里有一道山口，通进加洛韦（Galloway）。那本是他打算走的路，可他坐在里程碑上，心思动摇了。因为西边的乡野仿佛有更大的吸引力。须知麦坎先生寻找的不是棕色的荒原和蓬乱的树林；他要的是青翠和春天。

===Original===
Westward there ran out a peninsula in the shape of an isosceles triangle, of which his present high road was the base. At a distance of a mile or so a railway ran parallel to the road, and he could see the smoke of a goods train waiting at a tiny station islanded in acres of bog. Thence the moor swept down to meadows and scattered copses, above which hung a thin haze of smoke which betokened a village. Beyond it were further woodlands, not firs but old shady trees, and as they narrowed to a point the gleam of two tiny estuaries appeared on either side. He could not see the final cape, but he saw the sea beyond it, flawed with catspaws, gold in the afternoon sun, and on it a small herring smack flapping listless sails.

===Chinese===
西边伸出一座半岛，形如等腰三角形，他脚下的大路便是三角形的底边。一英里开外，一条铁路与公路并行，他望得见一列货车喷出的烟，正停在四面被几十英亩沼泽围成孤岛的小站上。从那里起，荒原一路倾泻到草场和疏疏落落的小树丛，树丛上方浮着一缕薄烟，昭示着一个村落。村子再过去又是成片的林地，不是杉树，而是苍老浓荫的大树，林带收拢成尖角的地方，两侧各闪出一小片河口的微光。最后的海角他看不见，但他望得见海角外的大海，海面被猫爪风皱起波纹，在午后阳光下一片金黄，海上一艘捕鲱小帆船懒懒地拍着帆。

===Original===
Something in the view caught and held his fancy. He conned his map, and made out the names. The peninsula was called the Cruives﻿—an old name apparently, for it was in antique lettering. He vaguely remembered that “cruives” had something to do with fishing, doubtless in the two streams which flanked it. One he had already crossed, the Laver, a clear tumbling water springing from green hills; the other, the Garple, descended from the rougher mountains to the south. The hidden village bore the name of Dalquharter, and the uncouth syllables awoke some vague recollection in his mind. The great house in the trees beyond﻿—it must be a great house, for the map showed large policies﻿—was Huntingtower.

===Chinese===
这片景色里有什么东西攫住了他的心，且不肯放手。他细细研读地图，认出了各处的名字。那座半岛叫克鲁夫斯（the Cruives）——显然是个古老的名字，因为地图上用的是古体字母。他依稀记得“克鲁夫斯”（cruives，即鱼梁）跟捕鱼有些关系，多半是指夹峙半岛的那两条河。一条他已经趟过了，是拉弗河（the Laver），一道从青山间奔流而下的清溪；另一条加普尔河（the Garple），则从南边更粗粝的群山中流下来。那座隐没的村子名叫达尔夸特（Dalquharter），这几个拗口的音节在他心里唤醒了某种模糊的记忆。树林深处的大宅——必定是座大宅，因为地图上标着大片园地——叫做亨廷塔（Huntingtower）。

===Original===
The last name fascinated and almost decided him. He pictured an ancient keep by the sea, defended by converging rivers, which some old Comyn lord of Galloway had built to command the shore road and from which he had sallied to hunt in his wild hills.﻿ ﻿… He liked the way the moor dropped down to green meadows, and the mystery of the dark woods beyond. He wanted to explore the twin waters, and see how they entered that strange shimmering sea. The odd names, the odd cul-de-sac of a peninsula, powerfully attracted him. Why should he not spend a night there, for the map showed clearly that Dalquharter had an inn? He must decide promptly, for before him a side-road left the highway, and the signpost bore the legend, “Dalquharter and Huntingtower.”

===Chinese===
最后这个名字叫他着迷，几乎一举定了他的主意。他想象着一座海边的古老堡楼，两条河在此汇拢护持，是加洛韦某位古老的科明家（Comyn）领主所建，为的是扼守滨海大道，他从那里出击，去自己荒凉的山中行猎。……他喜欢荒原一路降到青青草场的走势，喜欢再远处黑沉沉树林的神秘。他想探一探那对孪生的河水，看看它们怎样注入那片奇光闪闪的大海。这些古怪的名字，这条死胡同似的古怪半岛，强烈地吸引着他。何不在那儿过一夜呢？地图上明明白白，达尔夸特是有一家客栈的。他必须当机立断，因为面前一条岔路正从大路上分出去，路牌上写着：“达尔夸特与亨廷塔”。

===Original===
Mr. McCunn, being a cautious and pious man, took the omens. He tossed a penny﻿—heads go on, tails turn aside. It fell tails.

===Chinese===
麦坎先生素来谨慎而虔敬，便占了个卜。他抛起一枚便士——正面走原路，反面拐弯。落下来是反面。

===Original===
He knew as soon as he had taken three steps down the side-road that he was doing something momentous, and the exhilaration of enterprise stole into his soul. It occurred to him that this was the kind of landscape that he had always especially hankered after, and had made pictures of when he had a longing for the country on him﻿—a wooded cape between streams, with meadows inland and then a long lift of heather. He had the same feeling of expectancy, of something most interesting and curious on the eve of happening, that he had had long ago when he waited on the curtain rising at his first play. His spirits soared like the lark, and he took to singing. If only the inn at Dalquharter were snug and empty, this was going to be a day in ten thousand. Thus mirthfully he swung down the rough grass-grown road, past the railway, till he came to a point where heath began to merge in pasture, and dry-stone walls split the moor into fields. Suddenly his pace slackened and song died on his lips. For, approaching from the right by a tributary path, was the Poet.

===Chinese===
顺着岔路走了不到三步，他便知道自己正在做一桩非同小可的事，一种开创事业的兴奋悄悄潜进了他的灵魂。他忽然想到，这正是他一向特别向往、每逢乡愁发作时便在心里描画的那种风景——两水之间一座林木葱茏的海角，内陆是草场，再往里是一道长长的石楠高坡。他心头涌起同样的期待感，觉得顶有趣、顶奇妙的事情正要发生，就像多年前他等着第一出戏开锣、幕布将起时那样。他的兴致像云雀一般直冲云霄，不由得唱起歌来。只要达尔夸特的客栈温暖而清净，这一天便是万里挑一的好日子了。他就这样欢欢喜喜地荡下那条粗粝的草生土路，越过铁路，来到石楠荒原渐渐化入牧草、干垒石墙把荒原切割成田亩的地方。忽然间他的脚步慢了下来，歌声在唇边消散。因为从右边一条小径迎面走来的，正是诗人。

===Original===
Mr. Heritage saw him afar off and waved a friendly hand. In spite of his chagrin Dickson could not but confess that he had misjudged his critic. Striding with long steps over the heather, his jacket open to the wind, his face aglow and his capless head like a whin-bush for disorder, he cut a more wholesome and picturesque figure than in the smoking-room the night before. He seemed to be in a companionable mood, for he brandished his stick and shouted greetings.

===Chinese===
赫里蒂奇先生远远望见他，友好地挥了挥手。迪克森尽管懊恼，却不能不承认自己昨晚看错了这位批评家。他大步跨过石楠丛，衣襟敞开迎着风，满脸红光，没戴帽子的脑袋乱得像丛荆豆，比昨晚吸烟室里那副模样更健康、更入画。他像是很愿意与人做伴的样子，一面挥着手杖，一面高喊着打招呼。

===Original===
“Well met!” he cried; “I was hoping to fall in with you again. You must have thought me a pretty fair cub last night.”

===Chinese===
“幸会，幸会！”他喊道，“我还巴望再遇上你呢。昨晚你一定觉得我是个相当够格的愣头青了。”

===Original===
“I did that,” was the dry answer.

===Chinese===
“可不是嘛。”回答干巴巴的。

===Original===
“Well, I want to apologise. God knows what made me treat you to a university-extension lecture. I may not agree with you, but every man’s entitled to his own views, and it was dashed poor form for me to start jawing you.”

===Chinese===
“这个嘛，我要向你道歉。天知道是什么鬼使神差，叫我给你上了一堂大学推广讲座。我可以不同意你的看法，可人人都有权持有自己的见解，我那样劈头盖脸训你，实在差劲得很。”

===Original===
Mr. McCunn had no gift of nursing anger, and was very susceptible to apologies.

===Chinese===
麦坎先生素无怀怒的本事，而且极吃道歉这一套。

===Original===
“That’s all right,” he murmured. “Don’t mention it. I’m wondering what brought you down here, for it’s off the road.”

===Chinese===
“没什么，”他低声道，“别放在心上。俺正纳闷是什么把你引到这儿来的呢，这儿可不在正经路上。”

===Original===
“Caprice. Pure caprice. I liked the look of this butt-end of nowhere.”

===Chinese===
“一时兴起。纯粹的一时兴起。我喜欢这个天涯海角尽头的模样。”

===Original===
“Same here. I’ve aye thought there was something terrible nice about a wee cape with a village at the neck of it and a burn each side.”

===Chinese===
“俺也一样。俺一向觉得，一座小小的海角，颈子上有座村子，两边各一条溪水，那样子实在妙得很。”

===Original===
“Now that’s interesting,” said Mr. Heritage. “You’re obsessed by a particular type of landscape. Ever read Freud?”

===Chinese===
“这就有点意思了，”赫里蒂奇先生说，“你被一种特定的风景迷住了心窍。读过弗洛伊德（Freud）吗？”

===Original===
Dickson shook his head.

===Chinese===
迪克森摇了摇头。

===Original===
“Well, you’ve got an odd complex somewhere. I wonder where the key lies. Cape﻿—woods﻿—two rivers﻿—moor behind. Ever been in love, Dogson?”

===Chinese===
“那么，你心里某处准有个古怪的情结。我纳闷钥匙藏在哪里。海角——树林——两条河——背后是荒原。你恋爱过吗，狗逊？”

===Original===
Mr. McCunn was startled. “Love” was a word rarely mentioned in his circle except on deathbeds. “I’ve been a married man for thirty years,” he said hurriedly.

===Chinese===
麦坎先生吓了一跳。“爱情”这个词，在他的圈子里除了临终床前是难得出口的。“俺结了婚都三十年了。”他慌忙说道。

===Original===
“That won’t do. It should have been a hopeless affair﻿—the last sight of the lady on a spur of coast with water on three sides﻿—that kind of thing, you know. Or it might have happened to an ancestor.﻿ ﻿… But you don’t look the kind of breed for hopeless attachments. More likely some scoundrelly old Dogson long ago found sanctuary in this sort of place. Do you dream about it?”

===Chinese===
“那可不算数。应该是一场无望的恋情——最后望见那位女士立在一道三面环水的海岬上——你知道，是那一类的事情。要不然，就是哪位祖上经历过。……可你看上去不像会陷入无望痴恋的血统。倒更像是很久以前哪个恶棍老狗逊在这一类地方寻得过庇护。你梦见过这地方吗？”

===Original===
“Not exactly.”

===Chinese===
“说不上。”

===Original===
“Well, I do. The queer thing is that I’ve got the same prepossession as you. As soon as I spotted this Cruives place on the map this morning, I saw it was what I was after. When I came in sight of it I almost shouted. I don’t very often dream, but when I do that’s the place I frequent. Odd, isn’t it?”

===Chinese===
“我可是梦见的。怪就怪在，我同你怀着同一样的偏执。今天早上我在地图上一眼瞥见克鲁夫斯这地方，就知道它正是我要找的。望见它的那一刻，我差点欢呼出来。我不常做梦，可一旦做了，梦里常去的就是这地方。怪不怪？”

===Original===
Mr. McCunn was deeply interested at this unexpected revelation of romance. “Maybe it’s being in love,” he daringly observed.

===Chinese===
这番不期而至的浪漫揭秘让麦坎先生大感兴趣。“说不定就是恋爱闹的。”他斗胆发表意见。

===Original===
The Poet demurred. “No. I’m not a connoisseur of obvious sentiment. That explanation might fit your case, but not mine. I’m pretty certain there’s something hideous at the back of *my* complex﻿—some grim old business tucked away back in the ages. For though I’m attracted by the place, I’m frightened too!”

===Chinese===
诗人表示异议。“不。我这人不当那种一眼见底的感情的行家。这个解释或许适用于你的情形，却不适用于我。我相当确信，*我*的情结背后藏着什么可怖的东西——某种远在太古之前便深深掩埋的阴森旧事。因为这块地方虽然吸引着我，却也叫我害怕！”

===Original===
There seemed no room for fear in the delicate landscape now opening before them. In front in groves of birch and rowans smoked the first houses of a tiny village. The road had become a green “loaning” on the ample margin of which cattle grazed. The moorland still showed itself in spits of heather, and some distance off, where a rivulet ran in a hollow, there were signs of a fire and figures near it. These last Mr. Heritage regarded with disapproval.

===Chinese===
在眼前徐徐展开的这片精致风景里，似乎容不下恐惧二字。前方，桦树与花楸的树丛间，一座小小村落最初的几缕炊烟升起。路已变成一条绿草茵茵的“村巷”（loaning），巷道宽敞的边沿上牛群在吃草。荒原仍以一条条石楠楔子的模样露着脸；不远处，一条小溪流经洼地，那里有生过火的痕迹，旁边还有几个人影。对最后这几样，赫里蒂奇先生面露不以为然之色。

===Original===
“Some infernal trippers!” he murmured. “Or Boy Scouts. They desecrate everything. Why can’t the *tunicatus popellus* keep away from a paradise like this!” Dickson, a democrat who felt nothing incongruous in the presence of other holidaymakers, was meditating a sharp rejoinder, when Mr. Heritage’s tone changed.

===Chinese===
“该死的观光客！”他咕哝道，“要么就是童子军。他们糟蹋一切。这班*穿裤子的平头百姓*（tunicatus popellus）就不能离这样的天堂远一点吗！”迪克森是个民主派人士，丝毫不觉得别处来的度假客在场有什么碍眼，正想着不客气地回敬一句，赫里蒂奇先生的语气却变了。

===Original===
“Ye gods! What a village!” he cried, as they turned a corner. There were not more than a dozen whitewashed houses, all set in little gardens of wallflower and daffodil and early fruit blossom. A triangle of green filled the intervening space, and in it stood an ancient wooden pump. There was no schoolhouse or kirk; not even a post-office﻿—only a red box in a cottage side. Beyond rose the high wall and the dark trees of the demesne, and to the right up a byroad which clung to the park edge stood a two-storeyed building which bore the legend “The Cruives Inn.”

===Chinese===
“天神哪！好一个村子！”转过街角时他叫道。刷得雪白的房子统共不过十几座，座座都坐落在小花园里，园中开着桂竹香、水仙和早开的果树花。中间的空地是一块绿三角，立着一架古老的木头水泵。没有校舍，没有教堂；连个邮局都没有——只在一家村舍的山墙上钉着个红信箱。再过去，便是庄园的高墙和黑沉沉的树木；右手边，一条紧贴园圃边缘的小路坡上，立着一幢两层小楼，门上题着“克鲁夫斯客栈”（The Cruives Inn）几个字。

===Original===
The Poet became lyrical. “At last!” he cried. “The village of my dreams! Not a sign of commerce! No church or school or beastly recreation hall! Nothing but these divine little cottages and an ancient pub! Dogson, I warn you, I’m going to have the devil of a tea.” And he declaimed:

===Chinese===
诗人进入了抒情状态。“终于！”他喊道，“我梦中的村子！没有一丝买卖气！没有教堂、学校，也没有可恶的游乐厅！只有这些神圣的小村舍和一家老酒馆！狗逊，我警告你，我要放开肚皮大吃一顿茶点。”说罢他高声吟诵：

===Original===
“Thou shalt hear a song
					

===Chinese===
“你将听到一支歌

===Original===
					After a while which Gods may listen to;
					

===Chinese===
不多时，连诸神也会侧耳听；

===Original===
					But place the flask upon the board and wait
					

===Chinese===
且先把酒瓶摆上桌，等一等，

===Original===
					Until the stranger hath allayed his thirst,
					

===Chinese===
待客人润过了渴再听不迟，

===Original===
					For poets, grasshoppers and nightingales
					

===Chinese===
因为诗人、蚱蜢和夜莺

===Original===
					Sing cheerily but when the throat is moist.”

===Chinese===
唯有喉咙湿润时才唱得欢畅。”

===Original===
Dickson, too, longed with sensual gusto for tea. But, as they drew nearer, the inn lost its hospitable look. The cobbles of the yard were weedy, as if rarely visited by traffic, a pane in a window was broken, and the blinds hung tattered. The garden was a wilderness, and the doorstep had not been scoured for weeks. But the place had a landlord, for he had seen them approach and was waiting at the door to meet them.

===Chinese===
迪克森也怀着感官的馋劲儿向往着茶点。可随着两人走近，客栈却渐渐失去了好客的模样。院子里的鹅卵石缝长满野草，像是不大有人车光顾；一扇窗上破了块玻璃，百叶窗帘破破烂烂地挂着。花园成了荒野，门阶也有好几个星期没刷洗过。但这地方毕竟有个店主，因为他已望见他们走近，正候在门口迎客。

===Original===
He was a big man in his shirt sleeves, wearing old riding breeches unbuttoned at the knees, and thick ploughman’s boots. He had no leggings, and his fleshy calves were imperfectly covered with woollen socks. His face was large and pale, his neck bulged, and he had a gross unshaven jowl. He was a type familiar to students of society; not the innkeeper, which is a thing consistent with good breeding and all the refinements; a type not unknown in the House of Lords, especially among recent creations, common enough in the House of Commons and the City of London, and by no means infrequent in the governing circles of Labour; the type known to the discerning as the Licensed Victualler.

===Chinese===
他是个大块头，只穿衬衫，套一条膝弯处没扣的旧马裤，蹬一双庄稼汉的厚靴子。他没打绑腿，多肉的小腿肚靠毛线袜遮掩，遮得也不严实。他脸盘又大又白，脖子青筋虬结，一圈没刮的肥下巴。研究社会的人对这一类型是熟悉的；他不是那种可与良好教养和种种雅致并存一体的店主；这一类型，在上议院并非无人，在新近册封的贵族中尤其多见，在下议院和伦敦城里也够常见，在工党的执政圈子里更绝非罕见；有眼力的人管这一类型叫持照酒馆老板（Licensed Victualler）。

===Original===
His face was wrinkled in official smiles, and he gave the travellers a hearty good afternoon.

===Chinese===
他脸上堆起官方式的微笑，向两位旅人热热闹闹地道了声下午好。

===Original===
“Can we stop here for the night?” Dickson asked.

===Chinese===
“我们能在贵店过夜吗？”迪克森问。

===Original===
The landlord looked sharply at him, and then replied to Mr. Heritage. His expression passed from official bonhomie to official contrition.

===Chinese===
店主锐利地瞟了他一眼，随即转头答赫里蒂奇先生。他的表情从官方式的亲热转成了官方式的歉疚。

===Original===
“Impossible, gentlemen. Quite impossible.﻿ ﻿… Ye couldn’t have come at a worse time. I’ve only been here a fortnight myself, and we haven’t got right shaken down yet. Even then I might have made shift to do with ye, but the fact is we’ve illness in the house, and I’m fair at my wits’ end. It breaks my heart to turn gentlemen away and me that keen to get the business started. But there it is!” He spat vigorously as if to emphasise the desperation of his quandary.

===Chinese===
“不可能，二位先生。断然不可能。……你们来得真是再糟不过的时候了。俺自个儿到此地也才两个礼拜，还没安顿利索呢。若不是这样，俺好歹也能将就安置二位，可实情是俺家里有人害了病，俺简直没了主意。把先生们往外推，俺心里也着实不忍——俺是多盼着把这买卖开张起来啊。可事实就是这么回事！”他重重啐了一口，像是要强调自己处境的走投无路。

===Original===
The man was clearly Scots, but his native speech was overlaid with something alien, something which might have been acquired in America or in going down to the sea in ships. He hitched his breeches, too, with a nautical air.

===Chinese===
此人分明是苏格兰人，但他的家乡口音上覆着一层异样的东西，一层也许是在美洲挣来的、也许是跑船跑出来的东西。他往上提马裤的架势，也带着股水手气。

===Original===
“Is there nowhere else we can put up?” Dickson asked.

===Chinese===
“我们还能在别处投宿吗？”迪克森问。

===Original===
“Not in this one-horse place. Just a wheen auld wives that packed thegether they haven’t room for an extra hen. But it’s grand weather, and it’s not above seven miles to Auchenlochan. Say the word and I’ll yoke the horse and drive ye there.”

===Chinese===
“在这种鸟不拉屎的小地方，可没有第二家了。只剩下几个老婆子，挤挤挨挨住在一处，多一只母鸡也塞不下喽。不过天气这么好，去奥琴洛坎（Auchenlochan）也不过七英里光景。二位点个头，俺就套上马，送你们过去。”

===Original===
“Thank you. We prefer to walk,” said Mr. Heritage. Dickson would have tarried to inquire after the illness in the house, but his companion hurried him off. Once he looked back, and saw the landlord still on the doorstep gazing after them.

===Chinese===
“多谢。我们情愿步行。”赫里蒂奇先生说。迪克森本想逗留一下，问问店里的病人，同伴却催着他走了。他回头望了一回，只见那店主还立在门阶上，目送他们远去。

===Original===
“That fellow’s a swine,” said Mr. Heritage sourly. “I wouldn’t trust my neck in his pothouse. Now, Dogson, I’m hanged if I’m going to leave this place. We’ll find a corner in the village somehow. Besides, I’m determined on tea.”

===Chinese===
“那家伙是头猪，”赫里蒂奇先生尖刻地说，“把脖子交到他那种下等酒馆里，我可不放心。听好，狗逊，要我离开这地方，休想。村里随便哪个角落，咱们总能找一个出来。再说，这顿茶点我是吃定了。”

===Original===
The little street slept in the clear pure light of an early April evening. Blue shadows lay on the white road, and a delicate aroma of cooking tantalised hungry nostrils. The near meadows shone like pale gold against the dark lift of the moor. A light wind had begun to blow from the west and carried the faintest tang of salt.
