The village at that hour was pure Paradise, and Dickson was of the Poet’s opinion. At all costs they must spend the night there.

===Chinese===
小小的街巷睡在四月黄昏清澈纯净的光里。白色的路上铺着蓝影，一阵饭菜的幽香勾着饥饿的鼻孔。近处的草场映着荒原黑黢黢的高坡，闪着淡金色的光。一阵微风开始从西边吹来，捎着最淡最淡的一丝咸味。这时刻的村子是十足的乐园，迪克森完全赞成诗人的意见：说什么也得在此地过夜。

===Original===
They selected a cottage whiter and neater than the others, which stood at a corner, where a narrow lane turned southward. Its thatched roof had been lately repaired, and starched curtains of a dazzling whiteness decorated the small, closely-shut windows. Likewise it had a green door and a polished brass knocker.

===Chinese===
他们挑中了一座比别家更白净、更齐整的村舍，它立在街角，一条窄巷在那里拐向南去。茅草顶新近修葺过，关得严严实实的小窗上挂着浆洗得雪亮的窗帘。它还有一扇绿门和一只擦得锃亮的黄铜门环。

===Original===
Tacitly the duty of envoy was entrusted to Mr. McCunn. Leaving the other at the gate, he advanced up the little path lined with quartz stones, and politely but firmly dropped the brass knocker. He must have been observed, for ere the noise had ceased the door opened, and an elderly woman stood before him. She had a sharply-cut face, the rudiments of a beard, big spectacles on her nose, and an old-fashioned lace cap on her smooth white hair. A little grim she looked at first sight, because of her thin lips and Roman nose, but her mild curious eyes corrected the impression and gave the envoy confidence.

===Chinese===
出使的重任心照不宣地落到了麦坎先生头上。他把同伴留在院门口，自己踏上那条镶着石英石的甬道，礼貌而不失坚定地叩响了黄铜门环。他必定是早被瞧见了，因为敲声未歇，门已打开，一位上了年纪的妇人立在他面前。她面容棱角分明，下巴上初生出几茎胡须，鼻梁上架着大眼镜，平滑的白发上扣一顶老式蕾丝便帽。乍一看她有几分严厉，因为薄薄的嘴唇和鹰钩鼻，可她那双温和而好奇的眼睛纠正了这印象，给出了使的人以信心。

===Original===
“Good afternoon, mistress,” he said, broadening his voice to something more rustical than his normal Glasgow speech. “Me and my friend are paying our first visit here, and we’re terrible taken up with the place. We would like to bide the night, but the inn is no’ taking folk. Is there any chance, think you, of a bed here?”

===Chinese===
“午安，大娘，”他说道，把嗓音放得比他平日的格拉斯哥腔更土气了些，“俺和俺朋友是头一回到这地方来，实在喜欢得不得了。俺们想在此地过一夜，可客栈又不收人。您瞧，这儿可有希望能搭个铺？”

===Original===
“I’ll no tell ye a lee,” said the woman. “There’s twae guid beds in the loft. But I dinna tak’ lodgers and I dinna want to be bothered wi’ ye. I’m an auld wumman and no’ as stoot as I was. Ye’d better try doun the street. Eppie Home micht tak’ ye.”

===Chinese===
“俺不哄你，”妇人道，“阁楼里倒有两张好床。可俺不留宿，也懒得招你们麻烦。俺是个老婆子喽，身子骨也不比当年。你们还是往街那头问问去。埃皮·霍姆（Eppie Home）兴许肯留你们。”

===Original===
Dickson wore his most ingratiating smile. “But, mistress, Eppie Home’s house is no’ yours. We’ve taken a tremendous fancy to this bit. Can you no’ manage to put with us for the one night? We’re quiet auld-fashioned folk and we’ll no’ trouble you much. Just our tea and maybe an egg to it, and a bowl of porridge in the morning.”

===Chinese===
迪克森摆出他最讨好的笑脸。“可是大娘，埃皮·霍姆的家终究不是您的家呀。俺们相中了这块地方，相中了不得。您就不能设法留俺们住上一夜？俺们是安生的老派人，不会搅扰您多少。只要一顿茶点，兴许加个鸡蛋，明早一碗粥就行了。”

===Original===
The woman seemed to relent. “Whaur’s your freend?” she asked, peering over her spectacles towards the garden gate. The waiting Mr. Heritage, seeing her eyes moving in his direction, took off his cap with a brave gesture and advanced. “Glorious weather, Madam,” he declared.

===Chinese===
妇人似乎软了下来。“你那位朋友呢？”她从眼镜上方朝院门口张望，问道。候在那儿的赫里蒂奇先生见她的目光转向自己，潇洒地摘下便帽，走上前来。“天气真好，夫人。”他朗声道。

===Original===
“English,” whispered Dickson to the woman, in explanation.

===Chinese===
“英格兰人。”迪克森低声向妇人解释。

===Original===
She examined the Poet’s neat clothes and Mr. McCunn’s homely garments, and apparently found them reassuring. “Come in,” she said shortly. “I see ye’re wilfu’ folk and I’ll hae to dae my best for ye.”

===Chinese===
她打量了诗人齐整的衣装和麦坎先生家常的衣衫，显然觉得可以放心。“进来吧。”她简短地说，“俺看你们是犟脾气的人，俺也只好尽力和你们对付喽。”

===Original===
A quarter of an hour later the two travellers, having been introduced to two spotless beds in the loft, and having washed luxuriously at the pump in the back yard, were seated in Mrs. Morran’s kitchen before a meal which fulfilled their wildest dreams. She had been baking that morning, so there were white scones and barley scones, and oaten farles, and russet pancakes. There were three boiled eggs for each of them; there was a segment of an immense currant cake (“a present from my guid brither last Hogmanay”); there was skim-milk cheese; there were several kinds of jam, and there was a pot of dark-gold heather honey. “Try hinny and aitcake,” said their hostess. “My man used to say he never fund onything as guid in a’ his days.”

===Chinese===
一刻钟后，两位旅人已被领着看过阁楼上两张一尘不染的床，在后院的水泵边畅畅快快洗漱完毕，坐在了莫兰太太（Mrs. Morran）的厨房里，面对一桌远超他们最狂野梦想的饭食。她那天上午烤过面食，于是有白面烤饼、大麦烤饼、燕麦四角饼，还有焦黄的煎饼。每人三只煮蛋；一大块葡萄干蛋糕切下来的一角（“俺那好兄弟去年苏格兰除夕（Hogmanay）送的礼”）；有脱脂奶酪；有好几样果酱，还有一罐暗金色的石楠蜜。“尝尝蜜配燕麦饼，”女主人说，“俺那口子从前常讲，他一辈子没吃过这么好的东西。”

===Original===
Presently they heard her story. Her name was Morran, and she had been a widow these ten years. Of her family her son was in South Africa, one daughter a lady’s maid in London, and the other married to a schoolmaster in Kyle. The son had been in France fighting, and had come safely through. He had spent a month or two with her before his return, and, she feared, had found it dull. “There’s no’ a man body in the place. Naething but auld wives.”

===Chinese===
不多时他们听来了她的身世。她姓莫兰，守寡已经十年。她家中，儿子在南非，一个女儿在伦敦给人做贴身女仆，另一个嫁了凯尔（Kyle）的一位教书先生。儿子先前在法兰西打仗，安然无恙地回来了。归队之前他在她这儿住了一两个月，只怕是住得发闷。“这地方连一个男人影子都没有。净是些老婆子。”

===Original===
That was what the innkeeper had told them. Mr. McCunn inquired concerning the inn.

===Chinese===
这正是那客栈老板对他们说过的话。麦坎先生便问起客栈的事。

===Original===
“There’s new folk just come. What’s this they ca’ them?﻿—Robson﻿—Dobson﻿—aye, Dobson. What for wad they no’ tak’ ye in? Does the man think he’s a laird to refuse folk that gait?”

===Chinese===
“新来了户人家。他们叫个啥来着？——罗布森——多布森（Dobson）——对了，多布森。他们为啥不肯收留你们？莫非那男人当自个儿是老爷，还能这么打发上门的客？”

===Original===
“He said he had illness in the house.”

===Chinese===
“他说家里有人害病。”

===Original===
Mrs. Morran meditated. “Whae in the world can be lyin’ there? The man bides his lane. He got a lassie frae Auchenlochan to cook, but she and her box gaed off in the post-cairt yestreen. I doot he tell’t ye a lee, though it’s no for me to juidge him. I’ve never spoken a word to ane o’ thae new folk.”

===Chinese===
莫兰太太寻思起来。“这可怪了，家里躺着的能是谁呢？那男人独门独户过活。他从奥琴洛坎雇了个姑娘烧饭，可那姑娘连同她的箱子，昨儿晚上搭邮车走喽。俺疑心他是扯了个谎，不过这话轮不到俺来评断。这些新来的人，俺统共没同他们搭过一句话。”

===Original===
Dickson inquired about the “new folk.”

===Chinese===
迪克森问起那些“新来的人”。

===Original===
“They’re a’ new come in the last three weeks, and there’s no’ a man o’ the auld stock left. John Blackstocks at the Wast Lodge dee’d o’ pneumony last back-end, and auld Simon Tappie at the Gairdens flitted to Maybole a year come Mairtinmas. There’s naebody at the Gairdens noo, but there’s a man come to the Wast Lodge, a blackavised body wi’ a face like bend-leather. Tam Robison used to bide at the South Lodge, but Tam got killed about Mesopotamy, and his wife took the bairns to her guidsire up at the Garpleheid. I seen the man that’s in the South Lodge gaun up the street when I was finishin’ my denner﻿—a shilpit body and a lameter, but he hirples as fast as ither folk run. He’s no’ bonny to look at. I canna think what the factor’s ettlin’ at to let sic’ ill-faured chiels come about the toun.”

===Chinese===
“全是这三个礼拜里新来的，老户人家一个男子也没剩下。西门房（Wast Lodge）的约翰·布莱克斯托克斯（John Blackstocks）去年秋后害肺炎死喽，‘花园子’（the Gairdens）的老西蒙·塔皮（Simon Tappie）呢，打上回圣马丁节（Mairtinmas）算起，搬去梅博尔（Maybole）有一年喽。如今‘花园子’里没人，可西门房来了个男人，黑脸膛，一张脸跟鞣牛皮似的。南门房（South Lodge）原先住着塔姆·罗比森（Tam Robison），可塔姆在美索不达米亚那边送了命，他女人带着娃娃们投奔加普尔黑德（the Garpleheid）她祖父去喽。俺吃完晌午饭，瞧见眼下住南门房的那个男人正往街上走——面黄肌瘦的一个瘸子，可他一瘸一拐，走得跟旁人跑着一般快。那模样可不好看。俺想不明白，管产业的先生到底打的什么算盘，放这么些丑八怪到镇子附近来。”

===Original===
Their hostess was rapidly rising in Dickson’s esteem. She sat very straight in her chair, eating with the careful gentility of a bird, and primming her thin lips after every mouthful of tea.

===Chinese===
这位女主人在迪克森心目中的地位急速上升。她在椅子里坐得笔直，吃相像鸟儿一般细巧文雅，每啜一口茶，都要把薄嘴唇抿一抿。

===Original===
“Who bides in the Big House?” he asked. “Huntingtower is the name, isn’t it?”

===Chinese===
“大宅里住着谁？”他问，“那宅子叫亨廷塔，对吧？”

===Original===
“When I was a lassie they ca’ed it Dalquharter Hoose, and Huntingtower was the auld rickle o’ stanes at the sea-end. But naething wad serve the last laird’s faither but he maun change the name, for he was clean daft about what they ca’ antickities. Ye speir whae bides in the Hoose? Naebody, since the young laird dee’d. It’s standin’ cauld and lanely and steikit, and it aince the cheeriest dwallin’ in a’ Carrick.”

===Chinese===
“俺做姑娘那阵儿，人家管它叫达尔夸特府（Dalquharter Hoose），亨廷塔呢，原是海角尽头一堆老石垛。可末代老爷的爹，偏是什么都不依，非要改名不可，他对人家说的什么‘古物’，魔怔得很哩。你问府里如今住着谁？谁也没有喽，打少爷一死就空着。它立在那儿，又冷清又凄凉，门窗钉得死死的，可它从前是整个卡里克（Carrick）最热闹的一座宅子啊。”

===Original===
Mrs. Morran’s tone grew tragic. “It’s a queer warld wi’out the auld gentry. My faither and my guidsire and his faither afore him served the Kennedys, and my man Dauvit Morran was gemkeeper to them, and afore I mairried I was ane o’ the table-maids. They were kind folk, the Kennedys, and, like a’ the rale gentry, maist mindfu’ o’ them that served them. Sic’ merry nichts I’ve seen in the auld Hoose, at Halloween and Hogmanay, and at the servants’ balls and the waddin’s o’ the young leddies! But the laird bode to waste his siller in stane and lime, and hadna that much to leave to his bairns. And now they’ve a’ scattered or deid.”

===Chinese===
莫兰太太的语气悲了起来。“没了老贵族，这世道就邪性喽。俺爹、俺祖父、俺祖父他爹，都给肯尼迪家（the Kennedys）当差，俺男人道维特·莫兰（Dauvit Morran）给他们看管猎场，俺没过门以前，还是府上的桌面侍女呢。肯尼迪家是厚道人家，同一切真贵族一样，最惦记底下当差的人。俺在老府里见过多少快活夜晚啊，万圣节前夜（Halloween）、苏格兰除夕，还有仆人的舞会、年轻小姐们的婚礼！可老爷偏要把银钱糟蹋在砖石石灰上，到头来没剩多少留给儿女。如今他们散的散，死的死，一个不剩喽。”

===Original===
Her grave face wore the tenderness which comes from affectionate reminiscence.

===Chinese===
她庄重的脸上浮起一层温柔，那温柔来自深情的追忆。

===Original===
“There was never sic a laddie as young Maister Quentin. No’ a week gaed by but he was in here, cryin’, ‘Phemie Morran, I’ve come till my tea!’ Fine he likit my treacle scones, puir man. There wasna ane in the countryside sae bauld a rider at the hunt, or sic a skeely fisher. And he was clever at his books tae, a graund scholar, they said, and ettlin’ at bein’ what they ca’ a dipplemat. But that’s a’ bye wi’.”

===Chinese===
“从来没那么好的一个后生，昆廷少爷。隔不上一个礼拜，他准要跑进俺这屋来，喊一声：‘菲米·莫兰（Phemie Morran），俺来吃茶点啦！’俺的糖蜜烤饼他顶喜欢，可怜的人。打起猎来，乡里没人骑马比他更猛；钓鱼也是一把好手。他读书也灵，人家都说是个了不得的读书种子，一心要当人家说的什么‘外交官’。可这一切都完喽。”

===Original===
“Quentin Kennedy﻿—the fellow in the Tins?” Heritage asked. “I saw him in Rome when he was with the Mission.”

===Chinese===
“昆廷·肯尼迪（Quentin Kennedy）——就是‘铁罐头’（the Tins）里当差的那位？”赫里蒂奇问道，“他随使团在罗马那阵儿，我见过他。”

===Original===
“I dinna ken. He was a brave sodger, but he wasna long fechtin’ in France till he got a bullet in his breist. Syne we heard tell o’ him in far awa’ bits like Russia; and syne cam’ the end o’ the war and we lookit to see him back, fishin’ the waters and ridin’ like Jehu as in the auld days. But wae’s me! It wasna permitted. The next news we got, the puir laddie was deid o’ influenzy and buried somewhere about France. The wanchancy bullet maun have weakened his chest, nae doot. So that’s the end o’ the guid stock o’ Kennedy o’ Huntingtower, whae hae been great folk sin’ the time o’ Robert Bruce. And noo the Hoose is shut up till the lawyers can get somebody sae far left to himsel’ as to tak’ it on lease, and in thae dear days it’s no’ just onybody that wants a muckle castle.”

===Chinese===
“俺不知道。他是个勇敢的兵，可在法兰西没打多久，胸口就中了一颗子弹。后来听说他到了俄罗斯那样远得没影儿的地方；再后来仗打完了，俺们巴望着他回来，照老样子下河钓鱼，像耶户（Jehu）那样打马飞奔。可是天哪！天不许可。再来的消息，说那可怜的后生害流感死喽，埋在法兰西什么地方。不用问，准是那颗晦气的子弹伤了他的肺。亨廷塔肯尼迪家的好血脉，就此了结啦——打罗伯特·布鲁斯（Robert Bruce）那年代起，他们可一直是体面人。如今那府邸就那么锁着，等律师们寻着个闲得发慌、肯把租约接下来的人，这年头日子紧，想要一座大城堡的人可不多喽。”

===Original===
“Who are the lawyers?” Dickson asked.

===Chinese===
“律师是哪几位？”迪克森问。

===Original===
“Glendonan and Speirs in Embro. But they never look near the place, and Maister Loudoun in Auchenlochan does the factorin’. He’s let the public an’ filled the twae lodges, and he’ll be thinkin’ nae doot that he’s done eneuch.”

===Chinese===
“爱丁堡（Embro）的格伦多南和斯皮尔斯（Glendonan and Speirs）。可他们从不朝这地方看一眼，奥琴洛坎的劳登先生（Maister Loudoun）代管着产业。他把酒馆租了出去，把两座门房也塞进了人，这会儿只怕正想着，他干的已经够多喽。”

===Original===
Mrs. Morran had poured some hot water into the big slop-bowl, and had begun the operation known as “synding out” the cups. It was a hint that the meal was over and Dickson and Heritage rose from the table. Followed by an injunction to be back for supper “on the chap o’ nine,” they strolled out into the evening. Two hours of some sort of daylight remained, and the travellers had that impulse to activity which comes to all men who, after a day of exercise and emptiness, are stayed with a satisfying tea.

===Chinese===
莫兰太太往大涤具碗里倒了些热水，动手进行那项名为“涮盏”的操作。这是饭局已终的暗示，迪克森和赫里蒂奇便离了桌。临走她嘱咐一句，晚饭要“赶九点钟敲响时”回来。外头还剩下约莫两个钟头的天光，两位旅人身上涌起一股要活动的冲动——凡是走了一天路、饿了一天肚子、又被一顿惬意茶点填饱了的人，都会有这股冲动的。

===Original===
“You should be happy, Dogson,” said the Poet. “Here we have all the materials for your blessed romance﻿—old mansion, extinct family, village deserted of men and an innkeeper whom I suspect of being a villain. I feel almost a convert to your nonsense myself. We’ll have a look at the House.”

===Chinese===
“你该高兴了，狗逊，”诗人说，“你那宝贝浪漫的一切材料这儿都齐了——古老公馆，绝嗣的家族，没了男人的村子，还有一个我疑心是恶棍的客栈老板。我自己几乎都要皈依你那套胡说了。咱们去瞧瞧那座宅子。”

===Original===
They turned down the road which ran north by the park wall, past the inn which looked more abandoned than ever, till they came to an entrance which was clearly the West Lodge. It had once been a pretty, modish cottage, with a thatched roof and dormer windows, but now it was badly in need of repair. A windowpane was broken and stuffed with a sack, the posts of the porch were giving inwards, and the thatch was crumbling under the attentions of a colony of starlings. The great iron gates were rusty, and on the coat of arms above them the gilding was patchy and tarnished.

===Chinese===
他们拐上沿园圃高墙向北去的路，经过那座比先前更显破落的客栈，来到一处显然是西门房（West Lodge）的入口。它原是一座漂亮的时兴村舍，茅草顶带老虎窗，如今却亟需修缮。一块窗玻璃破了，塞着麻袋；门廊的柱子往里倾颓；一群椋鸟在茅草上忙活，把草顶啄得簌簌剥落。大铁门锈迹斑斑，门上的纹章，鎏金斑驳晦暗。

===Original===
Apparently the gates were locked, and even the side wicket failed to open to Heritage’s vigorous shaking. Inside a weedy drive disappeared among ragged rhododendrons.

===Chinese===
大门显然锁着，连旁门任凭赫里蒂奇使劲摇撼，也纹丝不动。门内一条荒草车道，消失在参差披离的杜鹃花丛中。

===Original===
The noise brought a man to the lodge door. He was a sturdy fellow in a suit of black clothes which had not been made for him. He might have been a butler *en deshabille*, but for the presence of a pair of field boots into which he had tucked the ends of his trousers. The curious thing about him was his face, which was decorated with features so tiny as to give the impression of a monstrous child. Each in itself was well enough formed, but eyes, nose, mouth, chin were of a smallness curiously out of proportion to the head and body. Such an anomaly might have been redeemed by the expression; good-humour would have invested it with an air of agreeable farce. But there was no friendliness in the man’s face. It was set like a judge’s in a stony impassiveness.

===Chinese===
响声引出一个男人，立在门房门口。这是个结实的汉子，穿一身黑衣裳，那衣裳显然不是照他的身量裁的。若不是脚上一双野战靴、裤脚统统塞进靴筒里，他原可以算是一位衣冠不整的管家（*en deshabille*）。他身上古怪的是那张脸：五官生得极小，小得给人一种巨型婴孩的印象。单看每一件都还算周正，可眼睛、鼻子、嘴巴、下巴，都小得同脑袋和身子奇异地不成比例。这样的反常本可靠神情来补救；一副好脾气本可给它添上一层讨喜的滑稽气。可这人脸上没有半点友善。它板着，像法官的脸，铁石般地不动声色。

===Original===
“May we walk up to the House?” Heritage asked. “We are here for a night and should like to have a look at it.”

===Chinese===
“我们可以走到宅子跟前去吗？”赫里蒂奇问，“我们在此地只宿一夜，想看上一眼。”

===Original===
The man advanced a step. He had either a bad cold, or a voice comparable in size to his features.

===Chinese===
那人上前一步。他不是重感冒缠身，就是那条嗓子也长得同他的五官一般小。

===Original===
“There’s no entrance here,” he said huskily. “I have strict orders.”

===Chinese===
“此地不得进入。”他嗓音沙哑地说，“我有严令在身。”

===Original===
“Oh, come now,” said Heritage. “It can do nobody any harm if you let us in for half an hour.”

===Chinese===
“嗳，何必呢，”赫里蒂奇说，“放我们进去半个钟头，碍不着任何人。”

===Original===
The man advanced another step.

===Chinese===
那人又上前一步。

===Original===
“You shall not come in. Go away from here. Go away, I tell you. It is private.” The words spoken by the small mouth in the small voice had a kind of childish ferocity.

===Chinese===
“你们不许进来。从这里走开。走开，听见没有。此地是私产。”小嘴里吐出的小声音说的这些话，带着一种孩子式的凶狠。

===Original===
The travellers turned their back on him and continued their way.

===Chinese===
两位旅人掉头不理他，继续赶路。

===Original===
“Sich a curmudgeon!” Dickson commented. His face had flushed, for he was susceptible to rudeness. “Did you notice? That man’s a foreigner.”

===Chinese===
“好一个小气鬼！”迪克森评道。他脸都红了，因为他最吃不得无礼。“你留意到没有？那家伙是个外国人。”

===Original===
“He’s a brute,” said Heritage. “But I’m not going to be done in by that class of lad. There can be no gates on the sea side, so we’ll work round that way, for I won’t sleep till I’ve seen the place.”

===Chinese===
“他是个畜生，”赫里蒂奇说，“可我不会叫这一路货色给难住。临海那面总不会也有门，咱们就从那边绕过去，不见识见识那地方，我是睡不着的。”

===Original===
Presently the trees grew thinner, and the road plunged through thickets of hazel till it came to a sudden stop in a field. There the cover ceased wholly, and below them lay the glen of the Laver. Steep green banks descended to a stream which swept in coils of gold into the eye of the sunset. A little further down the channel broadened, the slopes fell back a little, and a tongue of glittering sea ran up to meet the hill waters. The Laver is a gentle stream after it leaves its cradle heights, a stream of clear pools and long bright shallows, winding by moorland steadings and upland meadows; but in its last half-mile it goes mad, and imitates its childhood when it tumbled over granite shelves. Down in that green place the crystal water gushed and frolicked as if determined on one hour of rapturous life before joining the sedater sea.

===Chinese===
不多时树木稀疏下来，道路扎进榛树丛，到一块田边戛然而止。掩蔽在此全然断了，脚下便是拉弗河的峡谷。陡峭的绿坡直落到一道溪流，溪水拖着金色的辫子，一路盘旋，扑进落日的眼波里。再往下游去些，河道展宽，坡岸略略后退，一线波光粼粼的海水探上来，迎接山间的来水。拉弗河离开摇篮般的高地后原是一条温顺的河，一泓泓清水潭、一道道长长的明亮浅滩，绕着荒原田庄和高地草场蜿蜒而行；可在最后半英里，它发了狂，重演起幼年翻滚花岗岩石棚的把戏。在那片绿色天地里，水晶般的水花迸溅欢腾，仿佛打定主意，要在汇入沉稳的大海之前，痛痛快快活上一个时辰。

===Original===
Heritage flung himself on the turf.

===Chinese===
赫里蒂奇一头扑倒在草皮上。

===Original===
“This is a good place! Ye gods, what a good place! Dogson, aren’t you glad you came? I think everything’s bewitched tonight. That village is bewitched, and that old woman’s tea. Good white magic! And that foul innkeeper and that brigand at the gate. Black magic! And now here is the home of all enchantment﻿—‘island valley of Avilion’﻿—‘waters that listen for lovers’﻿—all the rest of it!”

===Chinese===
“这地方真好！老天爷，多好的地方！狗逊，来了这儿你不高兴吗？我觉得今晚样样东西都中了魔法。那村子中了魔法，那老婆婆的茶也是。善良的白魔法！还有那个卑鄙的客栈老板和门口那个强盗。邪恶的黑魔法！而现在，这儿是一切魔法的老家——‘阿维隆（Avilion）的海岛幽谷’——‘倾听恋人们心事的水波’——诸如此类，还有一大套哩！”

===Original===
Dickson observed and marvelled.

===Chinese===
迪克森瞧着，啧啧称奇。

===Original===
“I can’t make you out, Mr. Heritage. You were saying last night you were a great democrat, and yet you were objecting to yon laddies camping on the moor. And you very near bit the neb off me when I said I liked Tennyson. And now.﻿ ﻿…” Mr. McCunn’s command of language was inadequate to describe the transformation.

===Chinese===
“俺真摸不透你，赫里蒂奇先生。昨晚你还说你是个大大的民主派，可一转眼又看不惯那帮小伙子在荒原上露营。俺说俺喜欢丁尼生，你差点没把俺的鼻子咬下来。可是现在。……”麦坎先生的语言功力，不足以描摹这番变化。

===Original===
“You’re a precise, pragmatical Scot,” was the answer. “Hang it, man, don’t remind me that I’m inconsistent. I’ve a poet’s licence to play the fool, and if you don’t understand me, I don’t in the least understand myself. All I know is that I’m feeling young and jolly and that it’s the Spring.”

===Chinese===
“你是个一丝不苟、讲求实际的苏格兰人，”对方答道，“见鬼，老兄，别提醒我自相矛盾。我有诗人的特许状，可以装疯卖傻，你若是不懂我，我自己也一点都不懂自己。我只知道，我觉得年轻，觉得快活，而且现在是春天。”

===Original===
Mr. Heritage was assuredly in a strange mood. He began to whistle with a faraway look in his eye.

===Chinese===
赫里蒂奇先生确乎处在一种异样的心绪里。他吹起口哨来，眼里带着一种悠远的神色。

===Original===
“Do you know what that is?” he asked suddenly.

===Chinese===
“你知道这是什么曲子吗？”他突然问。

===Original===
Dickson, who could not detect any tune, said No.

===Chinese===
迪克森听不出任何调子，答了个“不”。

===Original===
“It’s an aria from a Russian opera that came out just before the war. I’ve forgotten the name of the fellow who wrote it. Jolly thing, isn’t it? I always remind myself of it when I’m in this mood, for it is linked with the greatest experience of my life. You said, I think, that you had never been in love?”

===Chinese===
“是一支俄罗斯歌剧里的咏叹调，大战前夕刚上演的那部。写它的人叫什么，我忘了。怪欢快的，是不是？每逢陷入这种心绪，我总会想起它，因为它同我平生最重大的经历连在一起。我记得你说过，你从来没有恋爱过？”

===Original===
Dickson replied in the native fashion. “Have you?” he asked.

===Chinese===
迪克森以本土的方式作答。“那你呢？”他问。

===Original===
“I have, and I am﻿—been for two years. I was down with my battalion on the Italian front early in 1918, and because I could speak the language they hoicked me out and sent me to Rome on a liaison job. It was Easter time and fine weather and, being glad to get out of the trenches, I was pretty well pleased with myself and enjoying life.﻿ ﻿… In the place where I stayed there was a girl. She was a Russian, a princess of a great family, but a refugee and of course as poor as sin.﻿ ﻿… I remember how badly dressed she was among all the well-to-do Romans. But, my God, what a beauty! There was never anything in the world like her.﻿ ﻿… She was little more than a child, and she used to sing that air in the morning as she went down the stairs.﻿ ﻿… They sent me back to the front before I had a chance of getting to know her, but she used to give me little timid good mornings, and her voice and eyes were like an angel’s.﻿ ﻿… I’m over my head in love, but it’s hopeless, quite hopeless. I shall never see her again.”

===Chinese===
“我爱过，而且还在爱着——爱了两年了。1918 年年初，我随我们营在意大利前线，因为会说那边的话，他们把我拽了出来，派去罗马干联络的差事。正是复活节前后，天气又好，出了战壕，我正对自己颇为满意，日子过得有滋有味。……我住的地方有个姑娘。她是俄罗斯人，名门望族的公主，却是个流亡者，自然穷得一贫如洗。……我记得她站在一群殷实的罗马人中间，衣衫多么寒素。可是天哪，多么美！世上从来不曾有过她这样的人。……她比个孩子大不了多少，每天早晨下楼时，总哼着那个调子。……还没等我来得及同她熟识，他们又把我送回了前线；可她总要怯生生地对我道一声早安，那嗓音、那双眼睛，像天使一样。……我正爱得神魂颠倒，可是无望，全然无望。我再也见不到她了。”

===Original===
“I’m sure I’m honoured by your confidence,” said Dickson reverently.

===Chinese===
“承您见信，俺实在不胜荣幸。”迪克森恭恭敬敬地说。

===Original===
The Poet, who seemed to draw exhilaration from the memory of his sorrows, arose and fetched him a clout on the back. “Don’t talk of confidence as if you were a reporter,” he said. “What about that House? If we’re to see it before the dark comes we’d better hustle.”

===Chinese===
诗人似乎能从对自身伤心的回忆里汲取快意，站起身来，在他背上啪地拍了一巴掌。“别一口一个见信，倒像你是个记者似的，”他说，“那宅子怎么办？要是想赶在天黑前看上一眼，咱们最好快着点。”

===Original===
The green slopes on their left, as they ran seaward, were clothed towards their summit with a tangle of broom and light scrub. The two forced their way through this, and found to their surprise that on this side there were no defences of the Huntingtower demesne. Along the crest ran a path which had once been gravelled and trimmed. Beyond through a thicket of laurels and rhododendrons they came on a long unkempt aisle of grass, which seemed to be one of those side avenues often found in connection with old Scots dwellings. Keeping along this they reached a grove of beech and holly through which showed a dim shape of masonry. By a common impulse they moved stealthily, crouching in cover, till at the far side of the wood they found a sunk fence and looked over an acre or two of what had once been lawn and flowerbeds to the front of the mansion.

===Chinese===
他们左手的绿坡一路伸向海去，坡顶一带覆满了纠缠的金雀花和稀疏的灌木。两人从中间硬闯过去，惊奇地发现，亨廷塔园圃的这一面竟没有任何防护。沿着坡顶有一条小径，从前铺过石屑、修整过的。再往前，穿过一片月桂和杜鹃的密林，他们遇上一条荒芜的长草巷，像是老式苏格兰宅邸常有的那种侧甬道。顺着它走到头，是一片山毛榉和冬青的树丛，树影间透出一段影影绰绰的砖石。两人不约而同地放轻脚步，猫着腰借掩体潜行，直到树林尽头，撞见一道隐垣，从垣上望过去，一两英亩旧日的草坪和花圃，一直铺到宅邸的正面。

===Original===
The outline of the building was clearly silhouetted against the glowing west, but since they were looking at the east face the detail was all in shadow. But, dim as it was, the sight was enough to give Dickson the surprise of his life. He had expected something old and baronial. But this was new, raw and new, not twenty years built. Some madness had prompted its creator to set up a replica of a Tudor house in a countryside where the thing was unheard of. All the tricks were there﻿—oriel windows, lozenged panes, high twisted chimney stacks; the very stone was red, as if to imitate the mellow brick of some ancient Kentish manor. It was new, but it was also decaying. The creepers had fallen from the walls, the pilasters on the terrace were tumbling down, lichen and moss were on the doorsteps. Shuttered, silent, abandoned, it stood like a harsh memento mori of human hopes.

===Chinese===
建筑的轮廓清晰地剪影在烧红的西天上，可他们看到的是东面，细部全都埋在暗影里。然而纵然昏暗，这一眼也足以给迪克森平生最大的意外。他原以为会见到一座古老而有豪门气派的宅子。可这是新的，簇新扎眼的新，建起不过二十年。它的建造者准是犯了什么疯病，才会在一个闻所未闻这种玩意儿的乡间，竖起一座都铎（Tudor）式宅邸的翻版。花样一样不缺——凸肚窗、菱格窗格、高耸的扭纹烟囱；连石头都是红的，仿佛要模仿肯特郡某座古老庄园的温润砖色。它是新的，却也在衰朽。爬墙虎从墙上剥落，露台上的壁柱摇摇欲坠，门阶上生了苔藓地衣。窗板紧闭，无声无息，人迹断绝，它立在那里，像人类希望的一座刺目的死亡警示（memento mori）。

===Original===
Dickson had never before been affected by an inanimate thing with so strong a sense of disquiet. He had pictured an old stone tower on a bright headland; he found instead this raw thing among trees. The decadence of the brand-new repels as something against nature, and this new thing was decadent. But there was a mysterious life in it, for though not a chimney smoked, it seemed to enshrine a personality and to wear a sinister aura. He felt a lively distaste, which was almost fear. He wanted to get far away from it as fast as possible. The sun, now sinking very low, sent up rays which kindled the crests of a group of firs to the left of the front door. He had the absurd fancy that they were torches flaming before a bier.

===Chinese===
迪克森从来没有被一件无生命的东西激起过如此强烈的不安。他想象中的，是明亮海岬上一座古老的石塔；他却找到了树林间这个毛糙扎眼的新物件。崭新之物的颓败最教人反感，因为那是逆乎自然，而这新东西正在颓败。可它体内又有一种神秘的生命，因为虽然连一座烟囱都不冒烟，它却仿佛供奉着一个什么人格，罩着一圈不祥的气息。他感到一阵强烈的憎厌，几乎近于恐惧。他想远远地离开它，越快越好。此刻太阳已沉得极低，射出的光线点燃了正门左手一小丛杉树的树梢。他忽然生出一个荒唐的幻觉：它们是灵柩前焚烧的火炬。

===Original===
It was well that the two had moved quietly and kept in shadow. Footsteps fell on their ears, on the path which threaded the lawn just beyond the sunk-fence. It was the keeper of the West Lodge and he carried something on his back, but both that and his face were indistinct in the half-light.

===Chinese===
幸亏两人走得轻，又一直藏在暗处。脚步声传进他们耳朵，就在隐垣外穿草坪而过的那条小径上。来的是西门房的看守人，背上驮着件什么东西，可那东西连同他的脸，在暮色里都分辨不清。

===Original===
Other footsteps were heard, coming from the other side of the lawn. A man’s shod feet rang on the stone of a flagged path, and from their irregular fall it was plain that he was lame. The two men met near the door, and spoke together. Then they separated, and moved one down each side of the house. To the two watchers they had the air of a patrol, or of warders pacing the corridors of a prison.

===Chinese===
又响起另一阵脚步声，从草坪另一头来。一个男人穿着硬底鞋，踏在石板道的石头上铿铿作响，从那错落的步点听来，他分明是个瘸子。两人在宅门附近碰了头，低声交谈了几句。随后分开，一人沿宅子的一面各走各的。在两位旁观者眼里，他们像一支巡逻队，又像在一座监狱走廊里踱步的狱卒。

===Original===
“Let’s get out of this,” said Dickson, and turned to go.

===Chinese===
“咱们离开这儿吧。”迪克森说着，转身要走。

===Original===
The air had the curious stillness which precedes the moment of sunset, when the birds of day have stopped their noises and the sounds of night have not begun. But suddenly in the silence fell notes of music. They seemed to come from the house, a voice singing softly but with great beauty and clearness.

===Chinese===
空气里有一种奇异的静谧，那是日落前一刻才有的静谧，白昼的鸟雀已经噤声，夜的声响尚未登场。可就在这片寂静里，忽然落下来几个音符。它们似乎出自宅子里，一个嗓音在轻轻歌唱，却唱得极美、极清亮。

===Original===
Dickson halted in his steps. The tune, whatever it was, was like a fresh wind to blow aside his depression. The house no longer looked sepulchral. He saw that the two men had hurried back from their patrol, had met and exchanged some message, and made off again as if alarmed by the music. Then he noticed his companion.﻿ ﻿…

===Chinese===
迪克森收住了脚步。那曲子，不管它是什么，像一阵新风，把他心头的阴郁吹到了一边。宅子看上去不再像座坟。他看见那两个人从巡逻中匆匆赶回，碰头交换了个口信，又匆匆走开，仿佛被那歌声惊动了。随后他注意到了他的同伴。……

===Original===
Heritage was on one knee with his face rapt and listening. He got to his feet and appeared to be about to make for the House. Dickson caught him by the arm and dragged him into the bushes, and he followed unresistingly, like a man in a dream. They ploughed through the thicket, recrossed the grass avenue, and scrambled down the hillside to the banks of the stream.

===Chinese===
赫里蒂奇单膝跪在地上，脸上是一片如醉如痴的倾听。他站起身，看样子正要朝宅子扑去。迪克森一把抓住他的胳膊，把他拖进灌木丛，他毫不抗拒地跟着走，像梦游的人一般。两人蹚过树丛，重新越过那条草巷，连滚带爬地下到山坡下溪水的岸边。

===Original===
Then for the first time Dickson observed that his companion’s face was very white, and that sweat stood on his temples. Heritage lay down and lapped up water like a dog. Then he turned a wild eye on the other.

===Chinese===
直到这时，迪克森才注意到同伴的脸白得吓人，鬓角上沁着汗珠。赫里蒂奇趴下去，像狗一样舔了几口水。然后他把一双狂乱的眼睛转向对方。

===Original===
“I am going back,” he said. “That is the voice of the girl I saw in Rome, and it is singing her song!”

===Chinese===
“我要回去，”他说，“那正是我在罗马见过的那位姑娘的声音，唱的正是她的歌！”

## IV. Dougal / 四、杜加尔

===Original===
“You’ll do nothing of the kind,” said Dickson. “You’re coming home to your supper. It was to be on the chap of nine.”

===Chinese===
“这种事你想都别想，”迪克森说，“你这就跟俺回家吃晚饭。说好了的，赶九点钟敲响时开饭。”

===Original===
“I’m going back to that place.”

===Chinese===
“我要回那地方去。”

===Original===
The man was clearly demented and must be humoured. “Well, you must wait till the morn’s morning. It’s very near dark now, and those are two ugly customers wandering about yonder. You’d better sleep the night on it.”

===Chinese===
这人分明是疯了，只能顺着他来。“唉，那你总得等到明儿早上。眼下天就快黑透了，那边还有两个凶恶的家伙在转悠呢。这事儿你最好搁上一夜再说。”

===Original===
Mr. Heritage seemed to be persuaded. He suffered himself to be led up the now dusky slopes to the gate where the road from the village ended. He walked listlessly like a man engaged in painful reflection. Once only he broke the silence.

===Chinese===
赫里蒂奇先生似乎被劝动了。他听任自己被领上此刻已暮色四合的山坡，一直走到村路尽头的那扇大门跟前。他走得无精打采，像个陷在痛苦思索里的人。只有一回，他打破了沉默。

===Original===
“You heard the singing?” he asked.

===Chinese===
“你听见那歌声了？”他问。

===Original===
Dickson was a very poor hand at a lie. “I heard something,” he admitted.

===Chinese===
迪克森极不擅长撒谎。“俺听见了点什么。”他承认道。

===Original===
“You heard a girl’s voice singing?”

===Chinese===
“你听见一个姑娘的嗓音在唱歌？”

===Original===
“It sounded like that,” was the admission. “But I’m thinking it might have been a seagull.”

===Chinese===
“听着是像那么回事，”他招认道，“不过俺在想，那兴许是只海鸥。”

===Original===
“You’re a fool,” said the Poet rudely.

===Chinese===
“你是个傻瓜。”诗人不客气地说。

===Original===
The return was a melancholy business, compared to the bright speed of the outward journey. Dickson’s mind was a chaos of feelings, all of them unpleasant. He had run up against something which he violently, blindly detested, and the trouble was that he could not tell why. It was all perfectly absurd, for why on earth should an ugly house, some overgrown trees and a couple of ill-favoured servants so malignly affect him? Yet this was the fact; he had strayed out of Arcady into a sphere that filled him with revolt and a nameless fear. Never in his experience had he felt like this, this foolish childish panic which took all the colour and zest out of life. He tried to laugh at himself but failed. Heritage, stumbling alone by his side, effectually crushed his effort to discover humour in the situation. Some exhalation from that infernal place had driven the Poet mad. And then that voice singing! A seagull, he had said. More like a nightingale, he reflected﻿—a bird which in the flesh he had never met.

===Chinese===
比起来时一路的轻快脚程，归途实在是一桩愁人的营生。迪克森的心绪乱作一团，且团团都是不快。他撞上了一样叫他剧烈地、盲目地憎恶的东西，麻烦在于，他说不出个所以然。这一切全然荒唐，因为一座丑陋的宅子、几丛疯长的树木、两个面目不善的仆役，凭什么就能这样恶毒地作践他？可事实偏偏如此；他已从阿卡迪亚（Arcady）误入了一个令他满心抗拒、又满怀无名恐惧的境地。以他的阅历，他从来不曾有过这样的感受——这种愚蠢的、孩子气的恐慌，把生活的全部色彩和滋味都夺了个干净。他试图笑话自己，却没能笑出来。赫里蒂奇独自在他身边跌跌撞撞地走着，把他想从这处境里觅出一点幽默来的努力，碾了个粉碎。那座地狱般的地方蒸腾出的一股瘴气，把诗人熏疯了。还有那歌声！他方才说是海鸥。倒更像夜莺，他想——那种鸟，他从来不曾见过活生生的一只。

===Original===
Mrs. Morran had the lamp lit and a fire burning in her cheerful kitchen. The sight of it somewhat restored Dickson’s equanimity, and to his surprise he found that he had an appetite for supper. There was new milk, thick with cream, and most of the dainties which had appeared at tea, supplemented by a noble dish of shimmering “potted-head.” The hostess did not share their meal, being engaged in some duties in the little cubbyhole known as the back kitchen.

===Chinese===
莫兰太太那间亮堂暖人的厨房里，灯已点起，炉火正旺。这景象多少恢复了迪克森的心平气和，而且他惊觉自己竟然有了吃晚饭的胃口。有新挤的牛奶，稠得凝着一层厚奶油；茶点上出现过的那些美味，大半又摆了上来，另添一大盘亮晶晶、颤巍巍的“肉冻”（potted-head）。女主人没有同他们一起用饭，她在那个被唤作“后厨房”的小格子间里忙些什么活计。

===Original===
Heritage drank a glass of milk but would not touch food.

===Chinese===
赫里蒂奇喝了一杯牛奶，饭食却一口不沾。

===Original===
“I called this place Paradise four hours ago,” he said. “So it is, but I fancy it is next door to Hell. There is something devilish going on inside that park wall and I mean to get to the bottom of it.”

===Chinese===
“四个钟头以前，我把这地方唤作乐园，”他说，“它也确实是乐园，可我瞧它就住在地狱隔壁。那道园墙里头正在进行着什么魔鬼的勾当，我打算把它的底细查个水落石出。”

===Original===
“Hoots! Nonsense!” Dickson replied with affected cheerfulness. “Tomorrow you and me will take the road for Auchenlochan. We needn’t trouble ourselves about an ugly old house and a wheen impident lodgekeepers.”

===Chinese===
“嗬！瞎说！”迪克森装出欢快的样子答道，“明儿俺跟你走大路去奥琴洛坎。一座丑八怪老宅子，外带一帮没规矩的门房，犯不着俺们操心。”

===Original===
“Tomorrow I’m going to get inside the place. Don’t come unless you like, but it’s no use arguing with me. My mind is made up.”

===Chinese===
“明天我要进到那地方里头去。你乐意就来，不乐意拉倒，跟我争没有用。我主意已定。”

===Original===
Heritage cleared a space on the table and spread out a section of a large-scale Ordnance map.

===Chinese===
赫里蒂奇在桌上清出一块地方，摊开一幅大比例尺陆军测量图（Ordnance map）的一角。

===Original===
“I must clear my head about the topography, the same as if this were a battleground. Look here, Dogson.﻿ ﻿… The road past the inn that we went by tonight runs north and south.” He tore a page from a notebook and proceeded to make a rough sketch.1﻿ ﻿… “One end we know abuts on the Laver glen, and the other stops at the South Lodge. Inside the wall which follows the road is a long belt of plantation﻿—mostly beeches and ash﻿—then to the west a kind of park, and beyond that the lawns of the house. Strips of plantation with avenues between follow the north and south sides of the park. On the sea side of the House are the stables and what looks like a walled garden, and beyond them what seems to be open ground with an old dovecot marked and the ruins of Huntingtower keep. Beyond that there is more open ground, till you come to the cliffs of the cape. Have you got that?﻿ ﻿… It looks possible from the contouring to get on to the sea cliffs by following the Laver, for all that side is broken up into ravines.﻿ ﻿… But look at the other side﻿—the Garple glen. It’s evidently a deep-cut gully, and at the bottom it opens out into a little harbour. There’s deep water there, you observe. Now the House on the south side﻿—the Garple side﻿—is built fairly close to the edge of the cliffs. Is that all clear in your head? We can’t reconnoitre unless we’ve got a working notion of the lie of the land.”

===Chinese===
“我得把地形在脑子里理清楚，就当这是一处战场。你瞧，狗逊。……今晚我们走过的、经过客栈的那条路，是南北向的。”他从笔记本上撕下一页，动手画一幅简图。1……“一头，我们知道，接在拉弗河峡谷上；另一头到南门房为止。沿路那道围墙里面，是一条长长的林带——多半是山毛榉和白蜡树——再往西是一带园圃，园圃过去便是宅前的草坪。一条条林带夹着当中的林荫道，顺着园圃的南北两侧延伸。宅子临海的一面是马厩，还有一座像是围墙花园的地方；再往外是一片开阔地，图上标着一座老鸽舍，还有亨廷塔古堡的残迹。再往外仍是开阔地，直到海角的峭壁为止。这些都装进你脑子里了吗？……从等高线看，顺着拉弗河一路走下去，应该能上到海边峭壁，因为那整个一面都被切割成一道道沟壑。……可再看另一面——加普尔河峡谷。那分明是一道深切下去的沟壑，到底部敞开成一个小小的港湾。你留意到，那儿是深水。再看宅子的南面——加普尔河那一面——造得离崖沿相当近。你脑子里都清楚了吗？不先有一个能用的地形概念，咱们就没法侦察。”

===Original===
Dickson was about to protest that he had no intention of reconnoitring, when a hubbub arose in the back kitchen. Mrs. Morran’s voice was heard in shrill protest.

===Chinese===
迪克森正要抗议，声明自己全无侦察的意思，后厨房里忽然闹腾起来。只听得莫兰太太尖声抗议。

===Original===
“Ye ill laddie! Eh﻿—ye﻿—ill﻿—laddie! [*crescendo*] Makin’ a hash o’ my back door wi’ your dirty feet! What are ye slinkin’ roond here for, when I tell’t ye this mornin’ that I wad sell ye nae mair scones till ye paid for the last lot? Ye’re a wheen thievin’ hungry callants, and if there were a polisman in the place I’d gie ye in chairge.﻿ ﻿… What’s that ye say? Ye’re no’ wantin’ meat? Ye want to speak to the gentlemen that’s bidin’ here? Ye ken the auld ane, says you? I believe it’s a muckle lee, but there’s the gentlemen to answer ye theirsels.”

===Chinese===
“你这坏小子！哎——你——坏——小子！（声调越拔越高）拿你那双脏蹄子，把俺的后门糟蹋得不成样子！今儿早上俺不是告诉过你，不把上回那批烤饼的钱结清，俺就一块也不再卖给你们？你们就是一帮偷嘴的饿死鬼小子，这地方要是有个警察，俺非把你们扭送上去不可。……你说什么？你不是来讨吃的？你要同住在这儿的两位先生说话？你说你认得那位老先生？俺看八成是天大的谎——不过先生们就在那儿，让他们自个儿答你去。”

===Original===
Mrs. Morran, brandishing a dishclout dramatically, flung open the door, and with a vigorous push propelled into the kitchen a singular figure.

===Chinese===
莫兰太太戏剧性地挥舞着一块抹布，猛地推开门，奋力一搡，把一个奇形怪状的人影搡进了厨房。

===Original===
It was a stunted boy, who from his face might have been fifteen years old, but had the stature of a child of twelve. He had a thatch of fiery red hair above a pale freckled countenance. His nose was snub, his eyes a sulky grey-green, and his wide mouth disclosed large and damaged teeth. But remarkable as was his visage, his clothing was still stranger. On his head was the regulation Boy Scout hat, but it was several sizes too big, and was squashed down upon his immense red ears. He wore a very ancient khaki shirt, which had once belonged to a full-grown soldier, and the spacious sleeves were rolled up at the shoulders and tied with string, revealing a pair of skinny arms. Round his middle hung what was meant to be a kilt﻿—a kilt of home manufacture, which may once have been a tablecloth, for its bold pattern suggested no known clan tartan. He had a massive belt, in which was stuck a broken gully-knife, and round his neck was knotted the remnant of what had once been a silk bandana. His legs and feet were bare, blue, scratched, and very dirty, and his toes had the prehensile look common to monkeys and small boys who summer and winter go bootless. In his hand was a long ash-pole, new cut from some coppice.

===Chinese===
那是个发育不良的男孩，看脸也许有十五岁，身量却只有十二岁孩子的个头。苍白的、生着雀斑的脸膛上，覆着一蓬火红的硬发。他鼻子上翘，眼睛是气鼓鼓的灰绿色，一张阔嘴咧开来，露出一口又大又残破的牙。可他的脸尽管出奇，他的衣着却比脸更出奇。头上是一顶制式童子军帽，却大出了好几号，直压在他那对硕大的红耳朵上。他穿一件旧得不能再旧的卡其衬衫，原先是哪个成年士兵的，宽宽大大的袖子在肩头卷起，拿绳子扎住，露出一对细胳膊。腰上围着一圈勉强算是苏格兰短裙的东西——自家手制的货色，从前兴许是块桌布，因为它那醒目的花纹，对不上任何已知的氏族格纹。他系一条粗大的皮带，皮带上别着一把豁了刃的大猎刀（gully-knife），脖子上打结系着一方丝巾的残余——那丝巾从前想必很讲究。他两腿两脚赤裸着，冻得发青，划满伤痕，脏得厉害，脚趾头带着猴子同一年到头光脚的男孩共有的、能抓能握的模样。手里握着一根长长的白蜡木杆，新从哪片矮林里砍下来的。

===Original===
The apparition stood glum and lowering on the kitchen floor. As Dickson stared at it he recalled Mearns Street and the band of irregular Boy Scouts who paraded to the roll of tin cans. Before him stood Dougal, Chieftain of the Gorbals Die-Hards. Suddenly he remembered the philanthropic Mackintosh, and his own subscription of ten pounds to the camp fund. It pleased him to find the rascals here, for in the unpleasant affairs on the verge of which he felt himself they were a comforting reminder of the peace of home.

===Chinese===
那幽灵般的人物阴沉着脸、气鼓鼓地立在厨房地上。迪克森盯着他瞧，想起了米恩斯街，想起那支合着铁罐头敲打声列队行进的非正规童子军。站在他面前的，是戈尔巴尔死硬帮的首领杜加尔。他猛然记起那位慈善家麦金托什，记起自己捐给营地基金的十英镑。发现这帮小家伙在这儿，他倒觉得高兴，因为在他自觉正要卷入的不快事端里，他们是家宅安宁捎来的一服宽慰。

===Original===
“I’m glad to see you, Dougal,” he said pleasantly. “How are you all getting on?” And then, with a vague reminiscence of the Scouts’ code﻿—“Have you been minding to perform a good deed every day?”

===Chinese===
“见到你很高兴，杜加尔。”他和颜悦色地说，“你们大家都过得怎么样？”接着，他隐约记起童子军的规条——“你们可记得天天做一件好事？”

===Original===
The Chieftain’s brow darkened.

===Chinese===
首领的眉头沉了下来。

===Original===
“ ‘*Good deeds*!’ ” he repeated bitterly. “I tell ye I’m fair wore out wi’ good deeds. Yon man Mackintosh tell’t me this was going to be a grand holiday. Holiday! Govey Dick! It’s been like a Setterday night in Main Street﻿—a’ fechtin’, fechtin’.”

===Chinese===
“‘*好事*！’”他恨恨地重复道，“跟你直说吧，做好事做得我都快散架了。那个叫麦金托什的跟我讲，这一趟会是多风光的度假。度假！好家伙！这日子过的，活像星期六晚上的大街上——全是打架，打架。”

===Original===
No collocation of letters could reproduce Dougal’s accent, and I will not attempt it. There was a touch of Irish in it, a spice of music-hall patter, as well as the odd lilt of the Glasgow vernacular. He was strong in vowels, but the consonants, especially the letter *t*, were only aspirations.

===Chinese===
世上没有任何一种字母的排列能再现杜加尔的口音，我也不打算尝试。那口音里掺着一点爱尔兰味儿、一点杂耍场子里插科打诨的油腔，还有格拉斯哥土话那种古怪的抑扬顿挫。他元音厚实有力，辅音——尤其是字母*t*——只是一丝丝送气。

===Original===
“Sit down and let’s hear about things,” said Dickson.

===Chinese===
“坐下，让俺们听听情况。”迪克森说。

===Original===
The boy turned his head to the still open back door, where Mrs. Morran could be heard at her labours. He stepped across and shut it. “I’m no’ wantin’ that auld wife to hear,” he said. Then he squatted down on the patchwork rug by the hearth, and warmed his blue-black shins. Looking into the glow of the fire, he observed, “I seen you two up by the Big Hoose the night.”

===Chinese===
男孩扭头朝还敞着的后门望了望，莫兰太太在那边干活的动静还听得见。他跨过去把门关上。“我不想叫那老太婆听见。”他说。随后他蹲到炉边的百衲地毯上，烘他那冻得乌青的小腿。他望着炉膛的火光，说道：“今儿晚上，我在大宅子边上瞧见你们俩了。”

===Original===
“The devil you did,” said Heritage, roused to a sudden attention. “And where were you?”

===Chinese===
“你居然瞧见了，”赫里蒂奇说，霍然警觉起来，“那你当时在哪儿？”

===Original===
“Seven feet from your head, up a tree. It’s my chief hidy-hole, and Gosh! I need one, for Lean’s after me wi’ a gun. He got a shot at me two days syne.”

===Chinese===
“离你的脑袋七英尺，在一棵树上。那是本头领的头号藏身洞，老天！我能不需要吗，莱恩（Lean）正扛着枪追我呢。前两天他还朝我放过一枪。”

===Original===
Dickson exclaimed, and Dougal with morose pride showed a rent in his kilt. “If I had had on breeks, he’d ha’ got me.”

===Chinese===
迪克森惊叫出声，杜加尔带着一股阴沉的得意，指给他看短裙上一道破口。“我要是穿了长裤，他那枪就打中我了。”

===Original===
“Who’s Lean?” Heritage asked.

===Chinese===
“莱恩是谁？”赫里蒂奇问。

===Original===
“The man wi’ the black coat. The other﻿—the lame one﻿—they ca’ Spittal.”

===Chinese===
“穿黑外套的那个。另一个——瘸腿的那个——他们叫他斯皮特尔（Spittal）。”

===Original===
“How d’you know?”

===Chinese===
“你怎么知道的？”

===Original===
“I’ve listened to them crackin’ thegither.”

===Chinese===
“我偷听过他们凑在一块儿闲磕牙。”

===Original===
“But what for did the man want to shoot at you?” asked the scandalised Dickson.

===Chinese===
“可那人凭什么要朝你开枪？”迪克森又惊又愤地问。

===Original===
“What for? Because they’re frightened to death o’ onybody going near their auld Hoose. They’re a pair of deevils, worse nor any Red Indian, but for a’ that they’re sweatin’ wi’ fright. What for? says you. Because they’re hidin’ a Secret. I knew it as soon as I seen the man Lean’s face. I once seen the same kind o’ scoondrel at the Picters. When he opened his mouth to swear, I kenned he was a foreigner, like the lads down at the Broomielaw. That looked black, but I hadn’t got at the worst of it. Then he loosed off at me wi’ his gun.”

===Chinese===
“凭什么？因为他们怕得要死，生怕任何人走近他们那座老宅子。他们是一对魔头，比什么印第安红番都狠，可饶是这样，他们还是吓出了一身汗。凭什么？你问。因为他们藏着一个小秘密。我一看见莱恩那张脸就知道了。我从前在影戏院里见过同一路的恶棍。他一张嘴骂人，我就断定他是个外国人，跟布鲁米洛（Broomielaw）码头底下混的那帮小子一个路数。那瞧着就不妙，可我还没摸到最糟的底儿。跟着他就端起枪来朝我放了。”

===Original===
“Were you not feared?” said Dickson.

===Chinese===
“你当时不害怕吗？”迪克森问。

===Original===
“Ay, I was feared. But ye’ll no’ choke off the Gorbals Die-Hards wi’ a gun. We held a meetin’ round the camp fire, and we resolved to get to the bottom o’ the business. Me bein’ their Chief, it was my duty to make what they ca’ a reckonissince, for that was the dangerous job. So a’ this day I’ve been going on my belly about thae policies. I’ve found out some queer things.”

===Chinese===
“怕，我是怕的。可你休想用一杆枪就把戈尔巴尔死硬帮吓回去。我们围着营火开了一个会，决议把这桩事的底细查个水落石出。我是他们的首领，理当由我来干他们所谓的‘侦察’，因为那是危险的差事。所以这一整天，我都在这片园地里肚皮贴地地爬。我发现了一些蹊跷事。”

===Original===
Heritage had risen and was staring down at the small squatting figure.

===Chinese===
赫里蒂奇已经起身，正俯视着那个蹲着的小小身影。

===Original===
“What have you found out? Quick. Tell me at once.” His voice was sharp and excited.

===Chinese===
“你发现了什么？快。立刻告诉我。”他的嗓音又尖又急。

===Original===
“Bide a wee,” said the unwinking Dougal. “I’m no’ going to let ye into this business till I ken that ye’ll help. It’s a far bigger job than I thought. There’s more in it than Lean and Spittal. There’s the big man that keeps the public﻿—Dobson, they ca’ him. He’s a Namerican, which looks bad. And there’s two-three tinklers campin’ down in the Garple Dean. They’re in it, for Dobson was colloguin’ wi’ them a’ mornin’. When I seen ye, I thought ye were more o’ the gang, till I mindit that one o’ ye was auld McCunn that has the shop in Mearns Street. I seen that ye didn’t like the look o’ Lean, and I followed ye here, for I was thinkin’ I needit help.”

===Chinese===
“稍安勿躁，”眼皮都不眨一下的杜加尔说，“在弄清楚你们肯出力之前，我不会放你们进这桩事里来。这活儿比我原先想的大得多。里头不止莱恩和斯皮特尔两个。还有那个开酒馆的大个子——他们叫他多布森。他是个北美佬，这瞧着可不妙。还有三两个补锅匠，扎营在加普尔峡谷（Garple Dean）里。他们也有份儿，因为多布森一上午都在同他们咬耳朵。我瞧见你们的时候，还当你们是那伙人的同党，后来才想起，你们里头的一位是米恩斯街开铺子的老麦坎。我看出来你们看不惯莱恩那副嘴脸，就跟到了这儿，因为我在琢磨，我需要帮手。”

===Original===
Heritage plucked Dougal by the shoulder and lifted him to his feet.

===Chinese===
赫里蒂奇一把攥住杜加尔的肩膀，把他提了起来。

===Original===
“For God’s sake, boy,” he cried, “tell us what you know!”

===Chinese===
“看在上帝份上，小伙子，”他喊道，“把你都知道的告诉我们！”

===Original===
“Will ye help?”

===Chinese===
“你们肯出力吗？”

===Original===
“Of course, you little fool.”

===Chinese===
“当然肯，你这个小傻瓜。”

===Original===
“Then swear,” said the ritualist. From a grimy wallet he extracted a limp little volume which proved to be a damaged copy of a work entitled *Sacred Songs and Solos*. “Here! Take that in your right hand and put your left hand on my pole, and say after me, ‘I swear no’ to blab what is telled me in secret and to be swift and sure in obeyin’ orders, s’help me God!’ Syne kiss the bookie.”

===Chinese===
“那就宣誓。”这位司仪说。他从一只乌糟糟的钱包里抽出一本软塌塌的小册子，原来是一部题为《圣歌与独唱曲》（*Sacred Songs and Solos*）的破损小书。“来！右手把它攥住，左手按在我的杆子上，跟着我念：‘我宣誓，不泄露人家秘密告诉我的话，服从命令，做到又快又稳，上帝保佑我！’念完亲一亲这本小书。”

===Original===
Dickson at first refused, declaring it was all havers, but Heritage’s docility persuaded him to follow suit. The two were sworn.

===Chinese===
迪克森起初不肯，一口咬定这全是胡闹，可赫里蒂奇的恭顺劲儿说服了他，使他照样行了这套礼。两人就此宣了誓。

===Original===
“Now,” said Heritage.

===Chinese===
“好了。”赫里蒂奇说。

===Original===
Dougal squatted again on the hearthrug, and gathered the eyes of his audience. He was enjoying himself.

===Chinese===
杜加尔重新蹲回炉边地毯上，聚拢起听众的目光。他正自得其乐。

===Original===
“This day,” he said slowly, “I got inside the Hoose.”

===Chinese===
“今天，”他慢慢地说，“我进到那宅子里头去了。”

===Original===
“Stout fellow,” said Heritage; “and what did you find there?”

===Chinese===
“好样儿的，”赫里蒂奇说，“你在那儿发现了什么？”

===Original===
“I got inside that Hoose, but it wasn’t once or twice I tried. I found a corner where I was out o’ sight o’ anybody unless they had come there seekin’ me, and I sklimmed up a rone pipe, but a’ the windies were lockit and I verra near broke my neck. Syne I tried the roof, and a sore sklim I had, but when I got there there were no skylights. At the end I got in by the coal-hole. That’s why ye’re maybe thinkin’ I’m no’ very clean.”

===Chinese===
“我是进了那宅子，可试了不止一两次。我寻了个角落，除非人家存心来寻我，谁也瞧不见；我顺着一根落水管往上爬，可所有的窗户全锁着，我差一点摔断了脖子。跟着我又上房顶，那一通好爬，可等我爬上去，那儿根本没有天窗。到末了，我是从进煤口钻进去的。所以你们兴许在想，我怎么这么不干净。”

===Original===
Heritage’s patience was nearly exhausted.

===Chinese===
赫里蒂奇的耐性眼看要耗尽了。

===Original===
“I don’t want to hear how you got in. What did you find, you little devil?”

===Chinese===
“你是怎么进去的我不稀罕听。你发现了什么，你个小魔鬼？”

===Original===
“Inside the Hoose,” said Dougal slowly (and there was a melancholy sense of anticlimax in his voice, as of one who had hoped to speak of gold and jewels and armed men)﻿—“inside that Hoose there’s nothing but two women.”

===Chinese===
“在宅子里头，”杜加尔缓缓说道（他的声音里带着一种虎头蛇尾的怅然，像一个人原本指望讲到黄金、宝石和全副武装的汉子的）——“那宅子里头，什么也没有，只有两个女人。”

===Original===
Heritage sat down before him with a stern face.

===Chinese===
赫里蒂奇在他面前坐下，绷着一张严厉的脸。

===Original===
“Describe them,” he commanded.

===Chinese===
“描述她们。”他命令道。

===Original===
“One o’ them is dead auld, as auld as the wife here. She didn’t look to me very right in the head.”

===Chinese===
“其中一个老得不能再老，跟这儿这位老太婆一般老。我看她脑筋不太对头。”

===Original===
“And the other?”

===Chinese===
“另一个呢？”

===Original===
“Oh, just a lassie.”

===Chinese===
“哦，不过是个小姑娘。”

===Original===
“What was she like?”

===Chinese===
“她长什么模样？”

===Original===
Dougal seemed to be searching for adequate words. “She is﻿ ﻿…” he began. Then a popular song gave him inspiration. “She’s pure as the lully in the dell!”

===Chinese===
杜加尔像是在搜寻够分量的字眼。“她是……”他起了个头。跟着，一支流行小曲给了他灵感。“她纯洁得就像幽谷里的百合花！”

===Original===
In no way discomposed by Heritage’s fierce interrogatory air, he continued: “She’s either foreign or English, for she couldn’t understand what I said, and I could make nothing o’ her clippit tongue. But I could see she had been greetin’. She looked feared, yet kind o’ determined. I speired if I could do anything for her, and when she got my meaning she was terrible anxious to ken if I had seen a man﻿—a big man, she said, wi’ a yellow beard. She didn’t seem to ken his name, or else she wouldn’t tell me. The auld wife was mortal feared, and was aye speakin’ in a foreign langwidge. I seen at once that what frightened them was Lean and his friends, and I was just starting to speir about them when there came a sound like a man walkin’ along the passage. She was for hidin’ me in behind a sofy, but I wasn’t going to be trapped like that, so I got out by the other door and down the kitchen stairs and into the coal-hole. Gosh, it was a near thing!”

===Chinese===
他丝毫没有被赫里蒂奇那副凌厉逼问的架势慑住，接着说：“她不是外国人就是英格兰人，因为她听不懂我的话，她那一口剪得七零八落的话，我也一个字听不出名堂。不过我看得出来，她先前哭过。她看上去害怕，可又有一股拿定了主意的劲儿。我问她有什么能效劳的地方，她弄懂我的意思后，急得不得了，问我见没见过一个男人——她说是个大个子，留一部黄胡子。她像是不知道他的名字，再不然就是不肯告诉我。那老太婆怕得要死，嘴里一直说着外国话。我一眼就看出来，吓着她们的是莱恩和他的同伙；我正要开口打听他们，走廊里就传来一个男人走道的声响。她要把我藏到沙发后头，我才不肯那样自投罗网，就从另一扇门出去，下了厨房的楼梯，钻进了进煤口。天哪，真是险到毫厘！”

===Original===
The boy was on his feet. “I must be off to the camp to give out the orders for the morn. I’m going back to that Hoose, for it’s a fight atween the Gorbals Die-Hards and the scoundrels that are frightenin’ thae women. The question is, Are ye comin’ with me? Mind, ye’ve sworn. But if ye’re no’, I’m going mysel’, though I’ll no’ deny I’d be glad o’ company. *You* anyway﻿—” he added, nodding at Heritage. “Maybe auld McCunn wouldn’t get through the coal-hole.”

===Chinese===
男孩一跃而起。“我得回营地去，把明天的号令发下去。我要回到那宅子里去，因为这是戈尔巴尔死硬帮和那帮吓唬女人的恶棍之间的一场仗。问题是：你们跟不跟我去？记住，你们宣过誓了。你们要是不去，我自己也去——不过我不否认，有个伴儿我很欢迎。*你*反正得去——”他朝赫里蒂奇点点头，补了一句。“老麦坎嘛，说不定就钻不过那个进煤口喽。”

===Original===
“You’re an impident laddie,” said the outraged Dickson. “It’s no’ likely we’re coming with you. Breaking into other folks’ houses! It’s a job for the police!”

===Chinese===
“你是个没上没下的小子，”被触犯了的迪克森说，“俺们才不会跟你去呢。闯别人家的宅子！那是警察的差事！”

===Original===
“Please yersel’,” said the Chieftain and looked at Heritage.

===Chinese===
“随你们的便。”首领说罢，把目光投向赫里蒂奇。

===Original===
“I’m on,” said that gentleman.

===Chinese===
“算我一个。”那位绅士说。

===Original===
“Well, just you set out the morn as if ye were for a walk up the Garple glen. I’ll be on the road and I’ll have orders for ye.”

===Chinese===
“那好，明儿你们只管动身，只当是往加普尔河峡谷上游散步去。我自会在路上，到时候给你们号令。”

===Original===
Without more ado Dougal left by way of the back kitchen. There was a brief denunciation from Mrs. Morran, then the outer door banged and he was gone.

===Chinese===
杜加尔不再多话，打后厨房走了。莫兰太太短短数落了他一通，随后外门砰地一响，人已经去了。

===Original===
The Poet sat still with his head in his hands, while Dickson, acutely uneasy, prowled about the floor. He had forgotten even to light his pipe.

===Chinese===
诗人双手抱头，呆坐不动；迪克森却心乱如麻，在地板上来回踱步。他连烟斗都忘了点。

===Original===
“You’ll not be thinking of heeding that ragamuffin boy,” he ventured.

===Chinese===
“你总不至于当真要听那野小子的话吧。”他试探道。

===Original===
“I’m certainly going to get into the House tomorrow,” Heritage answered, “and if he can show me a way so much the better. He’s a spirited youth. Do you breed many like him in Glasgow?”

===Chinese===
“我明天铁定要进那座宅子，”赫里蒂奇答道，“他若能给我指一条道，那就再好不过。他是个有血性的少年。你们格拉斯哥，养得出许多这号人物吗？”

===Original===
“Plenty,” said Dickson sourly. “See here, Mr. Heritage. You can’t expect me to be going about burgling houses on the word of a blagyird laddie. I’m a respectable man﻿—aye been. Besides, I’m here for a holiday, and I’ve no call to be mixing myself up in strangers’ affairs.”

===Chinese===
“多得很。”迪克森酸溜溜地说，“你听俺说，赫里蒂奇先生。你可不能指望俺凭一个小泼皮的一句话，就到处去撬人家的门。俺是个体面人——从来都是。再说，俺来这儿是度假的，犯不着把俺自己搅进外人的事体里。”

===Original===
“You haven’t. Only, you see, I think there’s a friend of mine in that place, and anyhow there are women in trouble. If you like, we’ll say goodbye after breakfast, and you can continue as if you had never turned aside to this damned peninsula. But I’ve got to stay.”

===Chinese===
“是犯不着。只不过，你瞧，我认为那地方里有一位我的朋友，况且不管怎样，那里有落难的女人。你若愿意，咱们吃过早饭就道别，你只管继续赶路，只当从来没拐进这个该死的半岛。可我非留下不可。”

===Original===
Dickson groaned. What had become of his dream of idylls, his gentle bookish romance? Vanished before a reality which smacked horribly of crude melodrama and possibly of sordid crime. His gorge rose at the picture, but a thought troubled him. Perhaps all romance in its hour of happening was rough and ugly like this, and only shone rosy in the retrospect. Was he being false to his deepest faith?

===Chinese===
迪克森呻吟了一声。他那田园牧歌的美梦，他那温文尔雅的书斋浪漫，都到哪里去了？在一种带着粗劣情节剧的可怕气味、说不定还带着肮脏罪案气味的现实面前，它们烟消云散了。这光景叫他作呕，可有一个念头困扰着他：也许一切浪漫，在它发生的时辰里，本来就是这样粗粝丑陋，只在回望时才泛出玫瑰色的光彩。他是不是在背叛自己最深底的信仰？

===Original===
“Let’s have Mrs. Morran in,” he ventured. “She’s a wise old body and I’d like to hear her opinion of this business. We’ll get common sense from her.”

===Chinese===
“咱们把莫兰太太请进来吧。”他试探道，“她是位明白事理的老太太，俺想听听她对这桩事的看法。咱们能从她那儿讨到些常识。”

===Original===
“I don’t object,” said Heritage. “But no amount of common sense will change my mind.”

===Chinese===
“我不反对，”赫里蒂奇说，“不过再多的常识，也改不了我的主意。”

===Original===
Their hostess forestalled them by returning at that moment to the kitchen.

===Chinese===
还没等他们去请，女主人恰在那一刻自己回到了厨房。

===Original===
“We want your advice, mistress,” Dickson told her, and accordingly, like a barrister with a client, she seated herself carefully in the big easy chair, found and adjusted her spectacles, and waited with hands folded on her lap to hear the business. Dickson narrated their pre-supper doings, and gave a sketch of Dougal’s evidence. His exposition was cautious and colourless, and without conviction. He seemed to expect a robust incredulity in his hearer.

===Chinese===
“俺们想讨您的高见，大娘。”迪克森对她说。于是，像一位接待当事人的出庭律师，她小心翼翼在那把大安乐椅里落了座，找出眼镜来戴正，双手交叠放在膝上，等着听取案情。迪克森讲述了他们晚饭前的所作所为，又概述了杜加尔的证词。他讲得谨慎而平淡，毫无说服力，倒像是一心指望听的人报以斩钉截铁的怀疑。

===Original===
Mrs. Morran listened with the gravity of one in church. When Dickson finished she seemed to meditate.

===Chinese===
莫兰太太听着，那份庄重，像在教堂里听道。迪克森讲完，她似乎沉吟起来。

===Original===
“There’s no blagyird trick that would surprise me in thae new folk. What’s that ye ca’ them﻿—Lean and Spittal? Eppie Home threepit to me they were furriners and these are no furrin names.”

===Chinese===
“这帮新来的人，就是使出什么无赖勾当，俺也不会吃惊。他们叫个啥来着——莱恩和斯皮特尔？埃皮·霍姆跟俺咬死说他们是外国人，可这两个不像外国名字。”

===Original===
“What I want to hear from you, Mrs. Morran,” said Dickson impressively, “is whether you think there’s anything in that boy’s story?”

===Chinese===
“俺想从您这儿听到的，莫兰太太，”迪克森郑重其事地说，“是您觉得那孩子的话里，有几分实在？”

===Original===
“I think it’s maist likely true. He’s a terrible impident callant, but he’s no’ a leear.”

===Chinese===
“俺瞧十有八九是真的。那小子浑得没边儿，可他不是个说谎的。”

===Original===
“Then you think that a gang of ruffians have got two lone women shut up in that House for their own purposes?”

===Chinese===
“那么您是认为，有一伙恶棍为了自家的目的，把两个孤身女人关在了那座宅子里？”

===Original===
“I wadna wonder.”

===Chinese===
“俺不奇怪。”

===Original===
“But it’s ridiculous! This is a Christian and law-abiding country. What would the police say?”

===Chinese===
“可这太荒唐了！这是个信奉基督、遵纪守法的国家。警察会怎么说？”

===Original===
“They never troubled Dalquharter muckle. There’s no’ a polisman nearer than Knockraw﻿—yin Johnnie Trummle, and he’s as useless as a frostit tattie.”

===Chinese===
“警察从来不大为达尔夸特劳神。最近的警察也在诺克劳（Knockraw）——一个叫约翰尼·特朗布尔（Johnnie Trummle）的，那人跟冻烂的土豆一样不顶事。”

===Original===
“The wiselike thing, as I think,” said Dickson, “would be to turn the Procurator-Fiscal on to the job. It’s his business, no’ ours.”

===Chinese===
“依俺看，”迪克森说，“妥当的办法，是把这事移交代理检察官（Procurator-Fiscal）去办。那是他的差事，不是俺们的。”

===Original===
“Weel, I wadna say but ye’re richt,” said the lady.

===Chinese===
“嗯，俺不能不说，你在理。”那位女士说。

===Original===
“What would you do if you were us?” Dickson’s tone was subtly confidential. “My friend here wants to get into the House the morn with that red-haired laddie to satisfy himself about the facts. I say no. Let sleeping dogs lie, I say, and if you think the beasts are mad report to the authorities. What would you do yourself?”

===Chinese===
“要是您换了俺们，您会怎么做？”迪克森的口气透着微妙的推心置腹。“俺这位朋友明儿要同那红头发小子进那宅子，好把事实弄个真切。俺说不行。俺说，睡着的狗莫去惹；您要是觉得那两头畜生疯了，就报到官府去。您自个儿会怎么做？”

===Original===
“If I were you,” came the emphatic reply, “I would tak’ the first train hame the morn, and when I got hame I wad bide there. Ye’re a dacent body, but ye’re no’ the kind to be traivellin’ the roads.”

===Chinese===
“俺要是你们，”回答斩钉截铁，“明儿一早就搭头班火车回家，到了家就老老实实待着。你是个正派人，可你不是该在大道上奔波的那号人。”

===Original===
“And if you were me?” Heritage asked with his queer crooked smile.

===Chinese===
“那您要是换了我呢？”赫里蒂奇问，脸上带着他那古怪的歪嘴笑。

===Original===
“If I was a young and yauld like you I wad gang into the Hoose, and I wadna rest till I had riddled oot the truith and jyled every scoondrel about the place. If ye dinna gang, ’faith I’ll kilt my coats and gang mysel’. I havena served the Kennedys for forty year no’ to hae the honour o’ the Hoose at my hert.﻿ ﻿… Ye speired my advice, sirs, and ye’ve gotten it. Now I maun clear awa’ your supper.”

===Chinese===
“俺要是像你这样年轻力壮血气旺，俺就进那宅子去，不把真相处处查明、不把那地场周围的恶棍一个个送进大牢，俺决不歇手。你们要是不去，说真的，俺就掖起裙子上俺自个儿去。俺给肯尼迪家当了四十年的差，不能不把府上的体面搁在心尖上。……你们讨了俺的主意，先生们，主意也到手了。这会儿俺得撤你们的晚饭喽。”

===Original===
Dickson asked for a candle, and, as on the previous night, went abruptly to bed. The oracle of prudence to which he had appealed had betrayed him and counselled folly. But was it folly? For him, assuredly, for Dickson McCunn, late of Mearns Street, Glasgow, wholesale and retail provision merchant, elder in the Guthrie Memorial Kirk, and fifty-five years of age. Ay, that was the rub. He was getting old. The woman had seen it and had advised him to go home. Yet the plea was curiously irksome, though it gave him the excuse he needed. If you played at being young, you had to take up the obligations of youth, and he thought derisively of his boyish exhilaration of the past days. Derisively, but also sadly. What had become of that innocent joviality he had dreamed of, that happy morning pilgrimage of Spring enlivened by tags from the poets? His goddess had played him false. Romance had put upon him too hard a trial.

===Chinese===
迪克森讨了一支蜡烛，像前一晚那样，突兀地上床去了。他求问的那座审慎的神谕背叛了他，反倒劝他行愚。可那是行愚吗？对他而言，准定是——对这位格拉斯哥米恩斯街的前店主、批零兼营的食品杂货商、格思里纪念教堂的长老、五十五岁的迪克森·麦坎而言，准定是。是啊，症结就在这儿。他上了年纪啦。那女人看出来了，才劝他回家。然而这托词叫他异样地憋闷，虽说它给了他正需要的台阶。人既然装年轻，就得把年轻人的义务也担起来；他想起这几日自己孩子气的兴高采烈，不由心生讥诮。带着讥诮，可也带着伤感。他梦寐以求的那份天真的快活哪里去了？那趟由诗人们的零章断句点染得生动起来的、春天的快活晨间朝圣，哪里去了？他的女神骗了他。浪漫给了他一场过于严酷的考验。

===Original===
He lay long awake, torn between common sense and a desire to be loyal to some vague whimsical standard. Heritage a yard distant appeared also to be sleepless, for the bed creaked with his turning. Dickson found himself envying one whose troubles, whatever they might be, were not those of a divided mind.

===Chinese===
他久久醒着躺在床上，一面是常识，一面是想对某种说不清道不明的古怪信条尽忠的执念，他被撕扯在中间。一码开外的赫里蒂奇似乎也睡不着，因为床随着他翻身吱呀作响。迪克森发觉自己在羡慕对方——那人的烦恼，不管是什么烦恼，总不是一颗心撕成两半的烦恼。

## V. Of the Princess in the Tower / 五、塔中公主

===Original===
Very early next morning, while Mrs. Morran was still cooking breakfast, Dickson and Heritage might have been observed taking the air in the village street. It was the Poet who had insisted upon this walk, and he had his own purpose. They looked at the spires of smoke piercing the windless air, and studied the daffodils in the cottage gardens. Dickson was glum, but Heritage seemed in high spirits. He varied his garrulity with spells of cheerful whistling.

===Chinese===
第二天清晨极早时分，莫兰太太还在做早饭，迪克森和赫里蒂奇两人已经可以在村街上遛弯儿了。是诗人执意要走这一趟，他自有他的用意。他们望着烟囱吐出的一柱柱炊烟笔直刺入无风的空中，又细细赏看农舍园子里的水仙。迪克森闷闷不乐，赫里蒂奇却似乎兴致很高，絮叨一阵，又换上几段欢快的口哨。

===Original===
They strode along the road by the park wall till they reached the inn. There Heritage’s music waxed peculiarly loud. Presently from the yard, unshaven and looking as if he had slept in his clothes, came Dobson the innkeeper.

===Chinese===
两人顺着园墙外的大路大步走去，一直走到客栈跟前。到了那儿，赫里蒂奇的曲调陡然拔高。不一会儿，从院子里走出客栈店主多布森，胡子没刮，一副和衣睡过觉的模样。

===Original===
“Good morning,” said the Poet. “I hope the sickness in your house is on the mend?”

===Chinese===
“早上好，”诗人说，“您店里的病人见好了吗？”

===Original===
“Thank ye, it’s no worse,” was the reply, but in the man’s heavy face there was little civility. His small grey eyes searched their faces.

===Chinese===
“多谢，没见坏。”对方答道，可他那张沉重的脸上没多少客气。一双灰色的小眼睛在两人脸上搜来搜去。

===Original===
“We’re just waiting on breakfast to get on the road again. I’m jolly glad we spent the night here. We found quarters after all, you know.”

===Chinese===
“我们正等着吃早饭，好重新上路。昨儿夜里能在这一带过夜，我高兴得很。您知道，我们到底还是寻着住处了。”

===Original===
“So I see. Whereabouts, may I ask?”

===Chinese===
“原来如此。能不能问一句，住在哪儿？”

===Original===
“Mrs. Morran’s. We could always have got in there, but we didn’t want to fuss an old lady, so we thought we’d try the inn first. She’s my friend’s aunt.”

===Chinese===
“莫兰太太家。我们本来随时都能上那儿落脚，只是不想惊动一位老太太，就想着先试试客栈。她是这位朋友的姨妈。”

===Original===
At this amazing falsehood Dickson started, and the man observed his surprise. The eyes were turned on him like a searchlight. They roused antagonism in his peaceful soul, and with that antagonism came an impulse to back up the Poet. “Ay,” he said, “she’s my Auntie Phemie, my mother’s half-sister.”

===Chinese===
听到这惊人的谎话，迪克森吓了一跳，那人把他的惊愕瞧在了眼里。那双眼睛像探照灯一样转到他身上。它们在他平和的心里激起了一股敌意，而随着这股敌意，又涌起一股要给诗人帮腔的冲动。“是呢，”他说，“她是俺的菲米姨妈，俺母亲同母异父的妹妹。”

===Original===
The man turned on Heritage.

===Chinese===
那人转身对着赫里蒂奇。

===Original===
“Where are ye for the day?”

===Chinese===
“你们今天打算上哪儿去？”

===Original===
“Auchenlochan,” said Dickson hastily. He was still determined to shake the dust of Dalquharter from his feet.

===Chinese===
“奥琴洛坎。”迪克森赶忙答道。他仍旧铁了心，要把达尔夸特的尘土从脚上抖落干净。

===Original===
The innkeeper sensibly brightened. “Well, ye’ll have a fine walk. I must go in and see about my own breakfast. Good day to ye, gentlemen.”

===Chinese===
客栈店主的脸色明显开朗起来。“好啊，那你们这趟走起来痛快。我得进去照应我自己的早饭了。两位先生，日安。”

===Original===
“That,” said Heritage as they entered the village street again, “is the first step in camouflage, to put the enemy off his guard.”

===Chinese===
“这一手，”两人重新走回村街时，赫里蒂奇说，“是伪装的第一步，好叫敌人放松戒备。”

===Original===
“It was an abominable lie,” said Dickson crossly.

===Chinese===
“那是个混账的谎话。”迪克森没好气地说。

===Original===
“Not at all. It was a necessary and proper *ruse de guerre*. It explained why we spent the night here, and now Dobson and his friends can get about their day’s work with an easy mind. Their suspicions are temporarily allayed, and that will make our job easier.”

===Chinese===
“一点也不混账。那是一步必要而得宜的*兵家诈术*（ruse de guerre）。它解释了我们为什么在这儿过夜，如今多布森和他的同伙就可以安安心心去干他们白天的营生了。他们的疑心暂时消了下去，咱们的事也就好办了。”

===Original===
“I’m not coming with you.”

===Chinese===
“俺不跟你去。”

===Original===
“I never said you were. By ‘we’ I refer to myself and the redheaded boy.”

===Chinese===
“我没说你要去。我说的‘我们’，指的是我自己和那个红头发小子。”

===Original===
“Mistress, you’re my auntie,” Dickson informed Mrs. Morran as she set the porridge on the table. “This gentleman has just been telling the man at the inn that you’re my Auntie Phemie.”

===Chinese===
“大娘，您如今是俺姨妈了。”迪克森在莫兰太太把麦片粥端上桌时告诉她，“这位先生方才跟客栈那人说，您是俺的菲米姨妈。”

===Original===
For a second their hostess looked bewildered. Then the corners of her prim mouth moved upwards in a slow smile.

===Chinese===
女主人有一瞬满脸茫然。跟着，她那抿得端正的嘴角缓缓向上弯起，露出一个慢慢的笑。

===Original===
“I see,” she said. “Weel, maybe it was weel done. But if ye’re my nevoy ye’ll hae to keep up my credit, for we’re a bauld and siccar lot.”

===Chinese===
“俺明白了，”她说，“嗯，这么着兴许着得对。可你既做了俺外甥，就得给俺撑住体面，因为俺们这一族，胆子大，靠得住。”

===Original===
Half an hour later there was a furious dissension when Dickson attempted to pay for the night’s entertainment. Mrs. Morran would have none of it. “Ye’re no’ awa’ yet,” she said tartly, and the matter was complicated by Heritage’s refusal to take part in the debate. He stood aside and grinned, till Dickson in despair returned his notecase to his pocket, murmuring darkly that “he would send it from Glasgow.”

===Chinese===
半小时后，迪克森试图支付这一晚的款待，掀起了一场激烈的争执。莫兰太太说什么也不肯收。“你们人还没走呢。”她说得冲。事情又因赫里蒂奇拒不出场辩论而愈发难办——他站在一边咧嘴直笑，直弄得迪克森绝望之下把皮夹子塞回口袋，含含糊糊地咕哝说他“回头从格拉斯哥把钱寄来”。

===Original===
The road to Auchenlochan left the main village street at right angles by the side of Mrs. Morran’s cottage. It was a better road than that which they had come yesterday, for by it twice daily the post-cart travelled to the post-town. It ran on the edge of the moor and on the lip of the Garple glen, till it crossed that stream and, keeping near the coast, emerged after five miles into the cultivated flats of the Lochan valley. The morning was fine, the keen air invited to high spirits, plovers piped entrancingly over the bent and linnets sang in the whins, there was a solid breakfast behind him, and the promise of a cheerful road till luncheon. The stage was set for good humour, but Dickson’s heart, which should have been ascending with the larks, stuck leadenly in his boots. He was not even relieved at putting Dalquharter behind him. The atmosphere of that unhallowed place lay still on his soul. He hated it, but he hated himself more. Here was one, who had hugged himself all his days as an adventurer waiting his chance, running away at the first challenge of adventure; a lover of Romance who fled from the earliest overture of his goddess. He was ashamed and angry, but what else was there to do? Burglary in the company of a queer poet and a queerer urchin? It was unthinkable.

===Chinese===
去奥琴洛坎的路，在莫兰太太的小屋旁边拐出村街，成一个直角。这条路比他们昨天来的那条好走，因为每天有两趟邮车打这儿驶往收寄邮件的邮镇。它沿着荒原的边缘、贴着加普尔河峡谷的唇沿前行，直到跨过那条溪流，又傍着海岸走，五英里之后便进入洛坎（Lochan）河谷耕作的平川。清晨天气晴好，凛冽的空气撩人兴致；鸻鸟在沙草坡上鸣声醉人，朱雀在荆豆丛里歌唱；一顿瓷实的早饭垫在肚里，前头还许着一条痛快的路直通到午饭时分。好心境的戏台都搭好了，可迪克森那颗本该随云雀一同升天的心，却铅一样坠在靴子里。把达尔夸特甩在身后，他甚至并不觉得轻松。那座不洁之地的气氛仍旧压在他的灵魂上。他恨它，可他更恨自己。瞧瞧这个人吧——一辈子自诩为等待时机的冒险家，冒险的头一声挑战刚响，就撒腿逃跑；一个浪漫的情人，女神的前奏曲方才奏起，就望风而遁。他又羞又恼，可除此之外还有什么可做？跟着一个古怪的诗人加一个更古怪的顽童去撬门入室？想都不能想。

===Original===
Presently as they tramped silently on they came to the bridge beneath which the peaty waters of the Garple ran in porter-coloured pools and tawny cascades. From a clump of elders on the other side Dougal emerged. A barefoot boy, dressed in much the same parody of a Boy Scout’s uniform, but with corduroy shorts instead of a kilt, stood before him at rigid attention. Some command was issued, the child saluted, and trotted back past the travellers with never a look at them. Discipline was strong among the Gorbals Die-Hards; no Chief of Staff ever conversed with his General under a stricter etiquette.

===Chinese===
不久，两人默默前行，来到了那座桥，桥下加普尔河泥炭色的溪水淌成一汪汪黑啤酒色的深潭和黄褐色的急瀑。杜加尔从对岸一丛接骨木后面钻了出来。一个赤脚男孩立在他面前，绷得笔直地立正——那身行头也是对童子军制服差不离的滑稽仿制，只是没穿短裙，换了条灯芯绒短裤。杜加尔发布了什么命令，那孩子敬了个礼，小跑着从两位旅人身边折返回去，眼角也不扫他们一下。戈尔巴尔死硬帮里纪律严明；没有哪位参谋总长同统帅谈话，讲究过比这更森严的礼数。

===Original===
Dougal received the travellers with the condescension of a regular towards civilians.

===Chinese===
杜加尔接待两位旅人，摆出正规军对待平民的那种居高临下的架子。

===Original===
“They’re off their gawrd,” he announced. “Thomas Yownie has been shadowin’ them since skreigh o’ day, and he reports that Dobson and Lean followed ye till ye were out o’ sight o’ the houses, and syne Lean got a spyglass and watched ye till the road turned in among the trees. That satisfied them, and they’re both away back to their jobs. Thomas Yownie’s the fell yin. Ye’ll no fickle Thomas Yownie.”

===Chinese===
“他们卸了防了。”他宣布道，“打天刚放亮，托马斯·尤尼（Thomas Yownie）就一直盯他们的梢。他报告说，多布森和莱恩一路跟着你们，直跟到你们走出房屋的视线，然后莱恩弄来一架望远镜，一路望着你们，直到大路拐进树林。这一来他们放了心，俩人都回各自岗位去了。托马斯·尤尼就是那号厉害角色。你可糊弄不了托马斯·尤尼。”

===Original===
Dougal extricated from his pouch the fag of a cigarette, lit it and puffed meditatively. “I did a reckonissince mysel’ this morning. I was up at the Hoose afore it was light, and tried the door o’ the coal-hole. I doot they’ve gotten on our tracks, for it was lockit﻿—ay, and wedged from the inside.”

===Chinese===
杜加尔从烟荷包里摸出一截烟头，点上，沉思地吐着烟。“今儿早上我自己去做了一次侦察。天没亮我就摸到了府跟前，去试进煤口那扇门。我怕他们是摸着咱们的道儿了，因为门上了锁——可不，还从里头打了楔子。”

===Original===
Dickson brightened. Was the insane venture off?

===Chinese===
迪克森眼睛一亮。这场疯癫的冒险取消了？

===Original===
“For a wee bit I was fair beat. But I mindit that the lassie was allowed to walk in a kind o’ a glass hoose on the side farthest away from the Garple. That was where she was singin’ yest’reen. So I reckonissinced in that direction, and I fund a queer place.” *Sacred Songs and Solos* was requisitioned, and on a page of it Dougal proceeded to make marks with the stump of a carpenter’s pencil. “See here,” he commanded. “There’s the glass place wi’ a door into the Hoose. That door must be open or the lassie must have the key, for she comes there whenever she likes. Now, at each end o’ the place the doors are lockit, but the front that looks on the garden is open, wi’ muckle posts and flowerpots. The trouble is that that side there’s maybe twenty feet o’ a wall between the pawrapet and the ground. It’s an auld wall wi’ cracks and holes in it, and it wouldn’t be ill to sklim. That’s why they let her gang there when she wants, for a lassie couldn’t get away without breakin’ her neck.”

===Chinese===
“有一阵子我是彻底没辙了。可我想起来，那小姑娘获准到一种玻璃房子里溜达，就在离加普尔河最远的那一面。她昨儿晚上唱歌的地方就在那儿。于是我便朝那个方向侦察了一番，发现一处古怪地方。”《圣歌与独唱曲》被征调了来，杜加尔用一截木匠铅笔头在其中一页上画起道道来。“瞧这儿，”他发号施令，“这是那玻璃地方，有扇门通进府里。那扇门必定是不上锁的，要么就是钥匙在小姑娘手里，因为她想什么时候来就什么时候来。那地方两头的门都锁着，可朝着花园的那一面是敞开的，立着大柱子，摆着花盆。麻烦的是，那一边从墙头顶到地面，怕不有二十英尺高。那是堵老墙，裂缝窟窿不少，爬起来不难。所以他们才随她高兴让她去那儿，因为一个小姑娘想逃出去，不摔断脖子是办不到的。”

===Original===
“Could we climb it?” Heritage asked.

===Chinese===
“我们爬得上那面墙吗？”赫里蒂奇问。

===Original===
The boy wrinkled his brows. “I could manage it mysel’﻿—I think﻿—and maybe you. I doubt if auld McCunn could get up. Ye’d have to be mighty carefu’ that nobody saw ye, for your hinder end, as ye were sklimmin’, wad be a grand mark for a gun.”

===Chinese===
男孩皱起眉头。“我自己对付得了——我想是——你说不定也行。老麦坎能不能上去，我犯嘀咕。你们可得千万当心别让人瞧见，因为你们爬墙的时候，那后腚可是给枪备下的好靶子。”

===Original===
“Lead on,” said Heritage. “We’ll try the verandah.”

===Chinese===
“头前带路，”赫里蒂奇说，“咱们去试试那道游廊。”

===Original===
They both looked at Dickson, and Dickson, scarlet in the face, looked back at them. He had suddenly found the thought of a solitary march to Auchenlochan intolerable. Once again he was at the parting of the ways, and once more caprice determined his decision. That the coal-hole was out of the question had worked a change in his views. Somehow it seemed to him less burglarious to enter by a verandah. He felt very frightened but﻿—for the moment﻿—quite resolute.

===Chinese===
两人都望着迪克森，迪克森涨红了脸，回望着他们。他忽然觉得，独自一人走去奥琴洛坎这念头已经叫人受不了了。他又一次站在了岔路口，而又一次，是一时兴之所至替他拿了大主意。进煤口既已断了指望，他的看法也随之起了变化。不知怎么，从游廊进去，在他看来不像撬门那样像贼。他心里怕得厉害，可——至少这一刻——主意拿得铁定。

===Original===
“I’m coming with you,” he said.

===Chinese===
“俺跟你们一起去。”他说。

===Original===
“Sportsman,” said Heritage and held out his hand. “Well done, the auld yin,” said the Chieftain of the Gorbals Die-Hards. Dickson’s quaking heart experienced a momentary bound as he followed Heritage down the track into the Garple Dean.

===Chinese===
“有种。”赫里蒂奇说着伸出手来。“干得好，老家伙。”戈尔巴尔死硬帮的首领说。迪克森那颗发颤的心猛地一跳，跟着赫里蒂奇踏上了通往加普尔峡谷的小径。

===Original===
The track wound through a thick covert of hazels, now close to the rushing water, now high upon the bank so that clear sky showed through the fringes of the wood. When they had gone a little way Dougal halted them.

===Chinese===
小径在茂密的榛树丛中蜿蜒，一会儿贴近奔涌的溪水，一会儿又高上堤岸，叫人从林子的边缘望得见明净的天空。走出一段路后，杜加尔叫他们停了下来。

===Original===
“It’s a ticklish job,” he whispered. “There’s the tinklers, mind, that’s campin’ in the Dean. If they’re still in their camp we can get by easy enough, but they’re maybe wanderin’ about the wud after rabbits.﻿ ﻿… Then we must ford the water, for ye’ll no’ cross it lower down where it’s deep.﻿ ﻿… Our road is on the Hoose side o’ the Dean and it’s awfu’ public if there’s onybody on the other side, though it’s hid well enough from folk up in the policies.﻿ ﻿… Ye must do exactly what I tell ye. When we get near danger I’ll scout on ahead, and I daur ye to move a hair o’ your head till I give the word.”

===Chinese===
“这活儿悬得很。”他压低嗓门说，“别忘了，还有那帮补锅匠，就在峡谷里扎营。他们要是还待在营地里，咱们过去不难；可他们兴许正在林子里转悠逮兔子呢。……再说咱们得蹚水过河，因为下游水深，你们过不去。……咱们的道儿在峡谷靠府这一边，对面要是有人，那可太招眼了，好在园地里高处的人瞧不见咱们。……你们必须一字不差照我说的做。一挨近险处，我就到前头放哨，我不发话，你们谁敢动一根头发试试。”

===Original===
Presently, when they were at the edge of the water, Dougal announced his intention of crossing. Three boulders in the stream made a bridge for an active man and Heritage hopped lightly over. Not so Dickson, who stuck fast on the second stone, and would certainly have fallen in had not Dougal plunged into the current and steadied him with a grimy hand. The leap was at last successfully taken, and the three scrambled up a rough scaur, all reddened with iron springs, till they struck a slender track running down the Dean on its northern side. Here the undergrowth was very thick, and they had gone the better part of half a mile before the covert thinned sufficiently to show them the stream beneath. Then Dougal halted them with a finger on his lips, and crept forward alone.

===Chinese===
不久，到了水边，杜加尔宣布就要渡河。溪中三块大圆石，给腿脚利索的人搭成了一座桥，赫里蒂奇轻轻一跃就过去了。迪克森可不行，他卡在第二块石头上进退不得，若不是杜加尔蹚进激流，用一只乌黑的小手扶稳了他，准得栽进水里。这一跳总算跳成了，三个人手脚并用地爬上一道粗粝的乱石坡——坡上处处渗着含铁的泉水，浸得通红——直爬到一条贴着峡谷北坡而下的小径上。这里的灌木丛生得极密，他们走了将近半英里，树丛才稀疏得能望见脚下的溪水。杜加尔把一根手指压在唇上叫他们停住，独自匍匐着向前去了。

===Original===
He returned in three minutes. “Coast’s clear,” he whispered. “The tinklers are eatin’ their breakfast. They’re late at their meat though they’re up early seekin’ it.”

===Chinese===
三分钟后他回来了。“没事了。”他耳语道，“补锅匠们正吃早饭呢。找食儿起个大早，吃食儿倒拖到这会儿。”

===Original===
Progress was now very slow and secret and mainly on all fours. At one point Dougal nodded downward, and the other two saw on a patch of turf, where the Garple began to widen into its estuary, a group of figures round a small fire. There were four of them, all men, and Dickson thought he had never seen such ruffianly-looking customers. After that they moved high up the slope, in a shallow glade of a tributary burn, till they came out of the trees and found themselves looking seaward.

===Chinese===
这会儿行进得极慢、极隐秘，大半程靠四肢着地爬。有一处，杜加尔朝下点了点头，另两位便望见在一片草皮上——加普尔河在那儿开始放宽，注入河口湾——一小堆火四周围着几个人影。一共四个，全是男人，迪克森心想，他从没见过面目这样凶恶的家伙。此后他们走高处坡路，沿一条支流小溪畔浅浅的林间空地走，直到走出树林，发觉眼前已是望海的去处。

===Original===
On one side was the House, a hundred yards or so back from the edge, the roof showing above the precipitous scarp. Halfway down the slope became easier, a jumble of boulders and boiler-plates, till it reached the waters of the small haven, which lay calm as a millpond in the windless forenoon. The haven broadened out at its foot and revealed a segment of blue sea. The opposite shore was flatter and showed what looked like an old wharf and the ruins of buildings, behind which rose a bank clad with scrub and surmounted by some gnarled and wind-crooked firs.

===Chinese===
一侧是大宅，离崖沿百码开外，屋顶从陡峭的断崖上露出来。半坡往下，坡势平缓下来，是一片大圆石和平板大石的乱堆，一直铺到小港的水边。无风的上午，小港的水静得像磨坊的水池。港面在崖脚下豁然开阔，露出一段蓝色的海。对岸平缓些，看得见一座像是老码头的东西和几处建筑的残迹，后面隆起一道生满灌木的土坡，坡顶立着几株虬结扭曲、被风拧歪的冷杉。

===Original===
“There’s dashed little cover here,” said Heritage.

===Chinese===
“这儿简直没处藏身。”赫里蒂奇说。

===Original===
“There’s no muckle,” Dougal assented. “But they canna see us from the policies, and it’s no’ like there’s anybody watchin’ from the Hoose. The danger is somebody on the other side, but we’ll have to risk it. Once among thae big stones we’re safe. Are ye ready?”

===Chinese===
“是没多少。”杜加尔附和道，“可园地里的人瞧不见咱们，府里眼下也不像有人望风。危险是对岸要是有人——那也只好豁出去了。一钻进这些大石头，咱们就保险了。预备好了吗？”

===Original===
Five minutes later Dickson found himself gasping in the lee of a boulder, while Dougal was making a cast forward. The scout returned with a hopeful report. “I think we’re safe, till we get into the policies. There’s a road that the auld folk made when ships used to come here. Down there it’s deeper than Clyde at the Broomilaw. Has the auld yin got his wind yet? There’s no time to waste.”

===Chinese===
五分钟后，迪克森发现自己正躲在一块大石头背风处直喘气，杜加尔则到前头兜了一圈侦察。斥候回来时带回一份叫人放心的报告。“我看咱们是安全的，直到进园地之前都安全。底下有一条路，是老辈人修的，那时候船还常到这儿来。那底下的水，比克莱德河布鲁米洛的水还深。老家伙喘匀气了没有？可没工夫磨蹭了。”

===Original===
Up that broken hillside they crawled, well in the cover of the tumbled stones, till they reached a low wall which was the boundary of the garden. The House was now behind them on their right rear, and as they topped the crest they had a glimpse of an ancient dovecot and the ruins of the old Huntingtower on the short thymy turf which ran seaward to the cliffs. Dougal led them along a sunk fence which divided the downs from the lawns behind the house, and, avoiding the stables, brought them by devious ways to a thicket of rhododendrons and broom. On all fours they travelled the length of the place, and came to the edge where some forgotten gardeners had once tended a herbaceous border. The border was now rank and wild, and, lying flat under the shade of an azalea, and peering through the young spears of iris, Dickson and Heritage regarded the northwestern façade of the house.

===Chinese===
他们顺着那片破碎的山坡往上爬，一路藏身于乱石之间，直爬到花园边界的一道矮墙跟前。大宅此时已在他们身后的右后方，登上网脊线时，他们瞥见一座古老公舍和亨廷塔旧堡的残迹，立在长满百里香的低草皮上，草皮一路向海边的峭壁铺去。杜加尔领他们沿一道隐垣走——隐垣隔开草坡与宅后的草坪——又避开马厩，七拐八绕地把他们带到一片山杜鹃和金雀花的树丛里。三人四肢着地，把那片地方从头爬到尾，爬到边缘上——那儿有几位早被遗忘的园丁昔日侍弄过的一方宿根花境。花境如今疯长荒芜，迪克森和赫里蒂奇平躺在一丛映山红的荫下，从鸢尾新抽的剑叶间窥望大宅西北面的立面。

===Original===
The ground before them had been a sunken garden, from which a steep wall, once covered with creepers and rock plants, rose to a long verandah, which was pillared and open on that side; but at each end built up halfway and glazed for the rest. There was a glass roof, and inside untended shrubs sprawled in broken plaster vases.

===Chinese===
他们面前的地界原是一座下沉式花园，一道陡墙从园中拔起——墙上古时爬满藤蔓与岩生花草——通到一道长长的游廊。游廊这一面立着柱子，敞亮通透；两端却砌起半墙，上半装了玻璃。顶上是玻璃棚顶，廊内无人打理的灌木东倒西歪，从破裂的石膏花盆里披散出来。

===Original===
“Ye must bide here,” said Dougal, “and no cheep above your breath. Afore we dare to try that wall, I must ken where Lean and Spittal and Dobson are. I’m off to spy the policies.” He glided out of sight behind a clump of pampas grass.

===Chinese===
“你们得待在这儿，”杜加尔说，“连大气儿也不许出。在敢去试那面墙之前，我必须摸清莱恩、斯皮特尔和多布森都身在何处。我到园地里探一探。”他一闪身，隐没在一丛蒲苇后面不见了。

===Original===
For hours, so it seemed, Dickson was left to his own unpleasant reflections. His body, prone on the moist earth, was fairly comfortable, but his mind was ill at ease. The scramble up the hillside had convinced him that he was growing old, and there was no rebound in his soul to counter the conviction. He felt listless, spiritless﻿—an apathy with fright trembling somewhere at the back of it. He regarded the verandah wall with foreboding. How on earth could he climb that? And if he did there would be his exposed hinder-parts inviting a shot from some malevolent gentleman among the trees. He reflected that he would give a large sum of money to be out of this preposterous adventure.

===Chinese===
此后的几个钟头——感觉上是几个钟头——迪克森被撇下独对自个儿那些不痛快的念头。身子匍匐在湿泥地上，倒还算舒服，心里却安生不下来。那一通爬坡叫他确信自己上了年纪，而他的灵魂里又没有一点弹力来反驳这个确信。他只觉无精打采，委靡不振——一种麻木，其后某个角落里颤着恐惧。他望着那道游廊的墙，心里直发预感：这墙他到底怎么爬得上去？就算爬上去了，他那亮在外头的后半身，岂不正邀请树林里哪位心怀歹意的爷台赏他一枪。他寻思，只要能脱出这场荒唐透顶的冒险，出一大笔钱他也情愿。

===Original===
Heritage’s hand was stretched towards him, containing two of Mrs. Morran’s jellied scones, of which the Poet had been wise enough to bring a supply in his pocket. The food cheered him, for he was growing very hungry, and he began to take an interest in the scene before him instead of his own thoughts. He observed every detail of the verandah. There was a door at one end, he noted, giving on a path which wound down to the sunk garden. As he looked he heard a sound of steps and saw a man ascending this path.

===Chinese===
赫里蒂奇的一只手朝他伸过来，手里托着两块莫兰太太做的抹了果子冻的烤饼——诗人够精明，兜里备下了这么一份。吃食叫他精神一振，因为他早已饥肠辘辘；他开始把心思从自己身上挪开，放到眼前的光景上。他把游廊的每一处细部都看了个遍。他注意到，游廊一头有扇门，门外一条小径蜿蜒通到下沉的花园。正看着，他听见脚步声，见一个男人正沿这条小径走上来。

===Original===
It was the lame man whom Dougal had called Spittal, the dweller in the South Lodge. Seen at closer quarters he was an odd-looking being, lean as a heron, wry-necked, but amazingly quick on his feet. Had not Mrs. Morran said that he hobbled as fast as other folk ran? He kept his eyes on the ground and seemed to be talking to himself as he went, but he was alert enough, for the dropping of a twig from a dying magnolia transferred him in an instant into a figure of active vigilance. No risks could be run with that watcher. He took a key from his pocket, opened the garden door and entered the verandah. For a moment his shuffle sounded on its tiled floor, and then he entered the door admitting from the verandah to the House. It was clearly unlocked for there came no sound of a turning key.
