===Chinese===
来的正是杜加尔称作斯皮特尔的那个瘸子，住在南门房的那位。近处看去，这是个模样古怪的人物，瘦得像只鹭鸶，脖子歪着，脚下却快得惊人。莫兰太太不是说过么——他一瘸一拐，走得比别人跑得还快？他一路眼睛盯着地面，像在自言自语，警觉却半点不缺——那株半死的木兰落下一根枯枝，刹那间他就变成了一副如临大敌的身形。对这么个望风的，一点风险也冒不得。他从口袋里掏出钥匙，打开花园门，进了游廊。他的趿拉声在廊子的瓷砖地上响了一阵，随后他推开那扇从游廊通进宅子的门进去了。那门分明没上锁，因为没听见转钥匙的声音。

===Original===
Dickson had finished the last crumbs of his scones before the man emerged again. He seemed to be in a greater hurry than ever, as he locked the garden door behind him and hobbled along the west front of the House till he was lost to sight. After that the time passed slowly. A pair of yellow wagtails arrived and played at hide-and-seek among the stuccoed pillars. The little dry scratch of their claws was heard clearly in the still air. Dickson had almost fallen asleep when a smothered exclamation from Heritage woke him to attention. A girl had appeared in the verandah.

===Chinese===
迪克森把烤饼的最后一星碎渣吃完，那人才重新露面。他好像比先前更匆忙了，锁上身后的花园门，一瘸一拐地沿着大宅的西面前墙走，直到走得没了影。此后时间过得很慢。一对黄鹡鸰飞来，在灰泥粉的柱子间玩起了捉迷藏，静止的空气里，连它们爪子干干的搔抓声都听得清清楚楚。迪克森几乎睡着了，赫里蒂奇一声压住的低呼把他惊醒过来。一个姑娘出现在游廊上。

===Original===
Above the parapet he saw only her body from the waist up. She seemed to be clad in bright colours, for something red was round her shoulders and her hair was bound with an orange scarf. She was tall﻿—that he could tell, tall and slim and very young. Her face was turned seaward, and she stood for a little scanning the broad channel, shading her eyes as if to search for something on the extreme horizon. The air was very quiet and he thought that he could hear her sigh. Then she turned and reentered the House, while Heritage by his side began to curse under his breath with a shocking fervour.

===Chinese===
矮墙之上，他只望得见她腰部以上的身量。她像穿着鲜亮的衣裳，肩头围着一点红色，头发上束着一条橙色的头巾。她个子高——这他看得出，高挑、纤瘦，而且非常年轻。她的脸朝着海，伫立了片刻，扫视宽阔的海峡水面，手搭在眼上遮光，仿佛要在极远的天际线上寻什么东西。空气静谧极了，他觉得自己听得见她的叹息。随后她转身回了宅子，他身边的赫里蒂奇开始低声咒骂起来，那股狠劲骇人。

===Original===
One of Dickson’s troubles had been that he did not really believe Dougal’s story, and the sight of the girl removed one doubt. That bright exotic thing did not belong to the Cruives or to Scotland at all, and that she should be in the House removed the place from the conventional dwelling to which the laws against burglary applied.

===Chinese===
迪克森的一桩苦恼，原是他并不真信杜加尔的话，而这姑娘一露面，便去掉了一层疑心。那样一个鲜亮的异域人物，根本不属于克鲁夫斯，也不属于苏格兰；而她既在这宅中，此地便算不得那种擅入即犯窃盗罪的寻常民宅了。

===Original===
There was a rustle among the rhododendrons and the fiery face of Dougal appeared. He lay between the other two, his chin on his hands, and grunted out his report.

===Chinese===
杜鹃丛里一阵窸窣，杜加尔那张火红的脸探了出来。他趴在两人中间，双手托着下巴，咕哝着报告起来。

===Original===
“After they had their dinner Dobson and Lean yokit a horse and went off to Auchenlochan. I seen them pass the Garple brig, so that’s two accounted for. Has Spittal been round here?”

===Chinese===
“他们吃过午饭，多布森和莱恩套了匹马，往奥琴洛坎去了。我亲眼瞧见他们过了加普尔桥，这就销掉两个。斯皮特尔到这一带转悠过没有？”

===Original===
“Half an hour ago,” said Heritage, consulting a wrist watch.

===Chinese===
“半个钟头前。”赫里蒂奇看了看腕表说。

===Original===
“It was him that keepit me waitin’ so long. But he’s safe enough now, for five minutes syne he was splittin’ firewood at the back door o’ his hoose.﻿ ﻿… I’ve found a ladder, an auld yin in ahint yon lot o’ bushes. It’ll help wi’ the wall. There! I’ve gotten my breath again and we can start.”

===Chinese===
“就是他害我等了这么半天。不过这会儿他保险得很，五分钟前他还在他家后门口劈柴呢。……我找到一架梯子，一架老古董，就搁在那片灌木丛后头。爬那面墙它顶用。好了！我气也喘匀了，可以动身了。”

===Original===
The ladder was fetched by Heritage and proved to be ancient and wanting many rungs, but sufficient in length. The three stood silent for a moment, listening like stags, and then ran across the intervening lawn to the foot of the verandah wall. Dougal went up first, then Heritage, and lastly Dickson, stiff and giddy from his long lie under the bushes. Below the parapet the verandah floor was heaped with old garden litter, rotten matting, dead or derelict bulbs, fibre, withies and strawberry nets. It was Dougal’s intention to pull up the ladder and hide it among the rubbish against the hour of departure. But Dickson had barely put his foot on the parapet when there was a sound of steps within the House approaching the verandah door.

===Chinese===
梯子由赫里蒂奇搬了来，一看果然老朽，缺了不少梯级，长度倒还够。三人静立了片刻，像三头竖起耳朵的公鹿，随即跑过中间那片草坪，来到游廊墙根。杜加尔头一个上去，跟着是赫里蒂奇，最后是迪克森——他在灌木底下趴得太久，浑身发僵，头昏眼花。矮墙里面的游廊地上，堆满了陈年的园圃破烂：朽烂的草席、死掉或废弃的球茎、棕丝、柳条和草莓罩网。杜加尔的原意是把梯子抽上来藏进破烂堆里，好等撤退的时辰用。可迪克森的脚才踏上矮墙，宅子里就响起脚步声，直朝游廊的门过来。

===Original===
The ladder was left alone. Dougal’s hand brought Dickson summarily to the floor, where he was fairly well concealed by a mess of matting. Unfortunately his head was in the vicinity of some upturned pot-plants, so that a cactus ticked his brow and a spike of aloe supported painfully the back of his neck. Heritage was prone behind two old water-butts, and Dougal was in a hamper which had once contained seed potatoes. The house door had panels of opaque glass, so the newcomer could not see the doings of the three till it was opened, and by that time all were in cover.

===Chinese===
梯子是顾不上了。杜加尔一只手把迪克森干脆利落地按倒在地，一堆乱草席倒把他遮得严实。不巧的是，他的脑袋正凑近几盆翻倒的盆花，一盆仙人掌扎着他的额头，一枝芦荟的尖刺疼楚楚地顶着他的后颈。赫里蒂奇趴在两只旧雨水桶后面，杜加尔则蹲进了一只从前装种薯的大提篮。宅子的门上镶着不透明的玻璃格，所以来人不到开门的一刻瞧不见三人的勾当，而到那时，三个都已各就各掩体了。

===Original===
The man﻿—it was Spittal﻿—walked rapidly along the verandah and out of the garden door. He was talking to himself again, and Dickson, who had a glimpse of his face, thought he looked both evil and furious. Then came some anxious moments, for had the man glanced back when he was once outside, he must have seen the telltale ladder. But he seemed immersed in his own reflections, for he hobbled steadily along the house front till he was lost to sight.

===Chinese===
那人——正是斯皮特尔——快步走过游廊，出了花园门。他又在自言自语，迪克森瞥见他的脸，觉得那面孔又凶恶又狂怒。接着是几阵叫人心焦的工夫，因为那人一旦出了门，只要回头看上一眼，就必定瞧见那架不打自招的梯子。可他像是整个沉在自己的心事里，一瘸一拐径直沿着宅子前墙走，直到没了影。

===Original===
“That’ll be the end o’ them the night,” said Dougal, as he helped Heritage to pull up the ladder and stow it away. “We’ve got the place to oursels, now. Forward, men, forward.” He tried the handle of the house door and led the way in.

===Chinese===
“他们今儿晚上就到此为止啦。”杜加尔说着，帮赫里蒂奇把梯子抽上来藏好。“这会儿这地场归咱们了。前进，弟兄们，前进。”他拧了拧宅门的把手，领头走了进去。

===Original===
A narrow paved passage took them into what had once been the garden room, where the lady of the house had arranged her flowers, and the tennis racquets and croquet mallets had been kept. It was very dusty and on the cobwebbed walls still hung a few soiled garden overalls. A door beyond opened into a huge murky hall, murky, for the windows were shuttered, and the only light came through things like portholes far up in the wall. Dougal, who seemed to know his way about, halted them. “Stop here till I scout a bit. The women bide in a wee room through that muckle door.” Bare feet stole across the oak flooring, there was the sound of a door swinging on its hinges, and then silence and darkness. Dickson put out a hand for companionship and clutched Heritage’s; to his surprise it was cold and all a-tremble. They listened for voices, and thought they could detect a faraway sob.

===Chinese===
一条铺砖的窄过道把他们引进一间从前的园艺间——宅子的女主人从前在这儿插弄花草，网球拍和槌球木槌也从前的搁在这儿。屋里积尘厚重，结满蛛网的墙上还挂着几件弄脏的园艺罩衣。里头一扇门开进去，是一间幽暗的大厅；说它幽暗，是因为窗户全上了窗板，仅有的光亮来自墙上高处几个像舷窗似的窗洞。杜加尔看样子对路道烂熟，叫他们止步。“在这儿候着，我探一探。两个女人就住在那扇大门里头的小间屋。”一双赤脚悄悄溜过橡木地板，一扇门在合页上吱呀一声，随后便只剩寂静与黑暗。迪克森伸手找依靠，一把攥住了赫里蒂奇的手——叫他吃惊的是，那只手冰凉，抖个不住。两人竖耳听人声，恍惚听得远远一声啜泣。

===Original===
It was some minutes before Dougal returned. “A bonny kettle o’ fish,” he whispered. “They’re both greetin’. We’re just in time. Come on, the pair o’ ye.”

===Chinese===
过了几分钟，杜加尔才回来。“好一锅烂粥。”他悄声道，“两个人都在哭。咱们来得正是时候。来吧，你们俩。”

===Original===
Through a green baize door they entered a passage which led to the kitchen regions, and turned in at the first door on their right. From its situation Dickson calculated that the room lay on the seaward side of the House next to the verandah. The light was bad, for the two windows were partially shuttered, but it had plainly been a smoking-room, for there were pipe-racks by the hearth, and on the walls a number of old school and college photographs, a couple of oars with emblazoned names, and a variety of stags’ and roebucks’ heads. There was no fire in the grate, but a small oil-stove burned inside the fender. In a stiff-backed chair sat an elderly woman, who seemed to feel the cold, for she was muffled to the neck in a fur coat. Beside her, so that the late afternoon light caught her face and head, stood a girl.

===Chinese===
穿过一扇绿呢包的门，他们进了通往厨房那一片的过道，在右手第一扇门里拐了进去。从方位上，迪克森推算这屋子在大宅临海的一面，紧挨着游廊。屋里光线昏暗，两扇窗都半掩着窗板，但它分明原是间吸烟室：壁炉边立着烟斗架，墙上挂着许多公学和老大学的旧照片、两支刻着饰章名号的赛艇桨，还有各式雄鹿和狍子的头颅标本。炉膛里没有火，炉围里燃着一只小油炉。一把直背椅上坐着位上了年纪的妇人，像是怕冷，一件皮大衣直裹到脖颈。她身旁立着一位姑娘，站的位置恰好让下午偏晚的光照亮她的脸和头。

===Original===
Dickson’s first impression was of a tall child. The pose, startled and wild and yet curiously stiff and self-conscious, was that of a child striving to remember a forgotten lesson. One hand clutched a handkerchief, the other was closing and unclosing on a knob of the chair back. She was staring at Dougal, who stood like a gnome in the centre of the floor. “Here’s the gentlemen I was tellin’ ye about,” was his introduction, but her eyes did not move.

===Chinese===
迪克森的第一印象，是一个高个子的孩子。那姿态又惊又野，却又出奇地僵直、出奇地怯场，活像个竭力回想忘掉的功课的孩子。一只手攥着手帕，另一只手在椅背的圆头上握了又松、松了又握。她直勾勾盯着杜加尔，杜加尔像个小地精似的立在屋子中央。“就是我跟你们说的那两位先生。”他这样引荐，可她的眼睛动也没动。

===Original===
Then Heritage stepped forward. “We have met before, Mademoiselle,” he said. “Do you remember Easter in 1918﻿—in the house in the Trinitá dei Monte?”

===Chinese===
随后赫里蒂奇上前一步。“我们从前见过，小姐，”他说，“您还记得 1918 年的复活节吗——在特里尼塔·代伊·蒙蒂（Trinitá dei Monte）山上的那座宅子里？”

===Original===
The girl looked at him.

===Chinese===
姑娘望着他。

===Original===
“I do not remember,” she said slowly.

===Chinese===
“我不记得了。”她缓缓地说。

===Original===
“But I was the English officer who had the apartments on the floor below you. I saw you every morning. You spoke to me sometimes.”

===Chinese===
“可我就是住在您楼下那层公寓的英国军官啊。我每天早晨都见到您。您有时还同我说话。”

===Original===
“You are a soldier?” she asked, with a new note in her voice.

===Chinese===
“您是军人？”她问，嗓音里透出一个新的调子。

===Original===
“I was then﻿—till the war finished.”

===Chinese===
“那时是——直到大战结束。”

===Original===
“And now? Why have you come here?”

===Chinese===
“那现在呢？您为什么到这儿来？”

===Original===
“To offer you help if you need it. If not, to ask your pardon and go away.”

===Chinese===
“如果您需要，向您提供帮助。若不需要，就请您宽恕，我们即刻离开。”

===Original===
The shrouded figure in the chair burst suddenly into rapid hysterical talk in some foreign tongue which Dickson suspected of being French. Heritage replied in the same language, and the girl joined in with sharp questions. Then the Poet turned to Dickson.

===Chinese===
椅子上那个裹得严严的身影突然开口，用一种外国话急速地、歇斯底里地说起来——迪克森猜那是法语。赫里蒂奇用同一种话应答，姑娘也插进来尖声发问。随后诗人转向迪克森。

===Original===
“This is my friend. If you will trust us we will do our best to save you.”

===Chinese===
“这是我的朋友。您若肯信任我们，我们必竭尽所能救您。”

===Original===
The eyes rested on Dickson’s face, and he realised that he was in the presence of something the like of which he had never met in his life before. It was a loveliness greater than he had imagined was permitted by the Almighty to His creatures. The little face was more square than oval, with a low broad brow and proud exquisite eyebrows. The eyes were of a colour which he could never decide on; afterwards he used to allege obscurely that they were the colour of everything in Spring. There was a delicate pallor in the cheeks, and the face bore signs of suffering and care, possibly even of hunger; but for all that there was youth there, eternal and triumphant! Not youth such as he had known it, but youth with all history behind it, youth with centuries of command in its blood and the world’s treasures of beauty and pride in its ancestry. Strange, he thought, that a thing so fine should be so masterful. He felt abashed in every inch of him.

===Chinese===
那双眼睛落到迪克森脸上，他意识到，自己正面对着一样毕生从未遇过的东西。那是一种他从不曾想象全能的上帝会容许授予受造物的美。那张小脸方正多于鹅蛋，额头低而开阔，眉毛高傲而精致。那双眼睛的颜色，他怎么也定不下来；事后他总爱含含糊糊地说，那是春天万物共有的颜色。双颊上有一层纤秀的苍白，脸上带着受苦与操劳的痕迹，说不定还有饥饿的痕迹；可饶是这样，那上面依然有青春，永恒而得胜的青春！不是他素来所识的那种青春，而是身后拖着全部历史的青春，血液里沉着多少个世纪的号令、门第里聚着人间宝藏之美丽与骄傲的青春。真奇怪，他想，如此精粹的一件造物，竟能如此发号施令。他浑身上下，无一处不自惭形秽。

===Original===
As the eyes rested on him their sorrowfulness seemed to be shot with humour. A ghost of a smile lurked there, to which Dickson promptly responded. He grinned and bowed.

===Chinese===
那双眼睛望着他时，眼底的哀伤里仿佛闪过一丝谐谑。一痕笑意潜藏在那里，迪克森立刻会意，咧嘴一笑，躬身行礼。

===Original===
“Very pleased to meet you, Mem. I’m Mr. McCunn from Glasgow.”

===Chinese===
“见到您非常高兴，夫人。俺是格拉斯哥来的麦坎先生。”

===Original===
“You don’t even know my name,” she said.

===Chinese===
“你们连我的名字都还不知道呢。”她说。

===Original===
“We don’t,” said Heritage.

===Chinese===
“确实不知道。”赫里蒂奇说。

===Original===
“They call me Saskia. This,” nodding to the chair, “is my cousin Eugénie.﻿ ﻿… We are in very great trouble. But why should I tell you? I do not know you. You cannot help me.”

===Chinese===
“人们叫我萨斯基亚（Saskia）。这位，”她朝椅子那边点点头，“是我的表姐欧仁妮（Eugénie）。……我们身陷极大的危难。可我为什么要告诉你们？我不认识你们。你们帮不了我。”

===Original===
“We can try,” said Heritage. “Part of your trouble we know already through that boy. You are imprisoned in this place by scoundrels. We are here to help you to get out. We want to ask no questions﻿—only to do what you bid us.”

===Chinese===
“我们可以试试。”赫里蒂奇说，“您的难处，我们已经通过那孩子知道了一部分。您被一伙恶棍囚禁在此地。我们是来帮您脱身的。我们不想多问一句——只想听凭差遣。”

===Original===
“You are not strong enough,” she said sadly. “A young man﻿—an old man﻿—and a little boy. There are many against us, and any moment there may be more.”

===Chinese===
“你们的力量不够。”她哀伤地说，“一个年轻人——一个上了年纪的人——还有一个小男孩。与我们作对的人很多，而且随时可能更多。”

===Original===
It was Dougal’s turn to break in. “There’s Lean and Spittal and Dobson and four tinklers in the Dean﻿—that’s seven; but there’s us three and five more Gorbals Die-Hards﻿—that’s eight.”

===Chinese===
这回轮到杜加尔插话了。“有莱恩、斯皮特尔、多布森，外加峡谷里四个补锅匠——一共七个；可我们有咱们仨，再添五个戈尔巴尔死硬帮——一共八个。”

===Original===
There was something in the boy’s truculent courage that cheered her.

===Chinese===
这男孩凶巴巴的勇气里有种东西，叫她精神一振。

===Original===
“I wonder,” she said, and her eyes fell on each in turn.

===Chinese===
“那可难说。”她说着，目光依次落在每个人身上。

===Original===
Dickson felt impelled to intervene.

===Chinese===
迪克森觉得不能不插嘴了。

===Original===
“I think this is a perfectly simple business. Here’s a lady shut up in this house against her will by a wheen blagyirds. This is a free country and the law doesn’t permit that. My advice is for one of us to inform the police at Auchenlochan and get Dobson and his friends took up and the lady set free to do what she likes. That is, if these folks are really molesting her, which is not yet quite clear to my mind.”

===Chinese===
“俺看这事再简单不过。有位太太让一帮小泼皮违背她的意愿关在这宅子里。这是个自由的国家，法律不许这样干。俺的主意是，咱们当中去一个人报知奥琴洛坎的警察，把多布森和他那伙人抓起来，放太太自由，爱做什么做什么。当然啦，前提是这伙人当真在欺负她——这一层，俺心里还不十分清楚。”

===Original===
“Alas! It is not so simple as that,” she said. “I dare not invoke your English law, for perhaps in the eyes of that law I am a thief.”

===Chinese===
“唉！事情没有这么简单。”她说，“我不敢求告你们的英格兰法律，因为在那法律眼里，我也许是个窃贼。”

===Original===
“Deary me, that’s a bad business,” said the startled Dickson.

===Chinese===
“了不得，那可是桩糟心事。”迪克森吃了一惊，说。

===Original===
The two women talked together in some strange tongue, and the elder appeared to be pleading and the younger objecting. Then Saskia seemed to come to a decision.

===Chinese===
两个女人用一种古怪的语言交谈起来，年长的像在恳求，年轻的像在反对。随后萨斯基亚像是拿定了主意。

===Original===
“I will tell you all,” and she looked straight at Heritage. “I do not think you would be cruel or false, for you have honourable faces.﻿ ﻿… Listen, then. I am a Russian and for two years have been an exile. I will not speak of my house, for it is no more, or how I escaped, for it is the common tale of all of us. I have seen things more terrible than any dream and yet lived, but I have paid a price for such experience. First I went to Italy where there were friends, and I wished only to have peace among kindly people. About poverty I do not care, for, to us, who have lost all the great things, the want of bread is a little matter. But peace was forbidden me, for I learned that we Russians had to win back our fatherland again and that the weakest must work in that cause. So I was set my task and it was very hard.﻿ ﻿… There were jewels which once belonged to my Emperor﻿—they had been stolen by the brigands and must be recovered. There were others still hidden in Russia which must be brought to a safe place. In that work I was ordered to share.”

===Chinese===
“我愿意把一切告诉你们。”她直望着赫里蒂奇。“我不认为你们会残忍或者虚伪，因为你们生着正派的脸。……那就听着吧。我是俄国人，流亡在外已经两年了。我的家门我不愿多谈，因为它已经不存在了；我是怎样逃出来的，我也不愿多谈，因为那是我们所有人共同的遭遇。我见过比任何噩梦都可怕的事情，却活了下来，但这份阅历我是付了代价的。我先到了意大利，那里有朋友，我只盼在好心的人们中间过一份安宁日子。贫穷我并不在意，因为对我们这些失去了一切大事的人来说，缺一口面包是小事。可是安宁不容我享有，因为我懂得，我们俄国人必须重新赢回我们的祖国，连最弱小的人也得为这桩事业出力。于是我领到了我的任务，而这任务非常艰巨。……有一些珠宝，原是我皇上的东西——它们被匪徒抢走了，必须追回来。另有一些还藏在俄国，必须送到安全的地方去。在这件工作里，分派给了我一份。”

===Original===
She spoke in almost perfect English, with a certain foreign precision. Suddenly she changed to French, and talked rapidly to Heritage.

===Chinese===
她说的几乎是无可挑剔的英语，带着一种外国式的精确。忽然她改说法语，向赫里蒂奇飞快地说起来。

===Original===
“She has told me about her family,” he said, turning to Dickson. “It is among the greatest in Russia, the very greatest after the throne.” Dickson could only stare.

===Chinese===
“她把她的家世讲给我听了。”他转向迪克森说，“那是俄国数一数二的门第，仅次于皇位的最显赫门第。”迪克森只能干瞪眼。

===Original===
“Our enemies soon discovered me,” she went on. “Oh, but they are very clever, these enemies, and they have all the criminals of the world to aid them. Here you do not understand what they are. You good people in England think they are well-meaning dreamers who are forced into violence by the persecution of Western Europe. But you are wrong. Some honest fools there are among them, but the power﻿—the true power﻿—lies with madmen and degenerates, and they have for allies the special devil that dwells in each country. That is why they cast their net as wide as mankind.”

===Chinese===
“我们的敌人很快发现了我，”她接着说，“唉，这些敌人非常精明，而且全世界罪犯都给他们帮凶。在你们这里，你们不明白他们是什么。你们英格兰的好人们以为，他们是一片好心的空想家，是被西欧的迫害逼上暴力的。可你们错了。他们中间固然有几个诚实的傻子，但权力——真正的权力——握在疯子和堕落者手里，而且他们还结有同盟：每一国里盘踞的那个特有的魔鬼。正因如此，他们才把网撒得同人类一样宽。”

===Original===
She shivered, and for a second her face wore a look which Dickson never forgot, the look of one who has looked over the edge of life into the outer dark.

===Chinese===
她打了个寒噤。有一瞬间，她脸上掠过一种迪克森终生难忘的神情——一个从生命的边缘望进外层黑暗里去的人的神情。

===Original===
“There were certain jewels of great price which were about to be turned into guns and armies for our enemies. These our people recovered and the charge of them was laid on me. Who would suspect, they said, a foolish girl? But our enemies were very clever, and soon the hunt was cried against me. They tried to rob me of them, but they failed, for I too had become clever. Then they asked the help of the law﻿—first in Italy and then in France. Oh, it was subtly done. Respectable bourgeois, who hated the Bolsheviki but had bought long ago the bonds of my country, desired to be repaid their debts out of the property of the Russian Crown which might be found in the West. But behind them were the Jews, and behind the Jews our unsleeping enemies. Once I was enmeshed in the law I would be safe for them, and presently they would find the hiding-place of the treasure, and while the bourgeois were clamouring in the courts, it would be safe in their pockets. So I fled. For months I have been fleeing and hiding. They have tried to kidnap me many times, and once they have tried to kill me, but I, too, have become very clever﻿—oh, very clever. And I have learned not to fear.”

===Chinese===
“有几件价值连城的珠宝，眼看就要变成敌人的枪炮和军队。我们的人把它们夺了回来，看管的责任落到了我身上。谁会怀疑一个傻姑娘呢——他们这么说。可我们的敌人十分精明，很快对我就喊起了追猎。他们想把我抢个精光，却没能得手，因为我也学精了。于是他们求助于法律——先在意大利，后在法兰西。唉，那手段做得真细。体面的资产阶级憎恨布尔什维克，却又在早年间买下过我国发行的债券，一心指望从西方可能发现的俄罗斯皇室财产里，把他们的债讨回来。可他们背后站着犹太人，犹太人背后，是我们那些从不合眼的敌人。我一旦陷进法律的罗网，对他们便万无一失；用不了多久，他们就会找到宝藏的藏处，等到资产阶级还在法庭里吵嚷，宝物早已安然进了他们的口袋。于是我逃了。几个月来，我一直逃着、躲着。他们试过许多次绑架我，还有一次想杀死我，可我也变得非常精明——哦，非常精明。而且我学会了不去害怕。”

===Original===
This simple recital affected Dickson’s honest soul with the liveliest indignation. “Sich doings!” he exclaimed, and he could not forbear from whispering to Heritage an extract from that gentleman’s conversation the first night at Kirkmichael. “We needn’t imitate all their methods, but they’ve got hold of the right end of the stick. They seek truth and reality.” The reply from the Poet was an angry shrug.

===Chinese===
这番平平常常的讲述，把迪克森老实的灵魂激起了最大的义愤。“竟有这样的事！”他叫出声来，还忍不住凑到赫里蒂奇耳边，低声引了一句这位先生头一晚在柯克迈克尔说的话：“咱们不必照搬他们的种种手段，可他们抓住了棍子的正确一端。他们寻求真理与现实。”诗人的回答是一下恼怒的耸肩。

===Original===
“Why and how did you come here?” he asked.

===Chinese===
“您为什么会到这儿来？怎么来的？”他问。

===Original===
“I always meant to come to England, for I thought it the sanest place in a mad world. Also it is a good country to hide in, for it is apart from Europe, and your police, as I thought, do not permit evil men to be their own law. But especially I had a friend, a Scottish gentleman, whom I knew in the days when we Russians were still a nation. I saw him again in Italy, and since he was kind and brave I told him some part of my troubles. He was called Quentin Kennedy, and now he is dead. He told me that in Scotland he had a lonely château where I could hide secretly and safely, and against the day when I might be hard-pressed he gave me a letter to his steward, bidding him welcome me as a guest when I made application. At that time I did not think I would need such sanctuary, but a month ago the need became urgent, for the hunt in France was very close on me. So I sent a message to the steward as Captain Kennedy told me.”

===Chinese===
“我一向打算来英格兰，因为我认为它是这个疯狂世界里最神志清明的地方。它也是个藏身的好国度，因为它与欧陆隔开，而且据我想，你们的警察不容恶人自立为王法。尤其是因为，我有一位朋友，一位苏格兰绅士，在我们俄国人还算一个国家的年月里，我就认识他。在意大利我又见到了他，他既善良又勇敢，我便把自己的难处对他说了一部分。他名叫昆廷·肯尼迪，如今已经去世了。他告诉我，他在苏格兰有一座僻静的庄园宅邸，我可以悄悄地、安全地躲在那里；又防着有朝一日我被逼到绝境，他给了我 一封写给他管家的信，吩咐管家，我一经提出请求，便将我当客人接纳。那时候我还想不着会用上这样的庇护所，可一个月前，这需要变得十万火急，因为在法兰西，追猎已经逼到我的脚跟。于是我便照肯尼迪上尉所嘱咐的，给那位管家捎去了消息。”

===Original===
“What is his name?” Heritage asked.

===Chinese===
“他叫什么名字？”赫里蒂奇问。

===Original===
She spelt it, “Monsieur Loudon﻿—L-O-U-D-O-N in the town of Auchenlochan.”

===Chinese===
她一个字母一个字母地拼了出来：“劳登先生——L-O-U-D-O-N，在奥琴洛坎镇上。”

===Original===
“The factor,” said Dickson. “And what then?”

===Chinese===
“就是那位代管产业的。”迪克森说，“后来呢？”

===Original===
“Some spy must have found me out. I had a letter from this Loudon bidding me come to Auchenlochan. There I found no steward to receive me, but another letter saying that that night a carriage would be in waiting to bring me here. It was midnight when we arrived, and we were brought in by strange ways to this house, with no light but a single candle. Here we were welcomed indeed, but by an enemy.”

===Chinese===
“一定是哪个密探把我查了出来。我收到这位劳登的信，唤我去奥琴洛坎。到了那里，却没有管家来接我，只有另一封信，说当夜会有一辆马车候着，把我送到这里。我们到达时已是半夜，人家领着我们绕着古怪的路径来到这座宅子，照明只有一支蜡烛。在这里我们确实受到了欢迎——可是出自敌人之口。”

===Original===
“Which?” asked Heritage. “Dobson or Lean or Spittal?”

===Chinese===
“是哪一个？”赫里蒂奇问，“多布森，还是莱恩，还是斯皮特尔？”

===Original===
“Dobson I do not know. Léon was there. He is no Russian, but a Belgian who was a valet in my father’s service till he joined the Bolsheviki. Next day the Lett Spidel came, and I knew that I was in very truth entrapped. For of all our enemies he is, save one, the most subtle and unwearied.”

===Chinese===
“多布森我不认识。当时在场的是莱昂（Léon）。他不是俄国人，是个比利时人，从前在我父亲手下当贴身仆人，后来投了布尔什维克。第二天，列特人斯皮德尔（Spidel）来了，我便知道我实实在在是落进了圈套。因为在我们所有的敌人里，除一人之外，就数他最阴险、最不死心。”

===Original===
Her voice had trailed off into flat weariness. Again Dickson was reminded of a child, for her arms hung limp by her side; and her slim figure in its odd clothes was curiously like that of a boy in a school blazer. Another resemblance perplexed him. She had a hint of Janet﻿—about the mouth﻿—Janet, that solemn little girl those twenty years in her grave.

===Chinese===
她的声音渐渐低下去，疲得平平淡淡。迪克森又一次想起一个孩子：她两臂松垂在身侧；那纤细的身量穿着一身古怪的衣裳，竟出奇地像一个穿公学运动夹克的男孩。还有一层相像叫他困惑。她眉眼间有点珍妮特的影子——在嘴巴上——珍妮特，那个严肃的小姑娘，躺进坟墓已二十个年头了。

===Original===
Heritage was wrinkling his brows. “I don’t think I quite understand. The jewels? You have them with you?”

===Chinese===
赫里蒂奇皱起了眉头。“我想我还没完全明白。那些珠宝？您把它们带在身边？”

===Original===
She nodded.

===Chinese===
她点了点头。

===Original===
“These men wanted to rob you. Why didn’t they do it between here and Auchenlochan? You had no chance to hide them on the journey. Why did they let you come here where you were in a better position to baffle them?”

===Chinese===
“这些人想抢您的珠宝。他们为什么不在奥琴洛坎到此地之间的路上动手？一路之上您没有机会把珠宝藏起来。他们为什么放您到这里来，让您占了一个更容易同他们周旋的位置？”

===Original===
She shook her head. “I cannot explain﻿—except perhaps, that Spidel had not arrived that night, and Léon may have been waiting instructions.”

===Chinese===
她摇了摇头。“我说不上来——兴许是因为，那晚斯皮德尔还没有到，莱昂可能在等候指示吧。”

===Original===
The other still looked dissatisfied. “They are either clumsier villains than I take them to be, or there is something deeper in the business than we understand. These jewels﻿—are they here?”

===Chinese===
对方仍旧一脸不满足的神气。“要么这伙恶棍比我估量的更笨手笨脚，要么这桩事里还有比我们所理解的更深的东西。这些珠宝——就在这儿吗？”

===Original===
His tone was so sharp that she looked startled﻿—almost suspicious. Then she saw that in his face which reassured her. “I have them hidden here. I have grown very skilful in hiding things.”

===Chinese===
他的口气太尖了，她惊得愣住——几乎起了疑心。随后她看见他脸上有点什么，叫她又安下心来。“我把它们藏在这儿了。藏东西的本事，我已经练得很到家了。”

===Original===
“Have they searched for them?”

===Chinese===
“他们搜寻过吗？”

===Original===
“The first day they demanded them of me. I denied all knowledge. Then they ransacked this house﻿—I think they ransack it daily, but I am too clever for them. I am not allowed to go beyond the verandah, and when at first I disobeyed there was always one of them in wait to force me back with a pistol behind my head. Every morning Léon brings us food for the day﻿—good food, but not enough, so that Cousin Eugénie is always hungry, and each day he and Spidel question and threaten me. This afternoon Spidel has told me that their patience is at an end. He has given me till tomorrow at noon to produce the jewels. If not, he says I will die.”

===Chinese===
“头一天，他们向我索要。我一口咬定毫不知情。跟着他们把这座宅子翻了个底朝天——我看他们天天都在翻，可我比他们精。不许我走出游廊一步，起初我不听话，总有他们中的一个埋伏着，用手枪顶着我后脑勺逼我回去。每天早晨，莱昂送来我们一天的吃食——食物是好的，却不够，所以欧仁妮表姐总挨饿。每天，他和斯皮德尔审问我、威胁我。今天下午，斯皮德尔告诉我，他们的耐性到头了。他限我明天正午以前交出珠宝。否则，他说，我就得死。”

===Original===
“Mercy on us!” Dickson exclaimed.

===Chinese===
“老天爷发发慈悲吧！”迪克森失声叫道。

===Original===
“There will be no mercy for us,” she said solemnly. “He and his kind think as little of shedding blood as of spilling water. But I do not think he will kill me. I think I will kill him first, but after that I shall surely die. As for Cousin Eugénie, I do not know.”

===Chinese===
“我们得不到什么慈悲。”她郑重地说，“他和他那一类人，流血流得像泼水一样不当回事。可我不认为他会杀了我。我想我会先杀了他，不过在那之后，我必定也活不成。至于欧仁妮表姐，我不知道。”

===Original===
Her level matter-of-fact tone seemed to Dickson most shocking, for he could not treat it as mere melodrama. It carried a horrid conviction. “We must get you out of this at once,” he declared.

===Chinese===
她那平平实实的语气，在迪克森听来最为骇人，因为他没法把它当作纯粹的情节剧。那话里带着一种叫人心寒的确凿。“俺们得马上把您救出去。”他斩钉截铁地说。

===Original===
“I cannot leave. I will tell you why. When I came to this country I appointed one to meet me here. He is a kinsman who knows England well, for he fought in your army. With him by my side I have no fear. It is altogether needful that I wait for him.”

===Chinese===
“我不能走。我告诉你们原因。我来这个国家时，约了一个人到这里同我会合。他是一位亲戚，对英格兰了如指掌，因为他在你们的军队里打过仗。有他在我身边，我便什么都不怕。我等他，是万万必要的。”

===Original===
“Then there is something more which you haven’t told us?” Heritage asked.

===Chinese===
“那么还有别的事，您没告诉我们？”赫里蒂奇问。

===Original===
Was there the faintest shadow of a blush on her cheek? “There is something more,” she said.

===Chinese===
她脸颊上可曾掠过最淡的一痕红晕？“是还有一件事。”她说。

===Original===
She spoke to Heritage in French and Dickson caught the name “Alexis” and a word which sounded like “prance.” The Poet listened eagerly and nodded. “I have heard of him,” he said.

===Chinese===
她用法语对赫里蒂奇说起话来，迪克森捕捉到“亚历克西斯”（Alexis）这个名字，还听见一个词，听着像英文里的“prance”（欢蹦）。诗人急切地听着，点了点头。“我听说过他。”他说。

===Original===
“But have you not seen him? A tall man with a yellow beard, who bears himself proudly. Being of my mother’s race he has eyes like mine.”

===Chinese===
“可你们没有见过他吗？一个高个子男人，留黄胡子，举止昂然。他属于我母亲的血统，所以眼睛同我的一样。”

===Original===
“That’s the man she was askin’ me about yesterday,” said Dougal, who had squatted on the floor.

===Chinese===
“她昨天打听的就是这个人。”杜加尔说，他早已蹲在了地板上。

===Original===
Heritage shook his head. “We only came here last night. When did you expect Prince﻿—your friend?”

===Chinese===
赫里蒂奇摇了摇头。“我们昨夜才到这里。您原来指望亲王——您的那位朋友——几时到呢？”

===Original===
“I hoped to find him here before me. Oh, it is his not coming that terrifies me. I must wait and hope. But if he does not come in time another may come before him.”

===Chinese===
“我原指望他比我先到这里。唉，正是不见他来，才叫我心惊胆战。我只能等待，只能盼望。可他若不及时赶到，另一个人也许会抢在他前头来。”

===Original===
“The ones already here are not all the enemies that threaten you?”

===Chinese===
“已经在这儿的这几个，还不是威胁您的全部敌人？”

===Original===
“Indeed, no. The worst has still to come, and till I know he is here I do not greatly fear Spidel or Léon. They receive orders and do not give them.”

===Chinese===
“确实不是。最坏的那个还在后头。只要还不知道他已经到了，我就不太怕斯皮德尔和莱昂。他们只会领受命令，不会发布命令。”

===Original===
Heritage ran a perplexed hand through his hair. The sunset which had been flaming for some time in the unshuttered panes was now passing into the dark. The girl lit a lamp after first shuttering the rest of the windows. As she turned it up the odd dusty room and its strange company were revealed more clearly and Dickson saw with a shock how haggard was the beautiful face. A great pity seized him and almost conquered his timidity.

===Chinese===
赫里蒂奇一只手苦恼地插进头发里抿了一把。有一阵子，落日一直在没上板的窗玻璃里燃烧，此刻正沉入黑暗。姑娘先把余下的窗都上了窗板，然后点起一盏灯。灯芯拧亮时，这间古怪的积尘的屋子和屋里那群古怪的人被照得更清楚了，迪克森震了一下才看清，那张美丽的脸枯槁到了什么地步。一阵巨大的怜惜攫住了他，几乎压倒了他的怯懦。

===Original===
“It is very difficult to help you,” Heritage was saying. “You won’t leave this place, and you won’t claim the protection of the law. You are very independent, Mademoiselle, but it can’t go on forever. The man you fear may arrive at any moment. At any moment, too, your treasure may be discovered.”

===Chinese===
“要帮您，实在很难。”赫里蒂奇说，“您不肯离开此地，又不肯求法律保护。您非常独立，小姐，可这撑不了多久。您害怕的那个人随时可能到。您的宝藏也随时可能被发现。”

===Original===
“It is that that weighs on me,” she cried. “The jewels! They are my solemn trust, but they burden me terribly. If I were only rid of them and knew them to be safe I should face the rest with a braver mind.”

===Chinese===
“压在我心上的正是这个。”她喊道，“这些珠宝！它们是我庄严的重托，可它们把我压得好苦。只要能卸掉它们，又知道它们安然无恙，余下的事我便能拿出更勇敢的心去面对。”

===Original===
“If you’ll take my advice,” said Dickson slowly, “you’ll get them deposited in a bank and take a receipt for them. A Scotch bank is no’ in a hurry to surrender a deposit without it gets the proper authority.”

===Chinese===
“您要是肯听俺的主意，”迪克森慢吞吞地说，“就把它们存进银行，取一张收据。一家苏格兰银行，不见着正经的授权文书，是不急着把存款交出去的。”

===Original===
Heritage brought his hands together with a smack. “That’s an idea. Will you trust us to take these things and deposit them safely?”

===Chinese===
赫里蒂奇啪地一击掌。“这是个主意。您肯信得过我们，让我们把这些东西带走，存到稳妥的地方吗？”

===Original===
For a little she was silent and her eyes were fixed on each of the trio in turn. “I will trust you,” she said at last. “I think you will not betray me.”

===Chinese===
她静了片刻，目光把三个人挨个儿盯了个遍。“我信得过你们。”她终于说，“我想你们不会出卖我。”

===Original===
“By God, we won’t!” said the Poet fervently. “Dogson, it’s up to you. You march off to Glasgow in double quick time and place the stuff in your own name in your own bank. There’s not a moment to lose. D’you hear?”

===Chinese===
“上帝作证，我们不会！”诗人热切地说，“狗逊，这就看你的了。你这就开步走，用最快的速度赶回格拉斯哥，把货色用你自己的名义存进你自己的银行。一分钟也耽误不得。听见没有？”

===Original===
“I will that.” To his own surprise Dickson spoke without hesitation. Partly it was because of his merchant’s sense of property, which made him hate the thought that miscreants should acquire that to which they had no title; but mainly it was the appeal in those haggard childish eyes. “But I’m not going to be tramping the country in the night carrying a fortune and seeking for trains that aren’t there. I’ll go the first thing in the morning.”

===Chinese===
“俺这就去。”连他自己都吃惊，迪克森答得毫不迟疑。一半是因为他那商人的产权意识——一想到歹人要染指他们并无权利的东西，他就恨；可主要还是那双枯槁的孩子般的眼睛里的恳求。“可俺不打算揣着一笔财宝，黑夜里在乡野里跋涉，去找根本不存在的火车。俺明儿一早头一件事就动身。”

===Original===
“Where are they?” Heritage asked.

===Chinese===
“它们在哪儿？”赫里蒂奇问。

===Original===
“That I do not tell. But I will fetch them.”

===Chinese===
“这个我不说。不过我会去取来。”

===Original===
She left the room and presently returned with three odd little parcels wrapped in leather and tied with thongs of raw hide. She gave them to Heritage, who held them appraisingly in his hand and then passed them to Dickson.

===Chinese===
她出了屋子，不多时便回来了，捧着三个古怪的小包裹，皮革裹着，生皮条捆着。她把它们交给赫里蒂奇，赫里蒂奇在手里掂了掂、估了估，又递给迪克森。

===Original===
“I do not ask about their contents. We take them from you as they are, and, please God, when the moment comes they will be returned to you as you gave them. You trust us, Mademoiselle?”

===Chinese===
“我不问它们里面是什么。您怎样交给我们的，我们便怎样收下；若蒙上帝保佑，时候一到，它们必将怎样如数奉还给您。您信得过我们吧，小姐？”

===Original===
“I trust you, for you are a soldier. Oh, and I thank you from my heart, my friends.” She held out a hand to each, which caused Heritage to grow suddenly very red.

===Chinese===
“我信得过你们，因为你是军人。啊，我发自内心地感谢你们，我的朋友们。”她向两人各伸出一只手，这一下弄得赫里蒂奇蓦地满脸通红。

===Original===
“I will remain in the neighbourhood to await developments,” he said. “We had better leave you now. Dougal, lead on.”

===Chinese===
“我就留在左近，静候事态的发展。”他说，“我们最好这就告辞。杜加尔，带路。”

===Original===
Before going, he took the girl’s hand again, and with a sudden movement bent and kissed it. Dickson shook it heartily. “Cheer up, Mem,” he observed. “There’s a better time coming.” His last recollection of her eyes was of a soft mistiness not far from tears. His pouch and pipe had strange company jostling them in his pocket as he followed the others down the ladder into the night.

===Chinese===
临走之前，他又一次执起姑娘的手，猛地俯身吻了一下。迪克森则热热乎乎地握了握。“别灰心，夫人，”他说，“好日子在后头呢。”他对她双眼的最后记忆，是一片迷蒙的温软，离眼泪不远。他跟着众人下梯子走进夜色时，口袋里的烟荷包和烟斗，身边挤上了好几位奇怪的旅伴。

===Original===
Dougal insisted that they must return by the road of the morning. “We daren’t go by the Laver, for that would bring us by the public-house. If the worst comes to the worst, and we fall in wi’ any of the deevils, they must think ye’ve changed your mind and come back from Auchenlochan.”

===Chinese===
杜加尔坚持必须原路返回。“咱们不敢走拉弗河那条道，那样就得打酒馆门口过。万一撞上那帮魔鬼里的哪一个，他们也只当你们回心转意，从奥琴洛坎折回来了。”

===Original===
The night smelt fresh and moist as if a break in the weather were imminent. As they scrambled along the Garple Dean a pinprick of light below showed where the tinklers were busy by their fire. Dickson’s spirits suffered a sharp fall and he began to marvel at his temerity. What in Heaven’s name had he undertaken? To carry very precious things, to which certainly he had no right, through the enemy to distant Glasgow. How could he escape the notice of the watchers? He was already suspect, and the sight of him back again in Dalquharter would double that suspicion. He must brazen it out, but he distrusted his powers with such telltale stuff in his pockets. They might murder him anywhere on the moor road or in an empty railway carriage. An unpleasant memory of various novels he had read in which such things happened haunted his mind.﻿ ﻿… There was just one consolation. This job over, he would be quit of the whole business. And honourably quit, too, for he would have played a manly part in a most unpleasant affair. He could retire to the idyllic with the knowledge that he had not been wanting when Romance called. Not a soul should ever hear of it, but he saw himself in the future tramping green roads or sitting by his winter fireside pleasantly retelling himself the tale.

===Chinese===
夜气闻着又新鲜又潮润，像天马上要变。三人沿着加普尔峡谷磕磕绊绊地走，谷底一点针尖大的火光，标出补锅匠们正围着火忙活。迪克森的心气一落千丈，开始惊讶于自己的胆大包天。他到底应承下了什么？要把一批绝非自己名下的贵重之物，穿过敌人的手，带去遥远的格拉斯哥。他怎能逃过望风人的眼睛？他早就是嫌疑人物，再让人瞧见他重回达尔夸特，嫌疑便要翻上一倍。他只好硬着头皮撑到底，可口袋里揣着这些招祸的物事，他信不过自己的本事。人家兴许在荒原大路上，或者在一节空火车车厢里，随时随地要了他的命。他读过的种种小说里这类情节的记忆，阴魂不散地缠着他。……只有一样安慰：这趟差事一了，他就同整个儿这件事两清。而且清得体面，因为在这桩顶不痛快的事体里，他总算尽了一份男子汉的本分。他可以退回他的田园牧歌里去，而且知道自己不曾辜负浪漫的召唤。这事他一个字也不会对人讲，可他望见将来的自己，走在青翠的大路上，或坐在冬天的炉边，把这个故事娓娓地讲给自己听。

===Original===
Before they came to the Garple bridge Dougal insisted that they should separate, remarking that “it would never do if we were seen thegither.” Heritage was despatched by a shortcut over fields to the left, which eventually, after one or two plunges into ditches, landed him safely in Mrs. Morran’s back yard. Dickson and Dougal crossed the bridge and tramped Dalquharter-wards by the highway. There was no sign of human life in that quiet place with owls hooting and rabbits rustling in the undergrowth. Beyond the woods they came in sight of the light in the back kitchen, and both seemed to relax their watchfulness when it was most needed. Dougal sniffed the air and looked seaward.

===Chinese===
还没到加普尔桥，杜加尔就坚持大家分头走，还说“让人瞧见咱们在一起，那可万万使不得”。赫里蒂奇被打发走左边穿田地的一条近路，那路在让他一头两脚栽进沟里之后，终于把他平安送进了莫兰太太的后院。迪克森和杜加尔过了桥，沿大公路向达尔夸特方向走去。那地方静悄悄不见人迹，只有猫头鹰啼叫，兔子在灌木丛里窸窣。过了树林，他们望见了后厨房的灯光，而两人偏偏在最该警惕的当口，都松懈了下来。杜加尔嗅了嗅空气，朝海那边望去。

===Original===
“It’s coming on to rain,” he observed. “There should be a muckle star there, and when you can’t see it it means wet weather wi’ this wind.”

===Chinese===
“要下雨了。”他说，“那本该有一颗大星星，这样的风里瞧不见它，就是要下雨。”

===Original===
“What star?” Dickson asked.

===Chinese===
“哪颗星？”迪克森问。

===Original===
“The one wi’ the Irish-lukkin’ name. What’s that they call it? O’Brien?” And he pointed to where the constellation of the Hunter should have been declining on the western horizon.

===Chinese===
“起了个爱尔兰名字模样的那颗。人家管它叫什么来着？奥布莱恩（O'Brien）？”他指向猎户座本该正在西边天际沉落的地方。

===Original===
There was a bend of the road behind them, and suddenly round it came a dogcart driven rapidly. Dougal slipped like a weasel into a bush, and presently Dickson stood revealed in the glare of a lamp. The horse was pulled up sharply and the driver called out to him. He saw that it was Dobson the innkeeper with Léon beside him.

===Chinese===
身后的路有个弯，一辆双轮马车突然从弯后疾驰而出。杜加尔黄鼠狼似的溜进一丛灌木，转眼间，迪克森便孤零零暴露在一盏车灯的光里。马被猛地勒住，车上的人朝他喊话。他看清了，是客栈店主多布森，身边坐着莱昂。

===Original===
“Who is it?” cried the voice. “Oh, you! I thought ye were off the day?”

===Chinese===
“谁在那儿？”那嗓门喊道，“哦，是你！我还当你今天走了呢？”

===Original===
Dickson rose nobly to the occasion.

===Chinese===
迪克森临危不乱，体面地应付了过去。

===Original===
“I thought myself I was. But I didn’t think much of Auchenlochan, and I took a fancy to come back and spend the last night of my holiday with my Auntie. I’m off to Glasgow first thing the morn’s morn.”

===Chinese===
“俺自个儿也当是走了呢。可俺瞅奥琴洛坎不怎么样，就起了意，回来跟俺姨妈一起过假期的最后一晚。明儿一大早，俺就动身回格拉斯哥。”

===Original===
“So!” said the voice. “Queer thing I never saw ye on the Auchenlochan road, where ye can see three mile before ye.”

===Chinese===
“是嘛！”那嗓门说，“怪了，去奥琴洛坎那条路上我怎么没瞧见你——那条路，三英里开外就能望见人影。”

===Original===
“I left early and took it easy along the shore.”

===Chinese===
“俺动身得早，沿海边一路慢慢悠悠走的。”

===Original===
“Did ye so? Well, good night to ye.”

===Chinese===
“是吗？那好，祝你晚安。”

===Original===
Five minutes later Dickson walked into Mrs. Morran’s kitchen, where Heritage was busy making up for a day of short provender.

===Chinese===
五分钟后，迪克森走进莫兰太太的厨房，赫里蒂奇正在那儿忙着补上这一天的亏空口粮。

===Original===
“I’m for Glasgow tomorrow, Auntie Phemie,” he cried. “I want you to loan me a wee trunk with a key, and steek the doors and windows, for I’ve a lot to tell you.”

===Chinese===
“俺明儿就回格拉斯哥，菲米姨妈。”他喊道，“俺想借您一只带锁的小提箱，再把门窗都闩严实，因为俺有一肚子话要跟您说。”

## VI. How Mr. McCunn Departed with Relief and Returned with Resolution / 六、麦坎先生如释重负而去、痛下决心而归

===Original===
At seven o’clock on the following morning the post-cart, summoned by an early message from Mrs. Morran, appeared outside the cottage. In it sat the ancient postman, whose real home was Auchenlochan, but who slept alternate nights in Dalquharter, and beside him Dobson the innkeeper. Dickson and his hostess stood at the garden-gate, the former with his pack on his back and at his feet a small stout wooden box, of the kind in which cheeses are transported, garnished with an immense padlock. Heritage for obvious reasons did not appear; at the moment he was crouched on the floor of the loft watching the departure through a gap in the dimity curtains.

===Chinese===
第二天早晨七点钟，莫兰太太头天一早打发口信唤来的邮车，出现在小屋门外。车上坐着那位上了年纪的邮差——他的家本在奥琴洛坎，只是隔天夜里在达尔夸特过夜——他身边坐的是客栈店主多布森。迪克森和女主人站在园门口，前者背着背包，脚边放着一只结实的木头小箱，就是运奶酪用的那一种，上头还挂着一把特大的挂锁。赫里蒂奇出于显而易见的原因没有露面；此刻他正蹲在阁楼地板上，从印花布窗帘的一道缝里望着这场出发。

===Original===
The traveller, after making sure that Dobson was looking, furtively slipped the key of the trunk into his knapsack.

===Chinese===
那位旅人先看清多布森正望着这边，才偷偷把小箱的钥匙塞进了自己的背包。

===Original===
“Well, goodbye, Auntie Phemie,” he said. “I’m sure you’ve been awful kind to me, and I don’t know how to thank you for all you’re sending.”

===Chinese===
“好啦，再见，菲米姨妈。”他说，“您待俺真是好得没话说，您让俺捎带的这一箱子东西，俺真不知道怎么谢您才好。”

===Original===
“Tuts, Dickson, my man, they’re hungry folk about Glesca that’ll be glad o’ my scones and jeelie. Tell Mirren I’m rale pleased wi’ her man and haste ye back soon.”

===Chinese===
“嘌，迪克森好孩子，格拉斯哥那边有的是饿肚子的人，正乐得吃俺的烤饼和果酱。告诉米兰（Mirren），俺对她家男人满意得很，你自己也快去快回。”

===Original===
The trunk was deposited on the floor of the cart and Dickson clambered into the back seat. He was thankful that he had not to sit next to Dobson, for he had telltale stuff on his person. The morning was wet, so he wore his waterproof, which concealed his odd tendency to stoutness about the middle.

===Chinese===
小箱安置在车厢地板上，迪克森爬上了后座。他暗自庆幸不必挨着多布森坐，因为他身上带着会露馅的东西。早晨下着雨，他便穿了雨衣，雨衣恰好遮住他腰身间那一处奇怪的发福趋势。

===Original===
Mrs. Morran played her part well, with all the becoming gravity of an affectionate aunt, but so soon as the post-cart turned the bend of the road her demeanour changed. She was torn with convulsions of silent laughter. She retreated to the kitchen, sank into a chair, wrapped her face in her apron and rocked. Heritage, descending, found her struggling to regain composure. “D’ye ken his wife’s name?” she gasped. “I ca’ed her Mirren! And maybe the body’s no mairried! Hech sirs! Hech sirs!”

===Chinese===
莫兰太太把自己那份戏演得极好，一副慈爱姨妈应有分寸的庄重；可邮车刚拐过路弯，她的做派就变了。她叫无声的大笑拧得五脏抽搐。她退回厨房，跌坐进椅子里，用围裙蒙住脸，前后直晃。赫里蒂奇从楼上下来，正撞见她竭力想恢复镇定。“你晓得他太太叫什么名字吗？”她喘着气说，“俺管人家叫米兰！说不定那位压根儿没结过婚呢！哎呀老天！哎呀老天！”

===Original===
Meantime Dickson was bumping along the moor-road on the back of the post-cart. He had worked out a plan, just as he had been used aforetime to devise a deal in foodstuffs. He had expected one of the watchers to turn up, and was rather relieved that it should be Dobson, whom he regarded as “the most natural beast” of the three. Somehow he did not think that he would be molested before he reached the station, since his enemies would still be undecided in their minds. Probably they only wanted to make sure that he had really departed to forget all about him. But if not, he had his plan ready.

===Chinese===
这时候，迪克森正颠簸在邮车后座上，走在荒原路上。他已经盘算好了一套方案，就像从前做食品生意时琢磨一桩买卖那样。他料到望风的会来一个，来的偏偏是多布森，他倒觉得宽心，因为在他看来，那三个里头数这头“牲口最本分”。不知怎么，他并不认为自己到车站之前会受到刁难，因为他的敌人心目中多半还没拿定主意。也许他们只想确认他真的走了，好从此把他忘个干净。可若不是这样，他也备好了对策。

===Original===
“Are you travelling today?” he asked the innkeeper.

===Chinese===
“你今天要出门？”他问客栈店主。

===Original===
“Just as far as the station to see about some oil-cake I’m expectin’. What’s in your wee kist? Ye came here wi’ nothing but the bag on your back.”

===Chinese===
“就到车站，去问问俺等着的一批油粕饼。你那小箱里装的什么？你来的时候，除了背上那只口袋，可是空着两手。”

===Original===
“Ay, the kist is no’ mine. It’s my auntie’s. She’s a kind body, and nothing would serve but she must pack a box for me to take back. Let me see. There’s a baking of scones; three pots of honey and one of rhubarb jam﻿—she was aye famous for her rhubarb jam; a mutton ham, which you can’t get for love or money in Glasgow; some homemade black puddings and a wee skim-milk cheese. I doubt I’ll have to take a cab from the station.”

===Chinese===
“可不，箱子不是俺的，是俺姨妈的。她是个热心人，非要给俺装一箱东西叫俺带回去不可。俺数数啊。一炉烤饼；三罐蜂蜜、一罐大黄酱——她的大黄酱向来是有名的；一只羊腿火腿，这玩意儿在格拉斯哥出多大价钱也买不着；几根自家做的血肠，还有一小块脱脂奶奶酪。俺看俺到了车站，怕是得叫辆车才行。”

===Original===
Dobson appeared satisfied, lit a short pipe and relapsed into meditation. The long uphill road, ever climbing to where far off showed the tiny whitewashed buildings which were the railway station, seemed interminable this morning. The aged postman addressed strange objurgations to his aged horse and muttered reflections to himself, the innkeeper smoked, and Dickson stared back into the misty hollow where lay Dalquharter. The southwest wind had brought up a screen of rain clouds and washed all the countryside in a soft wet grey. But the eye could still travel a fair distance, and Dickson thought he had a glimpse of a figure on a bicycle leaving the village two miles back. He wondered who it could be. Not Heritage, who had no bicycle. Perhaps some woman who was conspicuously late for the train. Women were the chief cyclists nowadays in country places.

===Chinese===
多布森像是信了，点上一只短烟斗，重新陷入沉思。那条长长的上坡路一路攀升，通向远处那一小片刷白的房子——那就是火车站——今天早晨它显得漫长得没有尽头。年老的邮夫对他那匹同样年老的骂出一串古怪的粗话，又自顾自咕哝着盘算；客栈店主吸着烟，迪克森则回望雾气迷蒙的洼地，达尔夸特就卧在那里面。西南风送来一道雨幕，把整个乡野洗成一片柔和的湿灰。但目力仍能望出好远，迪克森觉得，他瞥见两英里外有个人影骑着自行车出了村子。他纳闷那会是谁。不会是赫里蒂奇，他没有自行车。也许是哪个赶火车眼看要迟到的女人。如今在乡下，骑自行车的主力就是女人家。

===Original===
Then he forgot about the bicycle and twisted his neck to watch the station. It was less than a mile off now, and they had no time to spare, for away to the south among the hummocks of the bog he saw the smoke of the train coming from Auchenlochan. The postman also saw it and whipped up his beast into a clumsy canter. Dickson, always nervous about being late for trains, forced his eyes away and regarded again the road behind them. Suddenly the cyclist had become quite plain﻿—a little more than a mile behind﻿—a man, and pedalling furiously in spite of the stiff ascent.﻿ ﻿… It could only be one person﻿—Léon. He must have discovered their visit to the House yesterday and be on the way to warn Dobson. If he reached the station before the train, there would be no journey to Glasgow that day for one respectable citizen.

===Chinese===
随后他把自行车忘在了脑后，扭过脖子去看车站。车站离此已不足一英里，他们一点工夫也耽搁不起了，因为南边泥沼的草墩之间，他已望见从奥琴洛坎方向开来的火车的烟。邮夫也看见了，一鞭子催得他的牲口蹩脚地小跑起来。迪克森一向最怕误火车，此刻却强使自己把目光挪开，又去望身后的路。忽然，那骑车人看得一清二楚了——落下一英里多一点——是个男人，尽管上坡吃力，仍蹬得飞快。……那只可能是一个人——莱昂。他准是发现了他们昨天到大宅去的事，正赶去给多布森报信。他若赶在火车之前到了车站，那一天，某位体面市民就休想再启程去格拉斯哥了。

===Original===
Dickson was in a fever of impatience and fright. He dared not abjure the postman to hurry, lest Dobson should turn his head and descry his colleague. But that ancient man had begun to realise the shortness of time and was urging the cart along at a fair pace, since they were now on the flatter shelf of land which carried the railway. Dickson kept his eyes fixed on the bicycle and his teeth shut tight on his lower lip. Now it was hidden by the last dip of hill; now it emerged into view not a quarter of a mile behind, and its rider gave vent to a shrill call. Luckily the innkeeper did not hear, for at that moment with a jolt the cart pulled up at the station door, accompanied by the roar of the incoming train.

===Chinese===
迪克森急得像热锅上的蚂蚁，又怕得要命。他不敢催邮夫快走，生怕多布森一回头，认出他的同伙。好在那位老人也已意识到时间紧迫，正把车赶得相当快，因为眼下他们已上了铁路所经的那片较平的高地。迪克森眼睛死死盯着那辆自行车，牙齿紧紧咬住下唇。一会儿它隐没在最后一道山坡背后；一会儿它又冒了出来，落在身后不到四分之一英里，骑车人发出一声尖利的呼喊。幸亏客栈店主没有听见，因为就在那一刻，车身一晃，停在了车站门口，进站火车的轰鸣恰好撞在一起。

===Original===
Dickson whipped down from the back seat and seized the solitary porter. “Label the box for Glasgow and into the van with it. Quick, man, and there’ll be a shilling for you.” He had been doing some rapid thinking these last minutes and had made up his mind. If Dobson and he were alone in a carriage he could not have the box there; that must be elsewhere, so that Dobson could not examine it if he were set on violence, somewhere in which it could still be a focus of suspicion and attract attention from his person. He took his ticket, and rushed on to the platform, to find the porter and the box at the door of the guard’s van. Dobson was not there. With the vigour of a fussy traveller he shouted directions to the guard to take good care of his luggage, hurled a shilling at the porter and ran for a carriage. At that moment he became aware of Dobson hurrying through the entrance. He must have met Léon and heard news from him, for his face was red and his ugly brows darkening.

===Chinese===
迪克森一跃跳下后座，一把抓住站上唯一的那名搬运工。“给箱子贴上到格拉斯哥的签，搬进行李车去。快点儿伙计，赏你一先令。”这几分钟里他脑子飞转，已经拿定了主意。倘若他和多布森单独待在一节车厢里，箱子可不能随身；它得放在别处，叫多布森纵然动粗也没法开箱查验，同时它又还能做一块引人疑心的靶子，把众人的注意力从他本人身上引开。他买了票，冲上站台，只见搬运工和箱子已在行李车门口。多布森不在那里。他拿出爱挑剔的旅客那股劲儿，大声吩咐押运员照看好他的行李，扔给搬运工一先令，便奔向一节车厢。也就在这时，他察觉多布森正急匆匆从入口挤进来。他准是遇上了莱昂，从他那里听到了消息，因为他满脸通红，两道凶眉越拧越黑。

===Original===
The train was in motion. “Here, you!” Dobson’s voice shouted. “Stop! I want a word wi’ ye.” Dickson plunged at a third-class carriage, for he saw faces behind the misty panes, and above all things then he feared an empty compartment. He clambered on to the step, but the handle would not turn, and with a sharp pang of fear he felt the innkeeper’s grip on his arm. Then some Samaritan from within let down the window, opened the door and pulled him up. He fell on a seat and a second later Dobson staggered in beside him.

===Chinese===
火车已经动了。“喂，你！”多布森的嗓门喊道，“站住！我有话跟你说。”迪克森一头扎向一节三等车厢，因为他看见雾蒙蒙的车窗后面有人脸，而那一刻他最怕的就是一间空车厢。他攀上车门踏板，可门把怎么也拧不动，随着一阵尖锐的恐惧，他感到客栈店主的手已攥住他的胳膊。这时车里一位好心人放下车窗，打开车门，把他拽了上去。他跌坐在座位上，一秒钟后，多布森也跌跌撞撞地挤了进来，坐到了他身旁。

===Original===
Thank Heaven, the dirty little carriage was nearly full. There were two herds, each with a dog and a long hazel crook, and an elderly woman who looked like a ploughman’s wife out for a day’s marketing. And there was one other whom Dickson recognised with a peculiar joy﻿—the bagman in the provision line of business whom he had met three days before at Kilchrist.

===Chinese===
谢天谢地，这间肮脏的小车厢差不多坐满了。里面有两个牧人，各带一条狗和一根长长的榛木牧杖，还有一位老妇人，看模样是个出门赶一天集的佃农妻子。另外还有一位，迪克森认出来时心头涌起一阵特别的欢喜——正是三天前他在基尔克里斯特遇到过的那个食品行当的行商。

===Original===
The recognition was mutual. “Mr. McCunn!” the bagman exclaimed. “My, but that was running it fine! I hope you’ve had a pleasant holiday, sir?”

===Chinese===
对方也认出了他。“麦坎先生！”行商失声道，“好家伙，可真够悬的！您这趟假期想必过得愉快吧，先生？”

===Original===
“Very pleasant. I’ve been spending two nights with friends down hereaways. I’ve been very fortunate in the weather, for it has broke just when I’m leaving.”

===Chinese===
“愉快得很。俺在这一带朋友家住了两夜。运气也好，天赶在俺动身的时候正好变了。”

===Original===
Dickson sank back on the hard cushions. It had been a near thing, but so far he had won. He wished his heart did not beat so fast, and he hoped he did not betray his disorder in his face. Very deliberately he hunted for his pipe and filled it slowly. Then he turned to Dobson. “I didn’t know you were travelling the day. What about your oil-cake?”

===Chinese===
迪克森向后靠在硬座垫上。方才真是险到了极处，但到目前为止他是赢家。他只盼自己的心跳别这么快，希望自己没把心慌露在脸上。他不慌不忙地摸出烟斗，慢慢装上烟丝。然后他转向多布森。“俺不知道你今天也出门。你那油粕饼怎么办？”

===Original===
“I’ve changed my mind,” was the gruff answer.

===Chinese===
“我改主意了。”对方粗声粗气地答道。

===Original===
“Was that you I heard crying on me, when we were running for the train?”

===Chinese===
“咱们赶火车那会儿，俺听见后头喊俺的，是不是你？”

===Original===
“Ay. I thought ye had forgot about your kist.”

===Chinese===
“是我。我还当你把你的箱子给忘了。”

===Original===
“No fear,” said Dickson. “I’m no’ likely to forget my auntie’s scones.”

===Chinese===
“哪能呢，”迪克森说，“俺姨妈的烤饼，俺可忘不了。”

===Original===
He laughed pleasantly and then turned to the bagman. Thereafter the compartment hummed with the technicalities of the grocery trade. He exerted himself to draw out his companion, to have him refer to the great firm of D. McCunn, so that the innkeeper might be ashamed of his suspicions. What nonsense to imagine that a noted and wealthy Glasgow merchant﻿—the bagman’s tone was almost reverential﻿—would concern himself with the affairs of a forgotten village and a tumbledown house!

===Chinese===
他愉快地笑了两声，便转向那位行商。此后，整节车厢都嗡嗡响起了杂货业的行话。他使出浑身解数引同伴打开话匣，让他一口一个 D. 麦坎这家大商号，好叫客栈店主为自己的疑心害臊。一位鼎鼎大名、家资豪富的格拉斯哥商人——行商提起他来口吻近乎敬畏——竟会去操心一座被人遗忘的村庄、一幢塌倒破败的老宅的事，这想头何等荒唐！

===Original===
Presently the train drew up at Kirkmichael station. The woman descended, and Dobson, after making sure that no one else meant to follow her example, also left the carriage. A porter was shouting: “Fast train to Glasgow﻿—Glasgow next stop.” Dickson watched the innkeeper shoulder his way through the crowd in the direction of the booking office. “He’s off to send a telegram,” he decided. “There’ll be trouble waiting for me at the other end.”

===Chinese===
不久，火车停靠柯克迈克尔站。那妇人下了车，多布森在确认没有旁人打算效仿之后，也离开了车厢。一名搬运工正扯着嗓子喊：“去格拉斯哥的快车——下一站格拉斯哥。”迪克森望着客栈店主从人群里挤往售票厅的方向。“他是去发电报了，”他断定，“到了那一头，等着俺的准有麻烦。”

===Original===
When the train moved on he found himself disinclined for further talk. He had suddenly become meditative, and curled up in a corner with his head hard against the window pane, watching the wet fields and glistening roads as they slipped past. He had his plans made for his conduct at Glasgow, but Lord! how he loathed the whole business! Last night he had had a kind of gusto in his desire to circumvent villainy; at Dalquharter station he had enjoyed a momentary sense of triumph; now he felt very small, lonely and forlorn. Only one thought far at the back of his mind cropped up now and then to give him comfort. He was entering on the last lap. Once get this detestable errand done and he would be a free man, free to go back to the kindly humdrum life from which he should never have strayed. Never again, he vowed, never again. Rather would he spend the rest of his days in hydropathics than come within the pale of such horrible adventures. Romance, forsooth! This was not the mild goddess he had sought, but an awful harpy who battened on the souls of men.

===Chinese===
火车重新开动后，他发现自己再没兴致说话。他忽然变得心事重重，蜷在一个角落里，后脑紧抵着车窗玻璃，望着湿漉漉的田野和亮闪闪的道路从旁边滑过去。到了格拉斯哥该怎样行事，他的方案已经拟好，可是老天爷！他多么厌恶这整桩事情！昨夜他一心想挫败恶徒，还颇有几分兴头；在达尔夸特车站，他也尝到过一瞬的得意；此刻他却觉得自己无比渺小、孤单、凄惶。只有心底深处有一个念头不时冒上来安慰他：他已进入最后一程。只要把这桩可厌的差事办完，他就是个自由人了，自由得可以回到那种他压根儿就不该背离的、好心而乏味的日常生涯里去。再不干了，他起誓，再不干了。往后他宁可在水疗院里了却残生，也不愿再沾这种可怕冒险的边。浪漫，呸！这可不是他寻访的那位温柔女神，而是一头靠噬人灵魂把自己养肥的可怕鹰妖。

===Original===
He had some bad minutes as the train passed through the suburbs, and along the grimy embankment by which the southern lines enter the city. But as it rumbled over the river bridge and slowed down before the terminus, his vitality suddenly revived. He was a business man, and there was now something for him to do.

===Chinese===
火车穿过郊区，沿着南部各线进城所经的那条煤黑路基行驶时，他有几分钟很不好受。可当车轮隆隆碾过河上大桥、在终点站前放慢时，他的生气忽然回来了。他到底是买卖人，而眼下正有一桩事情等着他办。

===Original===
After a rapid farewell to the bagman, he found a porter and hustled his box out of the van in the direction of the left-luggage office. Spies, summoned by Dobson’s telegram, were, he was convinced, watching his every movement, and he meant to see that they missed nothing. He received his ticket for the box, and slowly and ostentatiously stowed it away in his pack. Swinging the said pack on his arm he sauntered through the entrance hall to the row of waiting taxicabs, and selected that one which seemed to him to have the oldest and most doddering driver. He deposited the pack inside on the seat, and then stood still as if struck with a sudden thought.

===Chinese===
匆匆与行商道过别，他找来一名搬运工，把箱子从行李车里弄出来，送往行李寄存处。他笃信，多布森那封电报召来的密探正盯着他的一举一动，他偏要让他们什么都看在眼里、一样不落。他领了箱子的存牌，慢条斯理、大张旗鼓地把它收进背包。他把这背包往臂弯一挎，慢悠悠穿过前厅，走到一排待雇的出租车前，挑了那辆在他看来车夫年纪最老、最老态龙钟的。他把背包放进车里的座位上，随后便一动不动地站住，仿佛猛然想起了一桩事。

===Original===
“I breakfasted terrible early,” he told the driver. “I think I’ll have a bite to eat. Will you wait?”

===Chinese===
“俺早饭吃得忒早了，”他对车夫说，“俺想再垫点东西。你肯等吗？”

===Original===
“Ay,” said the man, who was reading a grubby sheet of newspaper. “I’ll wait as long as ye like, for it’s you that pays.”

===Chinese===
“中。”那人正读着一张油乎乎的报纸，“你爱等多久等多久，反正掏钱的是你。”

===Original===
Dickson left his pack in the cab and, oddly enough for a careful man, he did not shut the door. He reentered the station, strolled to the bookstall and bought a *Glasgow Herald*. His steps then tended to the refreshment room, where he ordered a cup of coffee and two Bath buns, and seated himself at a small table. There he was soon immersed in the financial news, and though he sipped his coffee he left the buns untasted. He took out a penknife and cut various extracts from the *Herald*, bestowing them carefully in his pocket. An observer would have seen an elderly gentleman absorbed in market quotations.

===Chinese===
迪克森把背包留在了车里，而且——就一个精细人来说够古怪的——没关车门。他重新进了车站，踱到书报摊，买了一份《格拉斯哥先驱报》（*Glasgow Herald*）。随后他脚步一转进了茶点室，要了一杯咖啡、两只巴斯甜面包（Bath buns），在一张小桌旁坐定。不多时他便沉浸在财经新闻里，咖啡虽一口口啜着，那两只面包却一口未动。他掏出小折刀，从《先驱报》上裁下几段，仔细收进衣袋。在旁观者眼里，这是一位一心钻研行情报价的老绅士。

===Original===
After a quarter of an hour had been spent in this performance he happened to glance at the clock and rose with an exclamation. He bustled out to his taxi and found the driver still intent upon his reading. “Here I am at last,” he said cheerily, and had a foot on the step, when he stopped suddenly with a cry. It was a cry of alarm, but also of satisfaction.

===Chinese===
这套表演进行了一刻钟之后，他偶然瞥了一眼挂钟，喊了一声，站起身来。他风风火火出到自己车前，发现车夫仍在专心读报。“俺可算回来了，”他喜滋滋地说，一只脚已踏上踏板，忽然又猛地顿住，惊叫一声。那声叫里是惊惶，也是满意。

===Original===
“What’s become of my pack? I left it on the seat, and now it’s gone! There’s been a thief here.”

===Chinese===
“俺的背包哪儿去了？俺明明把它放在座位上，这会儿没了！这儿遭了贼了。”

===Original===
The driver, roused from his lethargy, protested in the name of his gods that no one had been near it. “Ye took it into the station wi’ ye,” he urged.

===Chinese===
车夫从昏沉里惊起，指天誓日地分辩说，没有人挨近过那包。“你自个儿把它带进站里去了。”他极力辩说道。

===Original===
“I did nothing of the kind. Just you wait here till I see the inspector. A bonny watch *you* keep on a gentleman’s things.”

===Chinese===
“俺才没有。你就在这儿等着，俺去找稽查。一位绅士的东西，你可真给看得好啊。”

===Original===
But Dickson did not interview the railway authorities. Instead he hurried to the left-luggage office. “I deposited a small box here a short time ago. I mind the number. Is it there still?”

===Chinese===
可迪克森并没有去找铁路当局。他反而急匆匆奔向行李寄存处。“俺方才在这儿存了一只小箱。俺记得牌子号码。它还在吗？”

===Original===
The attendant glanced at a shelf. “A wee deal box with iron bands. It was took out ten minutes syne. A man brought the ticket and took it away on his shoulder.”

===Chinese===
管寄存的人朝架子上一瞥。“一只带铁条的杉木小箱。十分钟前刚取走。来了个男人，交出存牌，扛在肩膀上带走了。”

===Original===
“Thank you. There’s been a mistake, but the blame’s mine. My man mistook my orders.”

===Chinese===
“多谢。是出了点岔子，不过错在俺自己。俺手下的人会错了俺的吩咐。”

===Original===
Then he returned to the now nervous taxi-driver. “I’ve taken it up with the stationmaster and he’s putting the police on. You’ll likely be wanted, so I gave him your number. It’s a fair disgrace that there should be so many thieves about this station. It’s not the first time I’ve lost things. Drive me to West George Street and look sharp.” And he slammed the door with the violence of an angry man.

===Chinese===
然后他回到此刻已经心神不定的车夫跟前。“俺已经跟站长说下了，他这就去报警。你们多半要被传去问话，所以俺把你的车号给了他。这车站四周贼竟这么多，真是天大的丢脸事。俺丢东西也不是头一回了。拉俺去西乔治街（West George Street），麻利点儿。”说完他砰地一声摔上车门，那架势怒气冲冲。

===Original===
But his reflections were not violent, for he smiled to himself. “That was pretty neat. They’ll take some time to get the kist open, for I dropped the key out of the train after we left Kirkmichael. That gives me a fair start. If I hadn’t thought of that, they’d have found some way to grip me and ripe me long before I got to the Bank.” He shuddered as he thought of the dangers he had escaped. “As it is, they’re off the track for half an hour at least, while they’re rummaging among Auntie Phemie’s scones.” At the thought he laughed heartily, and when he brought the taxicab to a standstill by rapping on the front window, he left it with a temper apparently restored. Obviously he had no grudge against the driver, who to his immense surprise was rewarded with ten shillings.

===Chinese===
他心里可一点儿也不狂怒，反倒暗暗直笑。“这一手干净利落。他们要想打开那口箱子，得费上一阵子，因为过了柯克迈克尔，俺就把钥匙从车窗扔出去了。这就给俺挣来了充裕的先手。俺要不是想到这一着，他们早就有法子攥住俺、搜俺的身，哪容俺走到银行。”想到自己逃过的种种凶险，他打了个寒噤。“照现在这样，他们至少有半个钟头摸不着头绪，只顾去翻菲米姨妈的烤饼了。”想到这里他放声大笑；待他敲敲前窗叫出租车停下，下车时那副脾气显然已经雨过天青。他对车夫显然并无芥蒂——车夫得了十先令赏钱，惊愕不已。

===Original===
Three minutes later Mr. McCunn might have been seen entering the head office of the Strathclyde Bank, and inquiring for the manager. There was no hesitation about him now, for his foot was on his native heath. The chief cashier received him with deference, in spite of his unorthodox garb, for he was not the least honoured of the bank’s customers. As it chanced he had been talking about him that very morning to a gentleman from London. “The strength of this city,” he had said, tapping his eyeglasses on his knuckles, “does not lie in its dozen very rich men, but in the hundred or two homely folk who make no parade of wealth. Men like Dickson McCunn, for example, who live all their life in a semidetached villa and die worth half a million.” And the Londoner had cordially assented.

===Chinese===
三分钟后，便可以看到麦坎先生走进斯特拉斯克莱德银行总行，指名求见经理。此刻他毫不迟疑，因为脚下是自家的地界。总出纳恭而敬之地接待了他，尽管他这一身行头未免不合规矩，因为他在银行的体面客户里也数得着。碰巧就在那天早晨，总出纳还在同一位伦敦来的绅士谈起他。“这座城市的力量，”他当时说，一面用夹鼻眼镜敲着指节，“不在于它那十来位大富翁，而在于那一两百户不事炫耀财富的殷实人家。比如迪克森·麦坎这样的人，一辈子住一幢半独立式小楼，死时留下的家产却有五十万镑。”那位伦敦人热诚地表示赞同。

===Original===
So Dickson was ushered promptly into an inner room, and was warmly greeted by Mr. Mackintosh, the patron of the Gorbals Die-Hards.

===Chinese===
于是迪克森立刻被引进一间内室，受到麦金托什先生——戈尔巴尔死硬帮的恩主——的热烈欢迎。

===Original===
“I must thank you for your generous donation, McCunn. Those boys will get a little fresh air and quiet after the smoke and din of Glasgow. A little country peace to smooth out the creases in their poor little souls.”

===Chinese===
“我得谢谢你那笔慷慨的捐款，麦坎。那些孩子总算能得着一点新鲜空气和清静，缓一缓格拉斯哥的烟尘和喧嚣。让乡间的安宁，把他们可怜的小灵魂里的褶皱熨平一些。”

===Original===
“Maybe,” said Dickson, with a vivid recollection of Dougal as he had last seen him. Somehow he did not think that peace was likely to be the portion of that devoted band. “But I’ve not come here to speak about that.”

===Chinese===
“但愿吧。”迪克森说，脑子里活现出他最后见到的杜加尔的样子。不知怎么，他觉得安宁多半不会是那支忠心耿耿的小队伍的份。“可俺今天来不是为说这个。”

===Original===
He took off his waterproof; then his coat and waistcoat; and showed himself a strange figure with sundry bulges about the middle. The manager’s eyes grew very round. Presently these excrescences were revealed as linen bags sewn on to his shirt, and fitting into the hollow between ribs and hip. With some difficulty he slit the bags and extracted three hidebound packages.

===Chinese===
他脱下雨衣，又脱去外衣和马甲，露出一副怪模样：腰身四周鼓鼓囊囊。经理的眼睛瞪得溜圆。不多时，这些累赘显出了原形：是缝在他衬衫上的几只亚麻口袋，正好嵌在肋骨与胯骨之间的凹处。他费了些力气割开口袋，取出三只皮革包扎的包裹。

===Original===
“See here, Mackintosh,” he said solemnly. “I hand you over these parcels, and you’re to put them in the innermost corner of your strong room. You needn’t open them. Just put them away as they are, and write me a receipt for them. Write it now.”

===Chinese===
“你听着，麦金托什，”他郑重其事地说，“俺把这几只包裹移交给你，你要把它们放进你金库最里头的角落。不必打开。原封不动收好，给俺写一张收据。现在就写。”

===Original===
Mr. Mackintosh obediently took pen in hand.

===Chinese===
麦金托什先生乖乖提笔在手。

===Original===
“What’ll I call them?” he asked.

===Chinese===
“我该管它们叫什么？”他问。

===Original===
“Just the three leather parcels handed to you by Dickson McCunn, Esq., naming the date.”

===Chinese===
“就写：迪克森·麦坎绅士移交的三只皮革包裹，注明日期。”

===Original===
Mr. Mackintosh wrote. He signed his name with his usual flourish and handed the slip to his client.

===Chinese===
麦金托什先生写了。他签名时照例笔走龙蛇，把纸条递给了他的客户。

===Original===
“Now,” said Dickson, “you’ll put that receipt in the strong box where you keep my securities, and you’ll give it up to nobody but me in person, and you’ll surrender the parcels only on presentation of the receipt. D’you understand?”

===Chinese===
“听好，”迪克森说，“你要把这张收据放进存放俺那些证券的保险箱，除了俺本人亲自来取，谁也不许给；那几只包裹，只有出示这张收据才能交出。你明白吗？”

===Original===
“Perfectly. May I ask any questions?”

===Chinese===
“完全明白。我可以提几个问题吗？”

===Original===
“You’d better not if you don’t want to hear lees.”

===Chinese===
“你最好别提，除非你想听谎话。”

===Original===
“What’s in the packages?” Mr. Mackintosh weighed them in his hand.

===Chinese===
“包裹里是什么？”麦金托什先生把它们掂在手里。

===Original===
“That’s asking,” said Dickson. “But I’ll tell ye this much. It’s jools.”

===Chinese===
“这一问问到根儿上了，”迪克森说，“不过俺可以给你透一句：是珠宝。”

===Original===
“Your own?”

===Chinese===
“你自己的？”

===Original===
“No, but I’m their trustee.”

===Chinese===
“不是，可俺是它们的受托人。”

===Original===
“Valuable?”

===Chinese===
“值钱吗？”

===Original===
“I was hearing they were worth more than a million pounds.”

===Chinese===
“俺听人说，它们值一百万镑还不止。”

===Original===
“God bless my soul,” said the startled manager. “I don’t like this kind of business, McCunn.”

===Chinese===
“老天爷保佑，”这位吓了一跳的经理说，“我不喜欢这种买卖，麦坎。”

===Original===
“No more do I. But you’ll do it to oblige an old friend and a good customer. If you don’t know much about the packages you know all about me. Now, mind, I trust you.”

===Chinese===
“俺也不喜欢。可你就当是赏一位老朋友、一位好主顾的面子。包裹的事你知道得不多，俺这个人你可知道得清清楚楚。记住，俺信得过你。”

===Original===
Mr. Mackintosh forced himself to a joke. “Did you maybe steal them?”

===Chinese===
麦金托什先生强打精神说了句笑话。“该不会是你偷来的吧？”

===Original===
Dickson grinned. “Just what I did. And that being so, I want you to let me out by the back door.”

===Chinese===
迪克森咧嘴一笑。“正是这么回事。既然如此，俺要你让俺打后门出去。”

===Original===
When he found himself in the street he felt the huge relief of a boy who had emerged with credit from the dentist’s chair. Remembering that there would be no midday dinner for him at home, his first step was to feed heavily at a restaurant. He had, so far as he could see, surmounted all his troubles, his one regret being that he had lost his pack, which contained among other things his *Izaak Walton* and his safety razor. He bought another razor and a new Walton, and mounted an electric tramcar en route for home.

===Chinese===
一到街上，他感到一种巨大的解脱，像一个刚从牙医椅子上全身而退、还挣足了面子的孩子。想到家里没人给他备午饭，他头一件事便是进一家餐馆饱餐了一顿。在他看来，他的麻烦已全部趟过，唯一遗憾是丢了背包——里头除了别的，还有他那本《伊扎克·沃尔顿》和安全剃刀。他另买了一把剃刀、一册新的沃尔顿，登上一辆开往家去的电车。

===Original===
Very contented with himself he felt as the car swung across the Clyde bridge. He had done well﻿—but of that he did not want to think, for the whole beastly thing was over. He was going to bury that memory, to be resurrected perhaps on a later day when the unpleasantness had been forgotten. Heritage had his address, and knew where to come when it was time to claim the jewels. As for the watchers, they must have ceased to suspect him, when they discovered the innocent contents of his knapsack and Mrs. Morran’s box. Home for him, and a luxurious tea by his own fireside; and then an evening with his books, for Heritage’s nonsense had stimulated his literary fervour. He would dip into his old favourites again to confirm his faith. Tomorrow he would go for a jaunt somewhere﻿—perhaps down the Clyde, or to the South of England, which he had heard was a pleasant, thickly peopled country. No more lonely inns and deserted villages for him; henceforth he would make certain of comfort and peace.

===Chinese===
电车载着他摇摇晃晃驶过克莱德河大桥时，他对自己心满意足。他干得不坏——可他不愿去想这个，因为那桩可憎的事情已经过去了。他要把这段记忆埋进土里，或许等不愉快的滋味淡忘了的将来哪一天，再让它复活。赫里蒂奇有他的地址，到了认领珠宝的时候，知道上哪儿去找他。至于那些望风的，等他们发现他背包里和莫兰太太箱子里那些清白无辜的内容，必定再不会疑心他。回家去，在自家炉边吃一顿奢华的下午茶；然后伴着他的书消磨一个晚上，因为赫里蒂奇的浑话勾起了他的文学热忱。他要重温旧日的心头好，好坚定自己的信心。明天他要出门溜达一趟——也许顺克莱德河而下，要么去英格兰南部，他听说那是个宜人的、人口稠密的地方。他再不要冷清清的客栈和荒凉的村庄了；从今往后，他要稳稳当当图个舒适安逸。

===Original===
The rain had stopped, and, as the car moved down the dreary vista of Eglinton Street, the sky opened into fields of blue and the April sun silvered the puddles. It was in such place and under such weather that Dickson suffered an overwhelming experience.

===Chinese===
雨已经停了，电车沿着埃格林顿街（Eglinton Street）凄清的长景驶下去，天上豁开一片片湛蓝，四月的太阳把水洼照得银亮。正是在这样的地方、这样的天气里，迪克森经受了一场势不可当的体验。

===Original===
It is beyond my skill, being all unlearned in the game of psychoanalysis, to explain how this thing happened. I concern myself only with facts. Suddenly the pretty veil of self-satisfaction was rent from top to bottom, and Dickson saw a figure of himself within, a smug leaden little figure which simpered and preened itself and was hollow as a rotten nut. And he hated it.

===Chinese===
此事如何发生，非我之才所能解释，因为我对精神分析这道行当全然外行。我只关心事实。忽然间，那幅自满的漂亮帷幕从头到脚撕裂开来，迪克森看见里面立着他自己的一个形象——一个沾沾自喜、铅灰色的小人像，赔着假笑，顾影自怜，内里却空得像一颗烂核桃。他恨它。

===Original===
The horrid truth burst on him that Heritage had been right. He only played with life. That imbecile image was a mere spectator, content to applaud, but shrinking from the contact of reality. It had been all right as a provision merchant, but when it fancied itself capable of higher things it had deceived itself. Foolish little image with its brave dreams and its swelling words from Browning! All make-believe of the feeblest. He was a coward, running away at the first threat of danger. It was as if he were watching a tall stranger with a wand pointing to the embarrassed phantom that was himself, and ruthlessly exposing its frailties! And yet the pitiless showman was himself too﻿—himself as he wanted to be, cheerful, brave, resourceful, indomitable.

===Chinese===
一个可怕的真相击中了他：赫里蒂奇是对的。他只是在玩弄生活。那副痴騃的画像不过是个看客，满足于鼓掌，却畏避现实的触碰。做食品商人的时候它无可指摘，可它一旦自以为能干更高尚的事，就把自己骗了。愚蠢的小画像，带着它豪迈的梦想和从布朗宁那里搬来的铿锵字句！全是顶孱弱的装腔作势。他是个懦夫，危险的威胁刚一露头就逃跑。这情形，就像他眼睁睁看着一个高个子的陌生人手持小棒，指着那个局促不安的幽灵——他自己——无情地数落它的种种短处！而那铁面无情的把戏主人，也是他自己——是他想成为的自己：快活，勇敢，足智多谋，不屈不挠。

===Original===
Dickson suffered a spasm of mortal agony. “Oh, I’m surely not so bad as all that,” he groaned. But the hurt was not only in his pride. He saw himself being forced to new decisions, and each alternative was of the blackest. He fairly shivered with the horror of it. The car slipped past a suburban station from which passengers were emerging﻿—comfortable black-coated men such as he had once been. He was bitterly angry with Providence for picking him out of the great crowd of sedentary folk for this sore ordeal. “Why was I tethered to sich a conscience?” was his moan. But there was that stern inquisitor with his pointer exploring his soul. “You flatter yourself you have done your share,” he was saying. “You will make pretty stories about it to yourself, and some day you may tell your friends, modestly disclaiming any special credit. But you will be a liar, for you know you are afraid. You are running away when the work is scarcely begun, and leaving it to a few boys and a poet whom you had the impudence the other day to despise. I think you are worse than a coward. I think you are a cad.”

===Chinese===
迪克森感到一阵致命痛苦的痉挛。“哦，俺总不至于糟到那个地步吧。”他呻吟道。可伤痛不止在自尊上。他看见自己正被逼着做新的决断，而每一条出路都黑得彻头彻尾。他当真为此吓得发抖。电车悄悄滑过一座郊区车站，旅客正从站里涌出——都是些舒舒服服的黑衣绅士，他从前也是其中一员。他怨愤天道不公，偏从一大群安坐不动的人里单单挑中他来受这场酷刑。“老天怎么偏给俺拴上这么一副良心？”这是他的哀鸣。可那位冷面的宗教法官还在那里，用他的教鞭探进他的灵魂。“你孤芳自赏，自以为尽了本分，”他正在说，“你会拿它给自己编出漂亮的故事，有朝一日或许讲给朋友们听，还要谦逊地推说无功。可你将是个说谎的人，因为你自己知道你害怕。活儿才刚刚开头你就逃之夭夭，把它撂给几个毛孩子和一个诗人——前两天你还放肆地小瞧过他们。我看你比懦夫还不如。我看你是个下流胚。”

===Original===
His fellow-passengers on the top of the car saw an absorbed middle-aged gentleman who seemed to have something the matter with his bronchial tubes. They could not guess at the tortured soul. The decision was coming nearer, the alternatives loomed up dark and inevitable. On one side was submission to ignominy, on the other a return to that place, which he detested, and yet loathed himself for detesting. “It seems I’m not likely to have much peace either way,” he reflected dismally.

===Chinese===
车顶上的同车乘客，只见一位出了神的中年绅士，支气管似乎有些毛病。他们猜不到那颗正受酷刑的灵魂。决断在步步逼近，两条出路黑沉沉地压了上来，无可回避。一边是忍受耻辱，另一边是回到那个地方去——他憎恶那地方，却又因这份憎恶而憎恶自己。“看样子，俺横竖都落不着多少安生。”他凄凄地想。

===Original===
How the conflict would have ended had it continued on these lines I cannot say. The soul of Mr. McCunn was being assailed by moral and metaphysical adversaries with which he had not been trained to deal. But suddenly it leapt from negatives to positives. He saw the face of the girl in the shuttered House, so fair and young and yet so haggard. It seemed to be appealing to him to rescue it from a great loneliness and fear. Yes, he had been right, it had a strange look of his Janet﻿—the wide-open eyes, the solemn mouth. What was to become of that child if he failed her in her great need?

===Chinese===
这场交战若照这样打下去会怎样收场，我说不上来。麦坎先生的灵魂正遭到道德与形而上敌人的围攻，而他从未受过对付它们的训练。可突然间，局面从否定跳到了肯定。他看见了那个被百叶窗封起来的大宅里的姑娘的脸，那么秀美、那么年轻，却又那么憔悴。那张脸仿佛在恳求他，把它从巨大的孤寂和恐惧里解救出来。是的，他没有看错，那脸上确有一股他家的珍妮特的奇异神情——那双睁得大大的眼睛，那张肃穆的嘴。在她最大的危难里他若负了她，那孩子会落得什么下场？

===Original===
Now Dickson was a practical man and this view of the case brought him into a world which he understood. “It’s fair ridiculous,” he reflected. “Nobody there to take a grip of things. Just a wheen Gorbals keelies and the lad Heritage. Not a business man among the lot.”

===Chinese===
迪克森到底是个务实的人，案情的这一面把他带回了他的熟地。“这简直荒唐透顶，”他想，“那边连个抓总的人都没有。只有一小帮戈尔巴尔街崽子和赫里蒂奇那小子。一堆人里挑不出一个买卖人。”

===Original===
The alternatives, which hove before him like two great banks of cloud, were altering their appearance. One was becoming faint and tenuous; the other, solid as ever, was just a shade less black. He lifted his eyes and saw in the near distance the corner of the road which led to his home. “I must decide before I reach that corner,” he told himself.

===Chinese===
那两条出路，原先像两大团乌云在他面前翻涌，此刻正在变样。一团渐渐稀薄黯淡下去；另一团依旧厚实，只是黑色淡了一分。他抬起眼睛，看见不远处那个通往他家的街角。“走到那个街角之前，俺必须拿定主意。”他对自己说。

===Original===
Then his mind became apathetic. He began to whistle dismally through his teeth, watching the corner as it came nearer. The car stopped with a jerk. “I’ll go back,” he said aloud, clambering down the steps. The truth was he had decided five minutes before when he first saw Janet’s face.

===Chinese===
随后他的心思变得麻木了。他开始从齿缝里凄凄地吹口哨，眼看着那个街角越来越近。车身一晃，停住了。“俺回去。”他一面出声说，一面跨下踏板。实情是，五分钟前他第一眼望见珍妮特的脸时，就已经拿定了主意。

===Original===
He walked briskly to his house, entirely refusing to waste any more energy on reflection. “This is a business proposition,” he told himself, “and I’m going to handle it as sich.” Tibby was surprised to see him and offered him tea in vain. “I’m just back for a few minutes. Let’s see the letters.”

===Chinese===
他脚步轻快地走回家去，断然不肯再浪费一点力气去反省。“这是一桩买卖，”他对自己说，“俺要按买卖的规矩来办它。”蒂比见他回来吃了一惊，请他用茶，白请了。“俺就回来几分钟。把信拿来俺瞧瞧。”

===Original===
There was one from his wife. She proposed to stay another week at the Neuk Hydropathic and suggested that he might join her and bring her home. He sat down and wrote a long affectionate reply, declining, but expressing his delight that she was soon returning. “That’s very likely the last time Mamma will hear from me,” he reflected, but﻿—oddly enough﻿—without any great fluttering of the heart.

===Chinese===
有一封是妻子的。她打算在纽克水疗院再住一星期，还提议他不妨过去同住，一道接她回家。他坐下来写了一封情深意长的回信，婉言谢绝，却说她的归期已近，他非常高兴。“这多半是‘妈妈’最后一回收到俺的信了。”他想道，可是——说来也怪——心里并没有多大的激荡。

===Original===
Then he proceeded to be furiously busy. He sent out Tibby to buy another knapsack and to order a cab and to cash a considerable cheque. In the knapsack he packed a fresh change of clothing and the new safety razor, but no books, for he was past the need of them. That done, he drove to his solicitors.

===Chinese===
接着他开始发疯似的忙碌起来。他打发蒂比出门，去买一只新背包、叫一辆马车、再兑一张数目可观的支票。背包里装上一身干净的换洗衣裳和那把新剃刀，书却一本不带——他已经过了需要书的地步。办完这些，他驱车前往他的律师事务所。

===Original===
“What like a firm are Glendonan and Speirs in Edinburgh?” he asked the senior partner.

===Chinese===
“爱丁堡的格伦多南与斯皮尔斯，是家怎么样的事务所？”他问资深合伙人。

===Original===
“Oh, very respectable. Very respectable indeed. Regular Edinburgh W.S. lot. Do a lot of factoring.”

===Chinese===
“哦，很体面。非常体面。地地道道爱丁堡 W.S.（Writer to the Signet，苏格兰事务律师）一派。经手大量代管产业。”

===Original===
“I want you to telephone through to them and inquire about a place in Carrick called Huntingtower, near the village of Dalquharter. I understand it’s to let, and I’m thinking of taking a lease of it.”

===Chinese===
“俺要你打电话过去，打听卡里克一个叫亨廷塔的地方，在达尔夸特村附近。俺听说那宅子要出租，俺有意把它租下来。”

===Original===
The senior partner after some delay got through to Edinburgh, and was presently engaged in the feverish dialectic which the long-distance telephone involves. “I want to speak to Mr. Glendonan himself.﻿ ﻿… Yes, yes, Mr. Caw of Paton and Linklater.﻿ ﻿… Good afternoon.﻿ ﻿… Huntingtower. Yes, in Carrick. Not to let? But I understand it’s been in the market for some months. You say you’ve an idea it has just been let. But my client is positive that you’re mistaken, unless the agreement was made this morning.﻿ ﻿… You’ll inquire? Oh, I see. The actual factoring is done by your local agent. Mr. James Loudon, in Auchenlochan. You think my client had better get into touch with him at once. Just wait a minute, please.”

===Chinese===
资深合伙人费了一阵周折才接通爱丁堡，随即投入了长途电话特有的那种火热的辩难。“我要同格伦多南先生本人通话。……对，对，帕顿与林克莱特事务所的考先生（Mr. Caw of Paton and Linklater）。……午安。……亨廷塔。对，在卡里克。不出租？可据我所知，它上市有好几个月了。您说您印象中它刚租出去。但我的委托人可以肯定您弄错了，除非租约是今天上午订立的。……您去问一问？哦，原来如此。实际的代管业务由贵方的地方经纪人经手。奥琴洛坎的詹姆斯·劳登（James Loudon）先生。您认为我的委托人最好马上同他接洽。请稍等片刻。”

===Original===
He put his hand over the receiver. “Usual Edinburgh way of doing business,” he observed caustically. “What do you want done?”

===Chinese===
他用手捂住话筒。“爱丁堡人做买卖的老派头。”他刻薄地说，“你要怎么办？”

===Original===
“I’ll run down and see this Loudon. Tell Glendonan and Speirs to advise him to expect me, for I’ll go this very day.”

===Chinese===
“俺亲自跑一趟，去见见这位劳登。叫格伦多南与斯皮尔斯知会他一声，说有客要来，俺今天就动身。”

===Original===
Mr. Caw resumed his conversation. “My client would like a telegram sent at once to Mr. Loudon introducing him. He’s Mr. Dickson McCunn of Mearns Street﻿—the great provision merchant, you know. Oh, yes! Good for any rent. Refer if you like to the Strathclyde Bank, but you can take my word for it. Thank you. Then that’s settled. Goodbye.”

===Chinese===
考先生继续通话。“我的委托人希望立刻给劳登先生拍一封引荐电报。他是米恩斯街的迪克森·麦坎先生——了不起的食品商，您知道的。哦，没错！出多少租金都不成问题。您若愿意，尽管向斯特拉斯克莱德银行征询，不过您信我的话就是了。谢谢。那就这么定了。再见。”

===Original===
Dickson’s next visit was to a gunmaker who was a fellow-elder with him in the Guthrie Memorial Kirk.

===Chinese===
迪克森的下一站是一位枪械商，此人在格思里纪念教堂与他同为长老。

===Original===
“I want a pistol and a lot of cartridges,” he announced. “I’m not caring what kind it is, so long as it is a good one and not too big.”

===Chinese===
“俺要一支手枪，还要一大批子弹。”他开门见山，“什么式样俺不在乎，只要是支好枪，别太大就行。”

===Original===
“For yourself?” the gunmaker asked. “You must have a licence, I doubt, and there’s a lot of new regulations.”

===Chinese===
“您自用？”枪械商问，“恐怕您得有执照，如今新章程可多了。”

===Original===
“I can’t wait on a licence. It’s for a cousin of mine who’s off to Mexico at once. You’ve got to find some way of obliging an old friend, Mr. McNair.”

===Chinese===
“俺等不得执照。是俺的一个堂弟要的，他马上就要动身去墨西哥（Mexico）。麦克奈尔先生（Mr. McNair），您总得想个法子，帮老朋友这个忙。”

===Original===
Mr. McNair scratched his head. “I don’t see how I can sell you one. But I’ll tell you what I’ll do﻿—I’ll lend you one. It belongs to my nephew, Peter Tait, and has been lying in a drawer ever since he came back from the front. He has no use for it now that he’s a placed minister.”

===Chinese===
麦克奈尔先生挠了挠头。“卖，我是没法子卖给您的。不过我可以这么办——借您一支。那是我外甥彼得·泰特（Peter Tait）的，打他从前线回来，就一直躺在抽屉里。他如今当上了有正式教席的牧师，用不着它了。”

===Original===
So Dickson bestowed in the pockets of his waterproof a service revolver and fifty cartridges, and bade his cab take him to the shop in Mearns Street. For a moment the sight of the familiar place struck a pang to his breast, but he choked down unavailing regrets. He ordered a great hamper of foodstuffs﻿—the most delicate kind of tinned goods, two perfect hams, tongues, Strasbourg pies, chocolate, cakes, biscuits and, as a last thought, half a dozen bottles of old liqueur brandy. It was to be carefully packed, addressed to Mrs. Morran, Dalquharter Station, and delivered in time for him to take down by the 7:33 train. Then he drove to the terminus and dined with something like a desperate peace in his heart.

===Chinese===
于是迪克森把一支军用左轮手枪和五十发子弹收进雨衣口袋，吩咐他的马车拉他到米恩斯街的店铺。一时之间，这熟悉的地方叫他胸口发疼，但他把无济于事的懊悔咽了下去。他订了一大筐食品——最精美的各色罐头、两只上好的火腿、几条口条、斯特拉斯堡肉派（Strasbourg pies）、巧克力、蛋糕、饼干，临了又想起，添上半打陈年白兰地。筐子要仔细捆好，写明寄交达尔夸特车站莫兰太太收，务必赶得及随他乘 7:33 的下行火车带走。然后他驱车前往总站，吃了晚饭，心里怀着一种近乎破釜沉舟的平静。

===Original===
On this occasion he took a first-class ticket, for he wanted to be alone. As the lights began to be lit in the wayside stations and the clear April dusk darkened into night, his thoughts were sombre yet resigned. He opened the window and let the sharp air of the Renfrewshire uplands fill the carriage. It was fine weather again after the rain, and a bright constellation﻿—perhaps Dougal’s friend O’Brien﻿—hung in the western sky. How happy he would have been a week ago had he been starting thus for a country holiday! He could sniff the faint scent of moor-burn and ploughed earth which had always been his first reminder of spring. But he had been pitchforked out of that old happy world and could never enter it again. Alas! for the roadside fire, the cosy inn, the *Complete Angler*, the Chavender or Chub!

===Chinese===
