===Chinese===
“谁也不准露一点光亮。”赫里蒂奇说。“绝不能让人知道我们在这儿。只有公主可以点一盏灯。是的”——这是回答迪克森——“她知道我们要来——也知道你要来。住处我们待会儿再上楼找。你们侦察兵，必须守住每一处可能进来的口子。窗户是安全的，我想，因为都从里面上了闩。正门也是。可有游廊那扇门，他们手里有钥匙；还有厨房旁边的后门；至于锅炉房那边是不是真没有一条进路，我也全无把握。你们明白。我们要据守此地，来者一概拒之门外。凶险之处必须设障堵死。守军的司令部设在大厅，那里必须始终有一名侦察兵值岗。哨子你们都带了？好。有人动游廊的门，哨兵吹一声；动后门，吹两声；别处有动静，吹三声。除了吹哨的那名哨兵，其余人等一概有责退回大厅听候号令。”

===Original===
“That’s so,” assented Dougal.

===Chinese===
“正是这话。”杜加尔附和道。

===Original===
“If the enemy forces an entrance we must overpower him. Any means you like. Sticks or fists, and remember that if it’s a scrap in the dark make for the man’s throat. I expect you little devils have eyes like cats. The scoundrels must be kept away from the ladies at all costs. If the worst comes to the worst, the Princess has a revolver.”

===Chinese===
“要是敌人强闯进来，我们就必须制服他。随便什么法子都行。棍子也好，拳头也好，记住一条：黑地里打架，照对方的喉咙下手。我谅你们这班小魔鬼都长着猫一样的眼睛。无论如何，不能让那帮恶棍挨近两位女眷半步。真到了最坏的地步，公主手里还有一把左轮枪。”

===Original===
“So have I,” said Dickson. “I got it in Glasgow.”

===Chinese===
“俺也有一把，”迪克森说，“在格拉斯哥弄到的。”

===Original===
“The deuce you have! Can you use it?”

===Chinese===
“好家伙，你还有这玩意儿！会使吗？”

===Original===
“I don’t know.”

===Chinese===
“不知道。”

===Original===
“Well, you can hand it over to me, if you like. But it oughtn’t to come to shooting, if it’s only the three of them. The eight of us should be able to manage three and one of them lame. If the others turn up﻿—well, God help us all! But we’ve got to make sure of one thing, that no one lays hands on the Princess so long as there’s one of us left alive to hit out.”

===Chinese===
“那么你愿意的话，就交给我好了。不过应该不至于闹到开枪，倘若来的只是他们三个。咱们八个对付三个——其中一个还是瘸子——本该绰绰有余。要是其余那些人也到了——唉，那就求上帝保佑我们大家吧！可有一条我们必须做到：只要咱们还剩一个活人能挥拳，就谁也别想碰公主一根指头。”

===Original===
“Ye needn’t be feared for that,” said Dougal. There was no light in the room, but Dickson was certain that the morose face of the Chieftain was lit with unholy joy.

===Chinese===
“这您就甭担心了。”杜加尔说。屋里没有一点光，可迪克森确信，首领那张阴沉的脸此刻正烧着一种邪门的狂喜。

===Original===
“Then off with you. Mr. McCunn and I will explain matters to the ladies.”

===Chinese===
“那就去吧。我和麦坎先生这就去跟两位夫人把事情说明白。”

===Original===
When they were alone, Heritage’s voice took a different key. “We’re in for it, Dogson, old man. There’s no doubt these three scoundrels expect reinforcements at any moment, and with them will be one who is the devil incarnate. He’s the only thing on earth that that brave girl fears. It seems he is in love with her and has pestered her for years. She hated the sight of him, but he wouldn’t take no, and being a powerful man﻿—rich and wellborn and all the rest of it﻿—she had a desperate time. I gather he was pretty high in favour with the old Court. Then when the Bolsheviks started he went over to them, like plenty of other grandees, and now he’s one of their chief brains﻿—none of your callow revolutionaries, but a man of the world, a kind of genius, she says, who can hold his own anywhere. She believes him to be in this country, and only waiting the right moment to turn up. Oh, it sounds ridiculous, I know, in Britain in the twentieth century, but I learned in the war that civilisation anywhere is a very thin crust. There are a hundred ways by which that kind of fellow could bamboozle all our law and police and spirit her away. That’s the kind of crowd we have to face.”

===Chinese===
只剩他们两人的时候，赫里蒂奇的声调换了一个调门。“咱们这回上了贼船了，狗逊老兄。毫无疑问，这三个恶棍时时刻刻都在等增援，而且随增援一起到的，还有一个魔鬼的化身。他是这世上那位勇敢的姑娘唯一害怕的东西。据说是他爱上了她，死缠了她好些年。她见了他就憎恶，可他不肯接受一个“不”字，加上他是个有势力的人物——有钱，出身好，诸如此类——她过了一段苦不堪言的日子。我揣摩，他在旧日的宫廷里相当得宠。后来布尔什维克一起事，他就投了过去，像许多别的大贵人一样，如今成了他们的头号智囊之一——不是那种乳臭未干的革命娃娃，而是个阅尽世故的人物，她说，某种天才，到哪儿都立得住脚。她相信他就在这个国家，只等一个合适的时机露面。唉，我知道，这在二十世纪的英国听着荒唐透顶，可我在大战里学到过：不论哪里的文明，都只是一层很薄的壳。那一路人有一百种法子，把我们全部的法律和警察哄得团团转，把她悄没声地劫走。我们要对付的，就是这么一伙人。”

===Original===
“Did she say what he was like in appearance?”

===Chinese===
“她说没说过，他长相如何？”

===Original===
“A face like an angel﻿—a lost angel, she says.”

===Chinese===
“一张天使般的脸——一个堕落的天使，她是这么说的。”

===Original===
Dickson suddenly had an inspiration.

===Chinese===
迪克森忽然灵光一现。

===Original===
“D’you mind the man you said was an Australian﻿—at Kirkmichael? I thought myself he was a foreigner. Well, he was asking for a place he called Darkwater, and there’s no sich place in the countryside. I believe he meant Dalquharter. I believe he’s the man she’s feared of.”

===Chinese===
“你还记不记得你说过那人是个澳大利亚人——在柯克迈克尔？俺自己当时就琢磨他是个外国人。他打听一个他叫作达克沃特的地方，这乡里压根没有这么个去处。俺琢磨他指的就是达尔夸特。俺琢磨他就是她怕的那个人。”

===Original===
A gasped “By Jove!” came from the darkness. “Dogson, you’ve hit it. That was five days ago, and he must have got on the right trail by this time. He’ll be here tonight. That’s why the three have been lying so quiet today. Well, we’ll go through with it, even if we haven’t a dog’s chance. Only I’m sorry that you should be mixed up in such a hopeless business.”

===Chinese===
黑暗里传来一声倒抽冷气的“天哪！”。“狗逊，你一箭正中靶心。那是五天前的事，到这会儿他必定已经摸上正路了。他今夜就会到。怪不得那三个今天一直按兵不动。好吧，咱们干到底，哪怕一线胜算也没有。只是对不起你，把你拖进了这么一件没指望的事里。”

===Original===
“Why me more than you?”

===Chinese===
“怎么就该是我，而不是你？”

===Original===
“Because it’s all pure pride and joy for me to be here. Good God, I wouldn’t be elsewhere for worlds. It’s the great hour of my life. I would gladly die for her.”

===Chinese===
“因为我在这儿，纯粹是骄傲和欢喜。老天在上，拿全世界来换我也不挪窝。这是我一生最伟大的时刻。我甘愿为她去死。”

===Original===
“Tuts, that’s no’ the way to talk, man. Time enough to speak about dying when there’s no other way out. I’m looking at this thing in a business way. We’d better be seeing the ladies.”

===Chinese===
“呸，可不能这么说话，老兄。等真到了没有第二条路的时候，再提死字也不迟。俺拿这桩事当一桩生意来盘算。咱们还是先去见两位夫人吧。”

===Original===
They groped into the pitchy hall, somewhere in which a Die-Hard was on picket, and down the passage to the smoking-room. Dickson blinked in the light of a very feeble lamp and Heritage saw that his hands were cumbered with packages. He deposited them on a sofa and made a ducking bow.

===Chinese===
他们摸黑进了漆黑的大厅——某处正有一名死硬帮成员站岗——再顺着走廊进了吸烟室。迪克森在一盏微弱得可怜的灯光下直眨眼睛，赫里蒂奇看见他两手抱满了包裹。他把包裹搁在一张沙发上，鸭点头似地鞠了一躬。

===Original===
“I’ve come back, Mem, and glad to be back. Your jools are in safe keeping, and not all the blagyirds in creation could get at them. I’ve come to tell you to cheer up﻿—a stout heart to a stey brae, as the old folk say. I’m handling this affair as a business proposition, so don’t be feared, Mem. If there are enemies seeking you, there’s friends on the road too.﻿ ﻿… Now, you’ll have had your dinner, but you’d maybe like a little dessert.”

===Chinese===
“俺回来了，夫人，回来得心里欢喜。您的首饰妥妥地存着呢，普天之下的流氓恶棍全凑到一块儿，也休想沾它们的边。俺回来就是要告诉您宽心——老话说得好，心壮不怕坡陡。这桩事俺是当一桩生意来经手的，所以您甭害怕，夫人。有仇家寻您，路上也就有朋友。……这会儿您二位晚饭是用过了，可兴许还想上来一点甜点。”

===Original===
He spread before them a huge box of chocolates, the best that Mearns Street could produce, a box of candied fruits, and another of salted almonds. Then from his hideously overcrowded pockets he took another box, which he offered rather shyly. “That’s some powder for your complexion. They tell me that ladies find it useful whiles.”

===Chinese===
他在她们面前摆开一大盒巧克力——米恩斯街拿得出的最好货色——一盒蜜饯果子，又一盒咸杏仁。随后他从塞得吓人的、鼓鼓囊囊的口袋里又摸出一只盒子，有点腼腆地递了上去。“这是扑脸的香粉。人家告诉俺，女士们有时候用得着它。”

===Original===
The girl’s strained face watched him at first in mystification, and then broke slowly into a smile. Youth came back to it, the smile changed to a laugh, a low rippling laugh like faraway bells. She took both his hands.

===Chinese===
姑娘那张绷紧的脸起初愕然望着他，随即慢慢绽开了笑意。青春回到了脸上，笑意化作笑声，一串低低的、涟漪般的轻笑，像远处的铃铎。她握住了他的双手。

===Original===
“You are kind,” she said, “you are kind and brave. You are a de‑ar.”

===Chinese===
“你真好，”她说，“你善良，又勇敢。你是个亲——爱的人儿。”

===Original===
And then she kissed him.

===Chinese===
接着，她吻了吻他。

===Original===
Now, as far as Dickson could remember, no one had ever kissed him except his wife. The light touch of her lips on his forehead was like the pressing of an electric button which explodes some powerful charge and alters the face of a countryside. He blushed scarlet; then he wanted to cry; then he wanted to sing. An immense exhilaration seized him, and I am certain that if at that moment the serried ranks of Bolshevism had appeared in the doorway, Dickson would have hurled himself upon them with a joyful shout.

===Chinese===
据迪克森记忆所及，除了妻子，还从没有谁吻过他。她的嘴唇在他额头轻轻一触，就像按下了某个电钮，引爆了一处威力巨大的炸药，把整个乡野的面貌都改了样。他涨红了脸；接着他想哭；接着他又想唱。一股巨大的欢欣攫住了他，我敢担保，倘若那一刻布尔什维主义的森严队伍出现在门口，迪克森准会欢呼一声，奋勇扑上去。

===Original===
Cousin Eugénie was earnestly eating chocolates, but Saskia had other business.

===Chinese===
表姐欧仁妮正一心一意吃着巧克力，萨斯基亚却另有正事。

===Original===
“You will hold the house?” she asked.

===Chinese===
“你们守得住这座宅子吗？”她问。

===Original===
“Please God, yes,” said Heritage. “I look at it this way. The time is very near when your three gaolers expect the others, their masters. They have not troubled you in the past two days as they threatened, because it was not worth while. But they won’t want to let you out of their sight in the final hours, so they will almost certainly come here to be on the spot. Our object is to keep them out and confuse their plans. Somewhere in this neighbourhood, probably very near, is the man you fear most. If we nonplus the three watchers, they’ll have to revise their policy, and that means a delay, and every hour’s delay is a gain. Mr. McCunn has found out that the factor Loudon is in the plot, and he has purchase enough, it seems, to blanket for a time any appeal to the law. But Mr. McCunn has taken steps to circumvent him, and in twenty-four hours we should have help here.”

===Chinese===
“上帝保佑，守得住。”赫里蒂奇说。“我是这么看这局棋的。您那三名狱卒等候其余那些人——他们的主子——的时刻已经迫在眉睫。过去两天他们没有像威胁的那样来搅扰您，因为犯不上。可到最后几个钟头，他们不会愿意让您离开视线，所以他们几乎一定会到这儿来就近盯着。我们的目标，就是把他们堵在门外，搅乱他们的步骤。就在这左近——多半近得很——藏着您最怕的那个人。倘若我们制住这三个看守，他们就得重新盘算方略，那就意味着耽搁，而每拖上一个钟头都是一分便宜。麦坎先生已经查明，代管人劳登也在这桩阴谋里，而且看样子他手腕够硬，能把任何报警求法的路子捂上一阵子。但麦坎先生已经布下去兜他的后路，二十四小时之内，我们这里就该有援兵了。”

===Original===
“I do not want the help of your law,” the girl interrupted. “It will entangle me.”

===Chinese===
“我不想要你们的法律帮忙，”姑娘插话道，“它只会把我缠进去。”

===Original===
“Not a bit of it,” said Dickson cheerfully. “You see, Mem, they’ve clean lost track of the jools, and nobody knows where they are but me. I’m a truthful man, but I’ll lie like a packman if I’m asked questions. For the rest, it’s a question of kidnapping, I understand, and that’s a thing that’s not to be allowed. My advice is to go to our beds and get a little sleep while there’s a chance of it. The Gorbals Die-Hards are grand watchdogs.”

===Chinese===
“一点儿也不会，”迪克森高高兴兴地说。“您瞧，夫人，那帮人对首饰的下落彻底两眼一抹黑，除了俺，谁也不知道它们在哪儿。俺本是句句实话的人，可要是有人盘问，俺扯起谎来赛过走街串巷的货郎。至于旁的，俺明白，这说到底是一桩绑架案，而绑架这号事是万万容不得的。俺的主意是：趁现在有得睡，都上床去打一个盹。戈尔巴尔死硬帮可是顶呱呱的看门狗。”

===Original===
This view sounded so reasonable that it was at once acted upon. The ladies’ chamber was next door to the smoking-room﻿—what had been the old schoolroom. Heritage arranged with Saskia that the lamp was to be kept burning low, and that on no account were they to move unless summoned by him. Then he and Dickson made their way to the hall, where there was a faint glimmer from the moon in the upper unshuttered windows﻿—enough to reveal the figure of Wee Jaikie on duty at the foot of the staircase. They ascended to the second floor, where, in a large room above the hall, Heritage had bestowed his pack. He had managed to open a fold of the shutters, and there was sufficient light to see two big mahogany bedsteads without mattresses or bedclothes, and wardrobes and chests of drawers sheeted in holland. Outside the wind was rising again, but the rain had stopped. Angry watery clouds scurried across the heavens.

===Chinese===
这主意听着如此在理，当即就照办了。两位女眷的卧房在吸烟室隔壁——原先是宅子里的儿童书房。赫里蒂奇同萨斯基亚讲定：灯捻得低低的、一直点着；非他来唤，她们千万不可挪动。然后他和迪克森进了大厅，楼上未关百叶的窗户透进一丝朦胧的月光——刚好照出小杰基在楼梯脚下值岗的身影。他们上了二楼，在大厅正上方的一间大屋里，赫里蒂奇卸下了他的背包。他设法掀开百叶窗的一页，光线刚好够看清两张不带褥垫被褥的红木大床，还有罩着荷兰麻布防尘罩的衣柜和五斗橱。外面风又起了，雨却停了。含怒的湿云在天空里疾驰。

===Original===
Dickson made a pillow of his waterproof, stretched himself on one of the bedsteads and, so quiet was his conscience and so weary his body from the buffetings of the past days, was almost instantly asleep. It seemed to him that he had scarcely closed his eyes when he was awakened by Dougal’s hand pinching his shoulder. He gathered that the moon was setting, for the room was pitchy dark.

===Chinese===
迪克森把雨衣卷成枕头，往一张床架上一躺——他的良心这样安稳，身子又被这几天的风浪折腾得这样疲乏——转眼就睡着了。他觉得自己眼睛才合上，就被杜加尔捏肩膀的手掐醒了。他估摸月亮正在落，因为屋里黑得像锅底。

===Original===
“The three o’ them is approachin’ the kitchen door,” whispered the Chieftain. “I seen them from a spyhole I made out o’ a ventilator.”

===Chinese===
“那三个家伙正朝厨房门摸过来，”首领咬着耳朵说。“俺从一处通风口上做的窥孔里瞧见的。”

===Original===
“Is it barricaded?” asked Heritage, who had apparently not been asleep.

===Chinese===
“堵上了吗？”赫里蒂奇问，他显然压根儿没睡。

===Original===
“Ay, but I’ve thought o’ a far better plan. Why should we keep them out? They’ll be safer inside. Listen! We might manage to get them in one at a time. If they can’t get in at the kitchen door, they’ll send one o’ them round to get in by another door and open to them. That gives us a chance to get them separated, and lock them up. There’s walth o’ closets and hidy-holes all over the place, each with good doors and good keys to them. Supposin’ we get the three o’ them shut up﻿—the others, when they come, will have nobody to guide them. Of course some time or other the three will break out, but it may be ower late for them. At present we’re besieged and they’re roamin’ the country. Would it no’ be far better if they were the ones lockit up and we were goin’ loose?”

===Chinese===
“堵是堵了，可俺想出了个好得多的主意。干吗非把他们挡在外头？他们进了里头反倒更安稳。听我说！咱们可以设法放他们一个一个进来。厨房门进不来，他们就会派一个绕到别的门进去，回头再给他们开。这就给了咱们机会，把他们拆散了，锁起来。这宅子里满处都是壁柜和藏人的小黑间，个个配着结实的门和结实的钥匙。设想咱们把那三个全关了——其余的人到了，就没个人给他们引路了。当然，迟早那三个会闯出来，可到那时候，兴许就太迟了。眼下是咱们挨围，他们在乡里游荡。要是倒个个儿，他们被锁着，咱们逍遥自在，岂不好得多？”

===Original===
“Supposing they don’t come in one at a time?” Dickson objected.

===Chinese===
“要是他们偏不一个一个来呢？”迪克森提出异议。

===Original===
“We’ll make them,” said Dougal firmly. “There’s no time to waste. Are ye for it?”

===Chinese===
“那咱们就逼他们一个一个来。”杜加尔斩钉截铁。“没工夫磨蹭了。你们干不干？”

===Original===
“Yes,” said Heritage. “Who’s at the kitchen door?”

===Chinese===
“干。”赫里蒂奇说。“厨房门口派的是谁？”

===Original===
“Peter Paterson. I told him no’ to whistle, but to wait on me.﻿ ﻿… Keep your boots off. Ye’re better in your stockin’ feet. Wait you in the hall and see ye’re well hidden, for likely whoever comes in will have a lantern. Just you keep quiet unless I give ye a cry. I’ve planned it a’ out, and we’re ready for them.”

===Chinese===
“彼得·帕特森。俺告诉他别吹哨，单等俺的令。……把靴子脱了。光穿袜子利索。你们俩在大厅里候着，务必藏严实，因为进来的人八成提着灯笼。俺不喊你们，你们就只管屏住不动。这一整套俺都盘算好了，咱们就等着他们上门。”

===Original===
Dougal disappeared, and Dickson and Heritage, with their boots tied round their necks by their laces, crept out to the upper landing. The hall was impenetrably dark, but full of voices, for the wind was talking in the ceiling beams, and murmuring through the long passages. The walls creaked and muttered and little bits of plaster fluttered down. The noise was an advantage for the game of hide-and-seek they proposed to play, but it made it hard to detect the enemy’s approach. Dickson, in order to get properly wakened, adventured as far as the smoking-room. It was black with night, but below the door of the adjacent room a faint line of light showed where the Princess’s lamp was burning. He advanced to the window, and heard distinctly a foot on the gravel path that led to the verandah. This sent him back to the hall in search of Dougal, whom he encountered in the passage. That boy could certainly see in the dark, for he caught Dickson’s wrist without hesitation.

===Chinese===
杜加尔走了。迪克森和赫里蒂奇用鞋带把靴子挂在脖子上，蹑手蹑脚摸到楼梯顶的平台上。大厅黑得密不透风，却满是你言我语，因为风正在天花板的大梁里说话，在长长的走廊里低吟。墙壁咯吱作响，嘟嘟囔囔，小片小片的灰泥簌簌落下。这一片响动对他们要玩的捉迷藏倒是个有利条件，却也让敌人的逼近难以察觉。迪克森为了把神醒透，壮着胆子一直摸到了吸烟室。那里夜色如墨，但隔壁房门底下透出一线微光，表明公主的灯还亮着。他走到窗前，清清楚楚听见通往游廊的砾石小径上响起了脚步声。这把他赶回大厅去找杜加尔，正巧在走廊里撞上了。那小子黑地里准是真看得见东西，因为他毫不犹豫地一把攥住了迪克森的手腕。

===Original===
“We’ve got Spittal in the wine-cellar,” he whispered triumphantly. “The kitchen door was barricaded, and when they tried it, it wouldn’t open. ‘Bide here,’ says Dobson to Spittal, ‘and we’ll go round by another door and come back and open to ye.’ So off they went, and by that time Peter Paterson and me had the barricade down. As we expected, Spittal tried the key again and it opens quite easy. He comes in and locks it behind him, and, Dobson having took away the lantern, he gropes his way very carefu’ towards the kitchen. There’s a point where the wine-cellar door and the scullery door are aside each other. He should have taken the second, but I had it shut so he takes the first. Peter Paterson gave him a wee shove and he fell down the two-three steps into the cellar, and we turned the key on him. Yon cellar has a grand door and no windies.”

===Chinese===
“斯皮特尔已经叫俺们关进酒窖了。”他咬着耳朵，声音里透着得意。“厨房门堵死了，他们一推，纹丝不动。‘你在这儿候着，’多布森对斯皮特尔说，‘我们绕别的门进去，回头给你开门。’他们就走了。他们前脚一走，俺跟彼得·帕特森后脚就把堵的东西撤了。果不其然，斯皮特尔又拿钥匙来试门，一开就开。他进来，反手把门锁上；多布森又把灯笼拎走了，他就一步一蹭，小心翼翼摸着黑往厨房去。有个地方，酒窖的门和洗碗间的门并排挨着。他本该走第二扇，俺把那扇关了，他就走了第一扇。彼得·帕特森轻轻一搡，他咕咚滚下两三级台阶，栽进酒窖，俺们咔哒一声给他上了锁。那酒窖的门没得挑，连个窗户都没有。”

===Original===
“And Dobson and Léon are at the verandah door? With a light?”

===Chinese===
“那么多布森和莱昂在游廊门口？带着灯？”

===Original===
“Thomas Yownie’s on duty there. Ye can trust him. Ye’ll no fickle Thomas Yownie.”

===Chinese===
“托马斯·尤尼在那儿站岗。您放一百个心。谁也糊弄不了托马斯·尤尼。”

===Original===
The next minutes were for Dickson a delirium of excitement not unpleasantly shot with flashes of doubt and fear. As a child he had played hide-and-seek, and his memory had always cherished the delights of the game. But how marvellous to play it thus in a great empty house, at dark of night, with the heaven filled with tempest, and with death or wounds as the stakes!

===Chinese===
接下来那几分钟，对迪克森是一场兴奋的狂热，中间还不时窜过几丝疑虑和恐惧，倒也不算难当。小时候他玩过捉迷藏，记忆里一直珍藏着那游戏的快活。可如今是在一座空荡荡的大宅里玩它，时值深夜，满天风暴，押上的赌注是死是伤——这该多么了不得！

===Original===
He took refuge in a corner where a tapestry curtain and the side of a Dutch awmry gave him shelter, and from where he stood he could see the garden-room and the beginning of the tiled passage which led to the verandah door. That is to say, he could have seen these things if there had been any light, which there was not. He heard the soft flitting of bare feet, for a delicate sound is often audible in a din when a loud noise is obscured. Then a gale of wind blew towards him, as from an open door, and far away gleamed the flickering light of a lantern.

===Chinese===
他躲进一个角落，一挂花毯帘子和一口荷兰式食橱的侧壁替他作了掩护，从他站的地方望出去，能看见花园屋，以及通往游廊门的瓷砖走廊的起点。也就是说，倘若有光，这些他本可以看见，可偏偏没有光。他听见光脚板轻悄掠过的声息——在一片喧闹里，只要更大的响动被遮住了，细微的声音反而往往听得见。跟着一阵狂风朝他扑来，像是从一扇敞开的门里灌进来的，远处闪动着一星灯笼的微光。

===Original===
Suddenly the light disappeared and there was a clatter on the floor and a breaking of glass. Either the wind or Thomas Yownie.

===Chinese===
光忽然灭了，接着地板上一阵哐啷，还有玻璃碎裂之声。不是风，就是托马斯·尤尼。

===Original===
The verandah door was shut, a match spluttered and the lantern was relit. Dobson and Léon came into the hall, both clad in long mackintoshes which glistened from the weather. Dobson halted and listened to the wind howling in the upper spaces. He cursed it bitterly, looked at his watch, and then made an observation which woke the liveliest interest in Dickson lurking beside the awmry and Heritage ensconced in the shadow of a window-seat.

===Chinese===
游廊的门关上了，一根火柴嗤地一响，灯笼重新点着。多布森和莱昂走进大厅，两人都裹着被雨水打得发亮的长雨衣。多布森站住脚，听了听高空中呼啸的风。他咬牙切齿地咒骂了一句，看了看表，然后开了一句口——这一句让守在食橱旁的迪克森和藏身窗座暗影里的赫里蒂奇，登时来了精神。

===Original===
“He’s late. He should have been here five minutes syne. It would be a dirty road for his car.”

===Chinese===
“他来迟了。搁在五分钟前，他就该到了。他坐车来，这一路泥泞有他受的。”

===Original===
So the Unknown was coming that night. The news made Dickson the more resolved to get the watchers under lock and key before reinforcements arrived, and so put grit in their wheels. Then his party must escape﻿—flee anywhere so long as it was far from Dalquharter.

===Chinese===
这么说，那位神秘人物当夜就要到了。这消息让迪克森愈发铁了心：务必赶在增援到达之前，把这几个看守锁进笼子，往他们的车轮里撒沙子。然后他这一伙人必须脱身——逃到哪里都行，只要离达尔夸特远远的。

===Original===
“You stop here,” said Dobson, “I’ll go down and let Spidel in. We want another lamp. Get the one that the women use and for God’s sake get a move on.”

===Chinese===
“你在这儿待着，”多布森说，“我下去放斯皮德尔进来。还得再要一盏灯。把那两个女人用的那盏拿来，看在上帝分上，手脚麻利点！”

===Original===
The sound of his feet died in the kitchen passage and then rung again on the stone stairs. Dickson’s ear of faith heard also the soft patter of naked feet as the Die-Hards preceded and followed him. He was delivering himself blind and bound into their hands.

===Chinese===
他的脚步声在厨房走廊里消逝，随即又在石阶上铮铮地响了起来。迪克森凭信心之耳还听得见，死硬帮众人在他前后赤脚疾行的轻响。他正蒙着眼、捆着手，把自己亲手送进他们的掌心。

===Original===
For a minute or two there was no sound but the wind, which had found a loose chimney cowl on the roof and screwed out of it an odd sound like the drone of a bagpipe. Dickson, unable to remain any longer in one place, moved into the centre of the hall, believing that Léon had gone to the smoking-room. It was a dangerous thing to do, for suddenly a match was lit a yard from him. He had the sense to drop low, and so was out of the main glare of the light. The man with the match apparently had no more, judging by his execrations. Dickson stood stock still, longing for the wind to fall so that he might hear the sound of the fellow’s boots on the stone floor. He gathered that they were moving towards the smoking-room.

===Chinese===
有一两分钟光景，除了风声再无别的动静——风在屋顶上寻着了一只松脱的烟囱风帽，从里面拧出一股怪响，像风笛的嗡鸣。迪克森再也按捺不住待在原地，挪到了大厅中央，他以为莱昂到吸烟室去了。这一步险得很，因为忽然间，离他一码远的地方划亮了一根火柴。他还算机灵，立刻蹲低，避开了火光的主要照面。听那咒骂声，划火柴的人手里的火柴显然用尽了。迪克森钉在原地一动不动，只巴望风势歇一歇，好让他听见那家伙的靴子蹭过石地板的声响。他听出他们正朝吸烟室挪动。

===Original===
“Heritage,” he whispered as loud as he dared, but there was no answer.

===Chinese===
“赫里蒂奇。”他放大了胆子悄声唤道，可没人应声。

===Original===
Then suddenly a moving body collided with him. He jumped a step back and then stood at attention, “Is that you, Dobson?” a voice asked.

===Chinese===
忽然间，一个移动的身躯撞上了他。他蹦退一步，随即直挺挺立定。“是你吗，多布森？”一个声音问。

===Original===
Now behold the occasional advantage of a nickname. Dickson thought he was being addressed as “Dogson” after the Poet’s fashion. Had he dreamed it was Léon he would not have replied, but fluttered off into the shadows and so missed a piece of vital news.

===Chinese===
且看诨名偶尔才有的好处。迪克森只当这一声是冲他来的——是照诗人的老叫法唤他“狗逊”。他若料到喊话的是莱昂，就绝不会搭腔，只会一抖身遁入暗影，那便要错过一条性命攸关的消息了。

===Original===
“Ay, it’s me,” he whispered.

===Chinese===
“哎，是俺。”他悄声应道。

===Original===
His voice and accent were Scotch, like Dobson’s, and Léon suspected nothing.

===Chinese===
他的嗓音和腔调都是苏格兰的，跟多布森一模一样，莱昂毫不起疑。

===Original===
“I do not like this wind,” he grumbled. “The Captain’s letter said at dawn, but there is no chance of the Danish brig making your little harbour in this weather. She must lie off and land the men by boats. That I do not like. It is too public.”

===Chinese===
“我不喜欢这风。”他咕哝道，“船长信里说的是拂晓，可是这样的天气，丹麦双桅船说什么也开不进你们这座小港。它只能在近海停着，再用小艇把人送上岸。这太招摇了。我不喜欢。”

===Original===
The news﻿—tremendous news, for it told that the newcomers would come by sea, which had never before entered Dickson’s head﻿—so interested him that he stood dumb and ruminating. The silence made the Belgian suspect; he put out a hand and felt a waterproofed arm which might have been Dobson’s. But the height of the shoulder proved that it was not the burly innkeeper. There was an oath, a quick movement, and Dickson went down with a knee on his chest and two hands at his throat.

===Chinese===
这消息——天大的消息，因为它说明新来的那拨人将从海上来，这是迪克森从前连想都不曾想过的——让他听得出了神，哑立当场，只顾寻思。这阵沉默让那比利时人起了疑心；他伸出一只手，摸到一条穿雨衣的胳膊，倒像是多布森的。可肩膀的高度证明，这不是那位膀大腰圆的客栈店主。一声咒骂，一个迅疾的动作，迪克森被掀翻在地，一膝顶住他的胸口，两手扼住了他的喉咙。

===Original===
“Heritage,” he gasped. “Help!”

===Chinese===
“赫里蒂奇，”他喘着气喊，“救命！”

===Original===
There was a sound of furniture scraped violently on the floor. A gurgle from Dickson served as a guide, and the Poet suddenly cascaded over the combatants. He felt for a head, found Léon’s, and gripped the neck so savagely that the owner loosened his hold on Dickson. The last-named found himself being buffeted violently by heavy-shod feet which seemed to be manoeuvring before an unseen enemy. He rolled out of the road and encountered another pair of feet, this time unshod. Then came a sound of a concussion, as if metal or wood had struck some part of a human frame, and then a stumble and fall.

===Chinese===
地板上传来家具被狠狠拖刮的声响。迪克森喉间一声咯咯，正好当了路标，诗人当即如瀑布一般泼向那两个缠斗的人。他摸到一个脑袋，是莱昂的，便狠狠掐住那脖子，掐得那家伙松开了迪克森。迪克森自己则被沉重的靴脚一顿好踢，那双脚仿佛正对着一个看不见的敌手腾挪。他滚出战圈，又撞上另一双脚，这回是光脚。接着传来一记闷响，像是金属或木头磕上了人身哪一处，随后一个趔趄，扑地倒了。

===Original===
After that a good many things all seemed to happen at once. There was a sudden light, which showed Léon blinking with a short loaded life-preserver in his hand, and Heritage prone in front of him on the floor. It also showed Dickson the figure of Dougal, and more than one Die-Hard in the background. The light went out as suddenly as it had appeared. There was a whistle, and a hoarse “Come on, men,” and then for two seconds there was a desperate silent combat. It ended with Léon’s head meeting the floor so violently that its possessor became oblivious of further proceedings. He was dragged into a cubbyhole, which had once been used for coats and rugs, and the door locked on him. Then the light sprang forth again. It revealed Dougal and five Die-Hards, somewhat the worse for wear; it revealed also Dickson squatted with outspread waterproof very like a sitting hen.

===Chinese===
此后一大摊事情仿佛一齐发生了。忽然亮起一道光，照见莱昂眨巴着眼睛，手里攥着一根灌铅的短棍，赫里蒂奇正仰面倒在他跟前的地板上。光里也让迪克森看见了杜加尔的身影，还有背景里不止一名的死硬帮。光又像来时一样骤然熄灭。一声哨响，一声嘶哑的“上啊，弟兄们”，接着是两秒钟拼死的闷斗。结局是莱昂的脑袋狠狠磕上地板，磕得那颗脑袋的主人对后续的一切程序人事不省。他被拖进一个从前挂外套、放毯子的小间，门上一把锁。随后灯光又猛地亮起，照出杜加尔和五名死硬帮，一个个都有几分挂彩；也照出迪克森——他撑开雨衣蹲在地上，活像一只抱窝的母鸡。

===Original===
“Where’s Dobson?” he asked.

===Chinese===
“多布森在哪儿？”他问。

===Original===
“In the boiler-house,” and for once Dougal’s gravity had laughter in it. “Govey Dick! but yon was a fecht! Me and Peter Paterson and Wee Jaikie started it, but it was the whole company afore the end. Are ye better, Jaikie?”

===Chinese===
“在锅炉房里。”杜加尔那份一贯的凝重，头一回透出了笑意。“乖乖，迪克！方才那可真叫一场恶战！开头是俺、彼得·帕特森和小杰基三人上的，到末了全连都压了上去。杰基，你好点儿没有？”

===Original===
“Ay, I’m better,” said a pallid midget.

===Chinese===
“嗯，好点儿了。”一个面无血色的小不点儿答道。

===Original===
“He kickit Jaikie in the stomach and Jaikie was seeck,” Dougal explained. “That’s the three accounted for. Now they’re safe for five hours at the least. I think mysel’ that Dobson will be the first to get out, but he’ll have his work letting out the others. Now, I’m for flittin’ to the old Tower. They’ll no ken where we are for a long time, and anyway yon place will be far easier to defend. Without they kindle a fire and smoke us out, I don’t see how they’ll beat us. Our provisions are a’ there, and there’s a grand well o’ water inside. Forbye there’s the road down the rocks that’ll keep our communications open.﻿ ﻿… But what’s come to Mr. Heritage?”

===Chinese===
“他一脚踹在杰基肚子上，杰基吐了。”杜加尔解释道。“这就把三个全数清了账。眼下他们安安稳稳，至少得关上五个钟头。俺自己琢磨，头一个闯出来的准是多布森，可他要放出那两个，也得费老大一番劲。现在，俺主张挪窝，挪进老塔里去。他们一时半会儿摸不着咱们的去向，再说那地方守起来容易得多。除非他们生火把咱们熏出来，俺看不出他们凭什么赢咱们。咱们的口粮全在那儿，塔里还有一口顶好的水井。另外还有一条下岩石的小路，保着咱们的交通不断。……咦，赫里蒂奇先生这是怎么了？”

===Original===
Dickson to his shame had forgotten all about his friend. The Poet lay very quiet with his head on one side and his legs crooked limply. Blood trickled over his eyes from an ugly scar on his forehead. Dickson felt his heart and pulse and found them faint but regular. The man had got a swinging blow and might have a slight concussion; for the present he was unconscious.

===Chinese===
迪克森惭愧得很，他竟把朋友忘了个干净。诗人一动不动地躺着，头歪向一边，双腿软塌塌地蜷着。血从他额头一道狰狞的伤口里渗出来，漫过了眼睛。迪克森探他的心口和脉搏，都微弱，但还匀整。他是挨了狠狠的一记，怕是有些轻微的脑震荡；眼下人事不省。

===Original===
“All the more reason why we should flit,” said Dougal. “What d’ye say, Mr. McCunn?”

===Chinese===
“那就更得赶紧挪窝了。”杜加尔说。“您怎么说，麦坎先生？”

===Original===
“Flit, of course, but further than the old Tower. What’s the time?” He lifted Heritage’s wrist and saw from his watch that it was half-past three. “Mercy! It’s nearly morning. Afore we put these blagyirds away, they were conversing, at least Léon and Dobson were. They said that they expected somebody every moment, but that the car would be late. We’ve still got that Somebody to tackle. Then Léon spoke to me in the dark, thinking I was Dobson, and cursed the wind, saying it would keep the Danish brig from getting in at dawn as had been intended. D’you see what that means? The worst of the lot, the ones the ladies are in terror of, are coming by sea. Ay, and they can return by sea. We thought that the attack would be by land, and that even if they succeeded we could hang on to their heels and follow them, till we got them stopped. But that’s impossible! If they come in from the water, they can go out by the water, and there’ll never be more heard tell of the ladies or of you or me.”

===Chinese===
“挪窝，自然要挪，可不能只挪到老塔为止。几点了？”他抬起赫里蒂奇的手腕，就着他的表看出是三点半。“我的天！都快天亮了。把这几个恶棍关起来之前，他们还聊过天——至少莱昂和多布森聊了。他们说眼巴巴候着一位大驾，只是车要晚点。那位大驾，咱们还得分神对付。后来莱昂在黑地里错把俺当成多布森，跟俺念叨，咒那阵风，说风要误了丹麦双桅船照原定拂晓进港。你们可看明白了？最凶的那一拨——两位夫人吓得要死的那一拨——是从海上来。是啊，他们还能从海上回去。咱们原先估摸，进攻会从陆路来，就算他们得手，咱们也能咬住他们的脚跟一路追下去，直到把他们截下来。可这办不到了！他们从水上来，就能从水上走，往后两位夫人，还有你们和俺，就再也听不见一丝音信了。”

===Original===
Dougal’s face was once again sunk in gloom. “What’s your plan, then?”

===Chinese===
杜加尔的脸重新沉进了阴霾。“那您打算怎么办？”

===Original===
“We must get the ladies away from here﻿—away inland, far from the sea. The rest of us must stand a siege in the old Tower, so that the enemy will think we’re all there. Please God we’ll hold out long enough for help to arrive. But we mustn’t hang about here. There’s the man Dobson mentioned﻿—he may come any second, and we want to be away first. Get the ladder, Dougal.﻿ ﻿… Four of you take Mr. Heritage, and two come with me and carry the ladies’ things. It’s no’ raining, but the wind’s enough to take the wings off a seagull.”

===Chinese===
“必须把两位夫人从这儿弄走——弄到内陆去，离海远远的。咱们其余的人守在老塔里顶一场围攻，让敌人只当咱们全在那儿。上帝保佑，咱们撑得到援兵来的那一刻。可一刻也不能在这儿耽搁了。多布森念叨过一个人——他随时可能到，咱们得抢先一步撤走。搬梯子去，杜加尔。……你们四个抬赫里蒂奇先生，两个跟俺来，搬夫人们的东西。雨倒是不下了，可这风，大得能把海鸥的翅膀都掀下来。”

===Original===
Dickson roused Saskia and her cousin, bidding them be ready in ten minutes. Then with the help of the Die-Hards he proceeded to transport the necessary supplies﻿—the stove, oil, dishes, clothes and wraps; more than one journey was needed of small boys, hidden under clouds of baggage. When everything had gone he collected the keys, behind which, in various quarters of the house, three gaolers fumed impotently, and gave them to Wee Jaikie to dispose of in some secret nook. Then he led the two ladies to the verandah, the elder cross and sleepy, the younger alert at the prospect of movement.

===Chinese===
迪克森唤醒萨斯基亚和她表姐，吩咐她们十分钟里收拾停当。随后他在死硬帮众人帮衬下，着手转运必要的物资——炉子、灯油、碗碟、衣裳和披肩；小家伙们一头扎在行李堆底下，来回跑了好几趟。东西都运完，他收齐了全部钥匙——宅子各处的门后，三名狱卒正干着急没脾气——把它们交给小杰基，叫他藏到什么隐秘的角落里去。然后他引着两位夫人来到游廊上：年长的那位又烦躁又困，年轻的那位一听要挪动，立马来了精神。

===Original===
“Tell me again,” she said. “You have locked all the three up, and they are now the imprisoned?”

===Chinese===
“再讲一遍给我听，”她说，“你们把那三个全都锁起来了，如今他们成了被囚的一方？”

===Original===
“Well, it was the boys that, properly speaking, did the locking up.”

===Chinese===
“呃，严格说来，动手锁人的是那帮小子。”

===Original===
“It is a great﻿—how do you say?﻿—a turning of the tables. Ah﻿—what is that?”

===Chinese===
“这真是一场大大的——你们怎么说来着？——反客为主。啊——那是什么？”

===Original===
At the end of the verandah there was a clattering down of pots which could not be due to the wind, since the place was sheltered. There was still only the faintest hint of light, and black night still lurked in the crannies. Followed another fall of pots, as from a clumsy intruder, and then a man appeared, clear against the glass door by which the path descended to the rock garden.

===Chinese===
游廊尽头传来一阵花盆坠地的哐啷声——这绝不可能是风刮的，因为那地方有遮蔽。天光至多才有一丝最淡的迹象，黑夜仍潜伏在犄角旮旯里。跟着又是一阵花盆落地，像是个笨手笨脚的不速之客，随后一个人影出现了，在玻璃门上映得清清楚楚——门外的小径就从那里通往岩石园。

===Original===
It was the fourth man, whom the three prisoners had awaited. Dickson had no doubt at all about his identity. He was that villain from whom all the others took their orders, the man whom the Princess shuddered at. Before starting he had loaded his pistol. Now he tugged it from his waterproof pocket, pointed it at the other and fired.

===Chinese===
来的正是那第四个人，三名囚徒翘首等候的那一个。对他的身份，迪克森没有半点疑问。他就是那帮恶棍个个听命于他的元凶，是公主一想起来就打寒噤的那个人。出发之前，他早已给手枪上了膛。此刻他把手枪从雨衣口袋里一拔，对准来人，开了火。

===Original===
The man seemed to be hit, for he spun round and clapped a hand to his left arm. Then he fled through the door, which he left open.

===Chinese===
那人像是中了枪，只见他原地打了个转，一只手捂住左臂。随即他夺门而逃，门也没顾上关。

===Original===
Dickson was after him like a hound. At the door he saw him running and raised his pistol for another shot. Then he dropped it, for he saw something in the crouching, dodging figure which was familiar.

===Chinese===
迪克森像猎犬一样追了上去。到了门口，他望见那人正一路奔逃，便举起手枪想再补一枪。随即又放下了，因为那缩着身子、左躲右闪的身影，有几分他眼熟的东西。

===Original===
“A mistake,” he explained to Jaikie when he returned. “But the shot wasn’t wasted. I’ve just had a good try at killing the factor!”

===Chinese===
“看走眼了，”他回来后向杰基解释道。“可这一枪没白放——俺方才结结实实地试了一回收拾那位代管人！”

## X. Deals with an Escape and a Journey / 十、记一场逃亡与一段旅程

===Original===
Five scouts’ lanterns burned smokily in the ground room of the keep when Dickson ushered his charges through its cavernous door. The lights flickered in the gusts that swept after them and whistled through the slits of window, so that the place was full of monstrous shadows, and its accustomed odour of mould and disuse was changed to a salty freshness. Upstairs on the first floor Thomas Yownie had deposited the ladies’ baggage, and was busy making beds out of derelict iron bedsteads and the wraps brought from their room. On the ground floor on a heap of litter covered by an old scout’s blanket lay Heritage, with Dougal in attendance.

===Chinese===
迪克森领着他照管的两位女眷穿过堡楼那洞穴般的大门时，五盏侦察兵的提灯正在底层的大屋里冒着烟燃着。灯光在跟着他们卷进门来的阵风里摇曳，风又在细缝似的窗洞间呜呜作响，于是满屋都是张牙舞爪的怪影，那惯常的霉腐和荒置的气味，也换成了带咸味的新鲜气息。楼上二层，托马斯·尤尼已经安顿好两位夫人的行李，正忙着用废弃的铁床架和她们房里带来的披肩铺床。底层一堆盖着侦察兵旧毯子的杂物上，躺着赫里蒂奇，杜加尔在一旁照看。

===Original===
The Chieftain had washed the blood from the Poet’s brow and the touch of cold water was bringing back his senses. Saskia with a cry flew to him, and waved off Dickson who had fetched one of the bottles of liqueur brandy. She slipped a hand inside his shirt and felt the beating of his heart. Then her slim fingers ran over his forehead.

===Chinese===
首领已经洗净了诗人额头上的血，冷水的刺激正把他的神智一点点唤回来。萨斯基亚惊呼一声，飞奔过去，挥手拦住了取来一瓶陈年白兰地的迪克森。她把一只手探进他的衬衫，探他的心跳，随后纤细的手指抚过他的额头。

===Original===
“A bad blow,” she muttered, “but I do not think he is ill. There is no fracture. When I nursed in the Alexander Hospital I learnt much about head wounds. Do not give him cognac if you value his life.”

===Chinese===
“伤得不轻，”她低声道，“可我想他没有大碍。没有骨折。我在亚历山大医院（Alexander Hospital）当护士时，学过不少头部创伤的知识。倘若你们还珍惜他的性命，就别给他干邑。”

===Original===
Heritage was talking now and with strange tongues. Phrases like “lined digesters” and “free sulphurous acid” came from his lips. He implored someone to tell him if “the first cook” was finished, and he upbraided someone else for “cooling off” too fast.

===Chinese===
此刻赫里蒂奇说起话来了，说的却是些陌生的行话。“衬里蒸煮锅”“游离亚硫酸”之类的字眼从他唇间冒出来。他恳求什么人告诉他“头道蒸煮”完了没有，又斥责别的什么“冷却”得太快。

===Original===
The girl raised her head. “But I fear he has become mad,” she said.

===Chinese===
姑娘抬起头来。“我怕他是疯了。”她说。

===Original===
“Wheesht, Mem,” said Dickson, who recognised the jargon. “He’s a paper maker.”

===Chinese===
“嘘，夫人，”迪克森说，他听得懂这门行话。“他是造纸的。”

===Original===
Saskia sat down on the litter and lifted his head so that it rested on her breast. Dougal at her bidding brought a certain case from her baggage, and with swift, capable hands she made a bandage and rubbed the wound with ointment before tying it up. Then her fingers seemed to play about his temples and along his cheeks and neck. She was the professional nurse now, absorbed, sexless. Heritage ceased to babble, his eyes shut and he was asleep.

===Chinese===
萨斯基亚在杂物堆上坐下，抬起他的头，让它枕在自己怀里。杜加尔奉她的吩咐，从她的行李里取来一只小药箱，她一双敏捷利落的手打好绷带，先往伤口上搽了药膏，这才包扎起来。随后她的手指仿佛在他的太阳穴上、脸颊和颈项间轻轻游走。此刻她全然是一位心无旁骛、不辨男女的职业护士。赫里蒂奇止住了呓语，合上眼，睡了过去。

===Original===
She remained where she was, so that the Poet, when a few minutes later he woke, found himself lying with his head in her lap. She spoke first, in an imperative tone: “You are well now. Your head does not ache. You are strong again.”

===Chinese===
她仍坐在原处，于是几分钟后诗人醒来，发觉自己正枕着她的膝。她先开了口，声调是不容置疑的：“你现在好了。你的头不疼了。你又强健了。”

===Original===
“No. Yes,” he murmured. Then more clearly: “Where am I? Oh, I remember, I caught a lick on the head. What’s become of the brutes?”

===Chinese===
“不。是的。”他含糊道。随后清楚了些：“我这是在哪儿？哦，想起来了，我头上挨了一记。那几个畜生怎么样了？”

===Original===
Dickson, who had extracted food from the Mearns Street box and was pressing it on the others, replied through a mouthful of biscuit: “We’re in the old Tower. The three are lockit up in the House. Are you feeling better, Mr. Heritage?”

===Chinese===
迪克森从米恩斯街的箱子里翻出吃食，正劝大家垫垫肚子，他满嘴饼干答道：“咱们在老塔里。那三个锁在府里了。您觉得好些了吗，赫里蒂奇先生？”

===Original===
The Poet suddenly realised Saskia’s position and the blood came to his pale face. He got to his feet with an effort and held out a hand to the girl. “I’m all right now, I think. Only a little dicky on my legs. A thousand thanks, Princess. I’ve given you a lot of trouble.”

===Chinese===
诗人猛然醒悟到萨斯基亚的姿势，血色涌上他苍白的脸。他费力地站起身，朝姑娘伸出一只手。“我想我现在没事了。只是两腿还有点发虚。多谢了，公主。给您添了这许多麻烦。”

===Original===
She smiled at him tenderly. “You say that when you have risked your life for me.”

===Chinese===
她温柔地朝他微笑。“您为我冒了性命之险，倒还说这样的话。”

===Original===
“There’s no time to waste,” the relentless Dougal broke in. “Comin’ over here, I heard a shot. What was it?”

===Chinese===
“没工夫磨蹭了，”那不肯通融的杜加尔插进来。“往这儿来的路上，俺听见一声枪响。是怎么回事？”

===Original===
“It was me,” said Dickson. “I was shootin’ at the factor.”

===Chinese===
“是俺，”迪克森说。“俺朝那位代管人开了枪。”

===Original===
“Did ye hit him?”

===Chinese===
“打中没有？”

===Original===
“I think so, but I’m sorry to say not badly. When I last saw him he was running too quick for a sore hurt man. When I fired I thought it was the other man﻿—the one they were expecting.”

===Chinese===
“俺琢磨打中了，可惜俺得说，伤得不重。俺最后一眼瞧见他时，他跑得飞快，不像受了重伤的人。俺开枪那会儿，还当他是另外那一个——他们正等着的那一个呢。”

===Original===
Dickson marvelled at himself, yet his speech was not bravado but the honest expression of his mind. He was keyed up to a mood in which he feared nothing very much, certainly not the laws of his country. If he fell in with the Unknown, he was entirely resolved, if his Maker permitted him, to do murder as being the simplest and justest solution. And if in the pursuit of this laudable intention he happened to wing lesser game it was no fault of his.

===Chinese===
迪克森自己也暗暗称奇，可他这番话并非虚张声势，而是他心里实打实的表露。他正绷在一股心气上，什么都不大怕了，尤其不怕他国家的法律。倘若撞上那位神秘人物，他已经拿定了主意——只要造物主容他下手——便痛痛快快行一回凶，因为那是最简单也最公道的解决办法。而在追求这项可嘉意图的途中，万一捎带着打伤几只小些的猎物，那也怨不得他。

===Original===
“Well, it’s a pity ye didn’t get him,” said Dougal, “him being what we ken him to be.﻿ ﻿… I’m for holding a council o’ war, and considerin’ the whole position. So far we haven’t done that badly. We’ve shifted our base without serious casualties. We’ve got a far better position to hold, for there’s too many ways into yon Hoose, and here there’s just one. Besides, we’ve fickled the enemy. They’ll take some time to find out where we’ve gone. But, mind you, we can’t count on their staying long shut up. Dobson’s no’ safe in the boiler-house, for there’s a skylight far up and he’ll see it when the light comes and maybe before. So we’d better get our plans ready. A word with ye, Mr. McCunn,” and he led Dickson aside.

===Chinese===
“唉，可惜你没撂倒他，”杜加尔说，“他是块什么料，咱们心里透亮。……俺主张开个军事会议，通盘斟酌局面。到眼下为止，咱们干得不算赖。咱们转移了基地，没有多大伤亡。咱们还占下一个好得多的阵地，因为进那座府里的口子太多了，这儿只有一条。再说，咱们还糊弄了敌人，他们得费上一阵子才摸得清咱们的去向。可是记着，别指望他们长久关得住。多布森在锅炉房里并不稳当，因为高头顶上有一扇天窗，天一亮他就会瞧见，兴许还更早。所以咱们最好先把章程备好。借一步说话，麦坎先生。”说着他把迪克森领到一旁。

===Original===
“D’ye ken what these blagyirds were up to,” he whispered fiercely in Dickson’s ear. “They were goin’ to pushion the lassie. How do I ken, says you? Because Thomas Yownie heard Dobson say to Lean at the scullery door, ‘Have ye got the dope?’ he says, and Lean says, ‘Ay.’ Thomas mindit the word for he had heard about it at the Picters.”

===Chinese===
“你可知道这帮流氓安的什么心？”他恶狠狠地贴着迪克森的耳朵说。“他们要给那姑娘下毒。你问俺怎么知道的？因为托马斯·尤尼听见多布森在洗碗间门口问莱恩：‘迷药弄到了没有？’莱恩答：‘弄到了。’托马斯记住了这个词，因为他在影戏院里听说过。”

===Original===
Dickson exclaimed in horror.

===Chinese===
迪克森惊骇得失声叫出来。

===Original===
“What d’ye make o’ that? I’ll tell ye. They wanted to make sure of her, but they wouldn’t have thought o’ dope unless the men they expectit were due to arrive any moment. As I see it, we’ve to face a siege not by the three but by a dozen or more, and it’ll no’ be long till it starts. Now, isn’t it a mercy we’re safe in here?”

===Chinese===
“这说明什么？俺告诉你。他们要把她稳稳攥在手里，可若不是他们等候的那些人眼看就要到，他们原想不到用迷药。照俺看，咱们要面对的不是三个人的围攻，而是十几个人的，而且为时不远了。你说，咱们安安稳稳待在这儿，岂不是老天保佑？”

===Original===
Dickson returned to the others with a grave face.

===Chinese===
迪克森板着一副凝重的脸回到众人跟前。

===Original===
“Where d’you think the new folk are coming from?” he asked.

===Chinese===
“你们估摸新来的那伙人会打哪儿来？”他问。

===Original===
Heritage answered, “From Auchenlochan, I suppose? Or perhaps down from the hills?”

===Chinese===
赫里蒂奇答道：“我猜是从奥琴洛坎来？要不就是从山里下来？”

===Original===
“You’re wrong.” And he told of Léon’s mistaken confidences to him in the darkness. “They are coming from the sea, just like the old pirates.”

===Chinese===
“你猜错了。”于是他把莱昂在黑暗里错把他当自己人而吐露的话讲了一遍。“他们要从海上来，跟老海盗一个路数。”

===Original===
“The sea,” Heritage repeated in a dazed voice.

===Chinese===
“海上。”赫里蒂奇失魂落魄地重复道。

===Original===
“Ay, the sea. Think what that means. If they had been coming by the roads, we could have kept track of them, even if they beat us, and some of these laddies could have stuck to them and followed them up till help came. It can’t be such an easy job to carry a young lady against her will along Scotch roads. But the sea’s a different matter. If they’ve got a fast boat they could be out of the Firth and away beyond the law before we could wake up a single policeman. Ay, and even if the Government took it up and warned all the ports and ships at sea, what’s to hinder them to find a hidy-hole about Ireland﻿—or Norway? I tell you, it’s a far more desperate business than I thought, and it’ll no’ do to wait on and trust that the Chief Constable will turn up afore the mischief’s done.”

===Chinese===
“是啊，海上。想想这意味着什么。他们要是走大路来，咱们就能盯住他们的踪迹，哪怕他们占了上风也一样，这些小子里的哪一个都能咬住他们一路跟到援兵来到。违背一位年轻姑娘的心意，在苏格兰的大路上硬架着她走，总归不是桩轻省差事。可海上就另当别论了。他们要是有一条快船，等咱们叫醒一名警察，他们早驶出河口湾，逃到法律够不着的地方去了。是啊，就算政府接了手，知会了所有的港口和海上的船只，又有什么拦得住他们去爱尔兰（Ireland）——或者挪威（Norway）——一带找个藏身的窟窿？俺告诉你，这桩买卖比俺原先想的凶险得多，再不能干等着，指望郡警察总长赶在大祸铸成之前从天而降了。”

===Original===
“The moral,” said Heritage, “is that there can be no surrender. We’ve got to stick it out in this old place at all costs.”

===Chinese===
“这故事的教训，”赫里蒂奇说，“便是绝不能投降。咱们得不惜一切代价，在这座老地方顶下去。”

===Original===
“No,” said Dickson emphatically. “The moral is that we must shift the ladies. We’ve got the chance while Dobson and his friends are locked up. Let’s get them as far away as we can from the sea. They’re far safer tramping the moors, and it’s no’ likely the new folk will dare to follow us.”

===Chinese===
“不对，”迪克森加重语气。“教训是必须把两位夫人转移。趁多布森和他那两个朋友还锁着，咱们正有这个机会。把她们送到离海尽可能远的地方去。她们在荒原上徒步，反倒安全得多，新来的那伙人也未必敢追。”

===Original===
“But I cannot go.” Saskia, who had been listening intently, shook her head. “I promised to wait here till my friend came. If I leave I shall never find him.”

===Chinese===
“可我不能走。”一直在凝神听着的萨斯基亚摇了摇头。“我答应过在这里等，等我的朋友到来。我一走，就再也找不到他了。”

===Original===
“If you stay you certainly never will, for you’ll be away with the ruffians. Take a sensible view, Mem. You’ll be no good to your friend or your friend to you if before night you’re rocking in a ship.”

===Chinese===
“您要是留下，那才真找不到呢，因为您会被那帮恶棍带走。往明白处想想吧，夫人。不等天黑，您要是在一条船上晃悠起来，您帮不上您朋友的忙，您朋友也帮不上您。”

===Original===
The girl shook her head again, gently but decisively. “It was our arrangement. I cannot break it. Besides, I am sure that he will come in time, for he has never failed﻿—”

===Chinese===
姑娘又摇头，轻轻地，却是决绝的。“那是我们说定的。我不能失约。再说，我确信他会及时赶到，因为他从来没有误过事——”

===Original===
There was a desperate finality about the quiet tones and the weary face with the shadow of a smile on it.

===Chinese===
那平静的声调，那张倦怠的、噙着一丝笑影的脸，都有一种破釜沉舟的决绝。

===Original===
Then Heritage spoke. “I don’t think your plan will quite do, Dogson. Supposing we all break for the hinterland and the Danish brig finds the birds flown, that won’t end the trouble. They will get on the Princess’s trail, and the whole persecution will start again. I want to see things brought to a head here and now. If we can stick it out here long enough, we may trap the whole push and rid the world of a pretty gang of miscreants. Once let them show their hand, and then, if the police are here by that time, we can jug the lot for piracy or something worse.”

===Chinese===
随后赫里蒂奇开了口。“我看你的计划行不通，狗逊。假如咱们全员逃奔内地，丹麦双桅船赶到时发现人去楼空，麻烦也不会就此了结。他们会循迹追上公主，那场迫害又要从头开始。我要让事情就在此地此刻见个分晓。只要咱们在这儿顶得够久，兴许能把那整整一伙一网打尽，替世上除掉可观的一帮恶棍。只消让他们亮出底牌，到那时警察若是到了，就能把整窝人以海盗罪或更重的罪名下狱。”

===Original===
“That’s all right,” said Dougal, “but we’d put up a better fight if we had the women off our mind. I’ve aye read that when a castle was going to be besieged the first thing was to rid get of the civilians.”

===Chinese===
“这话不差，”杜加尔说，“可要是能把女眷们从心上卸下来，咱们能打得更漂亮。俺一向读到，一座城堡眼看要被围攻，头一件事就是先把平民送出去。”

===Original===
“Sensible to the last, Dougal,” said Dickson approvingly. “That’s just what I’m saying. I’m strong for a fight, but put the ladies in a safe bit first, for they’re our weak point.”

===Chinese===
“到底还是你明白，杜加尔。”迪克森赞许道。“俺正是这个意思。俺死心塌地主张打一仗，可先得把两位夫人安置到稳妥去处，她们是咱们的软肋。”

===Original===
“Do you think that if you were fighting my enemies, I would consent to be absent?” came Saskia’s reproachful question.

===Chinese===
“你们以为，你们是在同我的敌人作战，我会肯躲开吗？”传来萨斯基亚含着责备的问话。

===Original===
“ ’Deed no, Mem,” said Dickson heartily. His martial spirit was with Heritage, but his prudence did not sleep, and he suddenly saw a way of placating both. “Just you listen to what I propose. What do we amount to? Mr. Heritage, six laddies, and myself﻿—and I’m no more used to fighting than an old wife. We’ve seven desperate villains against us, and afore night they may be seventy. We’ve a fine old castle here, but for defence we want more than stone walls﻿—we want a garrison. I tell you we must get help somewhere. Ay, but how, says you? Well, coming here I noticed a gentleman’s house away up ayont the railway and close to the hills. The laird’s maybe not at home, but there will be men there of some kind﻿—gamekeepers and woodmen and suchlike. My plan is to go there at once and ask for help. Now, it’s useless me going alone, for nobody would listen to me. They’d tell me to go back to the shop or they’d think me demented. But with you, Mem, it would be a different matter. They wouldn’t disbelieve you. So I want you to come with me and to come at once, for God knows how soon our need will be sore. We’ll leave your cousin with Mrs. Morran in the village, for bed’s the place for her, and then you and me will be off on our business.”

===Chinese===
“的确不会，夫人。”迪克森说得恳切。他的尚武之心站在赫里蒂奇一边，可他的谨慎也没有睡大觉，他忽然看出一条两全的路。“您且听俺的主张。咱们算多大的力量？赫里蒂奇先生，六个小子，加上俺——俺对打仗的历练，比一个老婆娘多不到哪儿去。同咱们作对的是七个亡命之徒，不等天黑，说不定就是七十个。咱们手里有座顶好的古堡，可要论防守，光有石头墙不够——咱们得有一支守军。俺告诉你们，咱们必须到什么地方搬救兵。是啊，可怎么搬呢，你说？是这样：来的路上，俺瞧见铁路那头、紧贴山根，有一户绅士人家的宅子。庄园主兴许不在家，可那儿总有几号男人——猎场看守、看林人之类。俺的主张是这就登门求援。只是俺一个人去白搭，没人会听俺的。他们会叫俺滚回铺子去，要不就把俺当个疯子。可有了您，夫人，那就另当别论。他们不会不信您。所以俺想请您同俺一道去，这就动身，天知道咱们的急难几时临头。您表姐就留在村里莫兰太太家，她正该上床歇着。然后您和俺就去办咱们的大事。”

===Original===
The girl looked at Heritage, who nodded. “It’s the only way,” he said. “Get every man jack you can raise, and if it’s humanly possible get a gun or two. I believe there’s time enough, for I don’t see the brig arriving in broad daylight.”

===Chinese===
姑娘望向赫里蒂奇，后者点点头。“只有这条路了。”他说。“能拉来的汉子一个不落全拉来，人力办得到的话，再弄一两杆枪。我相信时间还够，我不信那艘双桅船肯在大白天进港。”

===Original===
“D’you not?” Dickson asked rudely. “Have you considered what day this is? It’s the Sabbath, the best of days for an ill deed. There’s no kirk hereaways, and everybody in the parish will be sitting indoors by the fire.” He looked at his watch. “In half an hour it’ll be light. Haste you, Mem, and get ready. Dougal, what’s the weather?”

===Chinese===
“你不这么看？”迪克森没好气地问。“你想过今天是什么日子没有？今天是安息日，干坏事的顶好日子。这一带没有教堂，教区里的人家家都会守着炉火坐在屋里。”他看了看表。“半个钟头后就要天亮了。快着些，夫人，收拾停当。杜加尔，天气怎样？”

===Original===
The Chieftain swung open the door, and sniffed the air. The wind had fallen for the time being, and the surge of the tides below the rocks rose like the clamour of a mob. With the lull, mist and a thin drizzle had cloaked the world again.

===Chinese===
首领推开大门，嗅了嗅外面的空气。风一时歇了，岩石底下的潮声轰然升起，像乱民的鼓噪。趁着这阵风歇，雾气和蒙蒙细雨又把世界裹住了。

===Original===
To Dickson’s surprise Dougal seemed to be in good spirits. He began to sing to a hymn tune a strange ditty.

===Chinese===
令迪克森诧异的是，杜加尔像是很有些兴头。他按着一支圣歌的调子，唱起一支古怪的小曲。

===Original===
“Class-conscious we are, and class-conscious wull be
					

===Chinese===
“咱们讲阶级觉悟，往后还要一直讲——

===Original===
					Till our fit’s on the neck o’ the Boorjoyzee.”

===Chinese===
直到咱们的脚踏上布尔乔亚的脖颈。”

===Original===
“What on earth are you singing?” Dickson inquired.

===Chinese===
“你唱的这是个啥玩意儿？”迪克森问。

===Original===
Dougal grinned. “Wee Jaikie went to a Socialist Sunday school last winter because he heard they were for fechtin’ battles. Ay, and they telled him he was to jine a thing called an International, and Jaikie thought it was a fitba’ club. But when he fund out there was no magic lantern or swaree at Christmas he gie’d it the chuck. They learned him a heap o’ queer songs. That’s one.”

===Chinese===
杜加尔咧嘴一笑。“去年冬天，小杰基上过一所社会主义者主日学校，因为他听说那边专管打仗。是啊，人家还告诉他，要他参加一个叫‘国际’的名堂，杰基还当那是家足球俱乐部。可等他发现圣诞节既没有幻灯也没有游艺会，就把它给辞了。他们在那儿教会他一大堆怪歌。这就是一首。”

===Original===
“What does the last word mean?”

===Chinese===
“末尾那个词是啥意思？”

===Original===
“I don’t ken. Jaikie thought it was some kind of a draigon.”

===Chinese===
“俺不晓得。杰基琢磨着那是某种龙。”

===Original===
“It’s a daft-like thing anyway.﻿ ﻿… When’s high water?”

===Chinese===
“反正是个傻里傻气的东西。……几时满潮？”

===Original===
Dougal answered that to the best of his knowledge it fell between four and five in the afternoon.

===Chinese===
杜加尔回答说，据他所知，满潮在下午四点到五点之间。

===Original===
“Then that’s when we may expect the foreign gentry if they think to bring their boat in to the Garple foot.﻿ ﻿… Dougal, lad, I trust you to keep a most careful and prayerful watch. You had better get the Die-Hards out of the Tower and all round the place afore Dobson and Co. get loose, or you’ll no’ get a chance later. Don’t lose your mobility, as the sodgers say. Mr. Heritage can hold the fort, but you laddies should be spread out like a screen.”

===Chinese===
“那么，他们若打算把船开进加普尔河下游，咱们候那帮外国爷们儿，就该候在那几个钟头。……杜加尔，小子，俺把最细心、最虔诚的守望托付给你。趁多布森一伙还没脱身，你最好把死硬帮撤出塔外，散布到四下里围住这地方，不然往后你就没机会了。别丢了你的机动性，大兵们这么说。赫里蒂奇先生可以坐镇堡垒，你们这班小子该像一道屏风似的撒开。”

===Original===
“That was my notion,” said Dougal. “I’ll detail two Die-Hards﻿—Thomas Yownie and Wee Jaikie﻿—to keep in touch with ye and watch for ye comin’ back. Thomas ye ken already; ye’ll no fickle Thomas Yownie. But don’t be mistook about Wee Jaikie. He’s terrible fond of greetin’, but it’s no fright with him but excitement. It’s just a habit he’s gotten. When ye see Jaikie begin to greet, ye may be sure that Jaikie’s gettin’ dangerous.”

===Chinese===
“俺本来也是这个主意。”杜加尔说。“俺要派两名死硬帮——托马斯·尤尼和小杰基——同你们保持联络，守望你们回来。托马斯你已经识得了；谁也糊弄不了托马斯·尤尼。可对小杰基千万别看走了眼。他顶爱哭鼻子，可他不是害怕，是兴奋。那不过是他落下的一种习惯。你几时见杰基开哭，只管相信，杰基要发狠了。”

===Original===
The door shut behind them and Dickson found himself with his two charges in a world dim with fog and rain and the still lingering darkness. The air was raw, and had the sour smell which comes from soaked earth and wet boughs when the leaves are not yet fledged. Both the women were miserably equipped for such an expedition. Cousin Eugénie trailed heavy furs, Saskia’s only wrap was a bright-coloured shawl about her shoulders, and both wore thin foreign shoes. Dickson insisted on stripping off his trusty waterproof and forcing it on the Princess, on whose slim body it hung very loose and very short. The elder woman stumbled and whimpered and needed the constant support of his arm, walking like a townswoman from the knees. But Saskia swung from the hips like a free woman, and Dickson had much ado to keep up with her. She seemed to delight in the bitter freshness of the dawn, inhaling deep breaths of it, and humming fragments of a tune.

===Chinese===
大门在他们身后合上，迪克森带着他照管的两位女眷，走进一个被雾、雨和迟迟不散的夜色弄得昏蒙蒙的世界。空气凛冽，带着树叶还没长齐时湿土和涝枝发出的那种酸气。两位女眷的行头都委实应付不了这样一程远征。欧仁妮表姐拖着沉重的皮草，萨斯基亚肩头只有一条花色鲜亮的披肩，两人脚上又都是单薄的外国鞋。迪克森非要脱下他那件靠得住的雨衣，硬给公主披上——它罩在她纤瘦的身上，又肥又短。年长的那位磕磕绊绊、哼哼唧唧，时刻离不得他一条胳膊的搀扶，走路光凭膝盖底下使劲，活脱一副城里太太的做派。萨斯基亚却像个自由自在的女人，大步甩着胯，迪克森得好生费力才跟得上她。她像是专爱这黎明的苦冽清新，深深吸着它，嘴里哼着一支曲子的零星片段。

===Original===
Guided by Thomas Yownie they took the road which Dickson and Heritage had travelled the first evening, through the shrubberies on the north side of the House and the side avenue beyond which the ground fell to the Laver glen. On their right the House rose like a dark cloud, but Dickson had lost his terror of it. There were three angry men inside it, he remembered: long let them stay there. He marvelled at his mood, and also rejoiced, for his worst fear had always been that he might prove a coward. Now he was puzzled to think how he could ever be frightened again, for his one object was to succeed, and in that absorption fear seemed to him merely a waste of time. “It all comes of treating the thing as a business proposition,” he told himself.

===Chinese===
由托马斯·尤尼引路，他们走上迪克森和赫里蒂奇头一晚走过的那条路，穿过府邸北侧的灌木林，再走那条侧道，过了那里，地面便向拉弗河谷沉落下去。右手边，那座府邸像一团乌云般耸起，可迪克森对它的惧怕已经没了。他记得里头有三个怒气冲天的人：就让他们在那儿多待一阵吧。他对自己的心境暗暗称奇，又暗暗欢喜，因为他平生最怕的，就是自己到头来会是个懦夫。如今他反倒纳闷，自己怎么还会再害怕，因为他一心只想成事，在那份专注里，恐惧在他看来纯属浪费时间。“这全亏把事情当一桩生意来经办。”他对自己说。

===Original===
But there was far more in his heart than this sober resolution. He was intoxicated with the resurgence of youth and felt a rapture of audacity which he never remembered in his decorous boyhood. “I haven’t been doing badly for an old man,” he reflected with glee. What, oh, what had become of the pillar of commerce, the man who might have been a Bailie had he sought municipal honours, the elder in the Guthrie Memorial Kirk, the instructor of literary young men? In the past three days he had levanted with jewels which had once been an Emperor’s and certainly were not his; he had burglariously entered and made free of a strange house; he had played hide-and-seek at the risk of his neck and had wrestled in the dark with a foreign miscreant; he had shot at an eminent solicitor with intent to kill; and he was now engaged in tramping the world with a fairytale Princess. I blush to confess that of each of his doings he was unashamedly proud, and thirsted for many more in the same line. “Gosh, but I’m seeing life,” was his unregenerate conclusion.

===Chinese===
可他心里装着的，远不止这条清醒的决断。他陶醉于青春的回涌，感到一种循规蹈矩的少年时代从不记得的狂放狂喜。“一个老头儿干到这个地步，不算赖了。”他乐滋滋地想。那位商界的柱石——那个只要肯谋个市政荣衔、本可以当上市府参事（Bailie）的人，那位格思里纪念教堂的长老、文学青年的导师——唉，那人都到哪里去了？过去三天里，他卷着曾属一位皇帝、断不属于他的珠宝私逃在外；他如窃贼一般闯进过一座陌生的宅子，还在里面来去自如；他冒着脖颈的危险玩捉迷藏，在黑地里同一个外国恶徒角力；他开枪射一位显赫的律师，存的是杀心；如今更忙着陪一位童话里的公主徒步走天下。我红着脸承认：对做下的每一桩，他都不知羞耻地引以为豪，还巴望着同一行当里多多益善。“好家伙，俺这可算见过世面了。”——这便是他死不悔改的结论。

===Original===
Without sight or sound of a human being, they descended to the Laver, climbed again by the cart track, and passed the deserted West Lodge and inn to the village. It was almost full dawn when the three stood in Mrs. Morran’s kitchen.

===Chinese===
不见一个人影，不闻一声人息，他们下到拉弗河边，又顺着马车道爬上去，路过空无一人的西门房和客栈，进了村子。三个人站到莫兰太太的厨房里时，天已差不多大亮。

===Original===
“I’ve brought you two ladies, Auntie Phemie,” said Dickson.

===Chinese===
“菲米姨妈，俺给您领了两位夫人来。”迪克森说。

===Original===
They made an odd group in that cheerful place, where the new-lit fire was crackling in the big grate﻿—the wet undignified form of Dickson, unshaven of cheek and chin and disreputable in garb: the shrouded figure of Cousin Eugénie, who had sunk into the armchair and closed her eyes; the slim girl, into whose face the weather had whipped a glow like blossom; and the hostess, with her petticoats kilted and an ancient mutch on her head.

===Chinese===
在这间暖融融的屋子里——新点的火正在大炉膛里噼啪作响——他们凑成古怪的一伙：迪克森一身湿漉漉不成体统，腮帮和下巴没刮，衣着也寒碜；欧仁妮表姐裹成一团，整个人陷在扶手椅里，闭着眼睛；那苗条的姑娘，脸蛋被风雨打得红扑扑的，像开了花；还有女主人，裙子高高掖起，头顶一顶老式便帽。

===Original===
Mrs. Morran looked once at Saskia, and then did a thing which she had not done since her girlhood. She curtseyed.

===Chinese===
莫兰太太朝萨斯基亚望了一眼，随后做了一件自打做姑娘时起就不曾做过的事。她行了个屈膝礼。

===Original===
“I’m proud to see ye here, Mem. Off wi’ your things, and I’ll get ye dry claes. Losh, ye’re fair soppin’. And your shoon! Ye maun change your feet.﻿ ﻿… Dickson! Awa’ up to the loft, and dinna you stir till I give ye a cry. The leddies will change by the fire. And you, Mem”﻿—this to Cousin Eugénie﻿—“the place for you’s your bed. I’ll kinnle a fire ben the hoose in a jiffy. And syne ye’ll have breakfast﻿—ye’ll hae a cup o’ tea wi’ me now, for the kettle’s just on the boil. Awa’ wi’ ye, Dickson,” and she stamped her foot.

===Chinese===
“夫人，您光临俺这寒舍，俺脸上有光。快把湿衣裳宽了，俺给您寻干爽的换上。哎呀，您湿得透透的了。还有您这鞋！这双脚非换一换不可。……迪克森！上阁楼待着去，俺不喊你，你就别动窝。两位夫人就在火边更衣。至于您，夫人”——这是对欧仁妮表姐说的——“您呐，就该上床躺着去。俺这就到里屋生上火，一眨眼的工夫。回头就开早饭——眼下先陪俺喝杯茶，壶里的水正滚着呢。去你的吧，迪克森。”说着她跺了跺脚。

===Original===
Dickson departed, and in the loft washed his face, and smoked a pipe on the edge of the bed, watching the mist eddying up the village street. From below rose the sounds of hospitable bustle, and when after some twenty minutes’ vigil he descended, he found Saskia toasting stockinged toes by the fire in the great armchair, and Mrs. Morran setting the table.

===Chinese===
迪克森依言去了，在阁楼上洗了把脸，坐在床沿抽了一斗烟，望着雾气沿村街打着旋往上涌。楼底下腾起一片待客的忙乱。他守了约莫二十分钟下来，只见萨斯基亚正窝在那把大扶手椅里，伸着穿袜子的脚尖在炉火边烤着，莫兰太太在摆饭桌。

===Original===
“Auntie Phemie, hearken to me. We’ve taken on too big a job for two men and six laddies, and help we’ve got to get, and that this very morning. D’you mind the big white house away up near the hills ayont the station and east of the Ayr road? It looked like a gentleman’s shooting lodge. I was thinking of trying there. Mercy!”

===Chinese===
“菲米姨妈，您听俺说。两个人带六个小子，接下的这桩活计太大，救兵咱们非搬不可，还得就是今儿上午。您还记得那座大白宅子不——车站那头、快到山根、艾尔大路东边？瞧着像绅士家的猎场别墅。俺正琢磨上那儿试一试。哎呀妈呀！”

===Original===
The exclamation was wrung from him by his eyes settling on Saskia and noting her apparel. Gone were her thin foreign clothes, and in their place she wore a heavy tweed skirt cut very short, and thick homespun stockings, which had been made for someone with larger feet than hers. A pair of the coarse low-heeled shoes, which country folk wear in the farmyard, stood warming by the hearth. She still had her russet jumper, but round her neck hung a grey wool scarf, of the kind known as a “comforter.” Amazingly pretty she looked in Dickson’s eyes, but with a different kind of prettiness. The sense of fragility had fled, and he saw how nobly built she was for all her exquisiteness. She looked like a queen, he thought, but a queen to go gipsying through the world with.

===Chinese===
这声惊呼是被他的眼睛硬生生拽出来的——它们落到了萨斯基亚身上，看清了她的行头。那身单薄的外国衣裳不见了，换成一条裁得极短的重花呢裙，一双厚实的手织毛袜——那原是给一双比她更大的脚织的。一双乡下人进牲口院穿的粗劣矮跟鞋，正架在炉边烘着。她身上还是那件黄褐色针织衫，颈间却围了一条灰色绒线围巾，就是人们叫作“comforter”的那一种。在迪克森眼里，她美得惊人，却是另一种美。那份娇弱之感一扫而空，他看得出，她那份精致底下，身架生得何等端方挺拔。她像一位王后，他想，却是一位可以同你浪迹天涯的王后。

===Original===
“Ay, they’re some o’ Elspeth’s things, rale guid furthy claes,” said Mrs. Morran complacently. “And the shoon are what she used to gang about the byres wi’ when she was in the Castlewham dairy. The leddy was tellin’ me she was for trampin’ the hills, and thae things will keep her dry and warm.﻿ ﻿… I ken the hoose ye mean. They ca’ it the Mains of Garple. And I ken the man that bides in it. He’s yin Sir Erchibald Roylance. English, but his mither was a Dalziel. I’m no weel acquaint wi’ his forbears, but I’m weel eneuch acquaint wi’ Sir Erchie, and ‘better a guid coo than a coo o’ a guid kind,’ as my mither used to say. He used to be an awfu’ wild callant, a freend o’ puir Maister Quentin, and up to ony deevilry. But they tell me he’s a quieter lad since the war, and sair lamed by fa’in oot o’ an airyplane.”

===Chinese===
“是啊，都是埃尔斯佩思的一些衣裳，地道的厚实好货。”莫兰太太颇为得意地说。“那双鞋，是她在卡斯尔沃姆（Castlewham）奶牛场那阵子进牛棚穿的。这位夫人方才跟俺说，她要走山路，这些东西保她身上又干又暖。……俺知道你说的是哪座宅子。人家管它叫加普尔田庄（the Mains of Garple）。俺也认得住在里头的那个人。是位阿奇博尔德·罗伊兰斯爵士（Sir Archibald Roylance）。英格兰人，不过他母亲是达齐尔家（Dalziel）的闺女。他祖上俺不大熟，可埃尔奇爵士（Sir Erchie）本人，俺熟得很——“一头好牛强过一头名种牛”，俺娘从前就爱这么说。他从前是个野得没边的浑小子，跟可怜的昆廷少爷是朋友，什么捣蛋的勾当都干得出。可听说打完仗他安分多了，就是从飞机上摔下来，瘸得不轻。”

===Original===
“Will he be at the Mains just now?” Dickson asked.

===Chinese===
“这会儿他会在田庄上吗？”迪克森问。

===Original===
“I wadna wonder. He has a muckle place in England, but he aye used to come here in the back-end for the shootin’ and in Aprile for birds. He’s clean daft about birds. He’ll be out a’ day at the Craig watchin’ solans, or lyin’ a’ mornin’ i’ the moss lookin’ at bog-blitters.”

===Chinese===
“保不齐。他在英格兰有老大一处宅子，可他年年深秋都来这儿打猎，四月里来瞧鸟。他对鸟简直着了魔。他能一整天泡在克雷格岬（the Craig）上看塘鹅（solan），要么一清早趴在沼子里瞅沙锥鸟（bog-blitter）。”

===Original===
“Will he help, think you?”

===Chinese===
“您说，他会帮忙吗？”

===Original===
“I’ll wager he’ll help. Onyway it’s your best chance, and better a wee bush than nae beild. Now, sit in to your breakfast.”

===Chinese===
“俺敢说他准帮。横竖这是你顶好的机会，树丛再小，也强似没遮没拦。好了，坐下吃你的早饭吧。”

===Original===
It was a merry meal. Mrs. Morran dispensed tea and gnomic wisdom. Saskia ate heartily, speaking little, but once or twice laying her hand softly on her hostess’s gnarled fingers. Dickson was in such spirits that he gobbled shamelessly, being both hungry and hurried, and he spoke of the still unconquered enemy with ease and disrespect, so that Mrs. Morran was moved to observe that there was “naething sae bauld as a blind mear.” But when in a sudden return of modesty he belittled his usefulness and talked sombrely of his mature years he was told that he “wad never be auld wi’ sae muckle honesty.” Indeed it was very clear that Mrs. Morran approved of her nephew.

===Chinese===
这是一顿快活的早饭。莫兰太太斟着茶，也分发着格言式的智慧。萨斯基亚吃得畅快，话不多，只一两回把她的手轻轻覆在女主人疙疙瘩瘩的手指上。迪克森兴致那样高，人又饿又赶，吃得毫不客气，谈起那尚未征服的敌人，口气轻慢随便，惹得莫兰太太不由发了一句议论：“天底下就数瞎马胆子最大。”可等到他忽然谦虚回来，贬低自己的用处、面色沉沉地念叨自己上了年纪时，又得了这么一句：“心眼这么实诚的人，一辈子老不了。”莫兰太太看中她这个外甥，那真是明摆着的。

===Original===
They did not linger over breakfast, for both were impatient to be on the road. Mrs. Morran assisted Saskia to put on Elspeth’s shoes. “ ‘Even a young fit finds comfort in an auld bauchle,’ as my mother, honest woman, used to say.” Dickson’s waterproof was restored to him, and for Saskia an old raincoat belonging to the son in South Africa was discovered, which fitted her better. “Siccan weather,” said the hostess, as she opened the door to let in a swirl of wind. “The deil’s aye kind to his ain. Haste ye back, Mem, and be sure I’ll tak’ guid care o’ your leddy cousin.”

===Chinese===
早饭桌上两人都没有流连，因为都急着上路。莫兰太太帮萨斯基亚穿好埃尔斯佩思的鞋。“‘年轻的脚，蹬上双旧破鞋也舒坦’，俺娘——实诚人——从前常这么说。”迪克森的雨衣物归原主，又给萨斯基亚翻出一件在南非（South Africa）的儿子留下的旧雨衣，她穿着更合身。“瞧这天气！”女主人开了门，放进一股打着旋的风，说道。“魔鬼向来照应自家人。早去早回啊，夫人，您那位表姐夫人，俺保险给您照看周全。”

===Original===
The proper way to the Mains of Garple was either by the station and the Ayr road, or by the Auchenlochan highway, branching off half a mile beyond the Garple bridge. But Dickson, who had been studying the map and fancied himself as a pathfinder, chose the direct route across the Long Muir as being at once shorter and more sequestered. With the dawn the wind had risen again, but it had shifted towards the northwest and was many degrees colder. The mist was furling on the hills like sails, the rain had ceased, and out at sea the eye covered a mile or two of wild water. The moor was drenching wet, and the peat bogs were brimming with inky pools, so that soon the travellers were soaked to the knees. Dickson had no fear of pursuit, for he calculated that Dobson and his friends, even if they had got out, would be busy looking for the truants in the vicinity of the House and would presently be engaged with the old Tower. But he realised, too, that speed on his errand was vital, for at any moment the Unknown might arrive from the sea.

===Chinese===
去加普尔田庄的正路有两条：一条经车站走艾尔大路，一条走奥琴洛坎的大路，在加普尔桥那头半英里处分岔。可迪克森研读过地图，又自命寻路的行家，便挑了横穿长沼荒原（the Long Muir）的直路，图它又近又僻静。天一亮风又起了，只是转了向，朝西北刮，还冷了好几度。雾在山头像帆一样收拢，雨已经停了，放眼外海，一两英里狂野的水面尽收眼底。荒原湿得透透的，泥炭沼里涨满墨色的水洼，旅人们不多时膝盖以下就全泡透了。迪克森不怕有人追，他盘算着，多布森和他那两个朋友即便脱了身，也正忙着在府邸左近搜寻他们这群逃亡的人，转眼就要同老塔纠缠上。可他也明白，这趟差事快是性命攸关，因为那位神秘人物随时可能从海上来。

===Original===
So he kept up a good pace, half-running, half-striding, till they had passed the railway, and he found himself gasping with a stitch in his side, and compelled to rest in the lee of what had once been a sheepfold. Saskia amazed him. She moved over the rough heather like a deer, and it was her hand that helped him across the deeper hags. Before such youth and vigour he felt clumsy and old. She stood looking down at him as he recovered his breath, cool, unruffled, alert as Diana. His mind fled to Heritage, and it occurred to him suddenly that the Poet had set his affections very high. Loyalty drove him to speak a word for his friend.

===Chinese===
于是他脚程不停，半跑半跨步，直到过了铁路，才发觉自己胁下针扎似的疼，气喘不止，只好在一处旧羊圈的背风处歇脚。萨斯基亚叫他惊叹。她在坎坷的石楠丛里走得像头母鹿，倒是她的手搀他越过那些深陷的泥炭沟。在这样的青春活力跟前，他觉得自己又笨拙又老。他喘息未定，她站在那里低头看他，冷静、从容、机警，像狄安娜（Diana）。他的心飞到赫里蒂奇身上，忽然想到，那位诗人的心气放得真高。一片忠心驱着他替朋友进一言。

===Original===
“I’ve got the easy job,” he said. “Mr. Heritage will have the whole pack on him in that old Tower, and him with such a sore clout on his head. I’ve left him my pistol. He’s a terrible brave man!”

===Chinese===
“俺摊上的是轻省差事，”他说。“赫里蒂奇先生要在那座老塔里独对成群的豺狼，脑袋上还带着那么重的一记伤。俺把手枪留给他了。他是个天大的勇士！”

===Original===
She smiled.

===Chinese===
她微微一笑。

===Original===
“Ay, and he’s a poet too.”

===Chinese===
“是啊，他还是个诗人呢。”

===Original===
“So?” she said. “I did not know. He is very young.”

===Chinese===
“是吗？”她说。“这我不知道。他很年轻。”

===Original===
“He’s a man of very high ideels.”

===Chinese===
“他是个理想顶高的人。”

===Original===
She puzzled at the word, and then smiled. “I know him. He is like many of our young men in Russia, the students﻿—his mind is in a ferment and he does not know what he wants. But he is brave.”

===Chinese===
她被这个词儿难了一下，随即笑了。“我了解他。他像我们俄国许多年轻人，那些大学生——头脑正在发酵，却不知道自己要什么。不过，他是勇敢的。”

===Original===
This seemed to Dickson’s loyal soul but a chilly tribute.

===Chinese===
在迪克森那颗忠诚的心听来，这赞语未免太冷了些。

===Original===
“I think he is in love with me,” she continued.

===Chinese===
“我想，他爱着我。”她接着说。

===Original===
He looked up startled and saw in her face that which gave him a view into a strange new world. He had thought that women blushed when they talked of love, but her eyes were as grave and candid as a boy’s. Here was one who had gone through waters so deep that she had lost the foibles of sex. Love to her was only a word of ill omen, a threat on the lips of brutes, an extra battalion of peril in an army of perplexities. He felt like some homely rustic who finds himself swept unwittingly into the moonlight hunt of Artemis and her maidens.

===Chinese===
他吃了一惊，抬头望去，在她的脸上看见了一样东西，给他打开一扇通往陌生新世界的窗。他原以为女人谈起爱情总会脸红，可她的眼睛却像少年的眼睛，沉静而坦白。眼前这人蹚过的水太深了，深得连女性的种种娇态都失掉了。爱情于她只是一个凶兆的字眼，是恶棍唇边的威胁，是重重烦恼的大军里再添上的一营凶险。他觉得自己像个土头土脑的乡巴佬，一不留神被卷进了阿耳忒弥斯（Artemis）同她的侍女们的月下狩猎。

===Original===
“He is a romantic,” she said. “I have known so many like him.”

===Chinese===
“他是个浪漫派，”她说。“像他这样的人，我见得太多了。”

===Original===
“He’s no’ that,” said Dickson shortly. “Why, he used to be aye laughing at me for being romantic. He’s one that’s looking for truth and reality, he says, and he’s terrible down on the kind of poetry I like myself.”

===Chinese===
“他才不是呢。”迪克森不客气地说。“嗨，他还老笑话俺浪漫呢。照他说，他是那种寻找真理和真实的人，俺自己喜欢的那一路诗，他贬得一文不值。”

===Original===
She smiled. “They all talk so. But you, my friend Dickson” (she pronounced the name in two staccato syllables ever so prettily), “you are different. Tell me about yourself.”

===Chinese===
她笑了。“他们都这么说。可是你，我的朋友迪克森”（她把这个名字断成两个短促的音节念出来，好听极了）“你不一样。跟我讲讲你自己吧。”

===Original===
“I’m just what you see﻿—a middle-aged retired grocer.”

===Chinese===
“俺就是你眼前瞧见的——一个上了岁数、退了休的杂货铺老板。”

===Original===
“Grocer?” she queried. “Ah, yes, *épicier*. But you are a very remarkable *épicier*. Mr. Heritage I understand, but you and those little boys﻿—no. I am sure of one thing﻿—you are not a romantic. You are too humorous and﻿—and﻿—I think you are like Ulysses, for it would not be easy to defeat you.”

===Chinese===
“杂货铺老板？”她问道。“啊，对了，*épicier*。可你是一位非常了不起的 *épicier*。赫里蒂奇先生，我懂得；可是你，还有那些小男孩——我不懂。有一件事我确信——你不是浪漫派。你太幽默了，而且——而且——我想你像尤利西斯，因为要打败你，可不容易。”

===Original===
Her eyes were kind, nay affectionate, and Dickson experienced a preposterous rapture in his soul, followed by a sinking, as he realised how far the job was still from being completed.

===Chinese===
她的眼睛是和善的，不，简直是含着深情的，迪克森的魂灵里涌起一阵荒唐的狂喜，随即又沉了下去，因为他想起这桩差事离办成还有多远。

===Original===
“We must be getting on, Mem,” he said hastily, and the two plunged again into the heather.

===Chinese===
“咱们得赶路了，夫人。”他赶忙说，两人又扎进了石楠丛。

===Original===
The Ayr road was crossed, and the fir wood around the Mains became visible, and presently the white gates of the entrance. A windblown spire of smoke beyond the trees proclaimed that the house was not untenanted. As they entered the drive the Scots firs were tossing in the gale, which blew fiercely at this altitude, but, the dwelling itself being more in the hollow, the daffodil clumps on the lawn were but mildly fluttered.

===Chinese===
他们跨过艾尔大路，田庄四周的杉树林望得见了，跟着是大门口的白漆门扇。林子那头一柱被风吹得东倒西歪的炊烟，表明宅中并非无人居住。他们走上车道时，苏格兰冷杉在大风里翻腾——在这个高度，风正刮得凶——可宅子本身坐落在更深的洼处，草坪上一丛丛黄水仙只被吹得轻轻颤动。

===Original===
The door was opened by a one-armed butler who bore all the marks of the old regular soldier. Dickson produced a card and asked to see his master on urgent business. Sir Archibald was at home, he was told, and had just finished breakfast. The two were led into a large bare chamber which had all the chill and mustiness of a bachelor’s drawing-room. The butler returned, and said Sir Archibald would see him. “I’d better go myself first and prepare the way, Mem,” Dickson whispered and followed the man across the hall.

===Chinese===
开门的是一位独臂管家，通身上下全是老正规军的印记。迪克森递上一张名片，说有急事求见主人。回话说阿奇博尔德爵士在家，刚用完早饭。两人被领进一间空落落的大屋子，满带着单身汉客厅特有的阴冷和霉味。管家回来说，阿奇博尔德爵士愿意见他。“俺最好先自个儿进去，把话铺排好，夫人。”迪克森咬着耳朵说，便跟着那人穿过大厅。

===Original===
He found himself ushered into a fair-sized room where a bright fire was burning. On a table lay the remains of breakfast, and the odour of food mingled pleasantly with the scent of peat. The horns and heads of big game, foxes’ masks, the model of a gigantic salmon and several bookcases adorned the wall, and books and maps were mixed with decanters and cigar-boxes on the long sideboard. After the wild out of doors the place seemed the very shrine of comfort. A young man sat in an armchair by the fire with a leg on a stool; he was smoking a pipe, and reading the *Field*, and on another stool at his elbow was a pile of new novels. He was a pleasant brown-faced young man, with remarkably smooth hair and a roving humorous eye.

===Chinese===
他被引进一间大小适中的屋子，炉火烧得正旺。一张桌上摆着早饭的残席，食物的香气同泥炭的气息融在一处，甚是宜人。墙上装点着大猎物的角与头颅、狐狸的面皮、一条巨大鲑鱼的模型和几只书柜；长长的餐具柜上，书籍和地图同细颈酒瓶、雪茄盒混作一堆。从屋外的荒天野地进来，这地方简直是舒适的圣殿。一个年轻人坐在炉边的扶手椅里，一条腿搁在脚凳上；他抽着烟斗，读着《田野》（*Field*），肘边另一只脚凳上摞着一堆新小说。他是个讨人喜欢的棕红脸膛的年轻人，头发出奇地顺滑，一双滴溜溜打量的诙谐眼睛。

===Original===
“Come in, Mr. McCunn. Very glad to see you. If, as I take it, you’re the grocer, you’re a household name in these parts. I get all my supplies from you, and I’ve just been makin’ inroads on one of your divine hams. Now, what can I do for you?”

===Chinese===
“请进，麦坎先生。见到你非常高兴。要是我没猜错，你就是那位杂货商——在这一带，你的名字可是家喻户晓。我全部的货源都从你那儿进，方才还正大啃你的一只神仙火腿呢。好啦，我能为你效点什么劳？”

===Original===
“I’m very proud to hear what you say, Sir Archibald. But I’ve not come on business. I’ve come with the queerest story you ever heard in your life, and I’ve come to ask your help.”

===Chinese===
“听您这么说，俺脸上有光，阿奇博尔德爵士。可俺不是为生意来的。俺带来了您这辈子听过的最离奇的一桩故事，俺是来求您搭把手的。”

===Original===
“Go ahead. A good story is just what I want this vile mornin’.”

===Chinese===
“请讲。这个鬼天气的早晨，我正缺一个好故事呢。”

===Original===
“I’m not here alone. I’ve a lady with me.”

===Chinese===
“俺不是一个人来的。俺身边还带着一位夫人。”

===Original===
“God bless my soul! A lady!”

===Chinese===
“我的老天爷！一位夫人！”

===Original===
“Ay, a princess. She’s in the next room.”

===Chinese===
“是呀，还是一位公主。她就在隔壁屋里。”

===Original===
The young man looked wildly at him and waved the book he had been reading.

===Chinese===
年轻人瞪着他直瞧，手里挥了挥方才读的那本书。

===Original===
“Excuse me, Mr. McCunn, but are you quite sober? I beg your pardon. I see you are. But you know, it isn’t done. Princesses don’t as a rule come here after breakfast to pass the time of day. It’s more absurd than this shocker I’ve been readin’.”

===Chinese===
“恕我冒昧，麦坎先生，你没喝多吧？我说声失敬——我看得出你没喝。可是你知道，这不合规矩。公主们照例不会在早饭之后登门寒暄。这比我正读着的这本惊险小说还要荒唐。”

===Original===
“All the same it’s a fact. She’ll tell you the story herself, and you’ll believe her quick enough. But to prepare your mind I’ll just give you a sketch of the events of the last few days.”

===Chinese===
“可它千真万确是事实。她会亲口把故事讲给您听，您很快就会信她。不过为了叫您心里有个准备，俺先把这几天的事给您勾个轮廓。”

===Original===
Before the sketch was concluded the young man had violently rung the bell. “Sime,” he shouted to the servant, “clear away this mess and lay the table again. Order more breakfast, all the breakfast you can get. Open the windows and get the tobacco smoke out of the air. Tidy up the place for there’s a lady comin’. Quick, you juggins!”

===Chinese===
轮廓还没勾完，年轻人已经狠狠摇响了铃。“西姆（Sime），”他朝仆人吼道，“把这摊狼藉撤了，桌子重新摆。再叫些早饭来，能叫多少叫多少。把窗户全敞开，把烟气放出去。把这地方拾掇利索，有位夫人要驾到。快点，你个呆子！”

===Original===
He was on his feet now, and, with his arm in Dickson’s, was heading for the door.

===Chinese===
这时他已经站起身，一条胳膊挽住迪克森，朝门口走去。

===Original===
“My sainted aunt! And you topped off with pottin’ at the factor. I’ve seen a few things in my day, but I’m blessed if I ever met a bird like you!”

===Chinese===
“我的老天爷！末了你还朝代管人来了一枪。我这辈子也算见过些世面，可像你这样一只怪鸟，我要是见过，叫我不得好死！”

## XI. Gravity Out of Bed / 十一、庄重下床

===Original===
It is probable that Sir Archibald Roylance did not altogether believe Dickson’s tale; it may be that he considered him an agreeable romancer, or a little mad, or no more than a relief to the tedium of a wet Sunday morning. But his incredulity did not survive one glance at Saskia as she stood in that bleak drawing-room among Victorian watercolours and faded chintzes. The young man’s boyishness deserted him. He stopped short in his tracks, and made a profound and awkward bow. “I am at your service, Mademoiselle,” he said, amazed at himself. The words seemed to have come out of a confused memory of plays and novels.

===Chinese===
阿奇博尔德·罗伊兰斯爵士多半没有全信迪克森的故事；他说不定把他看成一个讨人喜欢的说故事的人，要么有点疯癫，要么不过是这个阴雨的星期天早晨解闷的一桩乐子。可他的不信只消朝萨斯基亚望上一眼便烟消云散——她正站在那间冷清的客厅里，四下是维多利亚时代的水彩画和褪了色的印花布。年轻人身上的孩子气弃他而去。他猛地收住脚步，深深鞠了一躬，姿势笨拙。“听候您的差遣，小姐。”他说，自己也暗暗吃惊。这句话仿佛是从戏剧和小说的一片混沌记忆里冒出来的。

===Original===
She inclined her head﻿—a little on one side, and looked towards Dickson.

===Chinese===
她颔首示意——头微微偏向一侧，眼睛望向迪克森。

===Original===
“Sir Archibald’s going to do his best for us,” said that squire of dames. “I was telling him that we had had our breakfast.”

===Chinese===
“阿奇博尔德爵士会尽力帮咱们的忙，”那位护花使者说。“俺方才正跟他说，咱们已经用过早饭了。”

===Original===
“Let’s get out of this sepulchre,” said their host, who was recovering himself. “There’s a roasting fire in my den. Of course you’ll have something to eat﻿—hot coffee, anyhow﻿—I’ve trained my cook to make coffee like a Frenchwoman. The housekeeper will take charge of you, if you want to tidy up, and you must excuse our ramshackle ways, please. I don’t believe there’s ever been a lady in this house before, you know.”

===Chinese===
“快别在这座坟墓里待着了，”主人说，他正在回过神来。“我书房里炉火烧得旺着呢。你们一定得吃点东西——无论如何来杯热咖啡——我把我的厨子调教得煮咖啡跟法国女人一个样。您要是想梳洗一下，女管家会照应您，咱们这家里散漫邋遢，千万请您包涵。您知道，我相信这宅子有史以来还没进来过一位夫人呢。”

===Original===
He led her to the smoking-room and ensconced her in the great chair by the fire. Smilingly she refused a series of offers which ranged from a sheepskin mantle which he had got in the Pamirs and which he thought might fit her, to hot whisky and water as a specific against a chill. But she accepted a pair of slippers and deftly kicked off the brogues provided by Mrs. Morran. Also, while Dickson started rapaciously on a second breakfast, she allowed him to pour her out a cup of coffee.

===Chinese===
他领她进了吸烟室，把她安置在炉火边的大扶手椅里。他献上一连串殷勤——从一件他在帕米尔（Pamirs）弄来、心想也许合她身的羊皮斗篷，到当作驱寒灵药的热威士忌兑水——她都含笑谢绝了。但她收下了一双拖鞋，利索地蹬掉了莫兰太太给她的那双粗皮鞋。此外，趁迪克森如狼似虎地开始第二顿早饭，她也容他给自己倒了一杯咖啡。

===Original===
“You are a soldier?” she asked.

===Chinese===
“您是军人？”她问。

===Original===
“Two years infantry﻿—5th Battalion Lennox Highlanders, and then Flying Corps. Top-hole time I had too, till the day before the Armistice when my luck gave out and I took a nasty toss. Consequently I’m not as fast on my legs now as I’d like to be.”

===Chinese===
“步兵干了两年——伦诺克斯高地兵团（Lennox Highlanders）第五营，后来进了飞行队（Flying Corps）。那段日子也快活透了，直到停战日（Armistice）前一天，我的运气用光了，狠狠摔了一跤。结果就是，如今我这双腿脚，不如我希望的那么利索。”

===Original===
“You were a friend of Captain Kennedy?”

===Chinese===
“您是肯尼迪上尉的朋友？”

===Original===
“His oldest. We were at the same private school, and he was at m’ tutor’s, and we were never much separated till he went abroad to cram for the Diplomatic and I started east to shoot things.”

===Chinese===
“他最老的。我们上同一所私立小学，他又在我那位家庭教师手下念过书，两人几乎没怎么分开过，直到他出国补习备考外交官，我动身去东方打猎。”

===Original===
“Then I will tell you what I told Captain Kennedy.” Saskia, looking into the heart of the peats, began the story of which we have already heard a version, but she told it differently, for she was telling it to one who more or less belonged to her own world. She mentioned names at which the other nodded. She spoke of a certain Paul Abreskov. “I heard of him at Bokhara in 1912,” said Sir Archie, and his face grew solemn. Sometimes she lapsed into French, and her hearer’s brow wrinkled, but he appeared to follow. When she had finished he drew a long breath.

===Chinese===
“那么我就把我对肯尼迪上尉说过的话，对您说一遍。”萨斯基亚凝望着泥炭火的深处，开始讲那个我们已经听过一个版本的故事，只是讲法不同，因为这一回听故事的人，多少算属于她自己的世界。她提到一些名字，对方听了点头。她讲到一位保罗·阿布列斯科夫（Paul Abreskov）。“1912 年我在布哈拉（Bokhara）听说过此人。”阿奇爵士说，脸色庄重起来。有时她说着说着转成法语，听的人眉头皱起，却似乎还跟得上。她讲完时，他长长吸了一口气。

===Original===
“My Aunt! What a time you’ve been through! I’ve seen pluck in my day, but yours! It’s not thinkable. D’you mind if I ask a question, Princess? Bolshevism we know all about, and I admit Trotsky and his friends are a pretty effective push; but how on earth have they got a worldwide graft going in the time so that they can stretch their net to an out-of-the-way spot like this? It looks as if they had struck a Napoleon somewhere.”

===Chinese===
“我的老天！您这些日子是怎么熬过来的！我这辈子也算见过胆气，可您这样的！简直无法想象。公主，您介意我问一个问题吗？布尔什维主义我们全知底细，我也承认托洛茨基（Trotsky）和他那伙人是相当厉害的一帮；可他们究竟怎么在这点时间里把一桩全球的勾当运转起来，把网一直撒到这样一个犄角旮旯？瞧这架势，像是他们里头出了个拿破仑（Napoleon）呢。”

===Original===
“You do not understand,” she said. “I cannot make anyone understand﻿—except a Russian. My country has been broken to pieces, and there is no law in it; therefore it is a nursery of crime. So would England be, or France, if you had suffered the same misfortunes. My people are not wickeder than others, but for the moment they are sick and have no strength. As for the government of the Bolsheviki it matters little, for it will pass. Some parts of it may remain, but it is a government of the sick and fevered, and cannot endure in health. Lenin may be a good man﻿—I do not think so, but I do not know﻿—but if he were an archangel he could not alter things. Russia is mortally sick and therefore all evil is unchained, and the criminals have no one to check them. There is crime everywhere in the world, and the unfettered crime in Russia is so powerful that it stretches its hand to crime throughout the globe and there is a great mobilising everywhere of wicked men. Once you boasted that law was international and that the police in one land worked with the police of all others. Today that is true about criminals. After a war evil passions are loosed, and, since Russia is broken, in her they can make their headquarters.﻿ ﻿… It is not Bolshevism, the theory, you need fear, for that is a weak and dying thing. It is crime, which today finds its seat in my country, but is not only Russian. It has no fatherland. It is as old as human nature and as wide as the earth.”

===Chinese===
“您不明白，”她说。“我无法让任何人明白——除了俄国人。我的祖国已经被砸得粉碎，国内没有了法律，于是成了罪恶的温床。英格兰也会这样，法国也会，倘若你们遭受了同样的不幸。我的人民不比别国人民更坏，只是眼下他们病了，没有力气。至于布尔什维克的政府，它无足轻重，因为它终会成为过去。它也许会留下几样残余，但那是一个病人和高热者的政府，在健康里维持不下去。列宁（Lenin）也许是个好人——我不这么认为，但我并不知道——可他纵然是大天使，也改变不了大局。俄国害着致命的病，因此一切罪恶都挣脱了锁链，罪犯们再没有人来管束。世上到处都有罪恶，而俄国这股挣脱了枷锁的罪恶如此强大，竟把手伸向全球各地的罪恶，恶人们在四面八方大举集结。从前你们夸口说法律是国际的，一国警察同各国警察通力合作。今天这话应在了罪犯身上。一场战争过后，邪恶的情欲便放了出来，而俄国既已破碎，他们便可以在她那里设立总部。……你们该害怕的不是布尔什维主义那套理论，那是虚弱而垂死的东西。是罪恶——它今天把宝座安在我的国家，却不仅仅是俄国的。它没有祖国。它同人性一样古老，同大地一样辽阔。”

===Original===
