“I see,” said Sir Archie. “Gad, here have I been vegetatin’ and thinkin’ that all excitement had gone out of life with the war, and sometimes even regrettin’ that the beastly old thing was over, and all the while the world fairly hummin’ with interest. And Loudon too!”

===Chinese===
“我明白了。”阿奇爵士说。“天哪，我竟一直在这儿发霉，还以为打完仗，生活里再没有刺激了，有时甚至惋惜那场该死的老仗打完了——殊不知这世界一直在嗡嗡地热闹着。还有劳登！”

===Original===
“I would like your candid opinion on yon factor, Sir Archibald,” said Dickson.

===Chinese===
“俺想听听您对那位代管人的坦率看法，阿奇博尔德爵士。”迪克森说。

===Original===
“I can’t say I ever liked him, and I’ve once or twice had a row with him, for he used to bring his pals to shoot over Dalquharter and he didn’t quite play the game by me. But I know dashed little about him, for I’ve been a lot away. Bit hairy about the heels, of course. A great figure at local race-meetin’s, and used to toady old Carforth and the huntin’ crowd. He has a pretty big reputation as a sharp lawyer and some of the thickheaded lairds swear by him, but Quentin never could stick him. It’s quite likely he’s been gettin’ into Queer Street, for he was always speculatin’ in horseflesh, and I fancy he plunged a bit on the Turf. But I can’t think how he got mixed up in this show.”

===Chinese===
“说不上我几时喜欢过他，我还跟他闹过一两回别扭，因为他惯常带他的哥儿们到达尔夸特来打猎，对我却不大按规矩来。可他的底细我知道得少得可怜，我离家的时候太多。脚后跟自然是有点毛糙的。本地赛马会上是个大红人，从前专会巴结老卡福思（Carforth）和那一帮子猎狐的。他顶着个精明律师的大名声，几位脑子不灵的庄园主把他当活菩萨供着，可昆廷从来就看他不入眼。他八成儿是进了拮据街（Queer Street），因为他一直倒腾马匹，我琢磨他在赛马场上还下过不小的注。可我想不出他怎么搅进了这一出。”

===Original===
“I’m positive Dobson’s his brother.”

===Chinese===
“俺敢担保，多布森就是他兄弟。”

===Original===
“And put this business in his way. That would explain it all right.﻿ ﻿… He must be runnin’ for pretty big stakes, for that kind of lad don’t dabble in crime for six-and-eightpence.﻿ ﻿… Now for the layout. You’ve got three men shut up in Dalquharter House, who by this time have probably escaped. One of you﻿—what’s his name?﻿—Heritage?﻿—is in the old Tower, and you think that *they* think the Princess is still there and will sit round the place like terriers. Sometime today the Danish brig will arrive with reinforcements, and then there will be a hefty fight. Well, the first thing to be done is to get rid of Loudon’s stymie with the authorities. Princess, I’m going to carry you off in my car to the Chief Constable. The second thing is for you after that to stay on here. It’s a deadly place on a wet day, but it’s safe enough.”

===Chinese===
“还是他把这桩买卖引荐给他的呢。这么一说就全对上了。……他一定是在奔挺大的赌注，那种人不为六先令八便士的小钱沾罪恶的边。……现在说正事。你们把三个人关在达尔夸特府里了，到这会儿他们多半已经脱身。你们中间有一个——他叫什么来着？——赫里蒂奇？——守在老塔里，你们料定*他们*当公主还在那儿，会像一群梗犬似的围着那地方蹲守。今天什么时候，那艘丹麦双桅船就要带增援到了，跟着就要有一场恶战。那么，头一件事，是除掉劳登在官府那边设下的绊子。公主，我要用汽车把您载去见郡警察总长。第二件事，是您在那之后留在这儿。落雨的日子这地方闷死人，可够安全。”

===Original===
Saskia shook her head and Dickson spoke for her.

===Chinese===
萨斯基亚摇了摇头，迪克森替她开了口。

===Original===
“You’ll no’ get her to stop here. I’ve done my best, but she’s determined to be back at Dalquharter. You see she’s expecting a friend, and besides, if there’s going to be a battle she’d like to be in it. Is that so, Mem?”

===Chinese===
“您劝不动她留下的。俺好话说尽，可她铁了心要回达尔夸特。您知道，她在等一位朋友，再说，真要打起来，她也想在场。是这样吧，夫人？”

===Original===
Sir Archie looked helplessly around him, and the sight of the girl’s face convinced him that argument would be fruitless. “Anyhow she must come with me to the Chief Constable. Lethington’s a slow bird on the wing, and I don’t see myself convincin’ him that he must get busy unless I can produce the Princess. Even then it may be a tough job, for it’s Sunday, and in these parts people go to sleep till Monday mornin’.”

===Chinese===
阿奇爵士束手无策地朝四下里看了看，姑娘脸上的神情让他明白，争辩也是白费。“不管怎样，她必须跟我去见郡警察总长。莱辛顿（Lethington）起飞是只慢鸟，我要是请不出公主本人，可想不出怎么让他相信必须马上行动起来。就算那样，这活计也未必轻松，今天是礼拜天，这一带的人要一觉睡到星期一早晨。”

===Original===
“That’s just what I’m trying to get at,” said Dickson. “By all means go to the Chief Constable, and tell him it’s life or death. My lawyer in Glasgow, Mr. Caw, will have been stirring him up yesterday, and you two should complete the job.﻿ ﻿… But what I’m feared is that he’ll not be in time. As you say, it’s the Sabbath day, and the police are terrible slow. Now any moment that brig may be here, and the trouble will start. I’m wanting to save the Princess, but I’m wanting too to give these blagyirds the roughest handling they ever got in their lives. Therefore I say there’s no time to lose. We’re far ower few to put up a fight, and we want every man you’ve got about this place to hold the fort till the police come.”

===Chinese===
“俺正要说的就是这个，”迪克森道。“您尽管去找郡警察总长，告诉他这是性命交关的事。俺格拉斯哥的律师考先生（Mr. Caw）昨天就该去催动他了，你们两位正好把这桩事办圆满。……俺怕的是他来不及。正如您所说，今天是安息日，警察慢得吓人。那艘双桅船随时可能到，一到，乱子就要开场。俺想救公主，俺还想叫这帮流氓挨上他们这辈子最狠的一顿收拾。所以俺说，一刻工夫也丢不得。咱们的人数远远不够打一仗，您这地方能抽出的男人俺们个个都要，好守住堡垒，撑到警察来。”

===Original===
Sir Archibald looked upon the earnest flushed face of Dickson with admiration. “I’m blessed if you’re not the most wholehearted brigand I’ve ever struck.”

===Chinese===
阿奇博尔德爵士带着钦佩，望着迪克森那张恳切涨红的脸。“你要不是我这辈子碰上的头一号死心塌地的土匪，叫我不得好死。”

===Original===
“I’m not. I’m just a business man.”

===Chinese===
“俺不是土匪。俺就是个生意人。”

===Original===
“Do you realise that you’re levying a private war and breaking every law of the land?”

===Chinese===
“你可知道你这是在打一场私人的战争，把这片土地上的法律违犯了个遍？”

===Original===
“Hoots!” said Dickson. “I don’t care a docken about the law. I’m for seeing this job through. What force can you produce?”

===Chinese===
“啐！”迪克森说。“法律在俺眼里连根杂草也算不上。俺一心只想把这桩事办到底。您能拉出多少人马来？”

===Original===
“Only cripples, I’m afraid. There’s Sime, my butler. He was a Fusilier Jock and, as you saw, has lost an arm. Then McGuffog the keeper is a good man, but he’s still got a Turkish bullet in his thigh. The chauffeur, Carfrae, was in the Yeomanry, and lost half a foot, and there’s myself, as lame as a duck. The herds on the home farm are no good, for one’s seventy and the other is in bed with jaundice. The Mains can produce four men, but they’re rather a job lot.”

===Chinese===
“只怕尽是些伤残。有西姆，我的管家。他当过燧发枪兵团的苏格兰汉子，你也看见了，缺了一条胳膊。再有猎场看守麦古福格（McGuffog），人是把好手，可大腿里还留着一颗土耳其子弹。司机卡弗雷（Carfrae）在义勇骑兵队待过，少了半只脚。还有我自己，瘸得像只鸭子。本庄农场上的牧工不顶事，一个七十岁，另一个害黄疸躺在床上。田庄还能出四个人，不过是一批杂凑的便宜货。”

===Original===
“They’ll do fine,” said Dickson heartily. “All sodgers, and no doubt all good shots. Have you plenty guns?”

===Chinese===
“够使了，”迪克森说得由衷。“全是当过兵的，不用问，个个都是好射手。枪多吗？”

===Original===
Sir Archie burst into uproarious laughter. “Mr. McCunn, you’re a man after my own heart. I’m under your orders. If I had a boy I’d put him into the provision trade, for it’s the place to see fightin’. Yes, we’ve no end of guns. I advise shotguns, for they’ve more stoppin’ power in a rush than a rifle, and I take it it’s a rough-and-tumble we’re lookin’ for.”

===Chinese===
阿奇爵士爆发出一阵哄堂大笑。“麦坎先生，你这个人正中我的下怀。我听你调遣。我要是有个儿子，我就送他进食品杂货这行，因为那正是见识打斗的地方。有，我们的枪多得数不清。我主张用猎枪，混战冲杀起来，猎枪的制止力比步枪强，何况我料想咱们要的就是一场乱仗。”

===Original===
“Right,” said Dickson. “I saw a bicycle in the hall. I want you to lend it me, for I must be getting back. You’ll take the Princess and do the best you can with the Chief Constable.”

===Chinese===
“好，”迪克森说。“俺在大厅里瞧见一辆自行车。想借俺用一用，俺必须赶回去。您带上公主，在郡警察总长面前尽力周旋。”

===Original===
“And then?”

===Chinese===
“然后呢？”

===Original===
“Then you’ll load up your car with your folk, and come down the hill to Dalquharter. There’ll be a laddie, or maybe more than one, waiting for you on this side the village to give you instructions. Take your orders from them. If it’s a red-haired ruffian called Dougal you’ll be wise to heed what he says, for he has a grand head for battles.”

===Chinese===
“然后您把您的人装上汽车，下山到达尔夸特来。村子这头会有一个小子——兴许不止一个——等着给您传话。您听他们调遣。要是碰上那个叫杜加尔的红头发浑小子，您听他的话准没错，他天生一颗打仗的脑袋。”

===Original===
Five minutes later Dickson was pursuing a quavering course like a snipe down the avenue. He was a miserable performer on a bicycle. Not for twenty years had he bestridden one, and he did not understand such new devices as freewheels and change of gears. The mounting had been the worst part and it had only been achieved by the help of a rockery. He had begun by cutting into two flowerbeds, and missing a birch tree by inches. But he clung on desperately, well knowing that if he fell off it would be hard to remount, and at length he gained the avenue. When he passed the lodge gates he was riding fairly straight, and when he turned off the Ayr highway to the side road that led to Dalquharter he was more or less master of his machine.

===Chinese===
五分钟后，迪克森正顺着车道冲下山去，路线歪歪扭扭，活像一只飞行时左摇右晃的鹬鸟。他骑起自行车来是个可怜的把式。他已有二十年没跨过这东西，也不懂得自由轮和换挡变速之类的新机关。上车是最难的一关，全亏一座假山石坛帮忙才得了手。他一上来就斜插进两个花坛，又差几英寸撞上一棵桦树。可他死死扒住不放，深知一旦摔下去再难骑上来，终于骑上了车道。过门房大门时，他已经骑得相当直了，等他从艾尔大路拐上通往达尔夸特的岔道，他多少算得上这辆车的主人了。

===Original===
He crossed the Garple by an ancient hunchbacked bridge, observing even in his absorption with the handlebars that the stream was in roaring spate. He wrestled up the further hill, with aching calf-muscles, and got to the top just before his strength gave out. Then as the road turned seaward he had the slope with him, and enjoyed some respite. It was no case for putting up his feet, for the gale was blowing hard on his right cheek, but the downward grade enabled him to keep his course with little exertion. His anxiety to get back to the scene of action was for the moment appeased, since he knew he was making as good speed as the weather allowed, so he had leisure for thought.

===Chinese===
他跨过一座年代久远、驼背拱起的老石桥，过了加普尔河——正同车把较着劲，他也留意到河水暴涨，咆哮轰鸣。他一路跟对面那道山坡摔跤，小腿肚子直发酸，赶在力气耗尽之前到了坡顶。随后道路转向海去，坡度帮了他，让他得了片刻喘息。这可不是把脚搁起来歇着的时候，狂风正狠狠拍他的右脸颊，好在下坡让他不必费什么力气就能保住方向。急于赶回战场的心，这一刻安定了些，因为他知道自己已赶出天气容许的最快脚程，于是有了闲工夫想心事。

===Original===
But the mind of this preposterous being was not on the business before him. He dallied with irrelevant things﻿—with the problems of youth and love. He was beginning to be very nervous about Heritage, not as the solitary garrison of the old Tower, but as the lover of Saskia. That everybody should be in love with her appeared to him only proper, for he had never met her like, and assumed that it did not exist. The desire of the moth for the star seemed to him a reasonable thing, since hopeless loyalty and unrequited passion were the eternal stock-in-trade of romance. He wished he were twenty-five himself to have the chance of indulging in such sentimentality for such a lady. But Heritage was not like him and would never be content with a romantic folly.﻿ ﻿… He had been in love with her for two years﻿—a long time. He spoke about wanting to die for her, which was a flight beyond Dickson himself. “I doubt it will be what they call a ‘grand passion,’ ” he reflected with reverence. But it was hopeless; he saw quite clearly that it was hopeless.

===Chinese===
可这个荒唐家伙的心思，并不在眼前的正事上。他在不相干的东西上流连——在青春和爱情的问题上打转转。他开始非常替赫里蒂奇揪心，不是把他当作老塔孤零零的守军，而是把他当作萨斯基亚的爱慕者。依他看，人人都该爱上她才算合宜，因为他从未见过她这等人，便认定这种女子举世无双。飞蛾对星星的渴慕，在他看来合情合理，因为无望的忠贞与得不到回报的痴情，本就是传奇故事永恒的老本钱。他但愿自己也回到二十五岁，好有机会对这样一位夫人纵这么一回感伤。可赫里蒂奇不比他，绝不会满足于一场浪漫的痴愚。……他爱慕她已经两年了——不短了。他说起甘愿为她去死，这一层高远，迪克森自己就够不着。“俺琢磨，这该就是人家所说的‘伟大的激情’了。”他怀着敬畏想。可这是无望的；他看得清清楚楚，这是无望的。

===Original===
Why, he could not have explained, for Dickson’s instincts were subtler than his intelligence. He recognised that the two belonged to different circles of being, which nowhere intersected. That mysterious lady, whose eyes had looked through life to the other side, was no mate for the Poet. His faithful soul was agitated, for he had developed for Heritage a sincere affection. It would break his heart, poor man. There was he holding the fort alone and cheering himself with delightful fancies about one remoter than the moon. Dickson wanted happy endings, and here there was no hope of such. He hated to admit that life could be crooked, but the optimist in him was now fairly dashed.

===Chinese===
个中缘由他自己也说不上来，因为迪克森的直觉比他的智力精细。他看出这两个人分属不同的存在圈子，两圈无处相交。那位神秘的女郎——那双眼睛已把生命看穿、望到了另一侧的女郎——不是诗人的良配。他那颗忠厚的心乱了，因为他早对赫里蒂奇生出一片真心。这会叫他心碎的，可怜的人。他正独自守着堡垒，拿一位比月亮还遥远的人儿的种种美妙幻想给自己打气。迪克森要的是圆满的结局，而这里偏偏没有那种指望。他不愿承认人生会走斜路，可他心里那个乐天派，这回委实蔫了。

===Original===
Sir Archie might be the fortunate man, for of course he would soon be in love with her, if he were not so already. Dickson like all his class had a profound regard for the country gentry. The business Scot does not usually revere wealth, though he may pursue it earnestly, nor does he specially admire rank in the common sense. But for ancient race he has respect in his bones, though it may happen that in public he denies it, and the laird has for him a secular association with good family.﻿ ﻿… Sir Archie might do. He was young, good-looking, obviously gallant.﻿ ﻿… But no! He was not quite right either. Just a trifle too light in weight, too boyish and callow. The Princess must have youth, but it should be mighty youth, the youth of a Napoleon or a Caesar. He reflected that the Great Montrose, for whom he had a special veneration, might have filled the bill. Or young Harry with his beaver up? Or Claverhouse in the picture with the flush of temper on his cheek?

===Chinese===
阿奇爵士说不定就是那个有福的人，因为不用说，他很快就会爱上她，倘若现在还没有的话。迪克森同他那个阶层的人一样，对乡间士绅怀着深深的敬意。经商的苏格兰人通常并不崇拜财富——尽管他可能起劲地追逐财富——也不特别看重寻常意义上的官爵。可对古老的血脉，他骨子里是敬重的，虽说当众也许要否认，而“庄园主”三个字在他心里，自古就同好人家连在一起。……阿奇爵士兴许成。他年轻，英俊，一看便知有胆气。……可是不成！他也不大对。只是分量轻了那么一点，太孩子气，太稚嫩。公主必须配上青春，但那得是雄强的青春，是拿破仑或恺撒（Caesar）那样的青春。他想到伟大的蒙特罗斯（the Great Montrose）——他对这位怀着特别的敬仰——倒也许够格。或者掀起面甲的小哈利（young Harry with his beaver up）？或者画像上那位颊边泛着怒色的克拉弗豪斯（Claverhouse）？

===Original===
The meditations of the matchmaking Dickson came to an abrupt end. He had been riding negligently, his head bent against the wind, and his eyes vaguely fixed on the wet hill-gravel of the road. Of his immediate environs he was pretty well unconscious. Suddenly he was aware of figures on each side of him who advanced menacingly. Stung to activity he attempted to increase his pace, which was already good, for the road at this point descended steeply. Then, before he could prevent it, a stick was thrust into his front wheel, and the next second he was describing a curve through the air. His head took the ground, he felt a spasm of blinding pain, and then a sense of horrible suffocation before his wits left him.

===Chinese===
做媒人做得入迷的迪克森，他的冥想戛然而止。他一路骑得大意，脑袋顶着风，眼睛恍恍惚惚落在路面湿漉漉的山地砾石上。身边方寸之地的动静，他差不多全无所觉。猛然间，他察觉两侧各有人影，正气势汹汹朝他逼近。他受了一激，拼命想提速——此处路面陡然下坡，车速本已不慢——却还没等他来得及，一根棍子已经插进他的前轮，下一秒他已在空中画出一道弧线。他的头先着了地，一阵剧痛炸得眼前发黑，随即是一阵可怕的窒息，他的神智便丢下了他。

===Original===
“Are ye sure it’s the richt man, Ecky?” said a voice which he did not hear.

===Chinese===
“你拿准是正主儿不，埃基（Ecky）？”一个声音说，这话他没有听见。

===Original===
“Sure. It’s the Glesca body Dobson telled us to look for yesterday. It’s a pund note atween us for this job. We’ll tie him up in the wud till we’ve time to attend to him.”

===Chinese===
“拿准。就是多布森昨天叫俺们留神的那位格拉斯哥人。这趟活计说好了，俺俩分一张一镑票子。先把他捆在树林里，等俺们腾出手再来发落他。”

===Original===
“Is he bad?”

===Chinese===
“他伤得重不？”

===Original===
“It doesna maitter,” said the one called Ecky. “He’ll be deid onyway long afore the morn.”

===Chinese===
“重不重没啥打紧，”那个叫埃基的说。“横竖到不了明天早晨，他就死定了。”

---

			

===Original===
Mrs. Morran all forenoon was in a state of un-Sabbatical disquiet. After she had seen Saskia and Dickson start she finished her housewifely duties, took Cousin Eugénie her breakfast, and made preparation for the midday dinner. The invalid in the bed in the parlour was not a repaying subject. Cousin Eugénie belonged to that type of elderly women who, having been spoiled in youth, find the rest of life fall far short of their expectations. Her voice had acquired a perpetual wail, and the corners of what had once been a pretty mouth drooped in an eternal peevishness. She found herself in a morass of misery and shabby discomfort, but had her days continued in an even tenor she would still have lamented. “A dingy body,” was Mrs. Morran’s comment, but she laboured in kindness. Unhappily they had no common language, and it was only by signs that the hostess could discover her wants and show her goodwill. She fed her and bathed her face, saw to the fire and left her to sleep. “I’m boilin’ a hen to mak’ broth for your denner, Mem. Try and get a bit sleep now.” The purport of the advice was clear, and Cousin Eugénie turned obediently on her pillow.

===Chinese===
莫兰太太一整个上午，都处在一种全不合安息日体统的心神不宁里。她目送萨斯基亚和迪克森动身之后，便做完了当家主妇的活计，给欧仁妮表姐送去早饭，又预备起正午的正餐。客厅里卧床的病人，却不是个照料起来叫人得着慰藉的主顾。欧仁妮表姐属于那一类上了年纪的女人：年轻时候娇宠惯了，便觉得余生处处够不上自己的指望。她的嗓音养成了一副经年不变的哀腔，那张从前挺俊的嘴，嘴角在永久的乖戾里向下耷拉着。她自觉陷在愁苦和寒酸不适的泥沼里，可就算她的日子一帆风顺，她也照样要长吁短叹。“一个晦气货。”莫兰太太心里评道，手上却好意操劳。不巧两人没有共同的语言，女主人只能靠比划探她的需要、表明自己的好意。她喂她吃饭，替她擦脸，照看炉火，然后留她睡觉。“俺正炖着一只母鸡，给您正餐熬汤，夫人。您且努力睡上一小觉。”这番劝告的意思是明白的，欧仁妮表姐乖乖在枕上翻了个身。

===Original===
It was Mrs. Morran’s custom of a Sunday to spend the morning in devout meditation. Some years before she had given up tramping the five miles to kirk, on the ground that having been a regular attendant for fifty years she had got all the good out of it that was probable. Instead she read slowly aloud to herself the sermon printed in a certain religious weekly which reached her every Saturday, and concluded with a chapter or two of the Bible. But today something had gone wrong with her mind. She could not follow the thread of the Reverend Doctor MacMichael’s discourse. She could not fix her attention on the wanderings and misdeeds of Israel as recorded in the Book of Exodus. She must always be getting up to look at the pot on the fire, or to open the back door and study the weather. For a little she fought against her unrest, and then she gave up the attempt at concentration. She took the big pot off the fire and allowed it to simmer, and presently she fetched her boots and umbrella, and kilted her petticoats. “I’ll be none the waur o’ a breath o’ caller air,” she decided.

===Chinese===
每逢礼拜天，莫兰太太的老习惯是整个上午在虔诚的默想中度过。若干年前，她就不肯再走那五英里路上教堂了，理由是：她规规矩矩做了五十年礼拜，这事能得的好处，她想必已经得尽了。此后，她便低声慢读登在一份每逢星期六送到她手里的宗教周刊上的布道文，读给自己听，末了再念一两章《圣经》。可是今天，她的心思出了岔子。麦克迈克尔博士（Doctor MacMichael）那篇布道文的线索，她跟不上；以色列人在《出埃及记》里记载的流浪和过犯，她也凝不定神。她总是一再起身去瞧炉上的锅，或是开了后门察看天气。她同自己的不安较了一小阵劲，随后便放弃了集中心思的打算。她把大锅从火上端下来，由它小火煨着，不多时便取来靴子和雨伞，把裙子高高掖起。“出去吸一口新鲜空气，只有好处没有坏处。”她拿定了主意。

===Original===
The wind was blowing great guns but there was only the thinnest sprinkle of rain. Sitting on the henhouse roof and munching a raw turnip was a figure which she recognised as the smallest of the Die-Hards. Between bites he was singing dolefully to the tune of “Annie Laurie” one of the ditties of his quondam Sunday school:

===Chinese===
狂风刮得如万炮齐轰，雨却只稀稀落落飘着一点。鸡棚顶上坐着一个人影，啃着一只生芜菁，她认出那是死硬帮里个子最小的一个。他啃一口唱一句，正按着《安妮·劳丽》（Annie Laurie）的调子，凄凄切切地唱着他昔日那所主日学校的一支小曲：

===Original===
“The Boorjoys’ brays are bonny,
					

===Chinese===
“布尔乔亚的驴叫顶动听，

===Original===
					Too-roo-ra-roo-raloo,
					

===Chinese===
嘟—噜—啦—噜—喽，

===Original===
					But the Worrkers o’ the Worrld
					

===Chinese===
可全世界的工人们

===Original===
					Wull gar them a’ look blue,
					

===Chinese===
定叫他们个个脸色发青，

===Original===
					Wull gar them a’ look blue,
					

===Chinese===
定叫他们个个脸色发青，

===Original===
					And droon them in the sea,
					

===Chinese===
还要把他们淹进大海，

===Original===
					And﻿—for bonnie Annie Laurie
					

===Chinese===
而为了美丽的安妮·劳丽——

===Original===
					I’ll lay me down and dee.”

===Chinese===
俺甘愿倒下把命丧。”

===Original===
“Losh, laddie,” she cried, “that’s cauld food for the stamach. Come indoors about midday and I’ll gie ye a plate o’ broth!” The Diehard saluted and continued on the turnip.

===Chinese===
“哎呀，小子，”她喊道，“这可是寒胃的吃食。晌午到俺屋里来，俺给你一盘汤喝！”那死硬帮小子行了个礼，接着啃他的芜菁。

===Original===
She took the Auchenlochan road across the Garple bridge, for that was the best road to the Mains and by it Dickson and the others might be returning. Her equanimity at all seasons was like a Turk’s, and she would not have admitted that anything mortal had power to upset or excite her: nevertheless it was a fast-beating heart that she now bore beneath her Sunday jacket. Great events, she felt, were on the eve of happening, and of them she was a part. Dickson’s anxiety was hers, to bring things to a businesslike conclusion. The honour of Huntingtower was at stake and of the old Kennedys. She was carrying out Mr. Quentin’s commands, the dead boy who used to clamour for her treacle scones. And there was more than duty in it, for youth was not dead in her old heart, and adventure had still power to quicken it.

===Chinese===
她走了奥琴洛坎那条大路，过加普尔桥去，因为那是去田庄最好的路，迪克森他们回来兴许也打这条道走。她这个人四季如常的镇定，赛得过土耳其人，她是决不肯承认有什么凡人俗事能搅乱她、激动她的：然而此刻她礼拜天的外套底下，跳动着一颗怦怦加速的心。她感到大事就在眼下，而她自己置身其中。迪克森的焦灼也就是她的焦灼：要把事情办出一个干脆利落的结果。亨廷塔的荣誉正系于一线，老肯尼迪家的荣誉也是。她在执行昆廷少爷的遗命——那个从前嚷着要吃她的糖蜜烤饼的死去的男孩。而这中间不止是本分，因为她那颗老心里青春没有死，冒险仍旧有让它跳快起来的力量。

===Original===
Mrs. Morran walked well, with the steady long paces of the Scots countrywoman. She left the Auchenlochan road and took the side path along the tableland to the Mains. But for the surge of the gale and the far-borne boom of the furious sea there was little noise; not a bird cried in the uneasy air. With the wind behind her Mrs. Morran breasted the ascent till she had on her right the moorland running south to the Lochan valley and on her left Garple chafing in its deep forested gorges. Her eyes were quick and she noted with interest a weasel creeping from a fern-clad cairn. A little way on she passed an old ewe in difficulties and assisted it to rise. “But for me, my wumman, ye’d hae been braxy ere nicht,” she told it as it departed bleating. Then she realised that she had come a certain distance. “Losh, I maun be gettin’ back or the hen will be spiled,” she cried, and was on the verge of turning.

===Chinese===
莫兰太太走得好，是苏格兰乡下女人那种沉稳的长步子。她离开奥琴洛坎大路，踏上沿着高地平台去田庄的小径。除了狂风的呼啸和远处传来的怒海轰隆，四下少有声息；不安的空气里，没有一只鸟啼叫。风从背后送着，莫兰太太一鼓作气上了坡，直上到右手是向南延伸到洛坎河谷去的荒原，左手是在幽深的林壑里翻腾受憋的加普尔河。她眼尖，饶有兴味地看见一只黄鼠狼从一座长满蕨草的石冢后头溜出来。再走一程，她遇见一只挣扎不起的老母羊，便帮它站了起来。“要不是俺，俺的老姐姐，你不到天黑就害急病死喽。”她冲着咩咩走开的老羊说。这时她才醒悟自己已走出了相当一段路。“哎呀，俺得回去了，不然那只母鸡要炖烂啦。”她喊了一声，正待转身。

===Original===
But something caught her eye a hundred yards further on the road. It was something which moved with the wind like a wounded bird, fluttering from the roadside to a puddle and then back to the rushes. She advanced to it, missed it, and caught it.

===Chinese===
可是前方百来码的路上，有一样东西撞进了她的眼睛。那东西随风飘动，像一只受伤的鸟，从路旁扑到水洼，又扑回灯心草丛。她朝它逼近，扑了个空，又一把捉住了它。

===Original===
It was an old dingy green felt hat, and she recognised it as Dickson’s.

===Chinese===
那是一顶发暗的绿色旧毡帽，她认出那是迪克森的。

===Original===
Mrs. Morran’s brain, after a second of confusion, worked fast and clearly. She examined the road and saw that a little way on the gravel had been violently agitated. She detected several prints of hobnailed boots. There were prints too, on a patch of peat on the south side behind a tall bank of sods. “That’s where they were hidin’,” she concluded. Then she explored on the other side in a thicket of hazels and wild raspberries, and presently her perseverance was rewarded. The scrub was all crushed and pressed as if several persons had been forcing a passage. In a hollow was a gleam of something white. She moved towards it with a quaking heart, and was relieved to find that it was only a new and expensive bicycle with the front wheel badly buckled.

===Chinese===
莫兰太太的脑子乱了一秒钟，随即飞快而清明地运转起来。她察看路面，看出前头不远处砾石被狠狠搅动过。她辨认出几个钉靴印。南边一垛高草皮后头的一小片泥炭地上也有脚印。“他们就是藏在那儿的。”她下了断语。随后她又到另一侧，钻进一片榛树和野覆盆子的密丛，不多时，她的坚韧得了报偿。灌木全被压倒踩实，仿佛有几个人硬开出一条通路。一处洼地里闪着一点白色的东西。她心惊胆战地朝它走去，随即松了口气——那不过是一辆崭新的高档自行车，前轮拧得不成样子。

===Original===
Mrs. Morran delayed no longer. If she had walked well on her out journey, she beat all records on the return. Sometimes she would run till her breath failed; then she would slow down till anxiety once more quickened her pace. To her joy on the Dalquharter side of the Garple bridge she observed the figure of a Diehard. Breathless, flushed, with her bonnet awry and her umbrella held like a scimitar, she seized on the boy.

===Chinese===
莫兰太太再不耽搁。去程她若算走得好的，回程便破了全部纪录。有时她一路小跑到气力不济；随后放慢脚步，焦灼又重新催快她的步子。叫她欢喜的是，在加普尔桥靠达尔夸特这一侧，她望见了一个死硬帮的身影。她气喘吁吁，满脸通红，帽子歪在一边，雨伞像一柄弯刀似的端着，一把揪住了那孩子。

===Original===
“Awfu’ doin’s! They’ve grippit Maister McCunn up the Mains road just afore the second milestone and forenent the auld bucht. I fund his hat, and a bicycle’s lyin’ broken in the wud. Haste ye, man, and get the rest and awa’ and seek him. It’ll be the tinklers frae the Dean. I’d gang mysel’, but my legs are ower auld. Oh, laddie, dinna stop to speir questions. They’ll hae him murdered or awa’ to sea. And maybe the leddy was wi’ him and they’ve got them baith. Wae’s me! Wae’s me!”

===Chinese===
“出大事啦！他们在田庄大路上、第二块里程碑前头、老羊圈正对面，把麦坎先生逮住啦。俺捡到了他的帽子，还有一辆自行车摔破在树林里。快去，孩子，把其余的人都叫上，快去寻他。准是峡谷里那帮补锅佬干的。俺恨不得自己去，可俺这两条腿太老啦。哎呀孩子，别站着问长问短。他们要么害了他，要么把人弄下海了。说不定那位夫人也同他在一起，让他们一锅端了。苦命啊！苦命啊！”

===Original===
The Diehard, who was Wee Jaikie, did not delay. His eyes had filled with tears at her news, which we know to have been his habit. When Mrs. Morran, after indulging in a moment of barbaric keening, looked back the road she had come, she saw a small figure trotting up the hill like a terrier who has been left behind. As he trotted he wept bitterly. Jaikie was getting dangerous.


===Chinese===
那死硬帮小子——正是小杰基——没有耽搁。听到她的消息，他两眼已经噙满泪水，我们知道，这是他的老习惯。莫兰太太由着性子嚎了一阵子哭丧调，回头望她来时的那条路，只见一个小小的身影正沿着山坡小跑上去，像一条被落在后头的梗犬。他一边小跑，一边嚎啕大哭。杰基要发狠了。

## XII. How Mr. McCunn Committed an Assault Upon an Ally / 十二、麦坎先生如何袭击了一位盟友
===Original===
Dickson always maintained that his senses did not leave him for more than a second or two, but he admitted that he did not remember very clearly the events of the next few hours. He was conscious of a bad pain above his eyes, and something wet trickling down his cheek. There was a perpetual sound of water in his ears and of men’s voices. He found himself dropped roughly on the ground and forced to walk, and was aware that his legs were inclined to wobble. Somebody had a grip on each arm, so that he could not defend his face from the brambles, and that worried him, for his whole head seemed one aching bruise and he dreaded anything touching it. But all the time he did not open his mouth, for silence was the one duty that his muddled wits enforced. He felt that he was not the master of his mind, and he dreaded what he might disclose if he began to babble.

===Chinese===
迪克森后来一口咬定，他的神智统共只丢开过一两秒钟，可他也承认，接下来那几个钟头的事，他记不大清楚了。他只觉得眼睛上方疼得厉害，有什么湿乎乎的东西顺着脸颊往下淌。耳朵里老响着水声，还有人声。他发觉自己被粗暴地撂到地上，又被架着往前走，还觉出两条腿直打晃。左右两条胳膊各有一只手攥着，他没法护住自己的脸去躲荆棘，这叫他发愁，因为整颗脑袋疼得像一块青肿，什么东西碰上都叫他害怕。可他自始至终没张过一次嘴，因为他那团浆糊似的脑子强派给他的唯一本分，就是沉默。他觉得自己管不住自己的心思，生怕一旦开了腔胡说八道，漏出什么底来。

===Original===
Presently there came a blank space of which he had no recollection at all. The movement had stopped, and he was allowed to sprawl on the ground. He thought that his head had got another whack from a bough, and that the pain put him into a stupor. When he awoke he was alone.

===Chinese===
跟着是一段全然的空白，他半点也不记得了。赶路停了下来，容他瘫在地上。他想是脑袋又让树枝磕了一记，那疼痛叫他陷入了昏沉。醒来时，只剩他孤零零一个人。

===Original===
He discovered that he was strapped very tightly to a young Scotch fir. His arms were bent behind him and his wrists tied together with cords knotted at the back of the tree; his legs were shackled, and further cords fastened them to the bole. Also there was a halter round the trunk and just under his chin, so that while he breathed freely enough, he could not move his head. Before him was a tangle of bracken and scrub, and beyond that the gloom of dense pines; but as he could only see directly in front his prospect was strictly circumscribed.

===Chinese===
他发觉自己被结结实实捆在一棵小苏格兰松上。双臂反剪到背后，两只手腕用绳子捆在一起，绳结打在树身后头；两腿上了绑，另有几道绳把它们拴在树干上。树干上还绕着一圈绳套，正卡在他下巴底下，所以喘气尽管不碍事，脑袋却动弹不得。眼前是一片乱糟糟的蕨草和灌木，再远处是茂密松林的幽暗；可他只能看见正前方，眼界被框得死死的。

===Original===
Very slowly he began to take his bearings. The pain in his head was now dulled and quite bearable, and the flow of blood had stopped, for he felt the incrustation of it beginning on his cheeks. There was a tremendous noise all around him, and he traced this to the swaying of treetops in the gale. But there was an undercurrent of deeper sound﻿—water surely, water churning among rocks. It was a stream﻿—the Garple of course﻿—and then he remembered where he was and what had happened.

===Chinese===
他慢吞吞地开始辨认方位。头上的疼这会儿钝了下去，完全忍得住，血也止了，因为他觉出脸颊上的血正开始结壳。四周一片天摇地动的巨响，他听出来那是树梢在大风里摇摆。可底下还垫着一层更深的声响——准是水，是水在岩石间翻搅。是一条河——自然是加普尔河——跟着他就想起了自己在什么地方，出了什么事。

===Original===
I do not wish to portray Dickson as a hero, for nothing would annoy him more; but I am bound to say that his first clear thought was not of his own danger. It was intense exasperation at the miscarriage of his plans. Long ago he should have been with Dougal arranging operations, giving him news of Sir Archie, finding out how Heritage was faring, deciding how to use the coming reinforcements. Instead he was trussed up in a wood, a prisoner of the enemy, and utterly useless to his side. He tugged at his bonds, and nearly throttled himself. But they were of good tarry cord and did not give a fraction of an inch. Tears of bitter rage filled his eyes and made furrows on his encrusted cheeks. Idiot that he had been, he had wrecked everything! What would Saskia and Dougal and Sir Archie do without a business man by their side? There would be a muddle, and the little party would walk into a trap. He saw it all very clearly. The men from the sea would overpower them, there would be murder done, and an easy capture of the Princess; and the police would turn up at long last to find an empty headland.

===Chinese===
我并不想把迪克森描画成英雄，因为没有比这更叫他恼火的事了；可我必须说，他清醒过来的头一个念头，不是自身的危险，而是对计划流产的一腔按捺不住的愤懑。眼下的时辰，他早该同杜加尔在一起部署行动，把阿奇爵士的消息报给他，打听赫里蒂奇的近况，定下怎样调遣即将开到的增援。他却偏偏被捆在树林里，做了敌人的俘虏，对自家这一方毫无用处。他挣了挣绳索，险些把自己勒死。可那是结实的浸油粗绳，一丁点也不肯让。又苦又恨的怒火涌上他的眼睛，在结着血壳的脸上冲出两道泪沟。他这个蠢材，把一切都葬送了！身边没了生意人，萨斯基亚和杜加尔和阿奇爵士可怎么办？准要乱成一团，这一小队人马会自己走进圈套。这一切他看得清清楚楚。海上来的那些人会把他们压倒，会出人命，公主会被轻而易举地掳走；警察磨到最后才赶到，只寻见一座空空的海岬。

===Original===
He had also most comprehensively wrecked himself, and at the thought the most genuine panic seized him. There was no earthly chance of escape, for he was tucked away in this infernal jungle till such time as his enemies had time to deal with him. As to what that dealing would be like he had no doubts, for they knew that he had been their chief opponent. Those desperate ruffians would not scruple to put an end to him. His mind dwelt with horrible fascination upon throat-cutting, no doubt because of the presence of the cord below his chin. He had heard it was not a painful death; at any rate he remembered a clerk he had once had, a feeble, timid creature, who had twice attempted suicide that way. Surely it could not be very bad, and it would soon be over.

===Chinese===
他把自己也葬送了个彻头彻尾，一想到这个，一阵再真切不过的恐慌便攫住了他。逃跑是半点指望也没有，他被塞在这片要命的密林里，单等敌人腾出工夫来发落。至于发落起来是什么光景，他毫无疑念，因为他们知道，他一直是对付他们的头号人物。那帮亡命恶徒要结果他，是不会有半分踌躇的。他的心思带着可怕的魔力，一味在割喉咙上打转，不用说，是下巴底下那道绳子的缘故。他听说那不算一种痛苦的死法；无论如何，他记得从前手底下有个文书，一个懦弱胆小的人物，用这个法子寻过两回短见。想来不会太难受，而且很快就会完事。

===Original===
But another thought came to him. They would carry him off in the ship and settle with him at their leisure. No swift merciful death for him. He had read dreadful tales of the Bolsheviks’ skill in torture, and now they all came back to him﻿—stories of Chinese mercenaries, and men buried alive, and death by agonising inches. He felt suddenly very cold and sick, and hung in his bonds for he had no strength in his limbs. Then the pressure on his throat braced him, and also quickened his numb mind. The liveliest terror ran like quicksilver through his veins.

===Chinese===
可跟着又来了一个念头。他们会把他弄上船，再从容不迫地跟他算账。一刀痛快的仁慈死法，他是别指望了。他读过种种讲布尔什维克用刑本事的骇人故事，这会儿全都涌了回来——中国雇佣兵的故事啦，活埋的故事啦，一寸一寸折磨死的故事啦。他突然觉得浑身发冷、直犯恶心，整个人吊在绳子上，四肢没有一点力气。随后喉咙上那一紧反倒给他提了神，也把他发麻的脑子催醒了。最活跃的恐怖像水银似的，在他的血管里四处奔流。

===Original===
He endured some moments of this anguish, till after many despairing clutches at his wits he managed to attain a measure of self-control. He certainly wasn’t going to allow himself to become mad. Death was death whatever form it took, and he had to face death as many better men had done before him. He had often thought about it and wondered how he should behave if the thing came to him. Respectably, he had hoped; heroically, he had sworn in his moments of confidence. But he had never for an instant dreamed of this cold, lonely, dreadful business. Last Sunday, he remembered, he had been basking in the afternoon sun in his little garden and reading about the end of Fergus MacIvor in *Waverley* and thrilling to the romance of it; and then Tibby had come out and summoned him in to tea. Then he had rather wanted to be a Jacobite in the ’45 and in peril of his neck, and now Providence had taken him most terribly at his word.

===Chinese===
这份煎熬他熬了一阵，直到绝望地一把又一把攥住自己的神智，才算挣得几分自制。他断断不能容许自己发疯。死就是死，不管披着什么模样，许多比他强的人都面对过死，他也得面对。他时常想到它，琢磨真到了那一步，自己会是什么德行。体体面面的，他曾这样指望；英雄气概的，他在信心十足的时刻曾这样赌咒。可他做梦也没有一时半刻梦到过眼前这桩又阴冷、又孤单、又可怕的事。他记得，上个礼拜天，他还在自家小花园里晒着午后的太阳，读《威弗利》（Waverley）里弗格斯·麦克艾弗（Fergus MacIvor）的结局，为那股传奇意味心头震颤；随后蒂比（Tibby）出来唤他进屋喝茶。那时候他倒满心想做个四五之年（the '45）的雅各布派（Jacobite），甘冒掉脑袋的风险，如今天意竟把他这句大话收取得可怕至极。

===Original===
A week ago﻿—! He groaned at the remembrance of that sunny garden. In seven days he had found a new world and tried a new life, and had come now to the end of it. He did not want to die, less now than ever with such wide horizons opening before him. But that was the worst of it, he reflected, for to have a great life great hazards must be taken, and there was always the risk of this sudden extinguisher.﻿ ﻿… Had he to choose again, far better the smooth sheltered bypath than this accursed romantic highway on to which he had blundered.﻿ ﻿… No, by Heaven, no! Confound it, if he had to choose he would do it all again. Something stiff and indomitable in his soul was bracing him to a manlier humour. There was no one to see the figure strapped to the fir, but had there been a witness he would have noted that at this stage Dickson shut his teeth and that his troubled eyes looked very steadily before him.

===Chinese===
一个礼拜以前——！想起那座洒满阳光的花园，他呻吟出声。七天里，他发现了一个新世界，试过了一桩新人生，如今走到了它的尽头。他不想死，眼下比任何时候都不想死，因为那么辽阔的地平线正在他面前展开。可坏就坏在这里，他想，要过伟大的日子，就得冒伟大的险，而那盏突然罩下来的灭灯罩，风险永远都在。……倘若再让他挑一回，那条平平坦坦、遮风避雨的便道，不知比这条他瞎撞上来的天杀的浪漫大道强出多少。……不，苍天在上，不！见鬼，真要再让他挑，他还要原样再来一遍。他灵魂里有个又硬又倔的东西，正把他撑向一股更有丈夫气的心绪。没有人看得见那个捆在松树上的身影，可倘若有个见证人在场，他会留意到，迪克森在这个关头咬紧了牙关，那双烦恼的眼睛十分镇定地望着前方。

===Original===
His business, he felt, was to keep from thinking, for if he thought at all there would be a flow of memories, of his wife, his home, his books, his friends, to unman him. So he steeled himself to blankness, like a sleepless man imagining white sheep in a gate.﻿ ﻿… He noted a robin below the hazels, strutting impudently. And there was a tit on a bracken frond, which made the thing sway like one of the seesaws he used to play with as a boy. There was no wind in that undergrowth, and any movement must be due to bird or beast. The tit flew off, and the oscillations of the bracken slowly died away. Then they began again, but more violently, and Dickson could not see the bird that caused them. It must be something down at the roots of the covert, a rabbit, perhaps, or a fox, or a weasel.

===Chinese===
他觉得自己当前的正事是不去想，因为只要一想，记忆便会涌来——妻子，家，藏书，朋友——把他的男儿气摧折掉。于是他硬起心肠做到一片空白，像失眠的人想象跳栅栏的白羊。……他注意到榛树底下有一只知更鸟，放肆地昂首踱步。还有一只山雀落在一茎蕨叶上，压得那东西摇来晃去，活像他小时候玩过的跷跷板。那一带的灌木丛里没有风，凡有动静，必是鸟雀或走兽闹的。山雀飞走了，蕨叶的摇晃慢慢止息。跟着又晃了起来，却晃得更凶，而迪克森看不见惹起晃动的鸟。准是藏丛根底下有什么东西，兴许是只兔子，也许是狐狸，或是黄鼠狼。

===Original===
He watched for the first sign of the beast, and thought he caught a glimpse of tawny fur. Yes, there it was﻿—pale dirty yellow, a weasel clearly. Then suddenly the patch grew larger, and to his amazement he looked at a human face﻿—the face of a pallid small boy.

===Chinese===
他盯着等那走兽露头的头一个迹象，自以为瞥见了一撮黄褐色的毛。没错，在那儿——浅淡的脏黄色，分明是只黄鼠狼。忽然间，那一小块影子大了起来，他瞠目结舌地望见了一张人脸——一张苍白的小男孩的脸。

===Original===
A head disentangled itself, followed by thin shoulders, and then by a pair of very dirty bare legs. The figure raised itself and looked sharply round to make certain that the coast was clear. Then it stood up and saluted, revealing the well-known lineaments of Wee Jaikie.

===Chinese===
一颗脑袋从枝叶里钻了出来，跟着是一对瘦肩膀，再跟着是两条脏得可以的赤腿。那身影直起身子，飞快地四下张望，确认四下无人。然后他站起身，行了个礼，露出小杰基那张人所熟知的面孔。

===Original===
At the sight Dickson knew that he was safe by that certainty of instinct which is independent of proof, like the man who prays for a sign and has his prayer answered. He observed that the boy was quietly sobbing. Jaikie surveyed the position for an instant with red-rimmed eyes and then unclasped a knife, feeling the edge of the blade on his thumb. He darted behind the fir, and a second later Dickson’s wrists were free. Then he sawed at the legs, and cut the shackles which tied them together, and then﻿—most circumspectly﻿—assaulted the cord which bound Dickson’s neck to the trunk. There now remained only the two bonds which fastened the legs and the body to the tree.

===Chinese===
一看见他，迪克森便知道自己安全了——凭的是那种不需要证据的直觉的确信，就像祷告求一个兆头、祷告便得了应允的人。他看出那孩子正无声地抽泣着。杰基用一双哭红的眼睛把局面扫视了一瞬，随即掰开一把折刀，拿拇指试了试刀锋。他一闪身蹿到松树后头，一秒钟后，迪克森的手腕就松开了。接着他锯腿上的绳子，割断了把两腿捆在一起的绑索，然后——万分小心地——对付起把迪克森的脖子拴在树干上的那道绳。如今只剩下把腿和身子拴在树上的两道了。

===Original===
There was a sound in the wood different from the wind and stream. Jaikie listened like a startled hind.

===Chinese===
树林里响起了一种声音，既不同于风，也不同于水。杰基侧耳细听，像一只受惊的母鹿。

===Original===
“They’re comin’ back,” he gasped. “Just you bide where ye are and let on ye’re still tied up.”

===Chinese===
“他们回来啦，”他喘着气说。“你就乖乖待在原地，装成还捆着的模样。”

===Original===
He disappeared in the scrub as inconspicuously as a rat, while two of the tinklers came up the slope from the waterside. Dickson in a fever of impatience cursed Wee Jaikie for not cutting his remaining bonds so that he could at least have made a dash for freedom. And then he realised that the boy had been right. Feeble and cramped as he was, he would have stood no chance in a race.

===Chinese===
他钻进灌木丛，像老鼠一样无声无息，这时两个补锅佬正从水边爬上坡来。迪克森心急如焚，暗骂小杰基为什么不割断剩下的绳索，好歹让他拼死夺路逃一遭。可随即他就明白，孩子做得对。凭他这会儿的虚弱僵麻，真赛起跑来，半点赢面也没有。

===Original===
One of the tinklers was the man called Ecky. He had been running hard, and was mopping his brow.

===Chinese===
两个补锅佬里有一个，是那个叫埃基的。他一路狂奔过来，正抹着脑门上的汗。

===Original===
“Hob’s seen the brig,” he said. “It’s droppin’ anchor ayont the Dookits whaur there’s a beild frae the wund and deep water. They’ll be landit in half an ’oor. Awa’ you up to the Hoose and tell Dobson, and me and Sim and Hob will meet the boats at the Garplefit.”

===Chinese===
“霍布（Hob）瞧见那条双桅船了，”他说。“船在杜基茨（Dookits）外头抛了锚，那地方背风，水又深。半个钟头人就上岸。你上府里去告诉多布森，俺跟西姆（Sim）还有霍布，到加普尔河下游去接船。”

===Original===
The other cast a glance towards Dickson.

===Chinese===
另一个朝迪克森瞟了一眼。

===Original===
“What about him?” he asked.

===Chinese===
“这一位怎么发落？”他问。

===Original===
The two scrutinised their prisoner from a distance of a few paces. Dickson, well aware of his peril, held himself as stiff as if every bond had been in place. The thought flashed on him that if he were too immobile they might think he was dying or dead, and come close to examine him. If they only kept their distance, the dusk of the wood would prevent them detecting Jaikie’s handiwork.

===Chinese===
两人隔着几步远，端详他们的俘虏。迪克森深知自家性命悬于一线，把身子绷得笔直，仿佛每一道绳索都还原封没动。他心头灵光一闪：要是他一动不动过了头，他们兴许当他快死了或已经死了，会凑近前来查看。只要他们隔着老远，树林里的昏暗就会叫他们瞧不出杰基动过的手脚。

===Original===
“What’ll you take to let me go?” he asked plaintively.

===Chinese===
“放俺走，你们要什么价？”他哀声哀气地问。

===Original===
“Naething that you could offer, my mannie,” said Ecky.

===Chinese===
“你出得起的东西，俺们一样也看不上，小老弟。”埃基说。

===Original===
“I’ll give you a five-pound note apiece.”

===Chinese===
“俺给你们一人一张五镑的票子。”

===Original===
“Produce the siller,” said the other.

===Chinese===
“先把钱拿出来。”另一个说。

===Original===
“It’s in my pocket.”

===Chinese===
“在俺口袋里。”

===Original===
“It’s no’ that. We riped your pooches lang syne.”

===Chinese===
“不是这么回事。你的口袋，俺们老早就翻遍啦。”

===Original===
“I’ll take you to Glasgow with me and pay you there. Honour bright.”

===Chinese===
“俺带你们跟俺去格拉斯哥，到那儿付钱。说话算数。”

===Original===
Ecky spat. “D’ye think we’re gowks? Man, there’s no siller ye could pay wad mak’ it worth our while to lowse ye. Bide quiet there and ye’ll see some queer things ere nicht. C’way, Davie.”

===Chinese===
埃基啐了一口。“你当俺们是傻瓜？老兄，你出多大的价钱，也抵不得俺们放你的干系。乖乖待着吧，天黑前有你瞧的热闹。走吧，戴维（Davie）。”

===Original===
The two set off at a good pace down the stream, while Dickson’s pulsing heart returned to its normal rhythm. As the sound of their feet died away Wee Jaikie crawled out from cover, dry-eyed now and very businesslike. He slit the last thongs, and Dickson fell limply on his face.

===Chinese===
两人迈开大步顺河去了，迪克森突突狂跳的心这才落回原处。脚步声一远，小杰基从藏身处爬出来，眼泪已干，一副干练样子。他割断最后几道绳，迪克森软软地扑倒在地。

===Original===
“Losh, laddie, I’m awful stiff,” he groaned. “Now, listen. Away all your pith to Dougal, and tell him that the brig’s in and the men will be landing inside the hour. Tell him I’m coming as fast as my legs will let me. The Princess will likely be there already and Sir Archibald and his men, but if they’re no’, tell Dougal they’re coming. Haste you, Jaikie. And see here, I’ll never forget what you’ve done for me the day. You’re a fine wee laddie!”

===Chinese===
“哎呀，小子，俺浑身都僵透啦，”他哼哼道。“现在听着。你使出全身的劲儿跑去找杜加尔，告诉他双桅船到了，不出一个钟头人就要上岸。告诉他，俺正由着两条腿能有多快赶多快地来。公主多半已经到了，阿奇博尔德爵士和他的人也到了；要是还没到，就叫杜加尔知道他们正在路上。快去，杰基。还有你听着，你今天对俺做的这份事，俺一辈子忘不了。你是条好样的小汉子！”

===Original===
The obedient Die-Hard disappeared, and Dickson painfully and laboriously set himself to climb the slope. He decided that his quickest and safest route lay by the high road, and he had also some hopes of recovering his bicycle. On examining his body he seemed to have sustained no very great damage, except a painful cramping of legs and arms and a certain dizziness in the head. His pockets had been thoroughly rifled, and he reflected with amusement that he, the well-to-do Mr. McCunn, did not possess at the moment a single copper.

===Chinese===
听话的死硬帮小子一溜烟去了，迪克森忍着酸痛，一步一挪地往坡上爬。他断定走大路是最快最稳的一条道，他还指望寻回他那辆自行车。他把浑身上下检视了一遍，似乎没受什么大伤，只有手脚抽得生疼、脑袋有些发晕。他的口袋被翻了个底朝天，他想想不觉好笑：他这位家道殷实的麦坎先生，眼下一枚铜子儿也不曾有了。

===Original===
But his spirits were soaring, for somehow his escape had given him an assurance of ultimate success. Providence had directly interfered on his behalf by the hand of Wee Jaikie, and that surely meant that it would see him through. But his chief emotion was an ardour of impatience to get to the scene of action. He must be at Dalquharter before the men from the sea; he must find Dougal and discover his dispositions. Heritage would be on guard in the Tower and in a very little the enemy would be round it. It would be just like the Princess to try and enter there, but at all costs that must be hindered. She and Sir Archie must not be cornered in stone walls, but must keep their communications open and fall on the enemy’s flank. Oh, if the police would only come in time, what a rounding-up of miscreants that day would see!

===Chinese===
可他的兴致正朝天上游，因为不知怎么，这一场脱险叫他笃定了终将成功。天意借着小杰基的手直接插手护佑了他，这便一定是打算保他到底。不过他心头最大的情绪，是一股恨不得立刻扑到战场上去的焦灼。他必须赶在海上来人之前到达尔夸特；他必须找到杜加尔，摸清他的部署。赫里蒂奇会在塔里守着，要不了多久，敌人就要围上来了。公主就干得出闯进塔去的事，可无论如何得拦住她。她同阿奇爵士绝不能被堵死在石头墙里，必须保持通路畅通，从侧翼扑击敌人。唉，警察要是能及时赶到，这一天将看到何等一场对恶棍的大围剿！

===Original===
As the trees thinned on the brow of the slope and he saw the sky, he realised that the afternoon was far advanced. It must be well on for five o’clock. The wind still blew furiously, and the oaks on the fringes of the wood were whipped like saplings. Ruefully he admitted that the gale would not defeat the enemy. If the brig found a sheltered anchorage on the south side of the headland beyond the Garple, it would be easy enough for boats to make the Garple mouth, though it might be a difficult job to get out again. The thought quickened his steps, and he came out of cover on to the public road without a prior reconnaissance.

===Chinese===
坡顶的树木渐渐稀疏，他望见了天，才醒悟下午已过去大半。准是快五点了。风依旧刮得凶猛，林子边上的橡树被抽打得像小树苗一样弯腰。他懊恼地承认，这场大风坏不了敌人的事。倘若那艘双桅船在加普尔河以外海岬的南侧寻见一处背风的锚地，小艇要划到加普尔河口并不难，虽说再出去兴许要费些周折。这个念头催快了他的脚步，他没先探一探，便出了藏身之处，上了大路。

===Original===
Just in front of him stood a motor-bicycle. Something had gone wrong with it for its owner was tinkering at it, on the side farthest from Dickson. A wild hope seized him that this might be the vanguard of the police, and he went boldly towards it. The owner, who was kneeling, raised his face at the sound of footsteps and Dickson looked into his eyes.

===Chinese===
就在他前头，停着一辆摩托车。车出了毛病，因为车主正俯身捣鼓它，蹲在离迪克森最远的那一侧。一个狂野的希望攫住了他：这说不定是警察的先头部队。他大着胆子走上前去。车主跪在地上，听见脚步声抬起了脸，迪克森望进了他的眼睛。

===Original===
He recognised them only too well. They belonged to the man he had seen in the inn at Kirkmichael, the man whom Heritage had decided was an Australian, but whom they now knew to be their arch-enemy﻿—the man called Paul who had persecuted the Princess for years and whom alone of all beings on earth she feared. He had been expected before, but had arrived now in the nick of time while the brig was casting anchor. Saskia had said that he had a devil’s brain, and Dickson, as he stared at him, saw a fiendish cleverness in his straight brows and a remorseless cruelty in his stiff jaw and his pale eyes.

===Chinese===
这双眼睛他认得太清楚了。它们属于他在柯克迈克尔的客栈里见过的那个人——赫里蒂奇断定是个澳大利亚人、他们如今却知道是死敌的那个人——那个叫保罗的，多年来迫害公主、普天之下她唯一惧怕的人。他早先就该到，却偏偏掐着时辰此刻赶到，正赶上双桅船落锚。萨斯基亚说过，他长着一副魔鬼的头脑，迪克森盯着他，只见他平直的眉宇间透着一股邪魔似的机敏，僵硬的下颚和淡色的眼睛里透着不容情的残忍。

===Original===
He achieved the bravest act of his life. Shaky and dizzy as he was, with freedom newly opened to him and the mental torments of his captivity still an awful recollection, he did not hesitate. He saw before him the villain of the drama, the one man that stood between the Princess and peace of mind. He regarded no consequences, gave no heed to his own fate, and thought only how to put his enemy out of action. There was a big spanner lying on the ground. He seized it and with all his strength smote at the man’s face.

===Chinese===
他成就了平生最勇敢的一举。他明明摇摇晃晃、头晕眼花，自由刚刚到手，被囚时那份心神上的苦刑还历历如在目前——他却毫不迟疑。他看见面前站着这出戏里的反派，那唯一横在公主与心境安宁之间的男人。他不计后果，不顾自家命数，一门心思只想着怎样叫敌人丧失战斗力。地上躺着一把大扳手。他抄将起来，用尽全身力气朝那人的脸砸下去。

===Original===
The motorcyclist, kneeling and working hard at his machine, had raised his head at Dickson’s approach and beheld a wild apparition﻿—a short man in ragged tweeds, with a bloody brow and long smears of blood on his cheeks. The next second he observed the threat of attack, and ducked his head so that the spanner only grazed his scalp. The motor-bicycle toppled over, its owner sprang to his feet, and found the short man, very pale and gasping, about to renew the assault. In such a crisis there was no time for inquiry, and the cyclist was well trained in self-defence. He leaped the prostrate bicycle, and before his assailant could get in a blow brought his left fist into violent contact with his chin. Dickson tottered back a step or two and then subsided among the bracken.

===Chinese===
那骑手本来跪着，埋头大干修他的车，迪克森走近时他抬起头来，看见了一副野鬼现形的景象——一个矮个子男人，穿着破烂的粗花呢衣裳，眉额带血，脸颊上拖着长长的血道子。下一秒他察觉了袭击的来势，一缩头，扳手只擦过他的头皮。摩托车翻倒在地，车主一跃而起，只见那矮个子男人脸色刷白、喘着粗气，正要再度扑上来。这样的危急关头，容不得盘问，而这位骑手受过良好的自卫训练。他跃过倒地的摩托车，趁袭击者还来不及出手，左拳已经重重磕上了他的下巴。迪克森踉跄着倒退一两步，随即瘫倒在蕨草丛里。

===Original===
He did not lose his senses, but he had no more strength in him. He felt horribly ill, and struggled in vain to get up. The cyclist, a gigantic figure, towered above him. “Who the devil are you?” he was asking. “What do you mean by it?”

===Chinese===
他没有昏过去，可浑身再无半点力气。他难受得厉害，挣扎着想爬起来，却是枉然。那骑手，一座巨人般的身影，高高立在他上方。“你这家伙到底是谁？”那声音在问。“你这是什么意思？”

===Original===
Dickson had no breath for words, and knew that if he tried to speak he would be very sick. He could only stare up like a dog at the angry eyes. Angry beyond question they were, but surely not malevolent. Indeed, as they looked at the shameful figure on the ground, amusement filled them. The face relaxed into a smile.

===Chinese===
迪克森喘不匀气来说话，也知道一开口准要呕吐。他只能像狗一样仰望着那双怒目。怒是真怒，却分明没有恶意。真的，当那双眼睛打量地上这副丢人现眼的形象时，里头竟盛满了笑意。那张脸松开来，化成了微笑。

===Original===
“Who on earth are you?” the voice repeated. And then into it came recognition. “I’ve seen you before. I believe you’re the little man I saw last week at the Black Bull. Be so good as to explain why you want to murder me?”

===Chinese===
“你到底是谁呀？”那声音又说了一遍。跟着，话音里透出了认出来的意思。“我从前见过你。我相信，你就是上礼拜我在黑牛客栈见过的那个小个子。劳您大驾，可否解释解释，您为什么要谋杀我？”

===Original===
Explanation was beyond Dickson, but his conviction was being woefully shaken. Saskia had said her enemy was as beautiful as a devil﻿—he remembered the phrase, for he had thought it ridiculous. This man was magnificent, but there was nothing devilish in his lean grave face.

===Chinese===
解释是迪克森办不到的，而他的确信正凄凄惶惶地动摇着。萨斯基亚说过，她的仇敌美得像个魔鬼——这句话他记得，因为他当时觉得荒唐。这个人仪表堂堂，可他清瘦庄重的脸上没有一丝魔鬼气。

===Original===
“What’s your name?” the voice was asking.

===Chinese===
“你叫什么名字？”那声音问。

===Original===
“Tell me yours first,” Dickson essayed to stutter between spasms of nausea.

===Chinese===
“先说说你的。”迪克森在一阵一阵的反胃之间，试着结结巴巴地说。

===Original===
“My name is Alexander Nicholson,” was the answer.

===Chinese===
“我名叫亚历山大·尼科尔森（Alexander Nicholson）。”对方答道。

===Original===
“Then you’re no’ the man.” It was a cry of wrath and despair.

===Chinese===
“那你就不是那个人。”这是一声怒极而绝望的喊叫。

===Original===
“You’re a very desperate little chap. For whom had I the honour to be mistaken?”

===Chinese===
“你这小个子可真敢玩命。敢问我何德何能，有幸被错认作了哪一位？”

===Original===
Dickson had now wriggled into a sitting position and had clasped his hands above his aching head.

===Chinese===
迪克森这时已扭着身子坐起，双手在作痛的头顶上交握起来。

===Original===
“I thought you were a Russian, name of Paul,” he groaned.

===Chinese===
“俺还当你是个俄国人，名字叫保罗。”他呻吟道。

===Original===
“Paul! Paul who?”

===Chinese===
“保罗！哪个保罗？”

===Original===
“Just Paul. A Bolshevik and an awful bad lot.”

===Chinese===
“就叫保罗。一个布尔什维克，顶坏顶坏的坏种。”

===Original===
Dickson could not see the change which his words wrought in the other’s face. He found himself picked up in strong arms and carried to a bog-pool where his battered face was carefully washed, his throbbing brows laved, and a wet handkerchief bound over them. Then he was given brandy in the socket of a flask, which eased his nausea. The cyclist ran his bicycle to the roadside, and found a seat for Dickson behind the turf-dyke of the old bucht.

===Chinese===
迪克森看不见自己这句话在那人脸上引起的变化。他发觉自己被一双有力的胳膊抱起来，抱到一泓沼泽水洼边，那张打得稀烂的脸被细细洗净，突突跳的眉骨被濡敷了水，一条湿手帕缠在了额上。随后有人用扁酒瓶的金属杯盖给他喝了白兰地，压住了恶心。骑手把车推到路边，又在老羊圈的草皮矮墙后头，给迪克森寻了个坐处。

===Original===
“Now you are going to tell me everything,” he said. “If the Paul who is your enemy is the Paul I think him, then we are allies.”

===Chinese===
“现在你要把一切都告诉我了，”他说。“如果你的仇敌保罗，就是我心目中的那个保罗，那么咱们就是盟友。”

===Original===
But Dickson did not need this assurance. His mind had suddenly received a revelation. The Princess had expected an enemy, but also a friend. Might not this be the long-awaited friend, for whose sake she was rooted to Huntingtower with all its terrors?

===Chinese===
可迪克森用不着这句保证。他的脑子里霎时得了天启。公主等的是一个仇敌，可还有一位朋友。这位会不会就是那位久候不至的朋友——为了他，她才甘心钉在处处凶险的亨廷塔，寸步不离？

===Original===
“Are you sure you name’s no’ Alexis?” he asked.

===Chinese===
“你敢保你的名字不叫亚历克西斯？”他问。

===Original===
“In my own country I was called Alexis Nicolaevitch, for I am a Russian. But for some years I have made my home with your folk, and I call myself Alexander Nicholson, which is the English form. Who told you about Alexis?”

===Chinese===
“在敝国，人家叫我亚历克西斯·尼古拉耶维奇（Alexis Nicolaevitch），因为我本是俄国人。可是近几年来，我在你们同胞中间安了家，便自称亚历山大·尼科尔森，这是它的英国化形式。是谁同你说起亚历克西斯的？”

===Original===
“Give me your hand,” said Dickson shamefacedly. “Man, she’s been looking for you for weeks. You’re terribly behind the fair.”

===Chinese===
“把手伸给俺，”迪克森讪讪地说。“老兄，她找你找了几个礼拜啦。你可把这场集误得不轻啊。”

===Original===
“She!” he cried. “For God’s sake tell me all you know.”

===Chinese===
“她！”他喊道。“看在上帝分上，把你知道的都告诉我。”

===Original===
“Ay, she﻿—the Princess. But what are we havering here for? I tell you at this moment she’s somewhere down about the old Tower, and there’s boatloads of blagyirds landing from the sea. Help me up, man, for I must be off. The story will keep. Losh, it’s very near the darkening. If you’re Alexis, you’re just about in time for a battle.”

===Chinese===
“是啊，她——公主。可咱们还在这儿闲扯个什么劲？俺告诉你，这会儿她就在老塔那一带的什么地方，海上正一船一船地往下卸那帮流氓。扶俺起来，老兄，俺必须动身。故事留着路上讲。哎呀，天都快擦黑了。你要真是亚历克西斯，来得正好，赶得上一场仗打。”

===Original===
But Dickson on his feet was but a frail creature. He was still deplorably giddy, and his legs showed an unpleasing tendency to crumple. “I’m fair done,” he moaned. “You see, I’ve been tied up all day to a tree and had two sore bashes on my head. Get you on that bicycle and hurry on, and I’ll hirple after you the best I can. I’ll direct you the road, and if you’re lucky you’ll find a Die-Hard about the village. Away with you, man, and never mind me.”

===Chinese===
可站起来了的迪克森不过是个虚弱的货色。他仍旧晕得厉害，两条腿还露出一股不讨人喜欢的打弯的倾向。“俺全垮啦，”他哼哼道。“你瞧，俺被拴在树上一整天，脑袋上挨了两记狠的。你骑上那车赶紧走，俺尽力一瘸一拐地在后头跟着撵。俺给你指路，运气好呢，村子左近就能碰上一个死硬帮。快走吧，老兄，别管俺了。”

===Original===
“We go together,” said the other quietly. “You can sit behind me and hang on to my waist. Before you turned up I had pretty well got the thing in order.”

===Chinese===
“咱们一起走，”那人平静地说。“你坐在我身后，搂住我的腰。你还没来之前，这机器我已修得差不离了。”

===Original===
Dickson in a fever of impatience sat by while the Russian put the finishing touches to the machine, and as well as his anxiety allowed put him in possession of the main facts of the story. He told of how he and Heritage had come to Dalquharter, of the first meeting with Saskia, of the trip to Glasgow with the jewels, of the exposure of Loudon the factor, of last night’s doings in the House, and of the journey that morning to the Mains of Garple. He sketched the figures on the scene﻿—Heritage and Sir Archie, Dobson and his gang, the Gorbals Die-Hards. He told of the enemy’s plans so far as he knew them.

===Chinese===
迪克森心急火燎地坐在一旁，看那俄国人给机器做收尾的工夫，趁着焦灼容许的程度，把故事的主要情节一一交了底。他讲了他和赫里蒂奇怎样来到达尔夸特，讲同萨斯基亚的初会，讲揣着珠宝去格拉斯哥的那一趟，讲代管人劳登的败露，讲昨夜府里的种种，还讲今天早晨去加普尔田庄的那段路程。他把场上人物勾了个轮廓——赫里蒂奇和阿奇爵士，多布森和他那伙人，戈尔巴尔死硬帮。又把敌人的计划，就他所知的，说了一遍。

===Original===
“Looked at from a business point of view,” he said, “the situation’s like this. There’s Heritage in the Tower, with Dobson, Léon and Spidel sitting round him. Somewhere about the place there’s the Princess and Sir Archibald and three men with guns from the Mains. Dougal and his five laddies are running loose in the policies. And there’s four tinklers and God knows how many foreign ruffians pushing up from the Garplefoot, and a brig lying waiting to carry off the ladies. Likewise there’s the police, somewhere on the road, though the dear kens when they’ll turn up. It’s awful the incompetence of our Government, and the rates and taxes that high!﻿ ﻿… And there’s you and me by this roadside, and I’m no more use than a tattie-bogle.﻿ ﻿… That’s the situation, and the question is what’s our plan to be? We must keep the blagyirds in play till the police come, and at the same time we must keep the Princess out of danger. That’s why I’m wanting back, for they’ve sore need of a business head. Yon Sir Archibald’s a fine fellow, but I doubt he’ll be a bit rash, and the Princess is no’ to hold or bind. Our first job is to find Dougal and get a grip of the facts.”

===Chinese===
“照生意人的眼光看，”他说，“局面是这样。赫里蒂奇在塔里，多布森、莱昂和斯皮特尔围着他不走。这地界的什么去处，有公主和阿奇博尔德爵士，还有田庄来的三个带枪的人。杜加尔带着他那五个小子，在庄园园地里四下游动。再有四个补锅佬，天知道多少外国恶棍，正从加普尔河下游往上推，一条双桅船泊在外头，专等把两位夫人运走。同样还有警察，在大道上的什么地方——虽说鬼才知道他们几时露面。咱们政府的无能真是吓死人，捐税又收得那样高！……再有就是路边上的你我，俺呢，比个吓鸟的稻草人（tattie-bogle）强不到哪儿去。……局面如此，问题是咱们的章程怎么定？咱们必须拖住那帮流氓，撑到警察来，同时还得让公主躲开凶险。所以俺才急着回去，那边太缺一颗生意人的脑袋。那位阿奇博尔德爵士是条好汉，可俺怕他有点鲁莽，公主呢，又是拴不住笼不住的人。咱们头一件差事，是找到杜加尔，把实情抓在手里。”

===Original===
“I am going to the Princess,” said the Russian.

===Chinese===
“我要到公主那边去。”俄国人说。

===Original===
“Ay, that’ll be best. You’ll be maybe able to manage her, for you’ll be well acquaint.”

===Chinese===
“是啊，那样最好。你兴许管得住她，你们到底是熟到家的。”

===Original===
“She is my kinswoman. She is also my affianced wife.”

===Chinese===
“她是我的亲属。她也是我订了婚的妻子。”

===Original===
“Keep us!” Dickson exclaimed, with a doleful thought of Heritage. “What ailed you then no’ to look after her better?”

===Chinese===
“我的老天！”迪克森失声道，心里替赫里蒂奇发苦。“那你当时是怎么了，不好好照看她？”

===Original===
“We have been long separated, because it was her will. She had work to do and disappeared from me, though I searched all Europe for her. Then she sent me word, when the danger became extreme, and summoned me to her aid. But she gave me poor directions, for she did not know her own plans very clearly. She spoke of a place called Darkwater, and I have been hunting half Scotland for it. It was only last night that I heard of Dalquharter and guessed that that might be the name. But I was far down in Galloway, and have ridden fifty miles today.”

===Chinese===
“我们分离已久，因为那是她的意愿。她有工作要做，从我眼前消失了，尽管我寻遍了整个欧洲。后来危局到了极处，她捎信给我，召我去援。可她指的路含糊得很，因为她自己的计划也不很清楚。她说起一个叫达克沃特的地方，我为此寻遍了半个苏格兰。直到昨天夜里，我才听人说起达尔夸特，猜着想必就是这个名字。可我那时远在加洛韦南边，今天已经骑了五十英里。”

===Original===
“It’s a queer thing, but I wouldn’t take you for a Russian.”

===Chinese===
“怪事儿，可俺真看不出你是个俄国人。”

===Original===
Alexis finished his work and put away his tools. “For the present,” he said, “I am an Englishman, till my country comes again to her senses. Ten years ago I left Russia, for I was sick of the foolishness of my class and wanted a free life in a new world. I went to Australia and made good as an engineer. I am a partner in a firm which is pretty well known even in Britain. When war broke out I returned to fight for my people, and when Russia fell out of the war, I joined the Australians in France and fought with them till the Armistice. And now I have only one duty left, to save the Princess and take her with me to my new home till Russia is a nation once more.”

===Chinese===
亚历克西斯做完了活计，把工具收拾起来。“眼下，”他说，“我是个英国人，直到我的祖国重新醒过神来。十年前我离开俄国，因为我厌透了本阶级的愚蠢，想到新世界过自由的生活。我去了澳大利亚（Australia），当工程师干出了名堂。我是一家公司的合伙人，那公司即便在英国也颇有名头。大战爆发，我回去为我的人民打仗；俄国退出战争后，我加入了在法国的澳大利亚部队，同他们并肩打到停战日。如今我剩下的只有一桩义务：救出公主，带她同我回我的新家，直到俄国重新成为一个国家。”

===Original===
Dickson whistled joyfully. “So Mr. Heritage was right. He aye said you were an Australian.﻿ ﻿… And you’re a business man! That’s grand hearing and puts my mind at rest. You must take charge of the party at the House, for Sir Archibald’s a daft young lad and Mr. Heritage is a poet. I thought I would have to go myself, but I doubt I would just be a hindrance with my dwaibly legs. I’d be better outside, watching for the police.﻿ ﻿… Are you ready, sir?”

===Chinese===
迪克森欢欢喜喜打了个呼哨。“这么说，赫里蒂奇先生说对了。他早就说你是个澳大利亚人。……你到底是个生意人！这话听着提气，俺的心放下了。府里那一队人马必须归你统管，阿奇博尔德爵士是个愣头小伙子，赫里蒂奇先生又是个诗人。俺原想着只好亲自去，可俺怕凭俺这两条软塌塌打晃的腿，去了反倒碍事。俺还是在外头好，守望警察。……先生，你预备好了么？”

===Original===
Dickson not without difficulty perched himself astride the luggage carrier, firmly grasping the rider round the middle. The machine started, but it was evidently in a bad way, for it made poor going till the descent towards the main Auchenlochan road. On the slope it warmed up and they crossed the Garple bridge at a fair pace. There was to be no pleasant April twilight, for the stormy sky had already made dusk, and in a very little the dark would fall. So sombre was the evening that Dickson did not notice a figure in the shadow of the roadside pines till it whistled shrilly on its fingers. He cried on Alexis to stop, and, this being accomplished with some suddenness, fell off at Dougal’s feet.

===Chinese===
迪克森费了番周折，跨坐上后头的行李架，把骑车人的腰紧紧搂定。机器发动了，可它显然不对劲，直到朝奥琴洛坎大路下坡之前，一直走得有气无力。一到坡上它就热起了身，两人以不算慢的速度过了加普尔桥。四月黄昏的怡人光景是没指望了，风雨的天空早已压下暮色，再有一会儿，天黑就要落下来。夜色如此阴沉，路边松影里立着一个人影，迪克森竟没有察觉，直到那人把手指插进嘴里，打出一声尖锐的唿哨。他喊亚历克西斯停车，车停得有些突兀，他便从上面摔下来，正摔在杜加尔脚边。

===Original===
“What’s the news?” he demanded.

===Chinese===
“有什么消息？”他问。

===Original===
Dougal glanced at Alexis and seemed to approve his looks.

===Chinese===
杜加尔扫了亚历克西斯一眼，似乎看得顺眼。

===Original===
“Napoleon has just reported that three boatloads, making either twenty-three or twenty-four men﻿—they were gey ill to count﻿—has landed at Garplefit and is makin’ their way to the auld Tower. The tinklers warned Dobson and soon it’ll be a’ bye wi’ Heritage.”

===Chinese===
“拿破仑刚报上来：三船人，凑起来二十三还是二十四个——数起来实在难数——在加普尔河下游上了岸，正往老塔去。补锅佬们给多布森报了信，要不了多久，赫里蒂奇就得全完了。”

===Original===
“The Princess is not there?” was Dickson’s anxious inquiry.

===Chinese===
“公主不在那边？”迪克森焦急地询问。

===Original===
“Na, na. Heritage is there his lone. They were for joinin’ him, but I wouldn’t let them. She came wi’ a man they call Sir Erchibald and three gemkeepers wi’ guns. I stoppit their cawr up the road and tell’t them the lie o’ the land. Yon Sir Erchibald has poor notions o’ strawtegy. He was for bangin’ into the auld Tower straight away and shootin’ Dobson if he tried to stop them. ‘Havers,’ say I, ‘let them break their teeth on the Tower, thinkin’ the leddy’s inside, and that’ll give us time, for Heritage is no’ the lad to surrender in a hurry.’ ”

===Chinese===
“不在，不在。赫里蒂奇独自个儿守在那儿。他们本来要同他会合，是俺不许。她同一位人家称作埃尔奇爵士的一起来的，还带着三个拿枪的猎场看守。俺在半道拦下他们的汽车，把地势给他们交代了个明白。那位埃尔奇爵士，战略上的见识可不怎么样。他要当场直闯老塔，多布森敢拦就毙了他。俺说：‘胡闹。让他们在塔上磕掉牙去吧，只当夫人还在里头，这就给咱们腾出工夫，赫里蒂奇可不是轻易投降的主儿。’”

===Original===
“Where are they now?”

===Chinese===
“这会儿他们在哪儿？”

===Original===
“In the Hoose o’ Dalquharter, and a sore job I had gettin’ them in. We’ve shifted our base again, without the enemy suspectin’.”

===Chinese===
“在达尔夸特府里，把那几位弄进去，可叫俺费了大劲。咱们又转移了一次基地，神不知鬼不觉，敌人没有起疑。”

===Original===
“Any word of the police?”

===Chinese===
“警察有信儿没有？”

===Original===
“The polis!” and Dougal spat cynically. “It seems they’re a dour crop to shift. Sir Erchibald was sayin’ that him and the lassie had been to the Chief Constable, but the man was terrible auld and slow. They convertit him, but he threepit that it would take a long time to collect his men and that there was no danger o’ the brig landin’ afore night. He’s wrong there onyway, for they’re landit.”

===Chinese===
“警察！”杜加尔冷冷地啐了一口。“瞧那样子，是一帮挪不动的倔货。埃尔奇爵士说，他同那位姑娘去找过郡警察总长，可那人又老又慢。他们把他说转了心，可他一口咬定，召集人手要好长一阵工夫，还说双桅船天黑前上不了岸。好在这一点他错了，人家已经上岸了。”

===Original===
“Dougal,” said Dickson, “you’ve heard the Princess speak of a friend she was expecting here called Alexis. This is him. You can address him as Mr. Nicholson. Just arrived in the nick of time. You must get him into the House, for he’s the best right to be beside the lady.﻿ ﻿… Jaikie would tell you that I’ve been sore mishandled the day, and am no’ very fit for a battle. But Mr. Nicholson’s a business man and he’ll do as well. You’re keeping the Die-Hards outside, I hope?”

===Chinese===
“杜加尔，”迪克森说，“你听公主说起过，她在这儿盼着一位朋友，叫亚历克西斯。就是他。你可以称他尼科尔森先生。来得正是时候，赶了个正着。你必须把他送进府里，要说守在那位夫人身边，谁的权利也大不过他。……杰基该告诉你了，俺今天遭了大罪，打不了什么仗。好在尼科尔森先生是个生意人，一样顶事。俺指望，死硬帮你都留在外头了吧？”

===Original===
“Ay. Thomas Yownie’s in charge, and Jaikie will be in and out with orders. They’ve instructions to watch for the polis, and keep an eye on the Garplefit. It’s a mortal long front to hold, but there’s no other way. I must be in the Hoose mysel’. Thomas Yownie’s headquarters is the auld wife’s henhoose.”

===Chinese===
“是的。托马斯·尤尼管着他们，杰基来回跑着传令。他们领了章程，一要盯住警察，二要看着加普尔河下游。这么一条战线守起来长得要命，可没有第二条路。俺本人必须进府里去。托马斯·尤尼的司令部，就设在老婆婆家的鸡棚里。”

===Original===
At that moment in a pause of the gale came the far-borne echo of a shot.

===Chinese===
正当此时，大风歇了一口，远处传来一声枪响的回音。

===Original===
“Pistol,” said Alexis.

===Chinese===
“手枪。”亚历克西斯说。

===Original===
“Heritage,” said Dougal. “Trade will be gettin’ brisk with him. Start your machine and I’ll hang on ahint. We’ll try the road by the West Lodge.”

===Chinese===
“赫里蒂奇打响了。”杜加尔说。“他那边的买卖要兴旺起来了。发动你的车，俺吊在后座上。咱们走西门房那条道试试。”

===Original===
Presently the pair disappeared in the dusk, the noise of the engine was swallowed up in the wild orchestra of the wind, and Dickson hobbled towards the village in a state of excitement which made him oblivious of his wounds. That lonely pistol shot was, he felt, the bell to ring up the curtain on the last act of the play.


===Chinese===
转眼间，那一对人影没入了暮色，马达的声响被风声的狂野交响吞没，迪克森朝村子一瘸一拐地赶去，那股兴奋劲儿叫他忘了自己的一身伤。他感到，那声孤零零的枪响，就是这出戏最后一幕开场拉幕的铃。

## XIII. The Coming of the Danish Brig / 十三、丹麦双桅船的到来
===Original===
Mr. John Heritage, solitary in the old Tower, found much to occupy his mind. His giddiness was passing, though the dregs of a headache remained, and his spirits rose with his responsibilities. At daybreak he breakfasted out of the Mearns Street provision box, and made tea in one of the Die-Hards’ camp kettles. Next he gave some attention to his toilet, necessary after the rough-and-tumble of the night. He made shift to bathe in icy water from the Tower well, shaved, tidied up his clothes and found a clean shirt from his pack. He carefully brushed his hair, reminding himself that thus had the Spartans done before Thermopylae. The neat and somewhat pallid young man that emerged from these rites then ascended to the first floor to reconnoitre the landscape from the narrow unglazed windows.

===Chinese===
约翰·赫里蒂奇先生独自守在老塔里，倒觉得有得忙活。晕眩渐渐过去了，头疼的余沥却还留着，而他的兴致随着肩上的担子一路高涨。天刚亮，他就着米恩斯街的食品箱用了早饭，用死硬帮的一只行军锅烧了茶。接着他略略打理了一下仪容——胡乱折腾了一夜之后，这道工序实在必要。他设法用塔井里的冰水擦洗了身子，刮了脸，把衣裳拾掇齐整，又从背包里翻出一件干净衬衫。他还细细梳了头，一面提醒自己：当年斯巴达人（Spartans）在温泉关（Thermopylae）前，也正是这般梳洗的。这番仪式之后出炉的那个整洁而略显苍白的青年，随即便登上二楼，从窄窄的无玻璃窗洞里侦察四野的景象。

===Original===
If anyone had told him a week ago that he would be in so strange a world he would have quarrelled violently with his informant. A week ago he was a cynical clear-sighted modern, a contemner of illusions, a swallower of formulas, a breaker of shams﻿—one who had seen through the heroical and found it silly. Romance and suchlike toys were playthings for fatted middle-age, not for strenuous and cold-eyed youth. But the truth was that now he was altogether spellbound by these toys. To think that he was serving his lady was rapture﻿—ecstasy, that for her he was single-handed venturing all. He rejoiced to be alone with his private fancies. His one fear was that the part he had cast himself for should be needless, that the men from the sea should not come, or that reinforcements would arrive before he should be called upon. He hoped alone to make a stand against thousands. What the upshot might be he did not trouble to inquire. Of course the Princess would be saved, but first he must glut his appetite for the heroic.

===Chinese===
倘若一个礼拜前有人告诉他，说他将落入这样一个光怪陆离的世界，他准会跟报信人激烈地吵上一架。一个礼拜前的他，是个愤世嫉俗、目光冷澈的现代人，一个鄙薄幻象的人，一个把万物吞成公式的人，一个戳穿假面的人——一个早已看穿英雄气概、只觉得它愚不可及的人。传奇这一路的玩物，本是给脑满肠肥的中年人消遣的，轮不到目光冷峻、血气方刚的青年来把玩。可实情是，如今他自己倒叫这些玩物迷了个神魂颠倒。一想到自己正在为心上人效劳，他便心花怒放——销魂蚀骨；为了她，他孤身一人，把身家性命全押上了。他乐意独自守着自己这些绮念。他唯一的忧惧，是自己派给自己的这个角色派不上用场：海上的人不来，或者增援赶在他被召唤之前先到了。他巴不得独力抵挡千军万马。结局如何，他懒得追问。公主自然会得救，可在那之前，他总得先过足一回英雄瘾。

===Original===
He made a diary of events that day, just as he used to do at the front. At twenty minutes past eight he saw the first figure coming from the House. It was Spidel, who limped round the Tower, tried the door, and came to a halt below the window. Heritage stuck out his head and wished him good morning, getting in reply an amazed stare. The man was not disposed to talk, though Heritage made some interesting observations on the weather, but departed quicker than he came, in the direction of the West Lodge.

===Chinese===
这一天的大事，他像从前在前线那样，一一记成日志。八点二十分，他望见第一个人影从大宅那边过来。是斯皮特尔。那人一瘸一拐地绕塔转了一圈，试着推了推门，在窗下站住了。赫里蒂奇探出脑袋道了声早安，得到的回答是一记愕然的瞪视。此人无意交谈，尽管赫里蒂奇就天气发表了几句有趣的见解，他还是走得比来时更急，往西门房方向去了。

===Original===
Just before nine o’clock he returned with Dobson and Léon. They made a very complete reconnaissance of the Tower, and for a moment Heritage thought that they were about to try to force an entrance. They tugged and hammered at the great oak door, which he had further strengthened by erecting behind it a pile of the heaviest lumber he could find in the place. It was imperative that they should not get in, and he got Dickson’s pistol ready with the firm intention of shooting them if necessary. But they did nothing, except to hold a conference in the hazel clump a hundred yards to the north, when Dobson seemed to be laying down the law, and Léon spoke rapidly with a great fluttering of hands. They were obviously puzzled by the sight of Heritage, whom they believed to have left the neighbourhood. Then Dobson went off, leaving Léon and Spidel on guard, one at the edge of the shrubberies between the Tower and the House, the other on the side nearest the Laver glen. These were their posts, but they did sentry-go around the building, and passed so close to Heritage’s window that he could have tossed a cigarette on their heads.

===Chinese===
九点差几分，他带着多布森和莱昂回来了。三个人把塔楼里外侦察了个通透，有一阵子赫里蒂奇还当他们要强行闯入。他们连拽带捶那扇大橡木门——他早先已在门后堆起一座料堆，都是他在此地寻得的最沉的木料，把门又加固了一道。万万不能放他们进来，他把迪克森的手枪备在手边，拿定主意必要时就开枪。可他们终究没动手，只在北面一百码开外的榛树丛里开了个会，会上多布森像是在大发宏论，莱昂语速飞快，两只手扑腾个不住。他们分明让赫里蒂奇这一现形弄糊涂了，本以为他早已离开这一带。随后多布森走开，留下莱昂和斯皮德尔守着，一个守在塔与大宅之间灌木林的边上，一个守在靠拉弗河谷的一侧。这些便是他们的岗位，但两人也绕着楼来回巡哨，走过赫里蒂奇窗下时近得很，他一扬手就能把支香烟丢到他们头上。

===Original===
It occurred to him that he ought to get busy with camouflage. They must be convinced that the Princess was in the place, for he wanted their whole mind to be devoted to the siege. He rummaged among the ladies’ baggage, and extracted a skirt and a coloured scarf. The latter he managed to flutter so that it could be seen at the window the next time one of the watchers came within sight. He also fixed up the skirt so that the fringe of it could be seen, and, when Léon appeared below, he was in the shadow talking rapid French in a very fair imitation of the tones of Cousin Eugénie. The ruse had its effect, for Léon promptly went off to tell Spidel, and when Dobson appeared he too was given the news. This seemed to settle their plans, for all three remained on guard, Dobson nearest to the Tower, seated on an outcrop of rock with his mackintosh collar turned up, and his eyes usually turned to the misty sea.

===Chinese===
他想到该着手摆弄伪装了。务必叫他们相信公主就在此地，因为他要他们把全副心思都押在这场围困上。他在两位夫人的行李里翻检了一通，抽出一条裙子和一方花头巾。他把头巾张罗得不时一飘，好叫下一个望风的进入视野时，能从窗口望见它。他又把裙子安置妥当，露出一线裙边；待莱昂走到下面时，他正隐在暗处，操着一口颇能乱真的欧仁妮表姐的腔调飞快讲法语。这条计策见了效：莱昂立刻跑去报知斯皮德尔，多布森露面时也得了这个信儿。他们的盘算似乎就此定了局，三人一概留守原地，多布森离塔最近，坐在一块石嘴子上，风雨衣的领子竖着，一双眼睛多半望着雾蒙蒙的海。

===Original===
By this time it was eleven o’clock, and the next three hours passed slowly with Heritage. He fell to picturing the fortunes of his friends. Dickson and the Princess should by this time be far inland, out of danger and in the way of finding succour. He was confident that they would return, but he trusted not too soon, for he hoped for a run for his money as Horatius in the Gate. After that he was a little torn in his mind. He wanted the Princess to come back and to be somewhere near if there was a fight going, so that she might be a witness of his devotion. But she must not herself run any risk, and he became anxious when he remembered her terrible sangfroid. Dickson could no more restrain her than a child could hold a greyhound.﻿ ﻿… But of course it would never come to that. The police would turn up long before the brig appeared﻿—Dougal had thought that would not be till high tide, between four and five﻿—and the only danger would be to the pirates. The three watchers would be put in the bag, and the men from the sea would walk into a neat trap. This reflection seemed to take all the colour out of Heritage’s prospect. Peril and heroism were not to be his lot﻿—only boredom.

===Chinese===
这当口已是十一点，往后的三个钟头，赫里蒂奇熬得缓慢。他不由得悬想朋友们的行止。迪克森和公主此刻该已深入内陆，脱了险，也走上了求援的门路。他确信他们会回来，但盼着别回来得太早，因为他指望自己能像桥头独战的霍拉提乌斯（Horatius）那样，痛痛快快打一场。这之后他心里就有点两头拉扯。他盼着公主回来，倘若真有一仗要打，就守在左近，好让她亲眼见证他的赤诚。可她本人绝不能担半点风险，一想起她那吓人的沉着，他就不免忧心。迪克森想拦住她，就像孩童想拽住一条灵缇。……不过事情当然绝不会到那步田地。警察早在双桅船露面之前就该赶到——杜加尔估摸那要等到满潮，四五点钟之间——到时候有险可冒的只剩下那伙海盗。三个望风的会被一网成擒，海上来的那批人会齐齐整整踏进圈套。这样一想，赫里蒂奇的前景仿佛褪尽了颜色。危险与英雄业绩都轮不到他——只剩无聊。

===Original===
A little after twelve two of the tinklers appeared with some news which made Dobson laugh and pat them on the shoulder. He seemed to be giving them directions, pointing seaward and southward. He nodded to the Tower, where Heritage took the opportunity of again fluttering Saskia’s scarf athwart the window. The tinklers departed at a trot, and Dobson lit his pipe as if well pleased. He had some trouble with it in the wind, which had risen to an uncanny violence. Even the solid Tower rocked with it, and the sea was a waste of spindrift and low scurrying cloud. Heritage discovered a new anxiety﻿—this time about the possibility of the brig landing at all. He wanted a complete bag, and it would be tragic if they got only the three seedy ruffians now circumambulating his fortress.

===Chinese===
十二点刚过，两个补锅佬现身，捎来的消息叫多布森咧嘴大笑，直拍他们的肩膀。他像是在面授机宜，一手朝海指，一手朝南指。他朝塔点了点头，赫里蒂奇便趁势又把萨斯基亚的头巾在窗口飘展了一回。补锅佬们小跑着去了，多布森点起烟斗，一副心满意足的样子。风里点这斗烟颇费了他一番手脚，因为风已经刮得邪门地狂暴。连结实的塔身都被摇得晃悠，海面成了一片浪沫翻腾、乱云低卷的荒白。赫里蒂奇添了一桩新的焦心——这一回是怕那双桅船压根儿靠不了岸。他要的是一网打尽、颗粒不漏，倘若只捞着眼下绕着他的堡垒逡巡转圈的那三个獐头鼠目的恶棍，那可就惨透了。

===Original===
About one o’clock he was greatly cheered by the sight of Dougal. At the moment Dobson was lunching off a hunk of bread and cheese directly between the Tower and the House, just short of the crest of the ridge on the other side of which lay the stables and the shrubberies; Léon was on the north side opposite the Tower door, and Spidel was at the south end near the edge of the Garple glen. Heritage, watching the ridge behind Dobson and the upper windows of the House which appeared over it, saw on the very crest something like a tuft of rusty bracken which he had not noticed before. Presently the tuft moved, and a hand shot up from it waving a rag of some sort. Dobson at the moment was engaged with a bottle of porter, and Heritage could safely wave a hand in reply. He could now make out clearly the red head of Dougal.

===Chinese===
一点钟光景，杜加尔的一现叫他大大振奋。其时多布森正坐在塔与大宅之间的正当中，拿一大块面包就干酪当午饭，坐处离山脊的脊线只差几步，脊线那一面便是马厩和灌木林；莱昂在北面，正对塔门，斯皮德尔在南头，挨着加普尔峡谷的崖沿。赫里蒂奇望着多布森背后的山脊和脊线上露出的大宅上层窗户，看见脊顶正中有一簇锈黄的蕨草似的东西，先前不曾留意过。不多时那簇蕨草动了，从中倏地探出一只手，挥着一小块什么破布。多布森这会儿正忙着对付一瓶黑啤酒，赫里蒂奇大可安然回挥一手。这时他已能把杜加尔那颗红脑袋看得清清楚楚了。

===Original===
The Chieftain, having located the three watchers, proceeded to give an exhibition of his prowess for the benefit of the lonely inmate of the Tower. Using as cover a drift of bracken, he wormed his way down till he was not six yards from Dobson, and Heritage had the privilege of seeing his grinning countenance a very little way above the innkeeper’s head. Then he crawled back and reached the neighbourhood of Léon, who was sitting on a fallen Scotch fir. At that moment it occurred to the Belgian to visit Dobson. Heritage’s breath stopped, but Dougal was ready, and froze into a motionless blur in the shadow of a hazel bush. Then he crawled very fast into the hollow where Léon had been sitting, seized something which looked like a bottle, and scrambled back to the ridge. At the top he waved the object, whatever it was, but Heritage could not reply, for Dobson happened to be looking towards the window. That was the last he saw of the Chieftain, but presently he realised what was the booty he had annexed. It must be Léon’s life-preserver, which the night before had broken Heritage’s head.

===Chinese===
首领摸清了三个望风人的方位，便着手给塔里这位孤身的住客表演一番自家身手。他借一垄蕨草作掩护，蛇行蠕进，一直摸到离多布森不足六码之处，赫里蒂奇有幸看见他那副咧嘴而笑的面孔，正悬在客栈老板头顶上不过一点点的地方。随后他爬回去，摸到莱昂左近——那人正坐在一株倒伏的苏格兰松上。恰在此时，那位比利时人起了去寻多布森的念头。赫里蒂奇的呼吸停住了，杜加尔却有备无患，一晃身凝成一团纹丝不动的暗影，隐进一丛榛树的荫翳里。待莱昂走开，他飞快地爬进那人方才坐着的洼坑，抓起一件瞧着像瓶子的东西，手脚并用地翻回山脊。到了脊顶，他把那件玩意儿——不管是什么——高高挥起，赫里蒂奇却无法回应，因为多布森恰好正朝窗口张望。这是他最后一次见到首领，不过片刻之后他就明白了那件战利品的来历。必是莱昂那根灌铅短棍，昨夜敲破赫里蒂奇脑袋的就是它。

===Original===
After that cheering episode boredom again set in. He collected some food from the Mearns Street box, and indulged himself with a glass of liqueur brandy. He was beginning to feel miserably cold, so he carried up some broken wood and made a fire on the immense hearth in the upper chamber. Anxiety was clouding his mind again, for it was now two o’clock, and there was no sign of the reinforcements which Dickson and the Princess had gone to find. The minutes passed, and soon it was three o’clock, and from the window he saw only the top of the gaunt shuttered House, now and then hidden by squalls of sleet, and Dobson squatted like an Eskimo, and trees dancing like a witch-wood in the gale. All the vigour of the morning seemed to have gone out of his blood; he felt lonely and apprehensive and puzzled. He wished he had Dickson beside him, for that little man’s cheerful voice and complacent triviality would be a comfort.﻿ ﻿… Also, he was abominably cold. He put on his waterproof, and turned his attention to the fire. It needed rekindling, and he hunted in his pockets for paper, finding only the slim volume lettered *Whorls*.

===Chinese===
那幕提气的插曲过后，无聊又卷土重来。他从米恩斯街的箱子里取了些吃食，又犒赏自己喝了一杯陈年白兰地。他开始觉得冷得难受，便搬了些碎木头上楼，在上层那间屋子的大壁炉里生起火来。焦虑又在心头聚拢，因为已经两点钟了，迪克森和公主去搬的援兵还不见一点影子。时间一分一分过去，转眼三点钟了，从窗口望去，只看得见那座百叶紧闭、形容枯槁的大宅的顶部，时不时被一阵阵雨雪遮没；多布森蹲踞在那里，活像个爱斯基摩人（Eskimo）；树木在大风里乱舞，好似一片着了魔的巫林。早晨那股血气仿佛全从他血液里泄尽了；他觉得孤单，觉得忐忑，又觉得茫然。他盼着迪克森守在身边，因为那位小个子男人快活的嗓音和自鸣得意的絮叨，会是一帖安慰。……还有，他冷得要命。他披上雨衣，把心思转向火堆。火该重引了，他在口袋里摸纸，只摸到那本薄薄的书，封皮上题着《旋涡》（*Whorls*）。

===Original===
I set it down as the most significant commentary on his state of mind. He regarded the book with intense disfavour, tore it in two, and used a handful of its fine deckle-edged leaves to get the fire going. They burned well, and presently the rest followed. Well for Dickson’s peace of mind that he was not a witness of such vandalism.

===Chinese===
我把这一笔记下，视作对他当时心境最传神的评注。他满脸嫌恶地睨着那本书，一把撕成两半，揪下一把精美的毛边书页去引火。书页烧得很旺，不多时，其余的也步了后尘。迪克森不曾亲眼见证这场暴殄天物的浩劫，对他的心境安宁，真是万幸。

===Original===
A little warmer but in no way more cheerful, he resumed his watch near the window. The day was getting darker, and promised an early dusk. His watch told him that it was after four, and still nothing had happened. Where on earth were Dickson and the Princess? Where in the name of all that was holy were the police? Any minute now the brig might arrive and land its men, and he would be left there as a burnt-offering to their wrath. There must have been an infernal muddle somewhere.﻿ ﻿… Anyhow the Princess was out of the trouble, but where the Lord alone knew.﻿ ﻿… Perhaps the reinforcements were lying in wait for the boats at the Garplefoot. That struck him as a likely explanation, and comforted him. Very soon he might hear the sound of an engagement to the south, and the next thing would be Dobson and his crew in flight. He was determined to be in the show somehow and would be very close on their heels. He felt a peculiar dislike to all three, but especially to Léon. The Belgian’s small baby features had for four days set him clenching his fists when he thought of them.

===Chinese===
身上暖和了些，心绪却没轻快半分，他又回到窗边守望。天色越来越沉，看来要早早入暮。表告诉他已过四点，却仍是什么动静也没有。迪克森和公主究竟在什么地方？看在一切神圣事物的分上，警察又究竟在什么地方？随时随刻，那双桅船都可能到岸卸人，把他孤零零撂在那儿，当他们怒火的燔祭。哪一处环节准是出了天大的乱子。……好在公主总算脱出了这场是非，只是身在何方，惟有上天才晓得。……兴许援兵正埋伏在加普尔河下游，专候那些小艇。这个解释在他看来颇为说得通，便得了些安慰。很快他兴许就能听见南边接火的动静，接下来就该轮到多布森一伙抱头鼠窜了。他打定主意，无论如何也要凑上这场热闹，到时紧咬他们的脚跟不放。他对那三个都怀着一种说不出的憎恶，对莱昂尤甚。那比利时人生就一张娃娃脸，四天来他一想起那张脸，就忍不住攥紧拳头。

===Original===
The next thing he saw was one of the tinklers running hard towards the Tower. He cried something to Dobson, which Heritage could not catch, but which woke the latter to activity. The innkeeper shouted to Léon and Spidel, and the tinkler was excitedly questioned. Dobson laughed and slapped his thigh. He gave orders to the others, and himself joined the tinkler and hurried off in the direction of the Garplefoot. Something was happening there, something of ill omen, for the man’s face and manner had been triumphant. Were the boats landing?

===Chinese===
随后他看见的，是一个补锅佬朝塔没命奔来。他朝多布森喊了句什么，赫里蒂奇没听真切，那句话却把后者从呆坐中唤醒了。客栈老板扬声喊来莱昂和斯皮德尔，众人围着那补锅佬急切盘问。多布森哈哈大笑，一巴掌拍在大腿上。他向另外两人交代了几句，自己跟上补锅佬，匆匆往加普尔河下游方向去了。那边出事了，出的是凶多吉少的事，因为那人的脸色和做派分明是得胜的欢喜。小艇靠岸了么？

===Original===
As Heritage puzzled over this event, another figure appeared on the scene. It was a big man in knickerbockers and mackintosh, who came round the end of the House from the direction of the South Lodge. At first he thought it was the advance-guard from his own side, the help which Dickson had gone to find, and he only restrained himself in time from shouting a welcome. But surely their supports would not advance so confidently in enemy country. The man strode over the slopes as if looking for somebody; then he caught sight of Léon and waved him to come. Léon must have known him, for he hastened to obey.

===Chinese===
赫里蒂奇正为这桩变故犯疑，场上又出现一个人影。那是个穿灯笼裤和风雨衣的大个子，从大宅一头绕出来，来路在南门房那头。起初他还当是自家这边的先头部队，是迪克森搬来的救兵，险些没忍住要出声相迎。可自家的援兵，哪会在敌境里推进得这样大模大样。那人大步流星地横过坡地，像在寻什么人；随后他望见了莱昂，挥手叫他过来。莱昂必然认得他，因为他赶紧领命去了。

===Original===
The two were about thirty yards from Heritage’s window. Léon was telling some story volubly, pointing now to the Tower and now towards the sea. The big man nodded as if satisfied. Heritage noted that his right arm was tied up, and that the mackintosh sleeve was empty, and that brought him enlightenment. It was Loudon the factor, whom Dickson had winged the night before. The two of them passed out of view in the direction of Spidel.

===Chinese===
两人离赫里蒂奇的窗子约莫三十码。莱昂滔滔不绝地讲着什么，一会儿指塔，一会儿指海。大个子点了点头，像是满意了。赫里蒂奇注意到他右臂吊着，风雨衣的袖管空落落的，这一下他恍然大悟。那是代管人劳登，昨夜叫迪克森打伤了胳膊。两人朝斯皮德尔那头走去，出了视野。

===Original===
The sight awoke Heritage to the supreme unpleasantness of his position.
